# PART III: SUNDAYS AND WEEKS OF PASCHALTIDE (SAINT THOMAS THROUGH BLIND MAN)

## Sunday of Saint Thomas (Antipascha)
*Which is the Touching of the Holy and Glorious Apostle Thomas.*

*It is fitting to know that on this holy First Sunday, Antipascha, the Resurrectional service is not sung, but all of the Feast. And the service of the Menaion saint occurring on this day is sung at Compline on the same Sunday.*  
*(Note: On Saturday evening, at Small Vespers, we set 4 stichera. And we sing on "Lord, I have cried" and on "Vouchsafe, O Lord" from the festal prosomoia, doubling the feast. In this manner it is done at Small Vespers when the Octoechos is not sung at Vigils. Glory: Both now: if the Ecclesiarch wills to bless Kolyvo for the Saint, the idiomelon of the day).*  
*On "Lord, I have cried", Stichera, Tone 1:*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the Celestial Ranks" (Не́бны чинѡ):*  
Christ patiently endures being touched in His love for mankind, even as He endured the Cross before this and the unjust slaughter; and having risen on the third day from the tomb, He did not break the seals, and while the doors were shut He stood among the disciples, being almighty!  
The unbelief of Thomas confirms the world-saving rising from the treasuries of Hades of the God-man, the Word; with searching right hand he boldly touches the wounds of His hands and feet unto the assurance of the world!  
When the Apostles were gathered together in fear because of Thy Passion, O Word, and while the doors were locked, Thou didst suddenly come into their midst granting them peace...  

...and stretching forth unto Thomas to touch the precious wounds of Thy side!  
*Glory: Both now: Tone 2:*  
After Thy rising, O Lord, when Thy disciples were gathered together and the doors were shut, Thou didst stand in their midst, granting them peace; and Thomas, assured by the sight of Thy hands and side, confessed Thee to be Lord and God, Who savest those that hope in Thee, O Lover of mankind!  
*At the Aposticha, Prosomoia, Tone 2:*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Еѵфра́ѳѡвъ):*  
With precious hymns, ye men, sing with the Angels unto Him Who is risen on the third day from the tomb and hath raised the world with Himself!  
*Stichos:* Praise the Lord, O Jerusalem; praise thy God, O Zion. (Ps. 147:1)  
Thou didst appear unto Thine ordained Apostles, O Savior, while the doors were shut, renewing for us through them Thy Holy Spirit!  
*Stichos:* For He hath strengthened the bars of thy gates. (Ps. 147:2)  
Having seen Thee now, not with our eyes, but having believed in God with heartfelt love, we magnify Thee in hymns!  
*Glory: Both now: In the same Tone and Podoben:*  
Granting the Savior and Redeemer unto the world, grant also unto Thy people remission of debts, through the prayers of the all-pure, only Mother of God!  
*Then:* Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master... *Troparion, Tone 7:*  
While the tomb was sealed, Thou didst shine forth Life from the tomb, O Christ God; and while the doors were shut, Thou didst stand among the disciples, the Resurrection of all, renewing an upright spirit in us through them, according to Thy great mercy!  

*At Great Vespers:*  
*After the Priest gives the blessing, "Christ is risen..." (thrice). And immediately we sing Psalm 103, the Introductory Psalm, with chanting according to custom. Likewise the Stichology: the entire 1st Kathisma. On "Lord, I have cried", we set 10 stichera. Idiomelon Stichera, Tone 1:[^527]*  
*Sticheron:* On the eighth day of Thy rising, O Lord, Thou didst appear unto Thy disciples in the place where they were gathered, and having proclaimed unto them: Peace be unto you! Thou didst show Thy hands and Thy most pure side unto the disbelieving disciple; and he, having believed, cried out unto Thee: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee!  
*Sticheron:* While the doors were shut and the disciples were gathered together, Thou didst enter suddenly, O Almighty Jesus our God; and standing in their midst, having given peace, Thou didst fill them with the Holy Spirit, and didst command them to wait, and by no means to depart from Jerusalem, until they were clothed with power from on high. Therefore do we cry aloud unto Thee: O our Enlightenment, and Resurrection, and Peace, glory to Thee!  
*Sticheron:* Thomas, called the Twin, was not with them when Thou didst enter, O Christ, while the doors were locked; therefore he disbelieved the things spoken unto him, seeking to be assured from unbelief into faith: Thou didst not disdain, O Good One, to show unto him Thy most pure side, and the wounds of Thy hands and feet...  

...and he, having touched and seen, confessed Thee to be neither naked God nor mere man, and cried out: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee! *(twice)*  
*Sticheron:* While the disciples were doubting, the Savior appeared on the eighth day where they were gathered, and having given peace, cried unto Thomas: Come, Apostle, touch the palms in which they drove the nails! O good unbelief of Thomas, which brought the hearts of the faithful unto understanding! And with fear he cried aloud: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee!  
*Sticheron, Tone 2:* After Thy rising, O Lord, when Thy disciples were gathered together and the doors were shut, Thou didst stand in their midst granting them peace; and Thomas, having believed by the sight of Thy hands and side, confessed Thee to be Lord and God, Who savest those that hope in Thee, O Lover of mankind!  
*Sticheron:* Standing among the disciples while the doors were shut, Jesus gave fearlessness and peace; then He says unto Thomas: Why dost thou disbelieve Me, that I am risen from the dead? Bring hither thy hand and place it into My side, and see! For while thou wast disbelieving, all were taught to proclaim My Passion and Resurrection together with thee: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee!  
*Glory: Both now: Tone 6, by John the Monk:[^528]*  
While the doors were shut, Thou camest unto the disciples, O Christ; then Thomas, by dispensation, was not found with them; for he said unto them in unbelief: Unless I also see the Master, and see the side whence flowed the Blood, the Water, the Baptism; unless I see the wound whence was healed the great wound of mankind; unless I see how He was not as a spirit, but flesh and bones! O Thou Who didst trample down death and convince Thomas: Lord, glory to Thee!  
*Entrance with Censer. "O Joyful Light". Prokeimenon of the Day: "The Lord is King..."*  
*At the Litiya, Idiomelon Stichera, Tone 3:*  
While the doors were shut, Thou didst come, O Lord, with the unendurable radiance of Thy Divinity; and standing in the midst of the disciples, Thou didst bare Thy side, and showing the prints of the nails in Thy hands and the marks of Thy feet, dispelling grief and sorrow, Thou didst cry aloud: Even as ye see in Me, My friends, the assumption of flesh, I bear not the nature of a spirit! And unto the doubting disciple Thou didst command to touch with trembling, saying: Having examined, go forth henceforth without doubting! And he, having perceived with his hand Thy fiery Divinity, with fear cried aloud faithfully, drawn by faith: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee!  
*Sticheron, Tone 8:* Touch My side with thy hand, says Christ, and come, feel the prints of the nails, examine in faith, and be faithful unto Me, and be not faithless! And Thomas, when he touched the Master with his finger, cried out with a loud voice: Thou art my God and Lord, O Tender-Hearted One, glory to Thee!  
*Glory: Both now: Tone 8, Anatolian:*  
While the doors were shut and the disciples were gathered together, the Savior came where they were gathered, and standing in their midst says unto Thomas: Come, touch, and behold the prints of the nails; stretch forth thy hand and touch My side, and be not faithless, but in faith proclaim My Resurrection from the dead!  
*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon Stichera, Tone 4:*  
*Sticheron:* O marvelous wonder! Unbelief gave birth to sure faith! For Thomas said: Unless I see, I will not believe; but having touched His side, he theologized the Incarnate One, recognized Him to be the same Son of God, and proclaimed that He suffered in the flesh...  

...Him Who is risen as God, and cried aloud in radiant voice: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee!  
*Stichos:* Praise the Lord, O Jerusalem. (Ps. 147:1)  
*Sticheron:* O marvelous wonder! Grass touching fire was saved! For Thomas put his hand into the fiery side of Jesus Christ God, and was not consumed by the touch; for the soul turned away from evil unto true faith, and he cried out warmly from the depths of his soul: Thou art my Master and my God, Who art risen from the dead, glory to Thee!  
*Stichos:* For He hath strengthened the bars of thy gates. (Ps. 147:2)  
*Sticheron:* O marvelous wonder! John leaned upon the breast of the Word; but Thomas was accounted worthy to touch His side: the one indeed from thence dreadly drew forth the deep contemplation of Theology; while the other was accounted worthy mystically to instruct us, presenting clear demonstrations of His rising, crying aloud: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee!  
*Glory: Both now: Tone 5:*  
O Lover of mankind, great and unsearchable is the multitude of Thy compassions! For Thou didst endure being buffeted by the Jews, touched by an Apostle, and greatly searched out by those who reject Thee: how Thou wast incarnate, and how Thou wast crucified, O Sinless One! But grant us understanding, even as Thou didst unto Thomas, to cry aloud unto Thee: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee!  
*Then:* Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace... *After "Our Father": Troparion, Tone 7 (thrice):* While the tomb was sealed... *(see fol. 200). And the Blessing of Loaves. And the Reading of the Acts of the Holy Apostles, or the Homilies of John of Golden Speech.*  

*If anywhere for a justifiable reason a Vigil cannot be celebrated, we sing the Triadic Canon of the Octoechos for the Angelic Tone, sung at the Midnight Office, whose acrostic or (*краегране́сїе*) is:[^529]*  
(`ΜΙΑΝ ΣΕ ΜΕЛΠΩ ΤΗΝ ТРІСНЛІОН ФҮСІН`)
*That is: "Singing, I hymn Thee, the one Tri-radiant Nature." A creation of Metrophanes of Smyrna, Tone 4: "Thy victorious right hand."*  
*It is fitting to know that from this Sunday even unto All Saints, we make the first reading from the Acts of the Apostles; and when they are finished, we begin the Homilies of Chrysostom, and begin them again. At the conclusion of such reading, we say: "Having heard...", that is, the Six Psalms, and the rest of Matins.*  

*Typikon Chapter Concerning the Concurrence of Saint George:*  
*It is fitting to know: If the commemoration of the Holy Great-Martyr George should coincide on this Sunday of Thomas, Chapter 105:[^530]*  
*At Small Vespers: Stichera of the feast on 4. Glory: to the Saint. Both now: of the feast. At the Aposticha: Stichera to the Saint with his verses. Glory: to the Saint. Both now: of the feast. Troparion to the Saint. Glory: Both now: of the feast. Little Litany, and Dismissal.*  
*At Great Vespers: According to custom: "Blessed is the man..." On "Lord, I have cried": Stichera of the feast on 6, and to the Saint on 4. Glory: to the Saint, Tone 6: "It is meet and right..." Both now: of the feast. Entrance, and Readings of the Saint. At the Litiya: of the feast, Tone 6: "O Lord, with the unendurable..."; then Tone 8: "Touch My side, O Thomas..." Glory: to the Saint, Tone 5: "Spring hath shone forth..." Both now: of the feast, Tone 8: "While the doors were shut..." At the Aposticha: of the feast. Glory: to the Saint, Tone 4: "Of the noetic Adam..." Both now: of the feast, Tone 5: "O Lover of mankind, great..." At the blessing of the loaves: Troparion of the feast (twice), and to the Saint (once). And reading on Sunday from the Acts of the Apostles.*  
*At Matins: At "God is the Lord": Troparion of the feast (twice). Glory: to the Saint. Both now: of the feast. After the Kathismata: Sessional hymns of the feast and the reading of the day. Polyeleos and Sessional hymns of the feast and reading of the Saint. Anabathmoi: 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon: "Let every breath..." Gospel of the feast, from Matthew, Pericope 116. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ" (thrice). Psalm 50. Glory: Through the intercessions of the Apostles... Both now: Through the intercessions of the Theotokos... Sticheron of the feast, Tone 6: "While the doors were shut..." Canons: of the feast with the Heirmos on 8, and to the Saint on 6. Katavasia: Heirmos: "The Day of Resurrection...", both choirs together. After Ode 3: Kontakion to the Saint and Sessional hymn; Glory: Both now: of the feast, and the reading in the Prologue: "Read the Renewal". After Ode 6: Kontakion and Ikos of the feast, and reading in the Prologue and Synaxarion. At Ode 9: we do not sing the verses, but "Holy is the Lord our God" in Tone 4. Svitylen of the feast: "My disciples...", Glory: to the Saint, Both now: of the feast: "Today..." At the Praises: Stichera of the feast, 3, and to the Saint, 3. Stichos in Tone 6: "God is magnified in the righteous...", and to the Saint, 4... Glory: of the feast. Both now: "Thou art all-glorious..." Great Doxology. Troparion to the Saint; Glory: Both now: of the feast. Litany, Dismissal, and customary procession. Glory: Both now: Gospel Sticheron. Chanted in the Narthex. At the Hours: Troparion of the feast; Glory: to the Saint; Both now: Kontakion of the feast. At the Divine Liturgy: Beatitudes of the feast, Ode 3 on 4, and to the Saint, Ode 6 on 4. After the Entrance: Troparion of the feast and to the Saint. Glory: Kontakion to the Saint; Both now: Kontakion of the feast. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion verse first of the feast, then of the Saint.*  

*Sunday of Saint Thomas: At Matins*  

*After the Six Psalms, at "God is the Lord", Troparion of the feast (thrice, see fol. 200).*  
*After the 1st Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "While the Stone was Sealed" (Ка́мени зна:):*  
When the disciples were hidden for fear of the Jews, and were gathered together on Zion, Thou didst come unto them, O Good One, and didst stand in their midst while the doors were shut, creating joy for them; and Thou didst show Thy hands and the wounds of Thy most pure side, saying unto the disbelieving disciple: Bring hither thy hand and examine, for I Myself am He Who suffered for thy sake!  
*Glory: Both now: The same.*  
*After the 2nd Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy Tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й сп҃се):*  
Thou didst stand among the disciples while the doors were locked, O Life, and showing Thy side, Thy hands, and Thy feet, confirming beforehand Thy rising from the dead; but Thomas was not found; therefore he said: Unless I see Him, I will not believe your words!  
*Glory: Both now: Tone 1:*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "The Angelic Choir" (Ли́къ а҆́гглскїй):*  
Thou didst rise from the dead, the Life of all, and didst appear unto the disciples on the eighth day. But Thomas the Twin was not with them when Thou didst appear unto them; therefore, O Master, Thou didst not disdain to show unto him the piercing of Thy divine side and of Thy hands, as the Lover of mankind!  
*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy Tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й сп҃се):*  
Beholding My side and the wounds of the nails, O Thomas, why dost thou disbelieve My Resurrection? the Lord said, having arisen from the tomb, appearing ineffably unto the Apostles; and the Twin, having believed, cried aloud unto the Creator: Thou art my God and Lord!  
*Glory: Both now: The same.*  
*Then the Anabathmoi: 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4:* Praise the Lord, O Jerusalem; praise thy God, O Zion. (Ps. 147:1) *Stichos:* For He hath strengthened the bars of thy gates. (Ps. 147:2)  
*Let every breath praise the Lord. Matins Gospel 1, from Matthew, Pericope 116 (Matt. 28:16–20):* At that time, the eleven disciples... *Then: "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ" (thrice). Psalm 50. Glory: Through the intercessions of the Apostles... Both now: Through the intercessions of the Theotokos... Stichos: Have mercy on me, O God... Sticheron, Tone 6: "While the doors were shut..." (written at Great Vespers on "Lord, I have cried", Glory: Both now, see fol. 201). Or as the Greek custom prefers: "Jesus having risen from the tomb..." And: "Save, O God, Thy people..."*  

*Canon of Thomas Sunday, a creation of John the Monk, Tone 1:[^531]*  
*Heirmos twice, and the troparia on 12.*  
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
Let us all sing a song of victory unto Him Who delivered Israel from bitter servitude to Pharaoh, and led them dryshod through the depth of the sea; for He is glorified!  
*Troparion:* Today is springtime for our souls, because Christ, having shone forth from the tomb like the sun on the third day, hath driven away the dark tempest of our sin; let us hymn Him, for He is glorified!  
*Troparion:* The Queen of seasons, bringing gifts unto the queen of days, the radiant day, adorns the chosen people of the Church, unceasingly hymning the Risen Christ!  
*Troparion:* Neither the gates of death, O Christ, nor the seals of the tomb, nor the bolts of the doors resisted Thee; but having risen, Thou didst stand among Thy friends, O Master, granting peace which passes all understanding!  
*Katavasia: "The Day of Resurrection...", both choirs together.*  
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
Establish me, O Christ, upon the immovable rock of Thy commandments, and enlighten me with the light of Thy countenance; for there is none holy above Thee, O Lover of mankind!  
*Troparion:* Having made us new instead of old, and having made us incorruptible through Thy corruptless Cross, O Christ, Thou didst command us to live worthily in newness of life!  
*Troparion:* In the tomb enclosed within Thy circumscribed flesh, O Uncircumscribed Christ, Thou didst rise, and while the doors were shut, Thou didst stand among Thine own disciples, O Almighty One!  
*Troparion:* Thy wounds, O Christ, which Thou didst willingly receive for the disciples...  

...preserving them for Thy disciples, Thou didst show forth as a witness of Thy glorious Resurrection!  
*Katavasia: "Come, let us drink a new drink..."*  
*Hypakoë, Tone 6:*  
Even as Thou didst come into the midst of Thy disciples, O Savior, granting them peace: come also unto us and save us!  
*Glory: Both now: The same.*  
*And the Reading in the Theologian (St. Gregory the Theologian), whereof the incipit is: "To honor renewal..."[^532]*  
*Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
A great mystery is the dispensation of Thy coming, O Christ; for Habakkuk, foreseeing this from on high, cried out unto Thee: Thou didst go forth for the salvation of Thy people, O Lover of mankind!  
*Troparion:* Healing of old the taste of the bitter fruit, Christ now by the honeycomb hath granted enlightenment unto our forefather, and His own sweet communion!  
*Troparion:* Having drawn wealth from an instealable treasure, by divine grace, from Thy side pierced with the spear, the Twin fills the world with wisdom and understanding!  
*Troparion:* Thou rejoicest when searched out, O Lover of mankind; therefore Thou dost command Thomas, stretching forth Thy pierced side, confirming unto the world, O Christ, Thy third-day rising!  
*Troparion:* Thy most blessed tongue is praised, O Twin! For it first piously proclaimed Jesus as God the Life-Giver, filled with grace from His breathing!  
*Katavasia: "Upon the divine watchtower..."*  
*Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
Rising early from the night, we hymn Thee, O Christ, Co-unoriginate with the Father, and Savior of our souls, Who grantest peace unto the world, O Lover of mankind!  
*Troparion:* Standing among His grieving friends, the Savior by His coming takes away their sorrow, and moves them to dance by His Resurrection!  
*Troparion:* O truly praised and awesome undertaking of Thomas! For boldly did he touch the side that shone with divine fire!  
*Troparion:* The unbelief of Thomas was shown unto us as the mother of faith; for Thou in Thy wisdom dost provide what is profitable, as the Lover of mankind!  
*Katavasia: "Let us awake at the dawn of early morning..."*  
*Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
Thou didst save the prophet from the whale, O Lover of mankind; lead me also up from the depth of sins, I pray Thee!  
*Troparion:* Thou didst not leave Thomas, O Master, sinking in the deep of unbelief, stretching forth Thy hands for examination!  
*Troparion:* Our Savior said: Touching Me, see that I bear flesh and bones; I have not changed!  
*Troparion:* Thomas touched Thy side, and having believed, recognized Thee, not having been present at Thy first coming, O our Savior!  
*Katavasia: "Thou didst descend into the deepest parts of the earth..."*  
*Kontakion, Tone 8:*  
With his searching right hand, Thomas probed Thy life-giving side, O Christ God; for when Thou didst enter while the doors were shut, he cried out unto Thee with the rest of the Apostles: Thou art my Lord and my God!  
*Ikos:* Who preserved the disciple's palm unconsumed at that time, when he approached the Lord's fiery side? Who gave it boldness and enabled it to touch the flaming bone, in every way untouchable? For if the side had not imparted power unto the clay right hand, how could it touch the Passions which shook all things above and below? This grace was given unto Thomas, to touch these things, and to cry aloud unto Christ: Thou art my Lord and my God!  
*Then the Prologue. And the Synaxarion of Thomas Sunday by Nikephoros Kallistos Xanthopoulos:[^533]*  
*Verses:*  
*If the key of the seals and the tomb lets Thee pass:*  
*How does the bolt of the doors hinder Thee as Thou comest, O Savior?*  
On this Second Sunday after Pascha, we celebrate the Renewal of Christ's Resurrection, and the Touching of the Holy Apostle Thomas. The ancient custom was to observe a renewal on certain notable events, as the year revolved; and on that day, since this work was done, when that event recurred, they made a yearly memorial...  

...lest great deeds fall into oblivion. Thence the Hebrews first kept the Passover in Gilgal, renewing the crossing of the Red Sea; thence the Tabernacle of the Testimony was renewed among them with great festivity; thence the Kingdom of David, and others, that I speak not of each one. Since therefore the Lord's Resurrection is a work more excellent and greater than all things in this life, and surpassing all human thought: not only every year do we renew it, but continually every eighth day. And the first renewal thereof is this present Sunday, which is truly called both the Eighth and the First: the Eighth, as being reckoned from Pascha; and the First, as the beginning of the others; and again the Eighth, as an image of that unending eighth day of the age to come, which shall be one and first, not cut asunder by any night. These things concerning renewals.  
Now concerning Thomas, it was on this wise:  
When He appeared late on that day of the Resurrection unto the disciples, Thomas was not there, not having yet assembled with the rest for fear of the Jews; then coming shortly after, and having heard of Christ's coming, he disbelieved not only the disciples, because they had seen Him as a man, but also that the Lord was risen on that day; and he was one of the Twelve. But God, full of good counsel, cares thus for one, and together with a greater dispensation, that He might assure those that should follow even more of the Resurrection, left eight days. That He might thoroughly sharpen his love, and that through his disbelief God might grant unto all a more certain assurance of the Resurrection, He comes again, and while the doors were shut, even as before, when Thomas also was present, He enters; and having given peace as was customary, He then stretches forth unto Thomas and says: Bring hither thy finger, and see My hands; and bring hither thy hand, and place it into My side, and be not faithless, but believing! For seeing that thou wast to be assured not by sight alone; He showed by this that when he spoke these things unto the disciples, He had heard him: "Place thy hand into My side." And it shows also that the wound in His side was broad, so as even to receive a hand within. And Thomas, having accurately searched and received faith by touching (for it was granted unto him to see and do these things, even though His flesh had inclined unto what is incorruptible and deified), for full assurance cried aloud: "My Lord and my God!" The one indeed on account of His flesh, and the other on account of His Godhead. And Christ said unto Thomas: "Because thou hast seen Me, thou hast believed; blessed are they that have not seen, and yet have believed."  
Thomas is called the Twin (Didymus), either because he was born a twin with another, or because he doubted concerning the Resurrection, or because from nature two fingers of his right hand were joined together,[^534] that is, the middle finger and that which by distinction is called the index finger, as though one should say, he was destined to doubt and to touch with these. Others say, which is more certain, that Thomas is interpreted as "Twin".  
This was the second appearance of Christ; the third was at the Sea of Tiberias, at the catching of the fish, when He also partook of food, which was consumed by divine fire as He Himself knows, presenting a most certain assurance of the Resurrection; then He appeared at Emmaus; fifth, in Galilee, unto the Eleven, as it says: He appeared, until He was taken up, working many and supernatural signs before the disciples after the Resurrection; for He showed not these things unto many. All these things the Evangelists were moved to write down, because it was not possible for many, and for men living in the world, to hear them, seeing that they were exceedingly supernatural.  
By the prayers of that Apostle Thomas, O Christ our God, have mercy on us, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.  

*Ode 7. Heirmos:[^535]*  
When the musical harmony summoned the people to worship the image, singing Zion-like songs from the images, the youths of the Virgin destroyed the tyrant's wicked decree, and turned the flame into dew, singing the hymn: O God of our fathers and our God, supremely exalted, blessed art Thou!  
*Troparion:* As the first of days and the sovereign, light-bearing son, wherein it is meet for the new and divine people to rejoice with trembling; for it brings the image of the age to come, as an eighth day: O God of our fathers and our God, supremely exalted, blessed art Thou!  
*Troparion:* A bold mind, bringing us faith through unbelief, Thomas the Twin dispels the dark ignorance of all the ends of the earth by his faithful unbelief, and weaves a crown for himself, saying unto Thee: Thou art the Lord, O God of our fathers and our God, supremely exalted, blessed art Thou!  
*Troparion:* Not in vain did Thomas doubt Thy rising, nor was he cast down; but he strove to show Christ without doubt unto all nations, whence...

...having assured all by disbelief, taught them to say: Thou art the Lord, supremely exalted above the fathers and our God, blessed art Thou!  
*Troparion:* With fear, Thomas placed his hand into Thy life-bearing side, O Christ, and trembling, he perceived the twofold operation, O Savior, in the two natures united in Thee without confusion; and with faith he cried aloud, saying: Thou art the Lord, supremely exalted above the fathers and our God, blessed art Thou!  
*Katavasia: "The Children delivered from the furnace..."[^536]*  

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
Unto Him Who preserved the children in the burning furnace of flame, and descended unto them in the likeness of an Angel: sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!  
*Troparion:* Having yearned for Thy joyful sight, Thomas, who formerly had disbelieved, was accounted worthy thereof; and he named Thee God and Lord, O Master, Whom we supremely exalt unto all ages!  
*Troparion:* Unto Him Who cast away the disbelief of Thomas, and showed His side, and was touched by his palm: sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!  
*Troparion:* Having theologically proclaimed with a God-bearing tongue Thy difficult-to-attain treasure hidden for ages, Thomas said: Sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!  
*Katavasia: "This chosen and holy day..." We do not sing the Magnificat ("More honorable...").*  

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
Thee, the radiant lamp, and Mother of God, all-rooted glory, and higher than all creatures, we magnify with hymns!  
*Troparion:* Thy radiant day and all-radiant Christ of all-radiant grace, wherein Thou didst appear in fair beauty unto Thy disciples, we magnify in hymns!  
*Troparion:* Thee, touched in the side with an earthen palm, yet not consuming them with the fire of the immaterial Divine Essence, we magnify in hymns!  
*Troparion:* Thee, Christ, as God risen from the tomb, not having beheld with our eyes, but having believed with heartfelt love, we magnify with hymns!  
*Katavasia: "Shine, shine, O New Jerusalem..."*  

*Holy is the Lord our God: In Tone 4 (thrice).*  
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Having Adorned the Heavens with Stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣ́здами):*  
Having searched out with thy hand the wounds of My members, be not faithless unto Me, O Thomas, Who was wounded for thy sake; be of one mind with the disciples, and proclaim the living God![^537] *(Twice).*  
*Glory: Both now: In the same Tone:*  
Today the spring breathes fragrance, and the new creation dances; today the bolts of the doors are taken away, as the friend of disbelief, Thomas, cries aloud: My Lord and my God!  

*At the Praises: Let every breath praise the Lord. We set 4 Stichera.*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "O All-Praised Martyrs" (Прехва́лнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*  
O Life-Giver, at Thy fearful rising from the tomb, even as Thou didst not break the seals of the sepulcher, O Christ, so while the doors were shut, Thou didst enter unto Thy most glorious Apostles, making joy for them, and granting Thy right Spirit unto them, because of Thy boundless mercy! *(Twice).*  
Thomas, who is also called the Twin, had not arrived when Thou didst appear unto the disciples, O Lord; therefore he disbelieved Thy Resurrection, and unto those who had seen Thee he cried out: Unless I place my finger into His side and into the wounds of the nails, I will not believe that He is risen!  
Touch as thou wilt, Christ cried out unto Thomas: place thy hand, and know Me that I have bones and earthen flesh, and be not faithless, but be assured equally with the rest! And he cried aloud: Thou art my God and Lord, glory to Thy rising!  

*Glory: Tone 6:*  
After eight days from Thy rising, O Jesus, King, Only-Begotten Word of the Father, Thou didst appear unto Thy disciples...  

...unto Thy disciples, while the doors were shut, granting them Thy peace; and unto the disbelieving disciple Thou didst show Thy wounds, saying: Come and touch My hands and My feet, and My incorruptible side! And he, having believed, cried aloud: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee!  
*Both now: "Thou art most blessed..." Great Doxology.*  
*Troparion of the feast: While the tomb was sealed... (see fol. 200). Litany, and Dismissal.*  

*From this day we begin the customary Litiya, or procession into the narthex, as also the Catechesis, which is the Instructions of Theodore the Studite.[^538]*  
*We go, singing the Self-Melodic Sticheron of Matins, Tone 1:*  
As the disciples were going up into the mountain for the earthly Ascension, the Lord stood in their midst; and having worshipped Him, and being instructed in the power given everywhere, they were sent under heaven to preach the Resurrection from the dead, and the Ascension into heaven; with whom Christ God, the true Savior of our souls, promised to abide unto the ages.  

*Take heed that the Litiya in the narthex takes place before the Third Hour, and the Studite Catechesis is read after the First Hour; and the Sticheron of Vespers is sung after the Matins Dismissal before the Litiya, even unto the Sunday of All Saints. First Hour, and the rest according to custom, and the final Dismissal.*  
*The Divine Liturgy takes place earlier on account of the fatigue of the Vigil. At the Hours, we read the three psalms according to custom in the narthex, that is, the 3rd, 6th, and 9th. Troparion and Kontakion of the feast. The customary psalms are read as we go out into the refectory.*  
*At the Divine Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the day from the Canon of the feast, Ode 3 and Ode 6. After the Entrance: Troparion: "While the tomb was sealed..." Glory: Both now: Kontakion: "With his searching right hand..." Trisagion. Prokeimenon, Tone 3: Great is our Lord, and great is His power, and His understanding is without measure. (Ps. 146:5 LXX) Stichos: Praise the Lord, for a psalm is good; let praise be sweet unto our God. (Ps. 146:1 LXX) Epistle from the Acts of the Apostles, Pericope 14 (Acts 5:12–20): "In those days, by the hands of the Apostles..." Alleluia, Tone 8: Come, let us rejoice in the Lord... Stichos: Let us come before His face with thanksgiving... Stichos: For the Lord is a great God... Gospel: 4th Matins Resurrection Gospel, Pericope 120 (John 20:19–31): "Being late on that day..." Communion Verse: Praise the Lord, O Jerusalem; praise thy God, O Zion. Alleluia. In the refectory there is consolation for the brethren.*  

## On the Same Sunday Evening

*At "Lord, I have cried", we set 6 Stichera: of the feast, 3; and of the Menaion, 3.*  
*Stichera of the feast, Tone 1:*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "O All-Praised Martyrs" (Прехва́лнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*  
Touch, the Lover of mankind, Who rose on the third day from the dead, says unto Thomas: and behold My hands, and the nailing of My feet, and the piercing of My side, and understand Me to be immutable God, fashioned in an ensouled body through earthly commingling, not taking suffering into the Divine Nature! *(Twice).*  
The Twin, having doubted, beholding the wounds of the feet and the nail-prints of the hands, was astonished at the strangeness of the miracles; and touching and beholding with his hand the most pure side, he showed forth unto the nations the third-day rising from the tomb, which is beyond understanding!  
I receive great joy, beholding Thy abundant rising, O my Savior, wherein Thomas was accounted worthy to touch daring things; therefore he confessed Thee as simple God and man, bearing two sacred operations, of which Thou wast composed!  
*Other Stichera in the Menaion of the day, 3. Glory: Both now: Tone 1:*  
While the doors were shut and the disciples were gathered together, Thou didst enter suddenly, O Almighty Jesus our God; and standing in their midst, having given peace, Thou didst fill them with the Holy Spirit, and didst command them to wait, and by no means to depart from Jerusalem, until they should be clothed with power from on high; therefore we cry unto Thee: Our Enlightenment and Resurrection, and our Peace, glory to Thee! *Entrance.*  
*O Gladsome Light. Great Prokeimenon, Tone 7: Who is so great a god as our God? Thou art the God Who workest wonders! (Ps. 76:14–15 LXX)[^539] Stichos: Thou hast made known Thy power among the peoples. Stichos: I said: Now have I begun; this change is of the right hand of the Most High. Stichos: I remembered the works of the Lord, for I will remember Thy wonders from the beginning. And again: Who is so great a god as our God? Litany. And: Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this evening without sin.*  
*At the Aposticha, Resurrection Sticheron of the Octoechos, Tone 1:*  
Receive our evening prayers, O Holy Lord, and grant us remission of sins, for Thou alone art He Who showed forth the Resurrection unto the world! *Stichos: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes... (Ps. 122:1 LXX)* Because a great deep of transgressions is in me, O Savior, and I am sorely sinking in mine iniquities: give me a hand, save me, O God, as Thou didst Peter, and have mercy on me! *Stichos: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us... (Ps. 122:3 LXX)*
Through the prayers of all the Saints and of the Theotokos, grant us Thy peace, O Lord, and have mercy on us, as the only compassionate One!  
*Glory: Both now: Tone 1:*  
After eight days from Thy rising, O Lord, Thou didst appear unto Thy disciples in the place where they were gathered; and having proclaimed unto them: Peace be unto you! Thou didst show Thy hands and Thy most pure side unto the disbelieving disciple; and he, having believed, cried aloud unto Thee: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee!  
*Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace... Trisagion. And after "Our Father": Troparion of the feast: While the tomb was sealed... (see fol. 200). Litany, and Dismissal. And the customary Litiya in the narthex. We sing the Self-Melodic Sticheron of the Holy Patron of the temple according to the tone of the day.*  
*Take heed: In this manner is the Order of Vespers celebrated throughout the entire week of Pentecostarion, exchanging the Stichera and Troparia, even as we have indicated.*  
*At Compline, the service of the Saint of the day who falls herein, as on fol. 200.*  

## On Monday of the Second Week after Pascha

*At Matins: After the Priest makes the Initial Blessing, we say: Christ is risen from the dead... (thrice), in a quiet voice.*  
*This we do at the beginning of every service, at the Hours, at Vespers, and at Compline, from this Matins of the Sunday of Antipascha. For be it known concerning the Midnight Office, as was set forth on this Sunday, that from thence also such is the beginning.[^540] Immediately after "Christ is risen":*  
*Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace... And the Six Psalms.*  
*This order continues until the Wednesday of the Eve of the Ascension of Christ into Heaven.*  
*At "God is the Lord", Troparion of the feast (thrice), Tone 7: While the tomb was sealed... (see fol. 200).*  
*Take heed: For this is said throughout the entire week. And we stichologize 2 Kathismata; and the third in order is read at Vespers; and thus we read the Psalter until the Kathisma appointed for the day.*  
*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymns, Tone 1:[^541]*  
The soldiers guarding Thy tomb, O Savior, became as dead from the dazzling brilliance of the Angel who appeared, preaching the Resurrection unto the women; we glorify Thee, the Destroyer of corruption; we fall down before Thee, Who didst rise from the tomb, our only God! *Stichos: O Lord, rebuke me not in Thine anger... (Ps. 6:2 LXX). Penitential:*  
In transgressions was I conceived, a prodigal;[^542] I dare not look up unto the height of heaven, but hoping in Thy love for mankind, I cry: O God, cleanse me a sinner, and save me! *Stichos: God is marvelous in His saints. (Ps. 67:36 LXX)*  
The passion-bearers clothed themselves in Thee, O Lord; for by the endurance of wounds they conquered the lawless, and by divine power received victory from heaven; by their intercessions grant us great mercy! *Glory: Both now: Theotokion:*  
O full of grace, beholding the wonder of wonders in thee, creation rejoices; for thou didst conceive ineffably, and didst bear without seed Him Whom the ranks of Angels cannot gaze upon; pray Him as God for our souls! *Then the Reading from the Commentary on the Gospel of Jesus.*  
*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy Tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й, Спа́се):*  
By touching the unendurable side with his hand, the Twin Thomas was not consumed by the touch, but remained examining the abundant wounds of Him Who was pierced for our sake: Thou art my Lord and my God, even though Thou didst endure suffering! *Glory: Both now: The same.*  
*Reading. Then: "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ" (once). And Psalm 50. Thus we do every day until the Ascension. But we do not chant the tone verses, that is, we do not stichologize the biblical canticles in this week, but begin the Canon from the Heirmoi of the feast, chanting on 12; and the Menaion on 4...*  

*...and the Canon of the Menaion on 4. After Ode 3, Sessional Hymn of the Saint; Glory: Both now: Sessional Hymn of the feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the feast and Ikos.*  
*If the commemoration of a Great Saint should occur, we sing the feast with the Heirmos on 6, and the Saint on 6. After Ode 3, Kontakion and Ikos of the feast, and Sessional Hymn in the Menaion; Glory: Both now: of the feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Saint and Ikos. And we read in the Prologue. At Ode 9, the Magnificat ("More honorable...") is always chanted, except on the Sundays of Pentecostarion; only on the Sunday of the Holy Fathers is "More honorable..." chanted. After Ode 9, Exapostilarion of the feast; Glory: to the Saint; Both now: of the feast.*  
*At the Praises, Stichera of the Octoechos on 4, Tone 1:*  
We hymn Thy saving Passion, O Christ, and we glorify Thy Resurrection! *Penitential:*  
Another world awaits thee, O my soul, and the Judge Who is about to expose thy hidden and evil deeds; remain not therefore in the things here below, but anticipate, crying aloud unto the Judge: O God, cleanse me and save me! *Martyric:*  
Come, all ye people, let us honor the passion-bearers of Christ with spiritual hymns and songs, the luminaries of the world and preachers of the faith, the ever-flowing fountains from whom healings pour forth unto the faithful; through their prayers, O Christ our God, grant peace unto the world, and unto our souls great mercy! *Glory: Both now: Tone 1:*  
Thomas, who is called the Twin, was not with them when Thou didst enter, O Christ, while the doors were shut; therefore he disbelieved what was told him, bringing certainty from disbelief into faith; but Thou didst not disdain, O Good One, to show him Thy most pure side and the wounds of Thy hands and feet; and having touched and seen, he confessed Thee to be God not bare, and man not simple, and cried aloud: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee!  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2:*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Еѵфра́ѳовъ):*  
With sacred branches, O men, sing with the Angels unto Him Who rose on the third day from the tomb and raised the world with Himself! *Stichos: Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth... (Ps. 18:5 LXX)*  
Thou didst appear, O Savior, unto Thy holy Apostles while the doors were shut, renewing through them Thy divine Spirit unto us! *Stichos: The heavens declare the glory of God... (Ps. 18:2 LXX)*  
Now having seen Thee, not with our eyes, but having believed in God with heartfelt love, we magnify Thee with hymns!  
*Glory: Both now: Tone 1:*  
While the disciples were in doubt, on the eighth day the Savior stood in their midst where they were gathered, and having given peace, cried out unto Thomas: Come, Apostle, touch the palms into which they drove the nails! O good disbelief of Thomas, which brought the hearts of the faithful unto understanding! And with fear he cried aloud: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee! *Troparion of the feast.*  
*Litany, and the Priest's prayer: "Blessed is Christ our God Who is..." And we confirm: "Amen." And immediately we combine the First Hour, with three psalms according to custom. And there is a complete Dismissal, and the customary procession into the narthex.*  
*Be it known that beginning today, the Mesoria of the 3rd and 6th Hours are recited, and the other is chanted in the cells of the brethren according to the prescribed form.[^543]*  
*At Compline, we sing the Supplicatory Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, and the Triodia, which are the compositions of the Lord Joseph the Hymnographer. For in our Slavonic codices these Triodia are appointed at the Beatitudes, and not wishing to oppose the holy-voiced custom, we have likewise arranged them in this Pentecostarion.[^544]*  
*It is fitting to know that in all the weeks, such is the order at Matins until the Ascension, even as was indicated on this evening (see fol. 204).*  

*On Monday at the Divine Liturgy, at the Beatitudes, the Triodion:*  
*Take heed: Know that these Triodia and Canons at Compline are chanted among us Slavs at the Beatitudes according to former custom; for in the Greek Pentecostarion, that is, the Flowery Triodion, I did not find them; therefore, according to the proper mind of Joseph the composer and according to the arrangement by acrostics (which, even if they were formerly placed, were corrupted from poorly translated Slavonic versions), we could not fully restore them; yet as I found them in the Slavonic, so have I set them forth, preserving the sense intact for Orthodox Christians, and correcting the orthography.*  
*A composition of Joseph, whereof the Acrostic is: (`АСЕІДЕССІѠСИФ`), that is: "Joseph also hymns Thee."[^545]*  
*Tone 1, Ode 1. Heirmos: Thy victorious right hand...*  
*Stichos: In Thy Kingdom remember us, O Lord, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom.*  
**A** Let us sing unto God as the Victor, Who by death cast down death, and raised the human race with Himself, honoring it most richly with incorruption!  
*Stichos: Blessed are the poor in spirit, for theirs is the Kingdom of Heaven.*  
**S** Being bound with bonds of death, as the Mighty One Thou didst tear them asunder by Thy strength; and having delivered men from corruption, Thou didst raise them from hades, as they hymn Thy dominion, O greatly merciful One!  
*Stichos: Blessed are they that mourn, for they shall be comforted.*  
**E** O compassionate Christ, Who for my sake wast willingly even unto hades, raise me who lie in the tomb of sin and in the shadow of transgressions by Thy visitation, and have mercy on me!  
*Stichos: Blessed are the meek, for they shall inherit the earth.*  
**I** Beholding Christ risen in the flesh from the dead, O Mother of God, thou didst rejoice; pray Him now to grant me also joy-creating tears of repentance, that I may then find divine consolation, O pure one!  

*Ode 8. Heirmos: The Children in the furnace... Stichos: Blessed are they that hunger and thirst after righteousness...*  
**D** By Thy Cross Thou didst destroy the curse, being laid in the tomb; and having cast down the dark kingdom of hades, O my Christ...  

...O Jesus, having cast down its kingdom, and having risen, Thou didst raise all creation with Thyself; therefore we hymn Thee unto all ages!  
*Stichos: Blessed are the merciful, for they shall obtain mercy.*  
**E** When Thou didst ascend the Cross, the sun was darkened; and when Thou didst descend into hades, O Lover of mankind, light shone forth unto the souls sitting in darkness and buried in bitter death; therefore we hymn Thee unto all ages!  
*Stichos: Blessed are the pure in heart, for they shall see God.*  
**S** Deliver me, O Christ, who am bound with the cords of sin, and through the divine dwelling of repentance, O Savior, deliver men from eternal darkness by Thy fearful rising, which we hymn unto all ages!  
*Stichos: Blessed are the peacemakers, for they shall be called the children of God.*  
**S** O Virgin who barest in thine arms Christ incarnate of thee: pray Him now to receive me also, who have gone astray in all the pathlessness of life and in countless sins, finding ever within me a sluggish disposition!  

*Ode 9. Heirmos: The Image of the pure birth-giving...*  
*Stichos: Blessed are they that are persecuted for righteousness' sake, for theirs is the Kingdom of Heaven.*  
**I** The sun, seeing Thee, the Word, the Unsetting Sun, crucified in the midst of thieves, was darkened; the mountains, the hills, the earth, and the sea shrank back; hades trembled, releasing the prisoners whom it held, as they sang Thy praises!  
*Stichos: Blessed are ye when men shall revile you, and persecute you...*  
**O** Thou didst appear as a great sun, setting beneath the earth, but unto those in hades Thou art an unsetting luminary from the dead, gloriously illumining the ends of the earth; therefore, O Life-Giver, we unceasingly magnify Thee!  
*Stichos: Rejoice, and be exceeding glad, for great is your reward in heaven.*  
**S** O Savior Jesus, Who by Thy death didst destroy death...  

...and having raised all who were held therein, raise me also who am in the lowest pit of deadly passions and sins, crying unto Thee, O Lover of mankind!  
*Glory:*  
**I** The Myrrh-bearing women came early unto the tomb weeping; and not finding Thee, they beheld an Angel sitting before them in white robes, crying aloud: The Lord is risen! Hasten to proclaim the rising unto His disciples!  
*Both now:*  
**Ph** All creation marvels, O blessed one, at the mystery of thy childbearing which surpasses understanding; for thou barest God Emmanuel, Who for our sake became a man, all-blameless Lady, and destroyed the tyranny of death by His Cross!  

*Then the Prokeimenon, Alleluia, and Communion Verse of the feast (see fol. 200). Epistle from the Acts of the Apostles. Gospel from John, Pericope 6 (John 2:1–11): "At that time, there was a marriage in Cana of Galilee..."[^546]*  

## On Monday Evening of the Second Week

*At "Lord, I have cried", we set 6 Stichera: of the feast, 3; and of the Menaion, 3.*  
*Stichera of the feast, Tone 4:*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As One Noble" (Ꙗ҆́ко до́бля):*  
Having risen from the tomb, while the doors were shut, Thou didst stand among Thy friends, O Almighty One; and unto Thomas who had disbelieved, Thou didst show the nail-prints and the side pierced by the spear, assuring and confirming him, O Word, that Thou Thyself art He Who for mercy's sake didst endure the saving Passions!  
Seeing Thy great condescension and boundless mercy, and Thy self-emptying without doubt, the Twin said: The Lord hath said that I Myself am He Who suffered, and rose on the third day from the dead, and emptied the treasuries of hades, and gave life unto dead man!  
Marveling as he beheld Thee, Thomas cried aloud, seeing Thee, O Almighty One: Thou art my God and Lord, knowing, O Lover of mankind, that Thou Thyself didst suffer and heal the passions of our souls; we worship Thy dominion, proclaiming unto the world Thy fearful and all-powerful rising!  
*Other Stichera in the Menaion, 3. Glory: Both now: Tone 2:*  
While the doors were shut, Jesus stood among the disciples, giving them fearlessness and peace; then He says unto Thomas: Why dost thou disbelieve Me, that I am risen from the dead? Bring hither thy hand, and place it into My side, and see; for because of thy disbelief, all have learned to acclaim My Passions and Resurrection with thee: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee!  
*O Gladsome Light. At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 1:*  
Compass Zion, ye people, and embrace her, and give glory in her unto Him Who is risen from the dead; for He is our God, Who hath delivered us from our iniquities! *Stichos: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes... (Ps. 122:1 LXX)*  
Because I am condemned by evil thoughts and deeds, O Savior, grant me the thought of return, O God, that I may cry out: Save me, O Good Benefactor, and have mercy on me! *Stichos: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us... (Ps. 122:3 LXX)*  
Your confession before the tribunal, O holy saints, terrified the power of the demons and delivered men from delusion; therefore, as your heads were being cut off, ye cried aloud: Let the sacrifice of our souls be acceptable before Thee, O Lord, for having loved Thee, we took no thought for temporal life, O Lover of mankind!  
*Glory: Both now: Tone 2:*  
After Thy rising, O Lord, when Thy disciples were gathered together and the doors were shut, Thou didst stand in their midst, giving them peace; and Thomas, having believed by the sight of Thy hands and side, confessed Thee to be Lord and God, Who savest them that hope in Thee, O Lover of mankind!  
*Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace... Troparion of the feast, and Dismissal.*  

## On Tuesday of the Second Week after Pascha

*At Matins: At "God is the Lord", Troparion of the feast (thrice).[^547]*  
*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "While the Stone was Sealed" (Ка́мени зна́менану):*  
The women came unto the tomb early, and seeing the angelic appearance, they trembled; the tomb shone with life, the wonder astonished them; therefore going forth, they proclaimed the rising unto the disciples: Christ hath despoiled hades, as the only mighty and strong One, and hath raised all who were corrupted, destroying the fear of condemnation by the Cross! *Stichos: O Lord, rebuke me not in Thine anger... (Ps. 6:2 LXX)*  
If the righteous scarcely be saved, where shall I a sinner appear? I have not borne the burden and heat of the day; but number me, O God, with the laborers of the eleventh hour, and save me! *Stichos: God is marvelous in His saints. (Ps. 67:36 LXX)*  
As good soldiers believing with one accord, not fearing the threats of the tyrants, O holy martyrs, ye approached eagerly unto Christ, the precious Vine; and having finished your course, O sons of victory, ye received the prize! Glory to Him Who strengthened you; glory to Him Who crowned you; glory to Him Who works healings for all through you! *Glory: Both now: Theotokion:*  
Thou who didst conceive the Divine Fire unconsumed, and didst bear without seed the Lord, the Fountain of Life, O Virgin full of grace: save them that magnify thee!  
*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5:*  
*Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word Co-Unoriginate" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*  
Let us praise with hymns the Apostle and disciple of Christ in his memory; for having touched the wounds of the nails and searched out the abundant faith in a God-befitting manner, he confirmed our thoughts in the world, and prays the Savior that our souls may find mercy! *Glory: Both now: The same.*  
*Then: "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ". Psalm 50. Canon of the feast with the Heirmos on 8, and of the Menaion on 4. After Ode 3, Sessional Hymn of the Menaion; Kontakion and Exapostilarion of the feast.*  
*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 4:*  
Thou Who didst endure the Crucifixion, and didst abolish death, and didst rise from the dead: bring peace into our life, O Lord, as the only Almighty One!  
Leave me not, O my Savior, held fast by sinful slothfulness; raise my thought unto repentance, and show me forth an approved laborer of Thy vineyard, granting me the reward of the eleventh hour, and great mercy! *Martyric:*  
These soldiers of the Great King resisted the decrees of the tyrants; bravely they took no account of torments, and having trampled underfoot all delusion, being worthily crowned, they ask peace and great mercy from the Savior! *Glory: Both now: Tone 2:*  
While the doors were shut, Jesus stood among the disciples, giving them fearlessness and peace; then He said unto Thomas: Why dost thou disbelieve Me, that I am risen from the dead? Bring hither thy hand, and place it into My side, and see; for because of thy disbelief, all have learned to proclaim My Passions and Resurrection with thee: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee!  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the feast, Tone 2:*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Еѵфра́ѳовъ):*  
Let us, the faithful, unceasingly sing with the Angels unto Christ risen on the third day from the tomb, and raising the world with Himself! *Stichos: Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth... (Ps. 18:5 LXX)* Having touched Thy side, O compassionate One, Thomas attained unto faith, and through him we also came to know Thee as God! *Stichos: The heavens declare the glory of God... (Ps. 18:2 LXX)*
Thou dost grant peace unto Thy people, O Deliverer, having risen from the tomb, and Thou didst raise the world with Thyself from hades, O Almighty One! *Glory: Both now: Tone 6:*  
While the doors were locked, Thou didst stand among the disciples, O Christ; then Thomas by divine dispensation was not found with them; for he said unto them in disbelief: Unless I see the Master, unless I see the side whence came forth Blood, Water, and Baptism, unless I see the wound whence the great bruise of man was healed, unless I see that He was not like a spirit, but flesh and bones: I will not believe! O Thou Who didst trample down death and assure Thomas: Lord, glory to Thee!  
*Then the First Hour, and the rest according to custom, and Dismissal.*  

*On Tuesday at the Beatitudes, Tone 1, Triodion:*  
*Whereof the Acrostic is: (`ѰАЛѠ ѲЕѠ ІѠСИФ`), that is: "I will sing unto God, Joseph."[^548] Ode 2. Heirmos: See, see that I am God...*  
*Stichos: In Thy Kingdom remember us, O Lord, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom.*  
**Ps** Let us sing today unto the Lord, and let us shout aloud unto Him Who was crucified for our sake, and cast down the author of evil, and was laid in the tomb, and gave life unto the dead by His rising!  
*Stichos: Blessed are the poor in spirit, for theirs is the Kingdom of Heaven.*  
**A** Holy is the Lord, Who rose on the third day from the dead as the Mighty One, and appeared unto the disciples in a God-befitting manner, and filled with ineffable joy the souls that formerly were filled with sorrow!  
*Stichos: Blessed are they that mourn, for they shall be comforted.*  
**L** O Word Co-unoriginate, enthroned together with the unoriginate Father, Who wast crucified and numbered among the dead, and didst rise as the Mighty One: quicken my soul which is slain by transgressions!  
*Stichos: Blessed are the meek, for they shall inherit the earth.*  
**O** O thy wonders! O fearful hearing! For in the flesh thou barest God, Who destroyed the bonds of death and delivered men from hades, O pure Lady full of divine grace!  

*Ode 8. Heirmos: Him Whom the Angels fear... Stichos: Blessed are they that hunger and thirst after righteousness...*  
**Th** All creation laments at Thy Passion, O God; the sun was darkened when Thou didst set upon the Cross; but they that sat in the darkness of night beheld a great light, singing unto Thee, O Compassionate One!  
*Stichos: Blessed are the merciful, for they shall obtain mercy.*  
**E** Hades trembled, beholding Thee coming unto it with Thy soul, O Christ, King of all; and it gave up the dead from the ages, who hymn Thy love for mankind!  
*Stichos: Blessed are the pure in heart, for they shall see God.*  
**O** Thou didst appear, O Christ, risen from the tomb, unto the God-bearing women who brought myrrh of old, and Thou didst overcome their lamentation, being an eternal consolation unto all!  
*Stichos: Blessed are the peacemakers, for they shall be called the children of God. Theotokion:*  
**I** Seeing Him Whom thou didst bear as God risen from the dead, thou wast shown forth filled with joy, O Virgin Mother; pray Him, O pure one, to blot out utterly every sorrow of our heart!  

*Ode 9. Heirmos: Light-bearing cloud... Stichos: Blessed are they that are persecuted for righteousness' sake...*  
**O** When the prudent Thief beheld Thee willingly crucified upon the Tree, he cried aloud: Remember me, O Jesus, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom! And he became the first inhabitant of Paradise, being justified by faith!  
*Stichos: Rejoice, and be exceeding glad, for great is your reward in heaven.*  
**S** Beholding Thee among the dead, O Savior, hades was slain, and gave up...  

...and gave up the dead whom it had swallowed of old, who glorify Thy ineffable power, O Christ, and Thy great tender-heartedness, whereby Thou hast saved us, O King of all! *Glory:*  
**I** The women bearing myrrh came unto the tomb; and beholding Thee, O Christ, risen from the tomb, they were filled with great joy, and announced unto Thine Apostles that Thou art risen and hast appeared, having raised the whole world with Thyself! *Both now: Theotokion:*  
**Ph** Seeing the Unsetting Light come forth from the dead, O pure one, and being filled with great joy, thou didst rejoice with the holy disciples; with them entreat forgiveness for our boundless transgressions, O Mother of God, Protectress of the faithful!  

## On Tuesday Evening of the Second Week

*At "Lord, I have cried", we set 6 Stichera: of the feast, 3; and of the Menaion, 3. Stichera of the feast, Tone 1:*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "O All-Praised Martyrs" (Прехва́лнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*  
Touch, the Lover of mankind, Who rose on the third day from the dead, says unto Thomas: and behold the wounds of My hands and feet, and the piercing of My side, and know Me to be immutable God, fashioned according to essence in earthly commingling, and having received suffering therein!  
The Twin was filled with awe, beholding the wounds of the feet and the piercing of the hands, and was astonished at the fearful miracles; and touching and seeing the most pure side with his hand, he showed forth unto the nations the third-day rising from the tomb, which is beyond understanding!  
I receive great joy, beholding Thy abundant rising, O my Savior, wherein Thomas was accounted worthy to touch daring things; therefore we confess Thee as simple God and man, bearing two sacred operations, of which Thou wast composed! *Glory: Both now: Tone 4:*  
While the doors were shut, Thou didst come with the unendurable brilliance of Thy Godhead; and standing in the midst of the disciples, Thou didst bare Thy side, showing the signs of the wounds of Thy hands and feet, resolving sorrow and despondency, as Thou didst cry aloud: In what manner ye see the assumption of flesh in Me, O friends, I bear not the nature of a spirit! And unto the doubting disciple Thou didst command to touch with trembling, saying: Having searched out, come, henceforth be not faithless! And he, perceiving Thy twofold nature with his hands, cried aloud with fear, drawn by faith: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee!  
*O Gladsome Light. At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 1:*  
The Cross was planted on Calvary, and immortality blossomed forth for us from the ever-flowing fountain of the Savior's side! *Stichos: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes... (Ps. 122:1 LXX)*  
Come, ye people, let us hymn and worship Christ, glorifying His Resurrection from the dead; for He is our God, Who hath delivered the world from the enemy's delusion! *Stichos: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us... (Ps. 122:3 LXX)*  
O goodly merchandise of yours, O holy saints! For ye gave your blood, and inherited the heavens; and having been tried for a time, ye rejoice eternally; truly goodly was your merchandise! For having forsaken corruptible things, ye received incorruptible things...  

...ye received incorruptible things, and dancing with the Angels, ye unceasingly sing unto the Consubstantial Trinity! *Glory: Both now: Tone 8:*  
Touch My side with thy hand, Christ says, and come touch the marks of the nails, examine with faith, and be faithful unto Me, and be not faithless! And Thomas, when he touched the Master with his finger, cried aloud with a loud voice: Thou art my God and Lord, O Compassionate One, glory to Thee!  
*Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace... And the rest according to custom, as was indicated above.*  

*Be it known that on Wednesdays and Fridays, the Stichera of the Cross, as also the Sessional Hymns, precede those of the Resurrection; because our Lord was first crucified, and thereafter arose from the dead.[^549]*  

## On Wednesday of the Second Week after Pascha

*At Matins: At "God is the Lord", Troparion of the feast (thrice). After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*  
When Thou wast crucified, O Christ, the tyranny perished, and the enemy's power was trampled down; for neither an Angel nor a man, but the Lord Himself saved us: glory to Thee! *Stichos: Exalt ye the Lord our God, and worship the footstool of His feet, for He is holy. (Ps. 98:5 LXX)*  
Isaac was led up unto the mount, Jonah was brought down into the deep, and both together prefigured Thy Passion, O Savior;[^550] the one indeed the bonds and slaughter, and the other the burial and the life of Thy most glorious rising: O Lord, glory to Thee! *Stichos: God is marvelous in His saints. (Ps. 67:36 LXX)*  
Be Thou entreated, O Lord, by the wounds which the holy saints received for Thy sake, and heal all our sicknesses and passions, O Lover of mankind, we pray Thee! *Glory: Both now: Theotokion:*  
Having acquired thy protection, O most pure Lady, and being delivered from tribulations by thy prayers, preserved in all things by the Cross of thy Son, we all piously magnify thee as is our duty!  
*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy Tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й, Спа́се):*  
By touching the unendurable side with his hand, the Twin Thomas was not consumed by the touch, but remained examining the abundant wounds of Him Who was pierced for our sake: Thou art my Lord and my God, even though Thou didst endure suffering!  
*Glory: Both now: The same.*  
*Then: "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ". Psalm 50. Canon of the feast with the Heirmos on 8, and of the Menaion on 4. After Ode 3, Sessional Hymn of the Menaion. Exapostilarion of the feast.*  
*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 1:*  
Thee Who wast crucified upon the Tree and didst grant life unto us, as Savior and Master we unceasingly hymn! *(Twice).*  
Thou Who didst despoil hades, and didst raise man by Thy Resurrection, O Christ, account us worthy to hymn and glorify Thee with a pure heart! *Martyric:*  
Neither tribulation nor distress, neither famine nor persecution, neither wounds nor the rage of beasts, neither sword nor threatening fire, could separate us, O all-praised Martyrs, from God; but rather through the love which was toward Him, as if contending in alien bodies, ye forgot nature, despising death! Therefore, having worthily received the reward of your labors, ye became heirs of the heavenly kingdom; having boldness before God the Lover of mankind, entreat peace for the world, and unto our souls great mercy! *Glory: Both now: Tone 8:*  
While the doors were shut and the disciples were gathered together, the Savior stood in their midst where they were gathered, and standing among them, He said unto Thomas: Come, touch, and behold the wounds...

...the marks of the nails, stretch forth thy hand and touch My side, and be not faithless, but with faith proclaim My Resurrection from the dead!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2:*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^551]  
O novel wonder! O marvelous image! How the Apostle's hand, like unconsumed grass, touched the fire of the Godhead! *Stichos: Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world. (Ps. 18:5 LXX)*  
Let us also hasten to cleanse our hands, to feast upon the Passion, and so touch the eternal side! *Stichos: The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands. (Ps. 18:2 LXX)*  
Renew, O soul, all thy senses unto divine vision; for Christ the Sun desires to be accomplished through renewal! *Glory: Both now: Podoben:*  
Behold, I pray thee, spiritually the change of the world, and adorn thyself with inner virtues as with foliage, O soul adorned!  
*Then: "It is good to give praise unto the Lord..." (Ps. 91:2 LXX). First Hour, and Dismissal.*

*On Wednesday, at the Beatitudes, Triodion, bearing the Acrostic: (ДІНОѲЕѠНІѠСИФ), that is: "Praise God, Joseph". Tone 1, Ode 1. Heirmos: Unto the Pre-eternal...*[^552]  
*Stichos: In Thy Kingdom remember us, O Lord, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom.*  
*Troparion ([Д]):* O Light of the saints, desiring to illumine the world, Thou wast willingly lifted up upon the wood of the Cross and slaughtered as a lamb; and by Thy life-giving blood Thou dost renew us who hymn Thee. *Stichos: Blessed are the poor in spirit, for theirs is the kingdom of heaven.*  
*Troparion ([І]):* The enemy's might was taken away, death was led captive, and corrupted nature was brought to life when Thou, O Christ Jesus, wast put to death; and the world was renewed, crying: Holy art Thou, O Lord! *Stichos: Blessed are they that mourn, for they shall be comforted.*  
*Troparion ([Н]):* Now mourn no more, cried the Angel unto the women; go rather with zeal, proclaiming words of joy unto the grieving disciples, that Christ, the life and resurrection of all, is risen! *Stichos: Blessed are the meek, for they shall inherit the earth.*  
*Theotokion ([Ѡ]):* Thou hast appeared, O pure Virgin, higher than the Angels, having incarnate God Who was crucified, buried, and poured forth incorruption unto the dead; Him do thou entreat to save us who hymn thee.  
*Ode 8. Heirmos: A marvelous wonder...*[^553] *Stichos: Blessed are they that hunger and thirst after righteousness, for they shall be filled.*  
*Troparion ([Ѳ]):* Thou didst willingly accept death, being exalted in the flesh upon the Tree; and beholding Thee, the sun was extinguished, and the veil of the temple was rent in twain, O All-powerful One! Therefore, singing hymns unto Thee, we cry: Let all creation bless the Lord, and supreme exalt Him unto the ages! *Stichos: Blessed are the merciful, for they shall obtain mercy.*  
*Troparion ([Е]):* They who of old sat in the shadow of death beheld Thee, the unwaning Radiance, when Thou, O Lord, didst descend into the nethermost parts and break asunder the eternal bonds; greatly hymning Thee and saying: Let all creation bless the Lord, and supreme exalt Him unto the ages! *Stichos: Blessed are the pure in heart, for they shall see God.*  
*Troparion ([Ѡ]):* The women, coming early in the morning unto the tomb and beholding the radiant Angel, who said: The Redeemer is risen; why do ye still reckon the Living with the dead? Filled with joy, cry aloud: Let all creation bless the Lord, and supreme exalt Him unto the ages! *Stichos: Blessed are the peacemakers, for they shall be called the children of God.*  
*Theotokion ([Н]):* Thy Son, O pure Lady, wrought the slaying of hades, rising from the dead as He foretold; Him do thou entreat, for deliverance from corrupting sin and eternal torments unto those who cry aloud: Let all creation bless the Lord, and supreme exalt Him unto the ages! *Ode 9. Heirmos: A strange mystery...*

*Stichos: Blessed are they that are persecuted for righteousness' sake, for theirs is the kingdom of heaven.*  
*Troparion ([І]):* Healing the sickness of the first-fashioned, Thou wast willingly shaken by scourging upon the Tree, O Savior; and being laid in the nethermost pit, Thou didst raise the dead whom death had previously been able to swallow up. *Stichos: Blessed are ye when men shall revile you, and persecute you, and shall say all manner of evil against you falsely for My sake.*  
*Troparion ([Ѡ]):* When the choir of the Apostles of old beheld Thee risen, O Lord, they worshipped in fear and joy; unto whom Thou didst cry: Behold the fulfillment of My words; grieve ye not, but rejoice! *Rejoice and be glad, for great is your reward in the heavens.*  
*Troparion ([С]):* Righteous Joseph, wrapping Thee in a shroud with love, O Savior, laid Thee in a new tomb; but having crushed the gates and bars of hades, Thou didst raise the dead whom death had previously been able to swallow up. *Glory:*  
*Troparion ([И]):* The Myrrh-bearing Women rejoiced, hearing of Christ's rising; therefore they cried unto the Apostles, running swiftly: Behold, grieve no more, but rejoice ye, for the Lord of all is risen from the dead! *Both now:*  
*Theotokion ([Ф]):* Beholding Christ as Bridegroom coming in the flesh from the bridal chamber of the tomb, thou didst call Him all-radiant in His condescension, O Virgin, and wast filled with joy; Him do thou entreat to deliver us from corruption and torment.

## On Wednesday of the Second Week after Pascha, in the Evening

*At "Lord, I have cried", we set 6 Stichera: of the Feast 3, and of the Menaion 3. Stichera of the Feast, Tone 2:*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*  
Without leaving the Father's bosom, Thou didst appear upon earth bearing flesh, through Thy compassion, O Christ; and accepting passion and death, Thou didst arise on the third day; and while the doors were shut, Thou didst enter within as all-powerful; therefore Thomas, rejoicing, touched Thy divine side and glorified Thee as Lord and Creator!  
Thou wast nailed with nails to the Cross, pierced in the side with a spear, and didst taste gall, O Christ, enduring death, and wast laid in the tomb as dead; but as God Thou didst loosen the bonds of hades, and didst raise those who were dead from the ages; therefore, leaving the seals unbroken, Thou didst appear unto Thy disciples, confirming Thy Resurrection.  
The present Feast is Pascha, mystical Pascha, divine Pascha, saving Pascha, Pascha leading us unto immortal life, Pascha taking away every sorrow from our midst, Pascha granting the grace of gladness unto the disciples;[^554] therefore Thomas cried aloud: Thou art my Lord and my God, Who hast despoiled the kingdoms of hades! *Glory: Both now: Tone 4:*  
O most marvelous wonder! Unbelief gave birth to abundant faith; for Thomas said: If I see not, I will not believe; but having touched His side, he theologized the Incarnate One, recognized the very Son of God, that He suffered in the flesh, proclaimed the risen God, and cried with a radiant voice: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee!  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 1:*  
By Thy Passion, O Christ, we have been set free from passions, and by Thy Resurrection we have been delivered from corruption: O Lord, glory to Thee! *Stichos: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, O Thou that dwellest in the heavens. (Ps. 122:1 LXX)*  
*Martyric:* The all-harmonious harp, moved by the Holy Spirit, hath abolished the boundless shrines of demons, preaching the one God; it hath delivered the nations from idol delusion, and taught them to worship the Consubstantial Trinity.[^555] *Stichos: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us... (Ps. 122:3 LXX)* O all-praised Martyrs, the earth did not hide you, but heaven received you, and the gates of paradise opened unto you; and being within, ye delight in the Tree of Life: pray unto Christ to grant our souls peace and great mercy! *Glory: Both now: Tone 4:*
O marvelous wonder! Fire, touching grass, was preserved; for Thomas thrust his hand into the fiery side of Jesus Christ God, and was not consumed by the touch; for he changed the soul's unbelief into fervent faith, and cried from the depths of his soul: Thou art my Master and God Who art risen from the dead, glory to Thee!  
*Then: "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace..." Trisagion. And after "Our Father": Troparion of the Feast (see fol. 200). And Dismissal.*

## On Thursday of the Second Week after Pascha

*At Matins: At "God is the Lord", Troparion of the Feast (thrice). After the 1st Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "The Stone Being Sealed" (Ка́мени зна́менану):*  
Thou wast nailed to the Cross, O Life of all, and wast numbered among the dead, O immortal Lord; but Thou didst arise on the third day, O Savior, raising Adam from corruption. Therefore the heavenly powers cried aloud unto Thee, O Life-giver Christ: Glory to Thy divine Passion, O Christ! Glory to Thy Resurrection! Glory to Thy dispensation, O Thou Who alone lovest mankind! *Stichos: Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world. (Ps. 18:5 LXX)*  
O all-wise fishers of the universe, having received mercy from God, pray also now for us who cry aloud: O Lord, save Thy King and Thy City, and from surrounding tribulations deliver our souls, for the Apostles' sake! *Stichos: God is marvelous in His saints. (Ps. 67:36 LXX)*  
O disciples of Christ, we pray you: for they pray for our salvation; and let us ever flee unto them with faith: these continually pour forth grace of healing, and drive away legions of demons, as guardians of the faith. *Glory: Both now: Theotokion:*  
The divine Apostles manifestly proclaimed thee in the world, O most pure Virgin, as Mother of God, and we all, having believed the same, honor thee piously and quietly, hymn thee, and ever name thee sovereignly Theotokos.  
*After the 2nd Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy Tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й, Спа́се):*  
Beholding My side, be not faithless unto Me any longer, the Word cried unto the wavering Thomas; touch with thy finger and examine with thy hand, and having known these My signs, proclaim unto the world My life-bearing Resurrection from the tomb. *Glory: Both now: The same.*  
*Then: "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ". Psalm 50. Canon of the Feast with Heirmos on 8, and of the Menaion on 4. After Ode 3, Sessional Hymn of Menaion, Kontakion and Exapostilarion of the Feast.*  
*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 1:*  
Glorifying Thy divine condescension, we cry unto Thee, O Christ: Thou wast born of the Virgin, yet wast not separated from the Father; Thou didst suffer as man, and willingly didst endure crucifixion; but Thou didst arise from the tomb as from a bridal chamber, that Thou mightest save the world: O Lord, glory to Thee!  
*Of the Apostles:* Let us with one voice praise Peter and Paul; let us worthily praise Luke, Matthew, Mark, John, Andrew, Thomas, Bartholomew, Simon the Cananite, James, Philip, and the whole choir of disciples![^556]  
*Martyric:* Rejoice in the Lord, O Martyrs, for ye have fought the good fight, ye stood against kings and conquered tyrants, ye feared neither fire nor sword, nor wild beasts devouring your bodies; sending up hymnody with the Angels, which the Theotokos received on our behalf, pray ye that peace be granted unto the world, and unto our souls great mercy! *Glory: Both now: Tone 4:*
O marvelous wonder! John leaned upon the bosom of the Word, and Thomas was accounted worthy to touch His side; but the one indeed drew forth from thence the deep dispensation of theology; while the other was accounted worthy mystically to teach us, presenting clear demonstrations of His Resurrection, and crying: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee![^557]  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2:*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*  
May the confusion of passions and the tempest of thoughts be driven far away, and so let the springtime of faith blossom forth! *Stichos: Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world. (Ps. 18:5 LXX)*  
Thou didst appear, O Savior, unto Thy holy Apostles while the doors were shut, renewing through them in us the Divine Spirit today. *Stichos: The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands. (Ps. 18:2 LXX)*  
Now having seen Thee, not with eyes, but with heartfelt love, having believed well, we magnify Thee with hymns. *Glory: Both now: Tone 6:*  
After eight days from Thy rising, O Jesus King, Only-begotten Word of the Father, Thou didst appear unto Thy disciples while the doors were shut, granting Thy peace; and unto the disbelieving disciple Thou didst show the signs, saying: Come, touch the hands and feet, and My incorruptible side! And he, faithfully crying unto Thee: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee!  
*First Hour, and Dismissal.*

*On Thursday, at the Beatitudes, Triodion, bearing the Acrostic: (ѴМНѠ ДІА ІѠСИФ), that is: "Joseph's Hymn". Tone 1, Ode 1. Heirmos: Flee not from suffering...*[^558]  
*Stichos: In Thy Kingdom remember us, O Lord, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom.*  
*Troparion ([Ѵ]):* For the sake of us humble ones Thou wast led unto death, O Christ the Life of all; and having despoiled the kingdoms of hades, Thou didst snatch Adam as a sheep, raising him with Thyself as a mighty and all-powerful King. *Stichos: Blessed are the poor in spirit, for theirs is the kingdom of heaven.*
