*Troparion ([М]):* Why seek ye the Living with the dead? the radiant Angel appearing at the tomb said unto the women: He is risen; come, see the grave-clothes lying alone, and hasten to proclaim His resurrection unto all the disciples! *Stichos: Blessed are they that mourn, for they shall be comforted.*  
*Troparion ([Н]):* Hades, becoming dead by Christ's descent, gave up all the dead, being utterly despoiled; and its all-devouring belly was emptied to the end by the all-powerful will of God, the King of all. *Stichos: Blessed are the meek, for they shall inherit the earth.*  
*Theotokion ([Ѡ]):* As into a most beauteous temple, God the King took up His abode in thy womb, O pure Lady; by His descent He abolished the dark prince of hades, raising mankind with Himself as all-powerful.  
*Ode 8. Heirmos: This chosen and holy day... Stichos: Blessed are they that hunger and thirst after righteousness, for they shall be filled.*  
*Troparion ([Д]):* Glory to Christ Who cast down the enemy, and despoiled hades by His descent unto it, and brought to life the dead whom it had been able to swallow up, who glorify Him unto the ages! *Stichos: Blessed are the merciful, for they shall obtain mercy.*  
*Troparion ([І]):* Behold, the radiant Angel appearing cried unto the Myrrh-bearing Women: Behold the place where He was laid! Seeing that He hath conquered death by His power, seek no longer the Life among the dead! *Stichos: Blessed are the pure in heart, for they shall see God.*  
*Troparion ([А]):* Hades was embittered below, O Word, being unable to draw near unto Thee, the Light; and those dead whom it had swallowed up, it quickly gave back at Thy command, who with one accord glorify Thy dominion. *Stichos: Blessed are the peacemakers, for they shall be called the children of God. Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion ([І]):* Thou didst behold Him Who is risen from the dead, O Virgin, the Word Whom thou didst ineffably bear in the flesh; and thou didst fill thy soul with joy, hymning His descent, O pure Lady, unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos: Shine, shine, O new Jerusalem... Stichos: Blessed are they that are persecuted for righteousness' sake, for theirs is the kingdom of heaven.*  
*Troparion ([Ѡ]):* Judge and Redeemer of hours and days, in the sixth day and hour Thou didst willingly endure crucifixion, O Compassionate One, that Thou mightest quell our reproaches and reconcile us unto the Father. *Stichos: Blessed are ye when men shall revile you, and persecute you, and shall say all manner of evil against you falsely for My sake.*  
*Troparion ([С]):* Hades groaned upon beholding Thee, O Redeemer, and quickly released the prisoners whom it held, who with divine voices sang unto Thee, O Word, Thy power beyond understanding and Thy awesome descent. *Glory:*  
*Troparion ([И]):* The women came with myrrh at deep dawn with haste, to anoint the most pure Body; and the Bodiless One said unto them: The Prince of Life is risen; be ye filled with divine joy! *Both now:*  
*Theotokion ([Ф]):* Having borne the Light Who shone forth upon those sitting in darkness and the shadow of death, when thou didst behold Him risen from the dead, O all-immaculate Lady, with the faithful Myrrh-bearing Women thou didst glorify Him, dancing with joy.

## On Thursday of the Second Week after Pascha, in the Evening

*At "Lord, I have cried", we set 6 Stichera: of the Feast 3, and of the Menaion 3. Stichera of the Feast, Tone 1:*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the Heavenly Hosts" (Небе́снымъ чино́мъ):*  
Clothed in the garment of righteousness, whiter than snow, on this present day of Pascha let us rejoice; for Christ God, having shone forth as the Sun of Righteousness from the dead, hath adorned us all with incorruption!  
The King and Lord of feasts, the present feast, is the day which the Lord hath truly made, wherein, as David sings, let us mystically rejoice, O ye people; for unto the disciples, while the doors were shut, He granted peace.  
Doubtful unbelief, shepherding the world unto the God-man Word, confirms the rising from the prison of hades, when the wounds of hands and feet were boldly touched for the world's assurance by the inquisitive morning star! *Glory: Both now: Tone 6:*  
While the doors were shut, Jesus stood in the midst of the disciples, granting fearless peace; then He said unto Thomas: Why art thou faithless unto Me that I am risen from the dead? Bring hither thy hand, and put it into My side, and see; for through thy disbelief all have come to know My Passion and My Resurrection, to cry with thee: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee! *O Gladsome Light.*  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 1:*  
An unbroken wall unto us is the honorable Cross of the Savior; for hoping in it, we are saved evermore. *Stichos: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, O Thou that dwellest in the heavens. (Ps. 122:1 LXX)*  
Accept our evening prayers, O holy Lord, and grant us remission of sins; for Thou alone art He Who hath manifested the Resurrection unto the world. *Stichos: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us... (Ps. 122:3 LXX)*  
By the prayers, O Lord, of all the saints and of the Theotokos, grant us Thy peace, and have mercy on us, as Thou alone art compassionate. *Glory: Both now: Tone 4:*  
Touch My side with thy hand, Christ says, and come touch the marks of the nails, examine with faith, and be faithful unto Me, and be not faithless! And Thomas, when he touched the Master with his finger, cried aloud with a loud voice: Thou art my God and Lord, O Compassionate One, glory to Thee!  
*Then: "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace..." And the rest according to custom, as was indicated above.*

## On Friday of the Second Week after Pascha

*At Matins: At "God is the Lord", Troparion of the Feast (thrice). After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "The Stone Being Sealed" (Ка́мени зна́менану):*  
We venerate the wood of Thy Cross, O Lover of mankind, for upon it Thou, the Life of all, wast nailed; Thou didst open paradise, O Savior, unto the thief who came unto Thee with faith,[^559] and didst account him worthy of delight when he confessed: Remember me, O Lord! Receive also us who cry as he did: We have sinned all our life, through Thy compassion despise us not! *Stichos: Exalt ye the Lord our God, and worship the footstool of His feet, for He is holy. (Ps. 98:5 LXX)*
While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb, O Savior, they became as dead from the dazzling of the appearing Angel, who proclaimed the Resurrection unto the women; we glorify Thee, the Destroyer of corruption; we fall down before Thee Who art risen from the tomb, our only God! *Stichos: God is marvelous in His saints. (Ps. 67:36 LXX)*  
*Martyric:* With the boast of suffering and the crown of honor, the glorious Passion-bearers were clothed by Thee, O Lord; for through patience of wounds they conquered the lawless, and by divine power received victory from heaven: by their prayers, O Christ God, grant us great mercy! *Glory: Both now: Stavrotheotokion:*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy Tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й, Спа́се):*  
The unblemished ewe-lamb, beholding the Lamb and Shepherd hung dead upon the Tree, weeping said, crying out maternally: How shall I endure Thy condescension beyond words, O my Son, and Thy voluntary sufferings, O all-good God?  
*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy Tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й, Спа́се):*  
Blessed art thou who didst touch the wounds, best of ribs! Behold the greatest wonder that healed Adam, and unto us who have believed granted ineffable blessedness, which the honored Apostles themselves proclaimed, of His Resurrection! *Glory: Both now: The same.*  
*Then: "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ". Psalm 50. Canon of the Feast with Heirmoi on 8, and of the Menaion on 4. After Ode 3, Sessional Hymn of Menaion, Kontakion and Exapostilarion of the Feast.*  
*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 1:*  
By Thy Cross, O Christ, there hath become one flock of Angels and men, and in one assembly heaven and earth rejoice: O Lord, glory to Thee! *(Twice).*  
We hymn Thy saving Passion, O Christ, and glorify Thy Resurrection!  
*Martyric:* Passion-bearers of Christ, come all ye people, let us honor with songs and spiritual hymns the enlighteners of the world and preachers of the faith, the ever-flowing fountains from which healings pour forth unto the faithful: by their prayers, O Christ our God, grant peace unto Thy world, and unto our souls great mercy! *Glory: Both now: Tone 8:*  
While the doors were shut and the disciples were gathered together, the Savior came where they were gathered, and standing in their midst, He said unto Thomas: Come, touch, and behold the marks of the nails; stretch forth thy hand and touch My side, and be not faithless, but with faith proclaim My Resurrection from the dead!  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Feast, Tone 4:*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As One Valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*  
Thou hast opened wounds of healing unto mankind, and raised the dead, O our Life; and Thou art risen, having despoiled death by Thy power, and truly united earthly things with heavenly; Thou hast filled the Apostolic choir with unmeasured joy, O Word of God, announcing peace unto them. *Stichos: Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world. (Ps. 18:5 LXX)*  
Corruption is overthrown, incorruption hath blossomed forth, the temporary separation is dissolved; let the heavens rejoice, the earth and what is beneath the earth! For Christ is risen, death hath been despoiled, a day of joy hath appeared; while the doors were shut, Thou didst enter within, O Life-giver and Lord! *Stichos: The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands. (Ps. 18:2 LXX)*  
This is the day which the Lord hath made, today let us rejoice and be glad! The Life-giver is risen, hades is despoiled, the Apostolic choir hears joy; Thomas hath touched the ribs of the Compassionate One; he who was once faithless, having touched, proclaimed the twofold nature! *Glory: Both now: Tone 4:*
Touch My side with thy hand, Christ says, and come touch the marks of the nails, examine with faith, and be faithful unto Me, and be not faithless! And Thomas, when he touched the Master with his finger, cried aloud with a loud voice: Thou art my God and Lord, O Compassionate One, glory to Thee!  
*Then: "It is good to give praise unto the Lord..." (Ps. 91:2 LXX). First Hour, and Dismissal.*

*On Friday, at the Beatitudes, Triodion, bearing the Acrostic: (ДО́ХА ТѠ ѲЕѠ АМИН), that is: "Glory to God, Amen". Tone 1, Ode 5. Heirmos: Thou Who didst enlighten us...*[^560]  
*Stichos: In Thy Kingdom remember us, O Lord, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom.*  
*Troparion ([Д]):* The Jewish assembly crucified Thee, O long-suffering Word; but an innumerable gathering of the nations believs, glorifying Thy crucifixion, O Lover of mankind, Christ our God. *Stichos: Blessed are the poor in spirit, for theirs is the kingdom of heaven.*  
*Troparion ([О]):* Thou didst not shake the seals of the stone by Thy Resurrection, even as Thou didst not open the enclosed door of virginity in her that bore Thee, O Christ our God. *Stichos: Blessed are they that mourn, for they shall be comforted.*  
*Troparion ([Х]):* Beholding the Bodiless One strangely and divinely shining, the Women were filled with awe, and heard: Weep not, the Lord is risen! *Stichos: Blessed are the meek, for they shall inherit the earth.*  
*Theotokion ([А]):* Thou, O most pure Lady, didst bear the Holy Power of the Lord, Who despoiled the kingdoms of hades, and arose, and raised with Himself human nature.  
*Ode 8. Heirmos: Hymn-makers... Stichos: Blessed are they that hunger and thirst after righteousness, for they shall be filled.*  
*Troparion ([Т]):* Praise and supreme exalt unto all ages Christ, Who willingly was crucified, laid in the tomb, and abolished the dominion of death! *Stichos: Blessed are the merciful, for they shall obtain mercy.*  
*Troparion ([Ѡ]):* Thou didst appear first unto the Women, rising from the tomb, sending them as heralds unto the Apostles, O Christ; therefore we glorify Thee unto all ages. *Stichos: Blessed are the pure in heart, for they shall see God.*  
*Troparion ([Ѳ]):* Having destroyed by Thy strength the power of death, O Compassionate One, and the kingdoms of hades, as King Thou didst empty them; therefore we glorify Thee unto all ages. *Stichos: Blessed are the peacemakers, for they shall be called the children of God.*  
*Troparion ([Е]):* Rising, Thou didst say unto the disciples: Hasten to preach My power throughout all the world, taking away the darkness of polytheism by the light of the truth. *Stichos: Blessed are they that are persecuted for righteousness' sake, for theirs is the kingdom of heaven.*  
*Theotokion ([Ѡ]):* As higher than the heavens and better than the Cherubim, more honorable than all creation, O all-holy Virgin, pray for those who hymn thee with faith.  
*Ode 9. Heirmos: Life-bearing Fountain... Stichos: Blessed are ye when men shall revile you, and persecute you, and shall say all manner of evil against you falsely for My sake.*  
*Troparion ([А]):* Let all the earth rejoice today, and let the clouds drop down gladness; for the Lord of Glory, Who was crucified upon the Tree, is risen, having freed us from mortal corruption, and enlightened us with incorruption, as He is compassionate. *Rejoice and be glad, for great is your reward in the heavens.*  
*Troparion ([М]):* Unto the holy Women who once brought myrrh with tears, the Angel shining brightly at the tomb answered: Why seek ye the Living among the dead? He is risen, having enlightened all things! *Glory:*  
*Troparion ([И]):* The radiant Resurrection of our God, shining brighter than the rays of the sun by the Spirit upon the faithful, enlightens all unto ages, and guides unto salvation: which we celebrate with a pure mind. *Both now:*  
*Theotokion ([Н]):* Having borne the Eternal Life, O Theotokos, quicken us who were slain by sins, and hasten to guide us unto true life, that with faith we may ever bless thee.

## On Friday of the Second Week after Pascha, in the Evening

*At "Lord, I have cried", we set 6 Stichera: of the Feast 3, and of the Menaion 3. Idiomelon Stichera of the Feast, Tone 1:*  
While the doors were shut and the disciples were gathered together, the Savior entered suddenly, O All-powerful Jesus our God, and standing in their midst, giving peace, Thou didst fill them with the Holy Spirit; and Thou didst command them to wait, and by no means depart from Jerusalem until they should be clothed with power from on high. Therefore we cry unto Thee: Our Illumination, and Resurrection, and Peace, glory to Thee!
After eight days from Thy rising, O Lord, Thou didst appear unto Thy disciples in the place where they were gathered, and didst call out unto them: Peace be unto you! Unto the wavering disciple Thou didst show Thy hands and Thy most pure side; and he, crying unto Thee with faith: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee!  
Thomas, called the Twin, was not with them when Thou didst enter, O Christ, while the doors were shut; therefore he did not believe what was told him, abounding from unbelief unto faith; nor didst Thou refuse, O Good One, to show him Thy most pure side, and the wounds of hands and feet; and he, touching and seeing, confessed Thee to be God not bare, and man not simple, and cried: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee! *Glory: Tone 6:*  
While the doors were shut, Thou didst come, O Christ, unto the disciples; then Thomas by dispensation was not found with them; for he said unto them in disbelief: If I see not the Master, if I see not the side whence came forth blood, water, and baptism; if I see not the wound whence the great bruise of mankind was healed; if I see not that He is not like a spirit, but flesh and bones: Thou Who hast trampled down death and confirmed Thomas, O Lord, glory to Thee! *Both now: Theotokion, Tone 4:*  
*The Dogmatic Theotokion: "The glory of all the world..." (Всеми́рнꙋю сла́вꙋ; see fol. 147)[^561]. O Gladsome Light. Prokeimenon of the day.*  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 1:*  
Accept our evening prayers, O holy Lord, and grant us remission of sins, for Thou alone art He Who hath manifested the Resurrection unto the world. *Stichos: The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty; the Lord is clothed with strength, and hath girded Himself. (Ps. 92:1 LXX)*  
Encompass Zion, ye people, and embrace her, and give glory within her unto Him Who is risen from the dead; for He is our God, Who hath delivered us from our iniquities! *Stichos: For He hath established the world, which shall not be shaken. (Ps. 92:1 LXX)*  
Come, ye people, let us hymn and worship Christ, glorifying His Resurrection from the dead; for He is our God, Who hath delivered the world from the enemy's delusion! *Stichos: Holiness becomes Thine house, O Lord, unto length of days. (Ps. 92:5 LXX)*  
By Thy Passion, O Christ, we have been set free from passions, and by Thy Resurrection we have been delivered from corruption: O Lord, glory to Thee! *Glory: Both now: Tone 5:*  
O Lover of mankind, great and boundless is the multitude of Thy compassions! For Thou didst endure being buffeted by the Jews, touched by an Apostle, and scrutinized by those who rejected Thee: How wast Thou incarnate? How wast Thou crucified, O Sinless One? But grant us understanding, that like Thomas we may cry unto Thee: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee!  
*Then: "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace..." Troparion: "From the stone of the tomb..." (Ка́мени зна́менану). Litany, and Dismissal.*  
*Be it known that the Canon for the Departed in the Oktoechos of the current Tone, across all Saturdays of Pentecostarion, must be sung on Friday evening at Compline.*[^562]

## On Saturday of the Second Week after Pascha

*At Matins: At "God is the Lord", Troparion of the Feast (thrice). After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*  
While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb, O Savior, they became as dead from the dazzling of the appearing Angel, who proclaimed the Resurrection unto the women; we praise Thee, the Destroyer of corruption; we fall down before Thee Who art risen from the tomb, our only God! *Stichos: Arise, O Lord my God, let Thine hand be lifted up; forget not Thy poor for ever. (Ps. 9:33 LXX)* The women came early in the morning unto the tomb, and beholding the angelic appearance they trembled; the tomb shone with life, the wonder astonished them; therefore going, they proclaimed the rising unto the disciples: Christ hath despoiled hades, as He alone is mighty and strong, and hath raised the dead, destroying the fear of condemnation by the power of the Cross. *Glory: Both now: Theotokion:*
When Gabriel announced unto thee, O Virgin, the "Rejoice!", with that voice the Master of all became incarnate in thee, the holy Ark, as righteous David said: Thou hast appeared wider than the heavens, having borne Thy Creator! Glory to Him Who took up His abode in thee! Glory to Him Who came forth from thee! Glory to Him Who hath set us free through thy childbearing!  
*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "The Stone Being Sealed" (Ка́мени зна́менану):*  
When the disciples were hidden through fear of the Jews, gathered in Zion, Thou didst enter unto them, O Good One, while the doors were shut, and didst stand in their midst, bringing joy; and Thou didst show them Thy hands and the wounds of Thy most pure side, saying unto the wavering disciple: Bring thy hand and examine, for I Myself am He Who suffered for thy sake.  
*Then, Reading. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ". Psalm 50. Canon of the Feast with Heirmoi on 6, and of the Saint to whom the temple is dedicated on 4, and in the Menaion of the Saint on 4. After Ode 3, Kontakion and Sessional Hymn of Menaion, and Sessional Hymn of the Feast. After Ode 6, Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast. After Ode 9, Exapostilarion of the Feast.*  
*At the Praises, Resurrectional Stichera of the Oktoechos, Tone 1:*  
We hymn Thy saving Passion, O Christ, and glorify Thy Resurrection!  
Thou Who didst endure crucifixion, and abolish death, and arise from the dead, pacify our life, O Lord, as Thou alone art all-powerful!  
Thou Who didst despoil hades, and raise man by Thy Resurrection, account us worthy to hymn and glorify Thee with a pure heart, O Christ!  
Glorifying Thy divine condescension, we cry unto Thee, O Christ: Thou wast born of the Virgin, yet wast not separated from the Father; Thou didst suffer as man, and willingly didst endure crucifixion; Thou didst arise from the tomb as from a bridal chamber, that Thou mightest save the world: O Lord, glory to Thee! *Glory: Both now: Tone 6:*  
After eight days from Thy rising, O Jesus King, Only-begotten Word of the Father, Thou didst appear unto Thy disciples while the doors were shut, granting Thy peace; and unto the disbelieving disciple Thou didst show the signs, saying: Come, touch the hands and feet, and My incorruptible side! And he, faithfully crying unto Thee: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee!  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 1:*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "O All-praised Martyrs" (Прехва́лнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*  
After Thy awesome rising from the tomb, O Life-giver, even as Thou didst not break the seals of the sepulcher, O Christ, so while the doors were barred Thou didst enter unto Thy glorious disciples, making them joyful, and giving them Thy upright Spirit, for Thine immeasurable mercy. *Stichos: Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world. (Ps. 18:5 LXX)*  
Thomas, called the Twin, was not with them when Thou didst appear unto the disciples, O Lord; therefore he did not believe in Thy Resurrection, and unto those who had seen Thee he cried: Unless I put my finger into His side, and into the marks of the nails, I will not believe that He is risen! *Stichos: The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands. (Ps. 18:2 LXX)*  
Touch as thou wilt, Christ cried unto Thomas; put in thy hand and know Me, that I have bones and an earthly body, and be not faithless, but together with the others yield and believe! And he cried aloud: Thou art my God and Lord, glory to Thy Resurrection! *Glory: Both now: Tone 4:*

While the disciples were doubting, on the eighth day the Savior came where they were gathered, and giving peace He cried unto Thomas: Come, Apostle, touch the palms which the nails pierced! O good unbelief of Thomas! It brought the hearts of the faithful unto understanding, and with fear he cried: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee!  
*And the rest of Matins. Troparion of the Feast, Litany, and Dismissal. First Hour, and the rest according to custom.*

*On Saturday, at the Beatitudes, Tetraodion, bearing the Acrostic: (ПРѠТОН МЕЛПО МЕЛѠС), that is: "I sing the first song". Tone 1, Ode 6. Heirmos: The deep encompassed us...*[^563]  
*Stichos: In Thy Kingdom remember us, O Lord, when Thou comest into Thy Kingdom.*  
*Troparion ([П]):* The lawless Jews nailed Thee, the Long-suffering One, to the Cross; seeing whose audacity, the sun and the moon straightway hid their light, and the dead, arising, sang hymns unto Thee, O Christ.  
*Troparion ([Р]):* Let the mountains drop down sweetness today, and let the hills rejoice; for Christ is risen on the third day, having destroyed the pangs of death: therefore unto Him, with the voice, let all of us faithful offer praise! *Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion ([Ѡ]):* When the divinely-wise Women came early in the morning unto Thy tomb, O Christ, beholding an Angel announcing unto them Thy light-bearing Resurrection, he cried: Why bring ye myrrh now unto the Living One?  
*Troparion ([Т]):* Without harming thy womb, O pure Lady, He was born as He willed; without breaking the seals of the tomb, He arose as He desired: for He works all good things, as Life-giver and Almighty.  
*Ode 7. Heirmos: Thee, the spiritual furnace, O Theotokos...*  
*Troparion ([О]):* Crucified with two thieves, O Master, and given vinegar to drink, suffering suffocation upon the Tree, O Long-suffering One, Thou didst willingly endure all and didst arise on the third day: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers, exceedingly praised!  
*Troparion ([Н]):* Taking Thee down from the Tree in a manner fitting a corpse, and committing Thee unto burial with love, Joseph lovingly embraced Thy most pure Body, and rejoicing cried unto Thee: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers, exceedingly praised!  
*Troparion ([М]):* Why seek ye the Living with the dead? the appearing Angel said unto the weeping Women; and why bring ye myrrh? But go, say unto His disciples: Christ Who was put to death is risen; hide yourselves no longer! *Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion ([Е]):* Thou alone art blessed, O full of grace, for thou didst bear the Giver of joy at the archangelic joyful voice: therefore we honor thee, and unto thy Son we cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers, exceedingly praised!  
*Ode 8. Heirmos: In the furnace, the children...*  
*Troparion ([Л]):* The formerly faithless Jewish people mock Thee, and in envy lift Thee hostilely upon the Cross, refusing to cry unto Thee: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, sing and supreme exalt Him unto all ages!  
*Troparion ([П]):* Joseph, having begged the Body, lovingly embraced it, and laid it in a tomb as dead; but He Himself broke all the keys, bars, and gates of death, and arose on the third day! *Glory:*  
*Troparion ([Ѡ]):* As Thou art spiritual Myrrh, very early in the morning the women's choir came to anoint Thy Body; and they were terrified at the Angel who cried unto them: All ye works of the Lord, bless the Lord, and supreme exalt Him unto all ages! *Both now:*  
*Theotokion ([М]):* Thou didst immutably bear a Son of the Divine Essence, O blessed Mary, although He appeared in bodily actions in the flesh as He was pleased; unto Whom let us cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, sing and supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*On the second Saturday, on the second week after Pascha:*  
*O sing unto the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supreme exalt Him unto all ages!*  
*Ode 9. Heirmos: An image of the pure...*  
*Troparion ([Б]):* Thou didst stand, O Master, upon the Tree of the Cross, and didst draw all nations unto Thy knowledge, O Lord; and Thou didst open Paradise, which the Forefathers had closed of old by tasting of the tree: therefore we all magnify Thee!  
*Troparion ([Л]):* They laid Thee in the tomb as dead, O Giver and Builder of life; and fulfilling the rites of burial, O Christ, the noble Joseph and Nicodemus, and the choir of women with them, wrapping Thee about, weeping lamentably, magnified Thee in a manner befitting God! *Glory:*  
*Troparion ([О]):* All enlightenment and desire, all consolation and strengthening, O my Savior, wast Thou unto all who devoutly celebrate Thy holy and third-day rising, O only Compassionate One: for Whose sake we all magnify Thee! *Both now:*  
*Theotokion ([С]):* From thy all-immaculate womb, the All-perfect One willingly assumed flesh, endowed with understanding and animated with a rational soul according to truth; and He is placed beside the Father's dominion in the heavens, O most pure Lady, from whence He shall come again!  
*At the Liturgy. Prokeimenon: Great is our Lord, and great is His power, and of His understanding there is no number. (Ps. 146:5 LXX) Another for the reposed: Their souls shall dwell among good things. (Ps. 24:13 LXX) Epistle: Acts, section 15 (Acts 5:21-33). Another to the Thessalonians, section 270 (1 Thess. 4:13-17). Alleluia of the feast with its stichoi: Come, let us rejoice in the Lord: Stichos: Praise the Lord, O Jerusalem! (Ps. 147:1 LXX) And for the reposed: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen. Gospel: John, section 14 (John 5:17-24). And another for the reposed. Communion Hymn of the feast: Praise the Lord, O Jerusalem! And for the reposed: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken unto Thyself, O Lord. Alleluia.*  
*And the feast of the passing week is dismissed. Thereafter, we chant every service on Saturday according to its full order until the Ascension of Christ.*[^564]

## Third Sunday after Pascha: Of the Holy Myrrh-bearing Women, and Righteous Joseph

*On Saturday evening, at Small Vespers. At "Lord, I have cried", we chant 4 Resurrectional Stichera from the Oktoechos, Tone 2:*  
*Tone 2:* Before the ages begotten of the Father: Christ our Savior:  
With the Archangels let us hymn: And the Anatolian Sticheron, in the same Tone:  
Thee Who wast crucified and buried: *Glory, Tone 4:*  
Why pour ye out myrrh with tears: *Seek all these Stichera below at Great Vespers. Both now, Theotokion, or the Dogmatikon which by custom is sung at Great Vespers; for the Oktoechos according to the Syncletics considers this hymn to be sung instead of our customary Theotokion at Great Vespers.*[^565]  
*Theotokion Dogmatikon, Tone 2:*  
O exceedingly great mystery! Beholding the wonders, I proclaim the Godhead; for Emmanuel opened the doors of nature as the Lover of mankind, yet as God He did not break the seals: but He came forth from the womb even as He entered in; thus in the flesh, even as the Bodiless One entered, ineffably He came forth, according to the Prophet who saith: This gate shall be shut, and no man shall pass through it, but only the Lord God of Israel, Who hath great mercy!  
*At the Aposticha:*  
*Prokeimenon: The Lord is King, He is robed in majesty; with its verses. And "Vouchsafe, O Lord". At the Aposticha:*

*On the second Sunday, the third after Pascha:*  
*At the Aposticha, the first Aposticha Sticheron, and Prosomoia from among the Festive ones, as the Superior desires. But if thou desirest, sing the following Prosomoia of the Theotokos:*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*[^566]  
*Stichos: I will remember Thy name in every generation and generation. (Ps. 44:18 LXX)*  
To all who sorrow, joy, and to the wronged, a protectress, and to the poor, a nurturer, and to strangers, consolation, and a staff unto the blind, visitation unto the infirm, a protection and shelter unto those who labor, and a helper unto orphans, art thou, O Mother of God on high, O most pure Lady: make haste, we pray thee, that thy servants may be saved!  
*Stichos: Give ear, O daughter, and see, and incline thine ear. (Ps. 44:11 LXX)*  
Every lawlessness have I committed, every sin without restraint have I, the wretched one, performed; of every condemnation have I become worthy: grant me images of repentance, O Virgin, that I may appear uncondemned there; for thee have I recorded as intercessor, thee do I invoke as protectress: do not put me to shame, O Bride of God!  
*Stichos: The rich among the people shall entreat thy face. (Ps. 44:13 LXX)*  
No other refuge have we, O pure Lady, nor towards the Creator have we gained any, save thee, O Bride of God: do not cast us away from thy fervent protection, neither remember the sins of those who with love flee unto thy shelter, O Mother of our God; make haste, and save us from the present wrath!  
*Glory: Both now: Tone 2:*  
Early the Myrrh-bearing women arose: *Seek it on fol. 134 (see fol. 134). At Vespers: "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart". Trisagion, and Troparion: The noble Joseph: (see fol. 131). And we enter the Trapeza, eating a little.*

*At Great Vespers.*  
*"Christ is risen" according to custom.*  
*And the Introductory Psalm, that is: "Bless the Lord, O my soul", we sing with melody. At the Kathisma of the Psalter, the first "Glory" we chant in the Tone of the week, and the remaining two in Tone 2.*  
*At "Lord, I have cried":*

*At Vespers.*  
*At "Lord, I have cried", we chant 8 Stichera, and we sing Resurrectional Stichera, Tone 2.*  
*Stichos: Bring my soul out of prison, that I may give thanks unto Thy name! (Ps. 141:8 LXX)*  
Come, let us worship God the Word, begotten of the Father before the ages, incarnate of the Virgin Mary; for having endured crucifixion, He delivered Himself unto burial as He willed, and arose from the dead, saving me, the wandering man!  
*Stichos: The righteous shall await me, until Thou rewardest me. (Ps. 141:9 LXX)*  
Christ our Savior tore up the bond written against us, nailing it to the Cross, and abolished the dominion of death. We worship His third-day Resurrection!  
*Stichos: Out of the depths have I cried unto Thee, O Lord; Lord, hear my voice! (Ps. 129:1-2 LXX)*  
With the Archangels let us hymn the Resurrection of Christ; for He is the Redeemer and Savior of our souls, and in fearful glory and mighty power He comes again to judge the world which He created!  
*Other Anatolian Stichera:*  
*Stichos: Let Thine ears be attentive to the voice of my supplication! (Ps. 129:2 LXX)*  
Thee Who wast crucified and buried, the Angel proclaimed, O Master, and said unto the women: Come, see where the Lord lay! For He is risen as He said, as He is almighty. Therefore we worship Thee, the only Immortal One: O Life-giver Christ, have mercy on us!  
*Stichos: If Thou, Lord, shouldest mark iniquities, O Lord, who shall stand? For with Thee there is forgiveness. (Ps. 129:3-4 LXX)*  
By Thy Cross Thou didst abolish the curse of the tree; by Thy burial Thou didst put to death the dominion of death; and by Thy Resurrection Thou didst enlighten the human race: therefore we cry unto Thee: O Benefactor Christ our God, glory to Thee!  
*Stichos: For Thy name's sake have I waited for Thee, O Lord; my soul hath waited for Thy word, my soul hath hoped in the Lord. (Ps. 129:5-6 LXX)*  
The gates of death were opened unto Thee in fear, O Lord, and the gatekeepers of hades, seeing Thee, were terrified; for Thou didst smash the brazen gates and shatter the iron bars, and Thou didst lead us out from darkness and the shadow of death, and didst break our bonds asunder!  
*Stichos: Let Israel hope in the Lord, from the morning watch until night! (Ps. 129:6 LXX) On the second Sunday, the third after Pascha:*
Singing the saving song, we send up praise from our lips: Come, let us all fall down in the house of the Lord, saying: O Thou Who wast crucified upon the Tree, and arose from the dead, and art in the bosom of the Father, cleanse our sins! *Then:*  
*Idiomel Stichera of the Myrrh-bearing Women, Tone 2, by Anatolius:*  
*Stichos: For with the Lord there is mercy, and with Him is plenteous redemption, and He shall redeem Israel from all his iniquities. (Ps. 129:7-8 LXX)*  
*Sticheron:* The Myrrh-bearing women in deep dawn, taking sweet spices, reached the Lord's Tomb; and having found what they did not expect, they pondered, reverencing the rolling away of the stone, and said one to another: Where are the seals of the sepulcher? Where is Pilate's guard and the secure custody? But a shining Angel became the announcer unto the unknowing women, and said unto them: Why seek ye with weeping the Living One, Who hath quickened the human race? Christ our God is risen from the dead, as He is almighty, granting unto us all incorruption and life, enlightenment and great mercy!  
*Stichos: Praise the Lord, all ye nations; praise Him, all ye peoples! (Ps. 116:1 LXX)*  
*Sticheron:* Why pour ye out myrrh with tears, O disciples? The stone is rolled away, the tomb is emptied! Behold corruption trampled by Life; the seals bear manifest witness; the fierce watchmen of the disobedient sleep; the mortal nature is saved by the flesh of God; hades laments! Running with joy, say ye unto the Apostles: Christ Who put death to death, the Firstborn from the dead, goes before you into Galilee!  
*Stichos: For His mercy is confirmed upon us, and the truth of the Lord endures unto the age. (Ps. 116:2 LXX)*  
*[These refrains are chanted after the Resurrectional ones.]*  
*Sticheron:* The Myrrh-bearing women, rising early and with haste reaching Thy tomb, sought Thee, O Christ, that they might anoint Thy most pure Body; and instructed by the Angel's voice, they proclaimed joyful tidings unto the Apostles: that Thou, the Author of our salvation, art risen, having despoiled death, and granting eternal life and great mercy unto the world!  
*Glory, Tone 6:*  
*Sticheron:* The Myrrh-bearing women, having reached Thy tomb and beheld the seals of the sepulcher, not finding Thy most pure Body, weeping, came with haste, saying: Who hath stolen away our hope? Who hath taken the naked corpse anointed with myrrh, the only consolation of His Mother? O, how is He Who quickens the dead put to death! How is He Who overthrew hades buried! But arise, O Savior, by Thine own sovereign power, even as Thou didst say, on the third day, saving our souls!  
*Both now, Theotokion, Tone 2: The shadow of the law hath passed away: (see fol. 147).*[^567]  
*Entrance. "O Gladsome Light". Prokeimenon: The Lord is King: with its verses.*  
*At the Litiya:*

*On the second Sunday, the third after Pascha:*  
*At the Litiya, Idiomel Stichera.*  
*If it happens that there is no vigil, we chant at "Lord, I have cried" 4 Stichera from the Oktoechos, and to the Myrrh-bearing Women 6. Tone 1:*  
*Sticheron:* O Myrrh-bearing women, why came ye unto the tomb? Why seek ye the Living among the dead? The Lord is risen; be of good courage! the Angel cries.  
*Sticheron:* With fear the women came unto the tomb, making haste to anoint Thy Body with myrrh; and not finding it, they were perplexed within themselves, not understanding the Resurrection; but the Angel stood before them and said: Christ is risen, granting us great mercy!  
*Sticheron:* The women of Magdalene, and the other Mary, came unto the tomb seeking the Lord; and they saw the Angel like lightning sitting upon the stone, and saying unto them: Why seek ye the Living with the dead? He is risen as He said; ye shall find Him in Galilee! Unto Whom let us cry aloud: O Lord Who art risen from the dead, glory to Thee!  
*Glory, Tone 6:*  
*Sticheron:* Joseph begged the Body of Jesus, and laid it in his new tomb; for it was fitting for Him to come forth from the tomb as from a bridal chamber: O Lord Who didst shatter the dominion of death and open the gates of Paradise unto men, glory to Thee!  
*Both now, Theotokion from the Alphabet, the same Tone:*  
*Theotokion:* My Maker and Redeemer, Christ the Lord, O most pure Lady, coming forth from thy womb, clothed Himself in me and freed Adam from the first curse: therefore unto thee, O most pure one, as Mother of God and Virgin in truth, we cry unceasingly with the Angel: Rejoice, O Lady, protectress, and shelter, and salvation of our souls!  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2:*  
*Resurrectional Sticheron:* Thy Resurrection, O Christ Savior, hath enlightened the whole universe, and Thou hast called back Thine own creation: O Almighty Lord, glory to Thee!  
*Stichos: The Lord is King, He is robed in majesty; the Lord is robed in strength and hath girded Himself. (Ps. 92:1 LXX) According to the Alphabet:*  
*Sticheron:* By the Tree, O Savior, Thou didst abolish the curse of the tree; the dominion of death by Thy burial Thou didst put to death; and Thou didst enlighten our race by Thy rising: therefore we cry unto Thee: O Life-giver Christ our God, glory to Thee!  
*Stichos: For He hath established the world, which shall not be shaken. (Ps. 92:1 LXX)*  
*Sticheron:* Appearing nailed upon the Cross, O Christ, Thou didst transform the beauty of creation; and the soldiers, showing their inhumanity, pierced Thy side with a spear; while the Jews asked that the tomb be sealed, ignorant of Thy power: but for the compassion of Thy mercy, receiving burial and rising on the third day: O Lord, glory to Thee!  
*Stichos: Holiness becomes Thy house, O Lord, unto length of days. (Ps. 92:5 LXX)*  
*Sticheron:* O Life-giver Christ, willingly enduring the Passion for the sake of the dead, descending into hades as the Mighty One, Thou didst snatch away those who were awaiting Thy coming there, as from a dominant beast; and Thou didst grant them to dwell in Paradise instead of hades: therefore also unto us who glorify Thy third-day rising, grant cleansing of sins and great mercy!  
*Glory: Both now: Tone 5:*  
*Doxastikon:* Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment: (see fol. 122).[^568]  
*Then: "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart". Trisagion. Troparion: When Thou didst descend: Glory: The noble Joseph: Both now: Unto the Myrrh-bearing women:*  
*Seek below at "God is the Lord". And the Dismissal.*  
*It is necessary to know that the hymns sung today with the Holy Resurrectional Stichera of Bright Week are sung on the present Sunday and on the remaining ones until the Ascension, both at Vespers and at Matins. If there is a vigil, the sacred hymns of the day: Tone of the day, and of the Resurrection of the day; but if there is no vigil, then after the Blessing of the Loaves: Troparion, Tone 1: O Theotokos Virgin, rejoice: thrice. And reading in the Homilies of Chrysostom on the Acts of the Holy Apostles; but if there is no vigil, then in the Acts themselves.*

*Typikon:*  
*Know that from April 23 until September 24, whenever a vigil occurrs, the blessed bread (emvle) is not divided nor eaten in the church, because the night is short; but it is distributed at the Trapeza and eaten at the beginning of the midday meal.*[^569]  
*It is necessary to know if the commemoration of the Holy Great-Martyr George happens to fall on this Sunday of the Myrrh-bearing Women, of the Paralytic, or of the Samaritan Woman: if the commemoration of Saint George or the Holy Evangelist happens, the service of the Venerable One is omitted, and we sing the Resurrectional, of the Samaritan Woman, and to Saint George, or of the Blind Man.*[^570]  
*On Saturday evening at Small Vespers. At "Lord, I have cried": 4 Resurrectional Stichera. Glory to the Saint. Both now, the first Theotokion of the Tone of Small Vespers. At the Aposticha: one Resurrectional Sticheron, and to the Saint from the Vespers Aposticha, with his verses. Glory to the Saint. Both now, Theotokion according to the Tone of the Saint. Troparion: Resurrectional. Glory to the Saint. Both now, Theotokion in the same Tone. Little Litany and Dismissal.*  
*At Great Vespers. After the customary Psalm, "Blessed is the man", the entire kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried": 3 Resurrectional Stichera, and 3 of the Feast, and to the Saint 4. Glory of the Feast. Both now, the first Theotokion of the Tone. Entrance, Prokeimenon of the day. Three Readings of the Saint. At the Litiya, Stichera of the Temple, and 3 Idiomel Stichera of the Saint. Glory, Tone 6: Worthily: Both now, of the Feast. At the Aposticha, according to the Alphabet. Glory to the Saint. Both now, of the Feast. At the Blessing of the Loaves: "O Theotokos Virgin" twice, and of the Saint once. And reading in the Acts; and the remaining readings of the day and of the Saint.*  
*At "God is the Lord": If it be the Sunday of the Myrrh-bearing Women, Troparion: Resurrectional: When Thou didst descend: once. The noble Joseph: once. Glory to George. Both now: Unto the Myrrh-bearing women. But if it be the Sunday of the Paralytic, or of the Samaritan Woman, or of the Blind Man, then Troparion: Resurrectional, twice. Glory to the Saint. Both now, the first Theotokion of the same Tone. After the Kathismata: Resurrectional Sessional Hymns with their Theotokia, and reading in the Gospel with Commentary. The Evlogitaria (Neporochny) with their Troparia. Hypakoe of the Tone, and reading in the Gospel. Polyeleos, and all Sessional Hymns of the Saint. Glory: Both now: the first Theotokion of that Tone, and reading of the Saint. Anavathmoi (Gradual). Prokeimenon of the Tone: "Let every breath". Resurrection Gospel. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ" thrice. Psalm 50. Resurrectional Sticheron. Canon: This Ode Heirmos and its Theotokion on 6; in which Ode two Troparia of the Heirmos twice, and two Theotokia; and at the end of the Odes, 3 Troparia of the Heirmos once, and Troparion of the Theotokos; and to the Saint on 4, and of the Feast on 4. We chant the Canon of the Feast thus: at each Ode, one Resurrectional Troparion, and one of the Feast, and Trinity Troparion, and Theotokion. Katavasia: Heirmos of the Feast, both choirs together. After Ode 3: Kontakion of the Saint and Ikos, and his Sessional Hymns, twice. Glory: Both now, of the Feast, and reading. After Ode 6: Kontakion of the Feast and Ikos. At Ode 9: "More honorable" is not sung. Exapostilarion: "Having fallen asleep in the flesh": Glory to the Saint. Both now, of the Feast.*  
*At the Praises: 4 Resurrectional Stichera, and to the Saint 3 conforming, Tone 2, or Prosomoia; then Idiomel of the Saint with verses. The verses we say unto the Saint: The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord: Second verse: The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree. And if there be his stichoi: Glory of the Feast. Both now: Most blessed art thou: Great Doxology. Troparion: Resurrectional of the Tone, Litany and Instruction, and Dismissal. And the customary procession into the Narthex. Glory: Both now: Gospel Sticheron. And the First Hour in the Narthex. At the Hours: Resurrectional Troparion. Glory to the Saint. Both now, Theotokion of the Hours. After the "Our Father", Kontakion alternating between the Feast and the Saint. At the Liturgy: Beatitudes of the Tone on 4, and of the Feast, Ode 3 on 4, and to the Saint, Ode 6 on 4. After the Entrance: Troparion: Resurrectional, and of the Saint. Glory: Kontakion of the Saint. Both now: Kontakion of the Feast. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Hymn: first of the Feast, then to Saint George.*  
*According to these rubrics, let the service also be sung to the Holy Apostle John the Theologian, if it happens in those same days.*  
*If there be no vigil on this Sunday, then the Trinity Canon at the Midnight Office is sung, bearing the Acrostic: (ТО ТРІССӦН ҮМНѠ ѲЕӒРХІАЄ СЕЛА́С), that is: "I sing the threefold light of the Godhead". Ode 1, Tone 2. Heirmos: In the deep He drowned of old: Chant it in the Oktoechos.*[^571]

*On the Third Sunday after Pascha: Of the Holy Myrrh-bearing Women:*  
*At Matins. At "God is the Lord": Troparion, Tone 2:*  
When Thou didst descend unto death, O Life Immortal, then didst Thou put hades to death by the splendor of Thy Godhead; and when Thou didst raise the dead from the nethermost parts, all the Heavenly Hosts cried aloud: O Life-giver, Christ our God, glory to Thee! *(Twice).* *Glory:*  
The noble Joseph, having taken down Thy most pure Body from the Tree, wrapped it in pure fine linen and sweet spices, and placed it closed in a new tomb: but on the third day Thou didst arise, O Lord, granting great mercy unto the world! *Both now:*  
The Angel standing by the tomb cried unto the Myrrh-bearing Women: Myrrh is fitting for the dead, but Christ hath shown Himself a stranger to corruption: but cry aloud: The Lord is risen, granting great mercy unto the world!  
*After the First Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*  
Thou didst not forbid the sealing of the stone of the tomb; and having arisen, the Rock of faith, Thou didst grant life unto all the faithful: O Lord, glory to Thee!  
*Stichos: Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up! (Ps. 9:33 LXX) Glory: Both now: Theotokion:*

*On the second Sunday, the third after Pascha:*  
Without being separated from the most pure Bosom in the highest, Thou didst accept burial and Resurrection for our sake: O Lord, glory to Thee! *Both now: Theotokion:*  
All thy mysteries are beyond comprehension, all thy mysteries are glorious, O Theotokos: with purity sealed and virginity preserved, thou wast known in truth to be the Mother who barest the true God: entreat Him that our souls may be saved! *And reading in the Gospel with Commentary.*  
*After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*  
The Myrrh-bearing women, having come early and seen the tomb, said unto the Apostles: The Mighty One hath overthrown corruption, and snatched those who were in hades from bonds; proclaim with boldness that Christ God is risen, granting great mercy unto us!  
*Glory: The same Tone:*  
Bearing the myrrh of burial, the women came secretly unto the tomb early in the morning, fearing the audacity of the Jews, and disregarding the guard of the soldiers; but frail nature overcame manly strength, for their compassionate purpose was pleasing unto God: fittingly therefore did they cry aloud: Arise, O Lord, help us, and deliver us for Thy name's sake! *Both now: Theotokion:*  
Exceedingly praised art thou, O Theotokos; we hymn thee: for by the Cross of thy Son hades was overthrown and death was put to death; we who were dead are arisen, and have been accounted worthy of life; we have received Paradise, the ancient delight: therefore giving thanks we glorify Christ our God as the Mighty One, and the only greatly merciful!  
*After the Evlogitaria (Neporochny) and the Troparia "The assembly of the Angels was amazed": (see fol. 133).*[^572]  
*Hypakoe, Tone 2:*  
Going after the Passion unto the tomb to anoint Thy Body, O Christ God, the women saw Angels in the tomb and were terrified; for they heard a voice from them saying that the Lord is risen, granting great mercy unto the world!  
*And reading in the Gospel of John. Then:*  
*Anavathmoi (Gradual), Tone 2. Antiphon 1:*  
Unto heaven I send the eyes of my heart unto Thee, O Savior: save me by Thine illumination!  
Have mercy on us who offend Thee greatly at every hour, O my Christ, and grant us a manner of repenting unto Thee before the end!  
*Glory: Both now:* Unto the Holy Spirit it belongs to reign, to sanctify, and to animate creation; for He is God, consubstantial with the Father and the Word!  
*Antiphon 2:*  
If the Lord had not been among us, who could have been kept whole from the enemy, who is both a slayer of men?  
Deliver not Thy servant unto their teeth, O Savior; for like lions do mine enemies move against me!  
*Glory: Both now:* Unto the Holy Spirit belongs the origin of life and honor; for all created things, as God the Son by power, He preserves in the Father through the Son!  
*Antiphon 3:*  
They that trust in the Lord are like Mount Zion, which cannot be moved by the assaults of Beliar!  
Unto iniquity let not those who live divinely stretch forth their hands; for Christ will not deliver the rod unto His heritage!  
*Glory: Both now:* By the Holy Spirit all wisdom gushes forth, whence grace comes unto the Apostles; and by their sufferings Martyrs are crowned, and Prophets behold!  
*Prokeimenon, Tone 2:*  
Arise, O Lord my God, in the precept which Thou hast commanded, and the congregation of the peoples shall surround Thee! (Ps. 7:7-8 LXX) *Stichos: O Lord my God, in Thee have I put my trust: save me! (Ps. 7:2 LXX)*  
*"Let every breath praise the Lord". Third Matins Resurrection Gospel, from Mark, section 69 (Mark 16:9-20): "Now when Jesus was risen early the first day of the week..." Then: "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ, let us worship..." thrice. Psalm 50. "Jesus having risen from the tomb as He foretold..."*  
*Canon:*

*On the second Sunday, the third after Pascha:*  
*Canon of Pascha with the proper Theotokia, on 6; which we chant even unto the Ascension of Christ on every Sunday; and to the Myrrh-bearing Women on 8 (see fol. 133). After each Kathisma, Katavasia: Heirmos of the present Canon of the Myrrh-bearers, or of Pascha: "The Day of Resurrection, let us be radiant, O peoples", both choirs together.*  
*Canon of Pascha, Tone 1. Ode 1. Heirmos: The Day of Resurrection...*  
*Refrain: Christ is risen from the dead!*  
*Troparion: Let us purify our senses... And all the rest unto the end.*  
*Another Canon of the Theotokos, Prosomoion, bearing the Acrostic on the first Troparia: (ѲЕѠФАНИС), that is, Theophanes, because he was the author thereof. The same Tone:*[^573]  
*Troparion ([Ѳ]):* O all-immaculate Virgin, who didst ineffably give birth unto Christ Who broke the cords of death, Who is eternal Life, Who hath shone forth today from the tomb, and enlightened the world!  
*Troparion:* Beholding Thy Son and God arisen, rejoice with the Apostles, O pure one graced of God; and thou who wast first to receive the greeting "Rejoice!" as the cause of joy for all, O all-immaculate Mother of God!  
*Ode 3. Heirmos: Come, let us drink a new drink...*  
*Troparion: Now are all things filled with light...*  
*Troparion: Yesterday I was buried with Thee, O Christ...*  
*Troparion ([Е]):* Unto incorruptible life do I come today, by the goodness of Him Who was born of thee, O pure one, and Who hath flashed forth light unto all the ends of the earth!  
*Troparion:* Beholding Christ, Whom thou didst bear in the flesh, arisen from the dead as He said, dance, O pure one, and magnify Him as God, O most pure Lady!  
*Ode 4. Heirmos: Upon the divine watch...*  
*Troparion: Unto the male gender...*  
*Troparion: As a yearling lamb...*  
*Troparion: David the ancestor of God...*  
*Troparion ([Ѡ]):* He Who created Adam, thy forefather, O pure one, is formed from thee, and hath destroyed the dwelling of death by His own death today, and hath illuminated all things with the divine radiance of the Resurrection!  
*Troparion:* Beholding Christ, Whom thou didst bear, shining forth wonderfully from the dead, O pure one, good and spotless among women and fair, today for the salvation of all, rejoicing with His Apostles, glorify Him!  
*Ode 5. Heirmos: Let us rise early in deep dawn...*  
*Troparion: Beholding Thy boundless compassion...*  
*Troparion: Bearing lamps, let us draw near...*  
*Troparion ([Ф]):* The singular festival of the devout is enlightened by the divine and life-bearing rays of the rising of thy Son, O most pure Mother of God, and is filled with joy!  
*Troparion:* Without opening the doors of virginity in Thine Incarnation, Thou didst not break the seals of the tomb, O King of creation; therefore, seeing Thee arisen, Thy Mother rejoiced!  
*Ode 6. Heirmos: Thou didst descend into the nethermost parts of the earth...*  
*Troparion: Preserving the seals unbroken...*  
*Troparion: O my Savior, the living and unslain sacrifice...*  
*Troparion ([А]):* That which was held of old by death and corruption is raised up by Him Who was incarnate from thy most pure womb unto incorruptible and everlasting life, O Virgin Theotokos!  
*Troparion:* He Who descended into the nethermost parts of the earth, descending into thy pure womb, and dwelling and becoming incarnate beyond understanding, raised Adam up with Himself, arising from the tomb!  
*Kontakion: Though Thou didst descend into the tomb... Ikos: Before the morning the sun...*  
*Ode 7. Heirmos: He Who delivered the Children from the furnace...*  
*Troparion: The women with myrrh...*  
*Troparion: We celebrate the death of death...*  
*Troparion: As truly sacred and all-festive...*  
*Troparion ([Н]):* Put to death, Thy Son slew death, O all-immaculate Virgin, and granted abiding life unto all the faithful unto ages of ages: the only blessed and exceedingly praised God of our fathers!  
*Troparion:* Reigning over all creation, becoming man He dwelt in thy womb graced of God, and enduring crucifixion and death, He arose in a manner befitting God, raising us together with Himself, as He is almighty!

*On the second Sunday, the third after Pascha:*  
*Ode 8. Heirmos: This chosen and holy day...*  
*Troparion: Come, on this auspicious day...*  
*Troparion: Lift up thine eyes round about, O Zion, and see...*  
*Troparion: O Father Almighty, the Word, and the Spirit...*  
*Troparion ([И]):* Through thee, O Virgin Theotokos, the Lord came into the world, and tearing open the belly of hades, granted resurrection unto us who were dead: therefore we bless Him unto all ages!  
*Troparion:* Overthrowing all the dominion of death in His Resurrection, Thy Son, O Virgin, as mighty God, exalted us together with Himself and deified us: therefore we sing praises unto Him unto all ages!  
*Ode 9. Heirmos: Shine, shine, O new Jerusalem...*  
*Troparion: O divine! O beloved! O sweetest voice!...*  
*Troparion: O great and most sacred Pascha, Christ!...*  
*Troparion ([С]):* With one accord, O Virgin, we the faithful bless thee: Rejoice, O gate of the Lord; rejoice, O living city; rejoice, through whom the light of the Resurrection from the dead of Him Who was born of thee hath shone upon us today!  
*Troparion:* Be glad and rejoice, O divine gate of light; for Jesus, setting into the tomb, hath shone forth, radiating brighter than the sun, and enlightening all the faithful, O Lady graced of God!

*Another Canon of the Myrrh-bearing Women, composed by the Lord Andrew of Crete, Tone 2:*[^574]  
*Ode 1. Heirmos: Taking up the song of Moses, cry aloud, O my soul: A helper and a protector became He unto my salvation; this is my God, and I will glorify Him!*  
*Troparion:* Thou wast torn in the flesh, Who art passionless in the Father's essence; Thou wast pierced in the side, gushing forth blood and water unto the world: Thou art our God, and we glorify Thee!  
*Troparion:* We reverence Thy Cross, we glorify Thy burial, O Good One, and we sing and worship Thy rising, and cry aloud: Thou art our God, and we glorify Thee!  
*Troparion:* Though Thou didst taste the gall of bitterness, O Sweetness of the Church, yet Thou didst pour forth incorruption unto us from Thy side: Thou art our God, and we praise Thee!  
*Troparion:* Thou wast numbered among the dead, O Savior, Who didst raise the dead; Thou didst see corruption, yet in no way sawest destruction: Thou art our God, and we praise Thee!  
*Troparion:* Let Zion be glad, let heaven also rejoice; Christ is risen, having raised the dead, who hymn: Thou art our God, and we praise Thee!  
*Troparion:* Wrapping Thy Body in fine linen, O Savior, Joseph laid Thee in a new tomb, O Savior; yet as God Thou didst raise the dead!  
*Troparion:* Early in the morning the women came, seeing Christ, and the blessed ones cried unto the disciples: Truly Christ is risen; come, sing praises unto Him with us! *Glory:*  
*Trinity Troparion:* All-holy Trinity, one Godhead, unoriginate Unity, Father, Son, and divine Spirit, save the world! Thou art our God, and we glorify Thee! *Both now: Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion:* Having annulled the first curse, O pure one, thou didst cause blessing to spring up in thy womb, bearing a Child; for He is God, though He wears flesh!  
*Katavasia: The Day of Resurrection...*  
*Ode 3. Heirmos: My barren mind make fruitful, O God, Thou Worker of good and Planter of good things, by Thy compassion!*  
*Troparion:* Thou didst stretch out Thy palms upon the Cross, O Jesus, gathering all nations from delusion unto Thy knowledge, and summoning them, O Savior!

*On the second Sunday, the third after Pascha:*  
*Troparion:* The Jewish people cry aloud unto Pilate: Release unto us the criminal robber; take away, take away, crucify the Sinless One!  
*Troparion:* When Thou wast crucified, O Christ, the light was darkened, the earth was shaken, and from the tombs many dead arose, through fear of Thy dominion!  
*Troparion:* Standing by the Cross, Thine ewe-lamb, O Jesus, cried with weeping: Whither goest Thou, my Son? Whither art Thou departing, O Lamb slaughtered for all?  
*Troparion:* I worship Thy Cross, I sing also Thy burial, I honor Thy sufferings, and the nails in Thy hands, and the spear, O Jesus, and Thy Resurrection!  
*Troparion:* Thou art risen, O Jesus; the enemy hath been despoiled, hades hath disgorged, and Eve is delivered from bonds and corruption by Thy Resurrection!  
*Troparion:* When Thou didst arise, O Christ, the bars and gates of hades were shattered, and the bonds of death were dissolved, through fear of Thy dominion!  
*Troparion:* O Joseph graced of God, come, stand with us, crying: Jesus the Redeemer is risen, Who raised Adam by Thy compassion!  
*Troparion:* Let the twelve-numbered band of the disciples rejoice with us, together with the Myrrh-bearing Women, and Joseph, and the other disciples and women-disciples of Christ! *Glory:*  
*Trinity Troparion:* With the Father and the Son, and the upright Spirit, one nature do I worship and hymn, dividing the Persons, yet uniting the Essence! *Both now: Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion:* Blessed art thou, O pure one, who art from the root of Jesse, from whom the Rod and Flower, Christ, sprang forth according to the flesh in deed!  
*Kontakion: Though Thou didst descend into the tomb... Then:*  
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the things on high" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щя̀):*  
The women bringing myrrh fervently unto the tomb, O Savior, rejoiced in their souls at the brightness of the Angel, and proclaimed Thee as God unto all, and cried unto the disciples: Truly from the tomb the Life of all is risen! *Glory: Both now: Theotokion:*  
Overthrowing all the dominion of death in His Resurrection, Thy Son, O Virgin, as almighty God, exalted us together with Himself and deified us: therefore we sing praises unto Him, glorifying thee, the pure Mother of God Mary!  
*Ode 4. Heirmos: Foreseeing Thy birth from the Virgin, the Prophet proclaimed, crying: I heard Thy report and was afraid; for from Teman and from the holy shadowed mountain hast Thou come, O Christ!*  
*Troparion:* By Thy Cross Thou didst despoil the belly of hades, and didst raise the dead, and abolish the torment of death: therefore those from Adam, worshipping, sing Thy burial and Thy rising, O Christ!  
*Troparion:* O compassionate Savior of ours, Who for the bowels of mercy wast nailed upon the Cross, and didst deliver us from the ancestral curse: loose the bonds of my many transgressions, for Thou canst do all things whatsoever Thou desirest!  
*Troparion:* O Savior, Who didst nail my ancient curse to the Cross, and didst pour forth blessing unto me by the blood from Thy side: loose the bonds of my many transgressions, for Thou canst do all things whatsoever Thou desirest!  
*Troparion:* Hades meeting Thee in the nethermost parts was embittered, O Savior, pondering that those whom it was able to devour of old, it now gives up unwillingly; and it is crushed in the depths below, despoiled and stripped of the dead!  
*Troparion:* Though ye seal the stone with the guard, O lawless enemies, yet the Lord is risen as He foretold, loosing the bonds of my many transgressions; for He can do all things whatsoever He desires!

*On the second Sunday, the third after Pascha:*  
*Troparion:* Arisen from the tomb, despoiling hades, quickening the dead, and gushing forth incorruption unto me by Thy rising: loose the bonds of my many transgressions, for Thou canst do all things whatsoever Thou desirest!  
*Troparion:* Truly be ashamed, ye lawless ones; for Christ is risen, and hath raised the dead with Himself, crying: Be of good cheer, I have overcome the world! Either believe in Him, or be silent, ye deceivers who reject His Resurrection!  
*Troparion:* Having uttered unto the Myrrh-bearing women "Rejoice!" when Thou wast risen from the tomb, O Good One, and unto the Apostles "Proclaim ye My rising": loose the bonds of my many transgressions, for Thou canst do all things whatsoever Thou desirest!  
*Troparion:* Let us honor the noble Joseph, zealous for piety, counselor and disciple, together with the Myrrh-bearing Women, crying with them and with the Apostles, and faithfully singing the Savior's Resurrection brightly! *Glory:*  
*Trinity Troparion:* Who can tell the indivisible glory of the all-radiant Godhead? For being one by nature, the Trinity is hymned, unoriginate, consubstantial, as a Monad sung in Trinity with simple Persons! *Both now: Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion:* Having received in thy womb and without husband given birth unto the Lord, O pure Virgin Mother, pray unceasingly as Mother of God, that He may loose the bonds of my many transgressions; for thou canst help in all things whatsoever thou desirest!  
*Ode 5. Heirmos: Dispersing the darkness of my soul, O my Savior, enlighten me with the light of Thy commandments, as Thou alone art King of peace!*  
*Troparion:* My first garment, which the body of sin wove for me, Thou didst strip off, O my Savior, clothing me with grace!  
*Troparion:* The fig leaves which my sin sewed for me, alas for me! because I kept not Thy most pure commandment, O my Savior, through the serpent's counsel!  
*Troparion:* My soul wounded by the thoughts of robbers, passing by, O Son of Mary, pouring out oil, heal it, O Christ!  
*Troparion:* Standing by the Cross, the most pure Mother of God cried with a mother's voice: Thou hast left me alone, O my Son and my God!  
*Troparion:* Overthrowing the malicious serpent by the weapon of Thy Cross, through Thy rising Thou didst crush the sting of death, O Jesus!  
*Troparion:* Where is thy sting, O death? Where is thy victory, O hades? Adam now cries aloud, concealed by the rising of Him Who quickens the dead!  
*Troparion:* Reaching Thy tomb, the Myrrh-bearing women heard a voice saying: Christ is risen, Who quickens those below!  
*Troparion:* Celebrating the memory of the devout Myrrh-bearing Women, and of all Thy disciples, in Thy radiant rising, we sing praises unto Thee, O Christ!  
*Troparion:* Let us all worthily honor the noble one who took down the Lord's Body from the Tree, and buried it faithfully! *Glory:*  
*Trinity Troparion:* Unto Thee, God the Father, and the Son, and the Spirit, I bow down, three holy Persons, and I believe in the Three as One, in one single Essence! *Both now: Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion:* Having given birth without seed beyond nature unto Christ, Who renewed nature, the only Good One, let us hymn thee unto all ages!  
*Ode 6. Heirmos: Held fast in the depth of sin, O Savior, and tossed about in the sea of life: yet even as the Prophet Jonah from the sea monster, so also lead me up from passions and save me!*

*On the Third Sunday after Pascha:*  
*Ode 6. Troparion:* Hades is dead! Take courage, ye earthborn! For Christ, hanging upon the Tree, cast His weapon against him, and he lies dead; and he is stripped of the dead whom he held, being despoiled!  
*Troparion:* Hades is despoiled! Take courage, ye dead! The tombs are opened; arise from hades, Christ cries unto you: He Who came to deliver all from death and corruption!  
*Troparion:* Even as thou wast able to swallow the dead of old, so now, O hades, cast them forth and give them up to Me, cries unto thee the Giver of life and God, Who came to deliver all from thine insatiable belly!  
*Troparion:* O Myrrh-bearing women, why do ye hasten further? Why do ye bring myrrh unto the Living? Christ is risen, as He said before: let your tears cease, having turned to joy!  
*Troparion:* Wrapping Thee in fine linen, O Christ, the noble Joseph laid Thee in a tomb, and anointed Thee with myrrh, in the destruction of the temple of His Body, and rolled a great stone unto the tomb.  
*Troparion:* The Lord is risen, despoiling the enemy, and bursting the bonds; He led all up, and raised the first-formed Adam, as He is compassionate and the Lover of mankind! *Glory:*  
*Trinity Troparion:* In unity let us the faithful hymn the Trinity, glorifying the Father with the Son, and the Spirit consubstantial with the Son, and being in the Father, unoriginate and ever-existing God! *Both now: Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion:* In thy womb untilled didst thou conceive, O pure one, the cluster of incorruption as a vine, from which the drop of immortality gushes forth unto us eternal life as wine!  
*Kontakion, Tone 2:*  
Thou didst command the Myrrh-bearing women to rejoice; Thou didst stay the weeping of the first mother Eve by Thy Resurrection, O Christ God; and Thou didst command Thine Apostles to proclaim: The Savior is risen from the tomb!  
*Ikos:*  
Going unto Thy tomb, O Savior, the Myrrh-bearing women were perplexed in mind within themselves, and said: Who shall roll away the stone of the tomb for us? And looking up, they saw that the stone was rolled away; and by the form of the Angel and the radiance of the face of Christ they were seized with trembling, and thought to flee. And the young man cried aloud unto them: Fear ye not; He Whom ye seek is risen! Come, see the place where the body of Jesus lay, and go quickly, announce unto the disciples: The Savior is risen from the tomb!  
*Then the Prologue. And:*  
*Synaxarion on the Third Sunday after Pascha: Commemoration of the Holy Myrrh-bearing Women, and of Pious Joseph and Nicodemus, and on the Taking Down from the Cross of the Body of our Lord Jesus Christ.*[^575]  
*Verses:*  
*The women disciples bring myrrh unto the Savior.*  
*And I, as myrrh, bring measures of song.*  
*On the Third Sunday after Pascha, we celebrate the Feast of the Holy Myrrh-bearing Women. We also keep memory of Joseph from Arimathea, who was a secret disciple, and also one of the Seventy; and with them of the Night-Disciple Nicodemus, who also came devoutly unto Christ. For these women are true and first witnesses of the Resurrection, and Joseph and Nicodemus of the Burial; which are the truest and most manifest testimonies delivered unto us. For when Nicodemus was expelled from the synagogue by the Jews because he refused to consent unto their council, and Joseph, after burying the Lord's Body, was cast into a pit by the Jews, he was snatched away thence by divine power, and came into Arimathea, his homeland; and the risen Christ appeared unto Joseph while the righteous one was held in bonds, and confirmed unto the disciple all the more the mystery of the Resurrection. He suffered many things from the Jews, not enduring to keep silent concerning the mysteries, but boldly proclaiming unto all the disciples what had come to pass. It is said also that Nicodemus, being descended from those who preserved the written record concerning the Consecration of the Bread and concerning the Resurrection, was the first of all to set these things forth in writing, being a son of the synagogue, and knowing more accurately the counsels, words, and all the customs of the Jews. For this reason, as we said, as truthful witnesses of the Burial, they were ranked after the women who came after the Resurrection, and after the first confirmation of Thomas; for it was so ordered that after eight days, as it is said, came the Good News. These women were the first to see the Resurrection, and announced it unto the disciples; for it was fitting that the sex that had first fallen...*

*On the Third Sunday after Pascha:*  
*...into sin and inherited the curse, should be the first to see the Resurrection and hear "Rejoice!", having first heard: "In sorrows shalt thou bring forth children."*  
*They were called Myrrh-bearers because, as Friday was preceding Pascha, and that Friday was great before the Sabbath, Joseph and Nicodemus hastened to bury the Lord's Body: although they anointed Him with ointments according to the Jewish custom, yet not as was fitting; for they applied only aloes and myrrh, and wrapping Him in a shroud, committed Him to the tomb. Therefore, having fervent love toward Christ as disciples, these women bought costly ointments and came by night, partly out of fear of the Jews, and partly because it was the custom to weep and anoint Him early, and then to supply what had been cut short by time. When they came, they saw various appearances: two Angels shining inside the tomb, and another sitting upon the stone; after these things they saw Christ and worshipped Him. But Mary Magdalene, supposing Him to be the gardener, questioned Him concerning His very Body. Many indeed were the Myrrh-bearing women, but the Evangelists, having made mention of the notable ones, omitted the others. These were: First of all, Mary Magdalene, from whom Christ cast out seven demons; who after the Ascension of Christ came to Rome, and as by a novel word brought Pilate and the high priest unto death, having reported concerning Christ the Savior unto Tiberius. Afterward she died in Ephesus, and was buried by John the Theologian; and by Leo the Most Wise was translated to the City of Constantine. The second was Salome, who is the daughter of Joseph [the Betrothed][^576] (for so he named his own, who was his daughter); she had Zebedee for her husband, of whom were born John the Evangelist and James. For Joseph begat four sons of the male sex: James called the Less, and Joses, Simeon, and Jude; and three daughters: Esther, Tamar, and Salome the wife of Zebedee. So that when thou hearest in the Gospel of Mary the mother of James the Less and of Joses, do not suppose her to be the Mother of God, for the Mother of God was accounted as mother of the children of Joseph; so that it came about that John the Evangelist was a nephew unto Christ, as the son of His sister. The third of the Myrrh-bearers is Joanna, wife of Chuza, who was overseer and steward of the house of King Herod. The fourth and fifth were Martha and Mary, sisters of Lazarus. The sixth was Mary of Cleopas. The seventh was Susanna; and there were many others, as the divine Scripture relates, who ministered unto Christ and His disciples from their possessions.*  
*Because these women proclaimed the Resurrection, and recounted many appearances for the confirmation and assurance of the true Resurrection of Christ, after Thomas the Church of Christ received them also to celebrate, as having first seen Christ risen from the dead, and having announced unto all the saving proclamation, and having lived well the life according to Christ, and as was fitting for women taught by Christ: by their prayers, O Christ God, have mercy on us, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.*  
*Ode 7. Heirmos: The Children in the furnace... with their Troparia and Theotokia (see fol. 133).[^577] After these, these customary Heirmoi:*  
*Troparion:* Befitting the highest, the Children in the furnace danced, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God, for in truth and in judgment hast Thou brought all these things upon us because of our sins, O Thou Who art exceedingly praised and glorified unto all ages!  
*Troparion:* As the Lover of mankind, desiring to save all whom Thou didst create from delusion, Thou didst deign to be nailed upon the Cross, that by Thy sufferings Thou mightest renew in Thy flesh the buried image, O Savior, and casting down hades, mightest raise the dead together with Thyself!  
*Troparion:* Lifted up upon the Cross, Thou didst summon all unto Thyself, O Merciful One, even as Thou didst promise beforehand, O Good One; for truly didst Thou deign to suffer all these things for our sins: therefore didst Thou open the gates of paradise unto the robber, O Savior!  
*Troparion:* Thou didst raise up the destroyed bodily temple from the tomb on the third day, even as Thou didst promise, O Good One, that Thou mightest truly show forth Thy glory, which Thou gushest forth unto us through faith, having loosed the bonds wherewith hades of old held those bound!  
*Troparion [For the Church]:* O Jewish madness! O counsel of the lawless! What unfaithful thing did ye see, that ye believed not in Christ? Did He raise the sick by a mere word, or did He not save the whole world?

*On the Third Sunday after Pascha:*  
*Troparion:* Did He not save that world? Let at least the soldiers convince you, or the dead who arose from among the dead!  
*Troparion:* Let the deadened guards recount how He was stolen away, Whom the foolish soldiers did not see: for if they saw Him not when He arose, nor perceived it, how could they ever understand that He was stolen? Let at least that stone convince you, and the burial shrouds of Christ!  
*Troparion:* Why do ye watch Him as a dead man? Why did ye place seals upon the stone, O Jews? With fear do ye guard against theft: behold, truly the tomb was sealed; how then did He arise, if Christ were not God? Let those who arose and were seen of many convince you! *Glory:*  
*Trinity Troparion:* Glorifying the Son and the Holy Spirit with the Father, crying with unceasing voices: O Holy Trinity, one and unique, have mercy and save us; O Unity in three Persons, O Father, have mercy on us, O God exceedingly praised unto all ages! *Both now: Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion:* In thy womb, O most pure one, how didst thou contain the Child before Whom the angelic powers tremble as God, except as He willed, as He was pleased, desiring to save all from Adam, and delivering Adam from the curse of that bitter eating of many kinds?  
*Ode 8. Heirmos: Unto Him Who of old in the bush on Mount Sinai prefigured unto Moses the Virgin's wonder, sing ye, bless, and pre-exalt Him unto all ages!*  
*Troparion:* The rays of the sun hid themselves through fear of Christ's sufferings, and the dead arose, and the mountains were bowed down, and the earth shook, and hades was laid bare.  
*Troparion:* Being in the furnace of old, the thrice-blessed Children, lifting up their hands, prefigured, O Good One, Thy most pure Cross, wherewith Thou didst overthrow the enemy's power, O Christ!  
*Troparion:* O blind Jews, deceivers and transgressors of the law, who believed not in Christ's Resurrection as though it were false: what did ye not see faithfully, that Christ is risen, having raised the dead?  
*Troparion:* O enemy Jews! Even if ye believe not, ask your soldiers: what suffered they? Who rolled away the stone from the tomb with his hands?  
*Troparion:* Who dried up the fig tree? Who healed the withered hand? Who filled the multitude of old in the wilderness, if not Christ God, Who raises the dead?  
*Troparion:* Who enlightened the blind man, and cleansed the lepers, and made the lame to walk, and walked upon the sea dryshod as upon dry land, if not Christ God alone, Who raises the dead?  
*Troparion:* Who raised the dead four-days man from the tomb, the widow's son? Who restored as God the paralytic on his bed, if not Christ God, Who raises the dead?  
*Troparion:* The stone itself cries aloud, the seals call out which ye set; ye placed a guard to keep the tomb: truly Christ is risen, and lives unto ages!  
*Troparion:* Truly Christ is risen; hades is despoiled, the serpent is put to death, Adam is delivered, the nether regions are shaken: why believe ye not henceforth, ye enemies and transgressors?  
*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*  
*Trinity Troparion:* With the Father we glorify the Son and the Holy Spirit, the Holy Trinity in one Godhead, crying: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou unto ages! *Both now: Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion:* In thy womb, O pure one, without seed didst thou give birth unto the Bread of everlasting life, commingled with our mingling, yet unchangeably one Christ God in two essences!

*On the Third Sunday after Pascha:*  
*Katavasia: We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord.*  
*Heirmos: This chosen and holy day... More honorable: is not sung.*  
*Ode 9. Heirmos: Her that conceived in her womb in the flesh beyond nature the Word Who shone forth agelessly from the Father, in songs unceasingly let us the faithful magnify!*  
*Troparion:* Having recognized Thee as God upon the Cross, Thou didst make the robber an heir of the spiritual paradise, as he cried: Remember me, O almighty Savior!  
*Troparion:* For our sake wast Thou buffeted and spat upon, O Jesus, by the lawless, Who didst inscribe the tablets of the Law on Sinai for Thy servant Moses.  
*Troparion:* With vinegar and gall, O Savior, wast Thou given to drink for our sake, that Thou mightest give us Thy Body and precious Blood for the food and drink of everlasting life.  
*Troparion:* Pierced in Thy life-creating side with a spear, O Christ, Thou didst pour forth unto the world Thy most pure Blood and precious Water, as an ever-living fountain.
