*Troparion:* Thou wast numbered among the dead, Quickener of the dead; Thou wast laid in the tomb, emptier of tombs; Thou didst despoil hades, having raised Adam.  
*Troparion:* Thou art risen, O Jesus; the enemy is bound, hades is despoiled, the tombs are emptied, they of the nether world are arisen, worshipping Thee, O Christ!  
*Troparion:* Who stole the dead man, and more so a naked one? Why are ye deceived, O Hebrews? Christ is risen, and the bonds and bars of hades are shattered!  
*Troparion:* Glory to Thee, O Christ Savior, Who didst pour forth life, and didst cause light to dawn upon those in the darkness of ignorance, and didst enlighten all the earth by Thy rising!  
*Troparion:* Let the noble counselor Joseph be hymned with the Myrrh-bearing women and the divine disciples, as a preacher and son of the rising of Christ!  
*Troparion:* Let us the faithful praise the marvelous Joseph, together with Nicodemus and the faithful Myrrh-bearing women, who cry: Truly the Lord is risen! *Glory:*  
*Trinity Troparion:* Unoriginate art Thou, O Father; uncreated art Thou, O Son; co-enthroned art Thou, O Holy Spirit: one name, three in essence, and three in Persons, O one true God! *Both now: Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion:* Let Jesse be glad, and let David dance: for behold, the Virgin, the branch sprung from God, hath budded forth the ever-living Flower, Christ!  
*Instead of "It is truly meet", Heirmos: Katavasia: Shine, shine... Then: Holy is the Lord our God, in Tone 2.*  
*Exapostilarion of Pascha: Having fallen asleep in the flesh... once.*  
*Another Exapostilarion, of the Myrrh-bearing Women:*  
Give ear, ye women, unto the voice of joy: having trampled down the tormentor hades, I have raised the world from corruption; run ye, tell the good tidings unto My friends; for I desire to illumine My creation with joy from whence sorrow came. *Twice.*  
*At the Praises, Let every breath praise the Lord: We set 8 verses.*  
*Stichera, Tone 2. Verse: To execute upon them the judgment written; this glory shall be to all His saints:*  
Let every breath and every creature glorify Thee, O Lord, for by the Cross Thou didst abolish death, that Thou mightest show unto the people Thy Resurrection from the dead, as Thou alone art the Lover of mankind!  
*Verse: Praise God in His saints; praise Him in the firmament of His power [LXX Ps. 150:1]:*  
Let the Jews say: How did the soldiers lose the King Whom they were guarding? Why did the stone not preserve the Rock of life? Either let them produce Him that was buried, or let them worship Him that is risen, saying with us: Glory to the multitude of Thy compassions, O our Savior, glory to Thee!

*On the Third Sunday after Pascha:*  
*Verse: Praise Him for His mighty acts, praise Him according to the multitude of His greatness [LXX Ps. 150:2]:*  
Rejoice, ye people, and be glad! For the Angel sitting upon the stone of the tomb, he proclaimed the good tidings unto us, saying: Christ the Savior of the world is risen from the dead, and hath filled all things with sweet fragrance; rejoice, ye people, and be glad!  
*Verse: Praise Him with the sound of the trumpet; praise Him with the psaltery and harp [LXX Ps. 150:3]:*  
An Angel indeed brought the "Rejoice!" unto her that was graced, before Thy conception, O Lord; and an Angel rolled away the stone of Thy glorious tomb at Thy Resurrection: the former announced tokens of gladness in place of sorrow, while the latter proclaimed unto us the Master and Giver of life in place of death: therefore we cry aloud unto Thee, O Benefactor of all, Lord, glory to Thee!  
*Verse [Anatolian]: Praise Him with timbrel and dance; praise Him with strings and flute [LXX Ps. 150:4]:*  
The women poured out myrrh with tears upon Thy tomb, and their mouths were filled with joy when they said: The Lord is risen!  
*Verse: Praise Him upon the loud cymbals; praise Him upon the cymbals of jubilation. Let every breath praise the Lord [LXX Ps. 150:5–6]:*  
Let the nations and peoples praise Christ our God, Who willingly endured crucifixion for our sake, and remained three days in hades, and let them worship His Resurrection from the dead, whereby the ends of the whole world were enlightened!  
*Verse: Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up; forget not Thy poor unto the end [LXX Ps. 9:33]:*  
Crucified and buried wast Thou, O Christ, even as Thou didst will; Thou didst overthrow death, and didst arise in glory as God and Master, granting everlasting life and great mercy unto the world!  
*Verse: I will give thanks unto Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart; I will speak of all Thy marvelous works [LXX Ps. 9:2]:*  
*(Note: These verses are appointed for all the Sundays).*  
Truly, O ye lawless ones who sealed the stone, ye have made us worthy of greater marvels! The guards have understanding; today He came forth from the tomb, and they said: Say ye that while we slept, the disciples came and stole Him away. And who steals a dead man, and more so a naked one? He arose Himself by His own power as God, leaving His burial garments in the tomb. Come and see, O Jews, how He broke not the seals, He that trampled down death and granted unending life and great mercy unto the human race!  
*Glory, Tone 6. Sunday Matins Gospel Sticheron [Eothinon 3]:*[^578]  
Unto the women who came with Mary bearing myrrh, and were perplexed how they should obtain their desire, the stone appeared taken away, and a divine young man, calming the turmoil of their souls, said: For the Lord is risen! Therefore proclaim unto His preachers, the disciples, to hasten into Galilee, and to see Him risen from the dead, as Giver of life and Lord!  
*Both now: Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos...*  
*Great Doxology. First Hour. Instruction; and the Dismissal.*  
*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes in Tone 2 on 6; and from the Canon, Ode 6 on 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 6: Save, O Lord, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance. Verse: Unto Thee, O Lord, will I cry, O my God... Epistle in the Acts of the Apostles, Section 16 (Acts 6:1–7): In those days, when the number of the disciples was multiplied... Alleluia, Tone 8: Lord, Thou hast been favorable unto Thy land... Verse: Mercy and truth are met together... Gospel from Mark, Section 69 (Mark 15:43–16:8): At that time, Joseph came... Communion Verse: Receive the Body of Christ... Another: Praise the Lord from the heavens. Alleluia.*  
## On the Same Sunday Evening
*At "Lord, I have cried", we set 6 stichera: 3 for the Feast, and 3 from the Menaion.*  
*Stichera of the Feast, Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, O container of ascetics" (Ра́дꙋйся):*  
Nailed with nails, Thou didst abolish the curse upon the tree by the Tree; and laid in the tomb, by divine power Thou didst raise the dead of all ages, who glorify Thy power with a loud voice: O divine Pascha, Jesus Almighty, our Life and Consolation! Therefore things heavenly rejoice with things earthly, singing songs of victory unto Thee, chanting unto the good Word, O Christ...

*On the Third Sunday after Pascha:*  
...Almighty, Who grantest great mercy unto the world!  
As living with the dead, yet upon earth the never-setting Sun, another company of the Myrrh-bearing women, weeping, cried aloud: Come, let us run with haste, and see at His tomb! And seeing an Angel shining within it, they marvelled in perplexity; but he, turning their weeping into joy, cried out: The Giver of life is risen! Fear not, ye women full of love; He is the good King, Who grants great mercy unto the world!  
Early the company of women sought before the sun the Sun that had set into the tomb; and the Angel, radiant with light, proclaimed unto them, radiating light upon those sleeping in darkness: Proclaim ye, radiant with light, unto the disciples; turn weeping into joy, and clapping your hands with unhesitating hearts, dance for the joyful and world-redeeming Pascha: Christ is risen, Who grants great mercy unto the world!  
*Glory... Both now... Tone 2:*  
The Myrrh-bearing women, having set forth early and reached Thy tomb with haste, sought Thee, O Christ, to anoint Thy most pure Body; and being instructed by the words of the Angel, they proclaimed joyful tokens unto the Apostles: that Thou art risen, O Author of our salvation, having despoiled death, and granting everlasting life and great mercy unto the world!  
*Then: O Gladsome Light:*  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2:*  
Come, let us worship God the Word, begotten of the Father before all ages, Who was incarnate of the Virgin Mary; for having endured crucifixion, He delivered Himself unto burial, as He Himself willed, and arising from the dead, He saved me, an errant man!  
*Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, unto Thee that dwellest in heaven [LXX Ps. 122:1]:*  
I have sinned against Thee, O Christ Savior, as the Prodigal Son; receive me, O Father, repenting, and have mercy on me, O God!  
*Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us [LXX Ps. 122:3]:*  
*For the Martyrs:* Not having loved earthly pleasures, the Passion-bearers were accounted worthy of heavenly good things, and became fellow-citizens with the Angels. By their prayers, O Lord, have mercy and save us!  
*Glory... Both now... Tone 4:*  
With fear the women came unto the tomb, hastening to anoint Thy Body with spices; and not finding it, they were perplexed within themselves, not understanding the Resurrection. But an Angel stood by them and said: Christ is risen, Who grants great mercy unto us!  
*Then: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart:*  
*Troparion: The noble Joseph: Glory: When Thou didst descend: Both now: The Myrrh-bearing women: all three written on Sunday at "God is the Lord". And the Dismissal.*  
*At Small Compline, sing the glory "To be pre-eternal" of the holy Sunday (see fol. 138).[^579]*  
## On Monday of the Third Week after Pascha
*At Matins, the customary beginning, as indicated on Monday of the Second Week after Pascha (see fol. 138).[^580]*  
*At "God is the Lord", Troparion, Tone 2: The noble Joseph, twice: Glory: When Thou didst descend: Both now: Unto the Myrrh-bearing women: and so throughout the entire week.*  
*After the First Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*  
Thou Who didst not forbid the stone of the tomb to be sealed, having arisen didst grant the rock of faith unto all: Lord, glory to Thee!  
As the waves of the sea my transgressions war against me, and as a ship in the deep, I alone am sinking through many sins; but lead me, O Lord, into the quiet harbor of repentance, and save me!  
*For the Martyrs:* Having Thee, Who clothest the heaven with clouds, for their vesture, the Saints...

*On Monday of the Third Week after Pascha:*  
...endured the torments of the lawless in the world, and abolished the delusion of idols. By their prayers deliver us also from the invisible enemy, O Savior, and save us! *Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
Being the fountain of compassion, vouchsafe mercy unto us, O Theotokos; look upon a people that hath sinned; show forth as always thy power: for hoping in thee, we cry unto thee "Rejoice!", as of old did Gabriel, supreme commander of the bodiless powers.  
*After the Second Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щя̀):*  
Bringing spices warmly unto the tomb, O Savior, the women were gladdened in soul by the radiance of the Angels, and proclaimed Thee as God of all, and cried unto the disciples: Truly the Life of all is risen from the tomb! *Glory... Both now... That same hymn.*  
*Then: Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ: Psalm 50. Canon of the Feast with the Heirmoi on 8, and of the Menaion on 4. Kontakion of the Feast, and the Exapostilarion.*  
*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 2:*  
Let every breath and every creature praise Thee, O Lord, for by the Cross Thou didst abolish death, that Thou mightest show unto the people Thy Resurrection from the dead, as Thou alone art the Lover of mankind! *Twice.*  
Pondering my mortal and grievous deeds, I flee unto Thy compassions, imitating the Publican, and the Harlot with her tears, and the Prodigal Son: therefore falling down before Thee in prayer before Thou judgest me, spare me, O God, and have mercy on me!  
*For the Martyrs:* Having suffered for Christ even unto death, O passion-bearing Martyrs, since ye have your souls in the hand of God in the heavens, and your relics work wonders throughout the whole world, priests and kings bow down, and all the people, rejoicing together, cry out in the customary manner: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His saints [LXX Ps. 115:6]!  
*Glory... Both now... Tone 4:*  
Mary Magdalene and the other Mary came unto the tomb seeking the Lord, and they saw an Angel as lightning sitting upon the stone, saying unto them: Why seek ye the Living among the dead? He is risen as He said; ye shall find Him in Galilee! Unto Him let us cry aloud: Arisen from the dead, Lord, glory to Thee!  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Тридне́венъ):*  
Dance, ye people, with psalms, sing unto Christ with joy: a great Pascha hath been granted unto us; Christ the Giver of life, and the Deliverer of His good and spiritual creation, is risen from the tomb!  
*Verse: Lord, Thou hast been favorable unto Thy land [LXX Ps. 84:2]:*  
Saying unto the Myrrh-bearing women "Rejoice!", Thou didst loose the condemnation of Eve, and having arisen, Thou sendest joy unto the disciples to proclaim Thy third-day Resurrection from the tomb!  
*Verse: Mercy and truth are met together; righteousness and peace have kissed each other [LXX Ps. 84:11]:*  
O marvelous and holy Pascha! Illumine us, O Christ! Come, let us all be enlightened in our souls: for behold, the day hath now blossomed forth; let us rejoice and be glad in it!  
*Glory... Both now... Tone 6:*  
The women poured out myrrh with tears upon Thy tomb, and their mouths were filled with joy when they said: The Lord is risen!  
*Then, Troparion of the Feast: The noble Joseph: Glory: When Thou didst descend: Both now: Unto the Myrrh-bearing women: Litany, and Dismissal. First Hour, and the customary procession into the narthex.*  
*On Monday, at the Beatitudes, the Triodion [Triodes], bearing the acrostic: "Adei tade Ioseph" ("Joseph sings these things"). Sung by Joseph [the Hymnographer]:*[^581]  
*Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos: Come, ye people... Blessed are the poor in spirit, for theirs is the kingdom of heaven:*  
*Troparion [A]:* Hanging upon the Cross willingly, O Christ, Thou didst destroy the strong kingdom of hades...

*On Monday of the Third Week after Pascha:*  
...and didst lead forth the eternal prisoners by Thy might: glory to Thy dominion!  
*Blessed are they that mourn, for they shall be comforted:*  
*Troparion [D]:* Come, said the Angel unto the Myrrh-bearing women, seeing the place where the Lord of all lay, and proclaim His Resurrection unto His friends!  
*Blessed are the meek, for they shall inherit the earth:*  
*Troparion [E]:* In a new tomb of old did righteous Joseph lay Thee, Who by Thy Resurrection didst renew the corrupted nature of mankind, O Giver of life!  
*Verse: Blessed are they that hunger and thirst after righteousness, for they shall be filled:*  
*Theotokion [I]:* Thou didst see Christ, O Lady graced of God, risen from the dead as the Sun of righteousness; pray Him to enlighten the hearts of all who hymn thee!  
*Ode 8. Heirmos: Into the fiery furnace... Verse: Blessed are the merciful, for they shall obtain mercy:*  
*Troparion [T]:* Glorify the Lord Who was lifted up upon the Cross and buried in the flesh willingly, and Who shattered the iron gates and bars of hades, and arose on the third day in glory!  
*Verse: Blessed are the pure in heart, for they shall see God:*  
*Troparion [A]:* Let heaven rejoice, let earth be glad with mankind: Christ is risen, and hath appeared unto His disciples, dwelling among them and filling their hearts with gladness!  
*Verse: Blessed are the peacemakers, for they shall be called sons of God:*  
*Troparion [D]:* Unto the weeping women the Giver of life proclaims joy, as they touched His sacred feet, the sound whereof our first mother Eve of old feared within paradise!  
*Verse: Blessed are they that are persecuted for righteousness' sake, for theirs is the kingdom of heaven:*  
*Troparion [E]:* We glorify the one God in three Hypostases, in one single essence, the Father, the Word, and the divine right Spirit, crying: Sing unto the Lord, and pre-exalt Him unto all ages! *Both now: Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion [I]:* With sacred voices let us hymn the Mother of God, as she who is truly holier than the highest ranks on high, and who by divine power guides mankind unto the choirs above the world!  
*Ode 9. Heirmos: Him Who from God was God the Word... Blessed are ye when men shall revile you:*  
*Troparion [O]:* O Jewish wrath! How unjustly do they condemn to death upon the Tree Christ, the Son of the one Father, Who by His divine authority breathes life into the dead who are in hades; Whom the divine choirs harmoniously hymn!  
*Rejoice and be exceeding glad, for great is your reward in heaven:*  
*Troparion [S]:* While the seals were lying unbroken, Thou didst arise from the tomb, O Christ, the Resurrection of all; and Thou didst appear unto the disciples, opening their lips, and crying: Go ye into the world, proclaiming My glory! *Glory:*  
*Trinity Troparion:* This radiant day of the Resurrection, being consolation unto us and joyful gladness, calls the faithful together divinely to celebrate and to cry aloud: O Father, Son, and divine Spirit, glory to Thee! *Both now: Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion:* O Virgin who gavest birth unto the Light, with thy divine light illumine our thoughts and hearts by thy prayers, dispersing the darkness of passions, and by grace making all partakers of the never-setting day!  
## On Monday Evening of the Third Week
*At "Lord, I have cried", we set 6 verses: 3 for the Feast, and 3 from the Menaion.*  
*Stichera of the Feast, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѡ҆ древа):*  
When the Myrrh-bearing women sought Thee early in the morning, O Christ, the Rock of life, they went carrying myrrh and spices, and heard the weeping Angel, the young man at the tomb, saying: Cease your weeping, but rejoicing in your salvation, cry aloud unto all: The Lord is risen!  
We know thee, O noble Joseph, as a Cherubic chariot, bearing upon thy shoulders Christ the King, Who was taken down from the Tree...

*On Monday of the Third Week after Pascha:*  
...we bless thy hands and divine eyes, we honor thy palms wherewith thou didst lay the Sun, the Word and God, in a new tomb: therefore with love do we honor thy memory!  
A feast hath appeared unto us of the divinely-honored Myrrh-bearing women and of noble Joseph, as another spring giving forth life, pouring out waters of grace unto the world, shedding forth the warm streams of Christ's rising; the assemblies of the faithful celebrate and cry aloud: Glory to Him Who hath granted His Resurrection unto the ends of the earth!  
*Glory... Both now... Tone 2: The Myrrh-bearing women early (see fol. 142).[^582]*  
*Then: O Gladsome Light:*  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2:*  
Jesus our Savior, having torn asunder the handwriting against us by nailing it to the Cross, hath abolished the power of death: we worship His third-day Resurrection!  
*Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, unto Thee that dwellest in heaven [LXX Ps. 122:1]:*  
I cry unto Thee, O Christ Savior, with the voice of the Publican: Be merciful unto me even as unto him, and have mercy on me, O God!  
*Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us [LXX Ps. 122:3]:*  
*For the Martyrs:* When the holy Martyrs pray for us and hymn Christ, all delusion ceases, and the human race is saved through faith!  
*Glory... Both now... Tone 2: Why do ye mingle tears with myrrh? (see fol. 121).[^583]*  
*Then: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart: Trisagion: Troparion: as customary: And the Dismissal.*  
## On Tuesday of the Third Week after Pascha
*At Matins, Troparion of the Feast (see fol. 144).[^584]*  
*After the First Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*  
Not having emptied the most pure bosom in the highest: Thou didst accept burial and Resurrection; Lord, glory to Thee!  
I am an unfruitful tree, O Lord, bearing no fruit of repentance at all, and I fear the cutting down, and dread that unquenchable fire: therefore I entreat Thee, before that calamity, turn me and save me!  
*For the Martyrs:* O Thou Who didst enlighten Thy Saints more than gold, and art glorified in the Venerable as God, being entreated by them, O Christ God, give peace unto our life as Lover of mankind, and direct our prayer as incense, O Thou Who alone dost rest in the Saints! *Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
Having escaped the laws of nature, in thy divine childbirth thou didst unite virginity: for thou alone didst give birth unto Him Who was begotten agelessly before thee; therefore we magnify thee as Theotokos!  
*After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щя̀):*  
The Myrrh-bearing women having arrived early and seen the tomb empty, said unto the Apostles: The Mighty One hath abolished corruption, and hath snatched those in hades from bonds; proclaim ye boldly that Christ God is risen, granting great mercy unto the world! *Glory... Both now... That same hymn. Canon of the Feast, and 2 from the Menaion.*  
*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 2:*  
Let the Jews say: How did the soldiers lose the King Whom they were guarding? Why did the stone not preserve the Rock of life? Either let them produce Him that was buried, or let them worship Him that is risen, saying with us: Glory to the multitude of Thy compassions, O our Savior, glory to Thee!  
Overlook my transgressions, O Lord Who wast born of the Virgin, and cleanse my heart, making it a temple of Thy Holy Spirit; cast me not away from Thy presence, Thou Who hast measureless mercy!  
*For the Martyrs:* Taking up the Cross of Christ as an invincible weapon, the holy Martyrs broke all the power of the devil, and receiving heavenly crowns, they have become a wall for us, praying on our behalf!

*On Tuesday of the Third Week after Pascha:*  
*Glory... Both now... Tone 2:*  
The Myrrh-bearing women, taking spices deep in the morning, reached the Lord's tomb, and finding what they did not expect, they pondered, reverencing the rolling away of the stone; and one said unto another: Where are the seals of the tomb? Where is Pilate's guard, and the secure protection? But the shining Angel became an instructor unto the uncomprehending women, and said unto them: Why seek ye with weeping the Living, Who hath made the human race alive? Christ our God is risen from the dead as almighty, granting unto us all incorruption and life, enlightenment and great mercy!  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѡ҆ древа):*  
The three-day light hath clearly shone forth on earth today, and the universal darkness of death is dispersed; the Resurrection of Christ appears, keeping the faithful; the apostolic ranks dance with love; Joseph rejoices, and the radiant memory of the honored Myrrh-bearing women crowns those who faithfully praise them and glorify the divine rising!  
*Verse: Lord, Thou hast been favorable unto Thy land [LXX Ps. 84:2]:*  
Taking upon thy shoulder, O Joseph, the Son Who sits at the right hand of the Father, thou didst bury with spices the inexhaustible Myrrh, the Resurrection unto the world; thou didst commit unto the tomb Him Who clothest Himself with light as with a garment, ineffably covering Him with a stone: therefore with songs do we hymn His light-bearing Passion and Resurrection!  
*Verse: Mercy and truth are met together; righteousness and peace have kissed each other [LXX Ps. 84:11]:*  
The multitude of Angels is amazed, seeing thee, O Joseph, burying Christ; the world blesses thee, and we the faithful marvel, divinely honoring the precious Resurrection, and warmly praising thee together with the Myrrh-bearing women: therefore we cry aloud unceasingly: By these prayers grant that we also may be saved from perils and tribulations!  
*Glory... Both now... Tone 4: With fear the women came (see fol. 142).[^585]*  
*Then: First Hour, and the Dismissal.*  
*On Tuesday, at the Beatitudes, the Triodion [Triodes], bearing the acrostic: "Christe, soson me" ("O Christ, save me"):*[^586]  
*Tone 2. Ode 2. Heirmos: Give ear, O ye people...*  
*Troparion [Ch]:* Having fashioned man with Thine own hands, Thou didst nail Thy hands upon the Tree, O Almighty One, delivering us from the enemy's hands!  
*Troparion [R]:* Having destroyed the dark prison of hades, O Christ, Thou didst take the prisoners whom it possessed, and as Mighty One didst raise Thyself!  
*Troparion [I]:* Seeing Thee, O Christ, arisen from the tomb on the third day, the Apostles worshipped Thee, and proclaimed Thy rising everywhere!  
*Troparion [S]:* Groaning and shedding tears, the divine women reached the holy tomb, but were instructed by the Resurrection of Christ!  
*Theotokion [T]:* Thou didst receive the Sustainer of all, O all-immaculate one, Who abolished the gates of hades, and arose as He said on the third day!  
*Ode 8. Heirmos: Unto Him Who in the bush unto Moses...*  
*Troparion [E]:* Lifted up upon the Cross, Thou didst reconcile the world, and laid in the tomb, O Savior, Thou didst raise those sleeping in the tombs, who glorify Thy great power!  
*Troparion [S]:* Descending among the dead, Thou didst put hades to death, and didst shatter the gates of death and its bars, and by Thy might didst lead forth the bound, unto the salvation of the faithful!

*On Tuesday of the Third Week after Pascha:*  
*Ode 8. Troparion [O]:* Thou didst appear, O Merciful One, unto Thy disciples in many signs during forty days, and being confirmed by them, we proclaim unto all the world God Who is risen!  
*Troparion [S]:* Thou art the Life of all, Thou art consolation unto the dead and the living, O Word, joy and enlightenment unto those who cry from the depths, and Who didst arise from the tomb on the third day, God in glory!  
*Ode 9. Heirmos: What earthborn man hath heard such a thing...*  
*Troparion [O]:* On the third day Thou didst arise from the tomb, even as Thou didst say before, and didst raise the dead, and didst raise the world by Thy Resurrection; unto the Myrrh-bearing women Thou didst first appear, and didst proclaim unto them to rejoice, bestowing the joy of all upon those who love Thee, O greatly Merciful One!  
*Troparion [N]:* The day of the destruction of corruption hath come, for Christ is incorruption and life, and the Raiser of the dead; He abides with His Apostles, teaching them the higher and divine things, and commanding them not to depart from Jerusalem! *Glory:*  
*Troparion [M]:* Unto the women who of old brought tears in the morning, the Angel said: Why seek ye today the Living among the dead? As He said before, He is risen, He is not here; but He empties the tombs, and breathed immortality into the dead, as the Lover of mankind! *Both now: Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion [E]:* Seeing Thine own Son and God risen from the dead, O Theotokos, thou wast filled with joy, for He appeared as the Cause of joy and gladness: therefore with joy every soul hymns thee, and ever glorifies thee, O Virgin!  
*On Tuesday Evening, at "Lord, I have cried":*  
*We set 6 verses: 3 for the Feast, and 3 from the Menaion.*  
*Stichera of the Feast, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѡ҆ древа):*  
The grieving Myrrh-bearing women stood at the tomb of the Giver of life, weeping and lamenting Christ; and seeing His stone suddenly rolled away, they were perplexed at the change. But hearing the words from the Angel, they rejoiced and announced unto the disciples of Christ the Resurrection of Him Who for our sake patiently endured being numbered among the dead!  
Bestowing unceasing and divine joy upon the divine and holy disciples, the women disciples of Christ, the Myrrh-bearers, appeared, having fulfilled the command of the divine Angel, at whom they were amazed, beholding him in white robes, hades despoiled by the Resurrection caused by the King Who died for our sake!  
Letting gush forth streams of weeping from the fountain of tears, the blessed women disciples of Christ now bring joyful words unto the eyewitnesses of grace, announcing unto them the rising of the Word, the joy that sprang from the tomb: therefore going quickly, they heard a voice most gloriously commanding them to rejoice!  
*Glory... Both now... Tone 6:*  
Having risen early in the morning, the women reached Thy tomb, and seeing the seals of the tomb, yet not finding Thy most pure Body, weeping came with haste, saying: Who stole our hope? Who took the dead naked one anointed with myrrh, the only consolation of His Mother? Oh, how He that makes the dead alive is mortified! How He that despoiled hades is buried! But arise, O Savior, by Thine own power, as Thou saidst, on the third day, saving our souls!  
*Then: O Gladsome Light:*  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 6:*  
Save me, O Christ Savior, by the power of the Cross, Thou Who didst save Peter in the sea, and have mercy on me, O God!  
*Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, unto Thee that dwellest in heaven [LXX Ps. 122:1]:*  

*On Tuesday of the Third Week after Pascha:*  
*Resurrectional Sticheron:* With the Archangels let us hymn the Resurrection of Christ; for He is the Redeemer and Savior of our souls, and in fearful glory and mighty power He comes again to judge the world which He created!  
*Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us [LXX Ps. 122:3]:*  
*For the Martyrs:* The choirs of the Martyrs opposed the tormentors, saying: We are soldiers of the King of powers; even if ye deliver us to fire and torments, we deny not the power of the Trinity!  
*Glory... Both now... Tone 6:*  
Joseph begged the Body of Jesus, and laid it in his own new tomb; for it was fitting for Him to go forth from the tomb as from a bridal chamber, Who shattered the dominion of death and opened the gates of paradise unto men: glory to Thee!  
*Then: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart: Trisagion: And the Dismissal.*  
## On Wednesday of the Third Week after Pascha
*At Matins, at "God is the Lord", Troparion of the Feast.*  
*After the First Stichologia, Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*  
Thou didst work salvation in the midst of the earth, O Christ God: having stretched out Thy most pure hands upon the Cross, gathering all the nations who cry: O Lord, glory to Thee!  
Thou didst arise from the tomb and burst the bonds of hades; Thou didst loose the condemnation of death, O Lord, delivering all from the snare of the enemy; Thou didst manifest Thyself unto Thine Apostles, sending them forth unto the preaching, and by them Thou didst bestow Thy peace upon all the universe, O Thou Who alone art greatly Merciful!  
*For the Martyrs:* O Passion-bearers of the Lord, blessed is the earth that was drenched with your blood, and holy are the temples that received your bodies! For at the tribunal ye rebuked the enemy, and with boldness ye preach Christ: entreat Him as good, we pray, that our souls may be saved! *Glory... Both now... Theotokion, Podoben: "Exceedingly blessed" (Пребл҃гослове́нна):*  
Running unto Thy Cross, she that bare Thee by name, unable to bear seeing Thee suffering unrighteously, lamented with weeping and cried unto Thee: How sufferest Thou, Who art passionless by nature, O my sweetest Son? I hymn Thine extreme goodness!  
*After the Second Stichologia, Sessional Hymn, Tone 2, Podoben: "Of compassion" (Милосе́рдїа):*  
Unto Thy tomb, O Savior, the honored Myrrh-bearing women came, and having taken myrrh to anoint Thee, they thought Thee to be a man; but the Angel announced joy unto them: The Lord is risen! Therefore proclaim ye unto the Apostles His fearful rising from the tomb! *Glory... Both now... That same hymn. "Having seen the Resurrection of Christ": Psalm 50: Canon of the Feast, and 2 from the Menaion. Kontakion and Synaxarion of the Feast.*  
*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 2:*  
Having impoverished Thyself with Adam's poverty, O Christ God, Thou camest upon earth, taking flesh of the Virgin, and didst accept the Cross, that Thou mightest deliver us from the enemy's bondage: O Lord, glory to Thee! *(Twice).*  
Rejoice, ye people, and be glad: the Angel sitting upon the stone of the tomb, he announced the good news unto us, saying: Christ the Savior of the world is risen from the dead, and all things are filled with sweet fragrance; rejoice, ye people, and be glad!  
*For the Martyrs:* The multitudes of Thy Saints entreat Thee, O Christ: have mercy on us as the Lover of mankind! *Glory... Both now... Tone 4:*  
With fear the women came unto the tomb, striving to anoint Thy Body with spices; and not finding it, they were perplexed within themselves, not understanding the Resurrection; but the Angel stood before them and said: Christ is risen, granting us great mercy!  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѡ҆ древа):*  
Is the Deliverer among the dead? weeping, the Myrrh-bearing women cried, the disciples of Christ. The exceedingly great Sun...  

*On Wednesday of the Third Week after Pascha:*  
...is even until this day beneath the earth, or is He risen as He said? And unto them thus weeping from the tomb the Angel proclaimed truly divine joy: Christ is risen! Run ye, proclaim unto all His divine Resurrection from the tomb!  
*Verse: Lord, Thou hast been favorable unto Thy land [LXX Ps. 84:2]:*  
The honored women prepared myrrh, while it was yet dark on the first day of the week, and came unto Thy tomb, O Christ, to anoint Thy most pure and divine Body: therefore having learned of Thy Resurrection, they hastened to announce it also unto the initiates, whom Thou didst meet from the tomb, crying: Rejoice! O most loving Master!  
*Verse: Mercy and truth are met together; righteousness and peace have kissed each other [LXX Ps. 84:11]:*  
The disciples of the Most High God, buried on earth as a dead man, strive to anoint Him with precious spices; and seeing a new young man sitting in the tomb, they were amazed, beholding him shining with radiance: therefore having learned of the third-day Resurrection of the Word from the tomb, they announced it unto the Apostles!  
*Glory... Both now... Tone 5: Thou Who clothest Thyself with light: (see fol. 122).*[^587]  
*Then: Troparion of the Feast: First Hour: And the Dismissal.*  
*On that same Wednesday, at the Beatitudes, the Triodion [Triodes], bearing the acrostic: "Igegon sosotne", that is: "Arisen, O Savior":*[^588]  
*Tone 2. Ode 3. Heirmos: The barren has blossomed...*  
*Troparion [I]:* All creation, seeing Thee upon the Cross suffering willingly, O Lord, was altered and troubled, and the dead arose from the tombs!  
*Troparion [G]:* When Thou wast in hades, O Giver of life and Lord, with Thy soul, all-destructive death seeing Thee was terrified, shaking out the prisoners whom it had swallowed!  
*Troparion [E]:* All creation was filled with joy, O Merciful Lord, and by Thy raising of the dead Thou didst overthrow the realm of death, leaving us all behind in peace!  
*Theotokion [R]:* A sword pierced through thy soul, O pure and all-immaculate one, when thou sawest Christ crucified; but thou didst rejoice, beholding Him risen from the tomb!  
*Ode 8. Heirmos: The fiery furnace...*  
*Troparion [O]:* Thou diest upon the Cross, being the Life of all, and as a dead man without breath Thou wast laid in the tomb, O Long-suffering One, and on the third day Thou didst arise unto the resurrection of the world: therefore, O Giver of life, with gladness do we glorify Thee!  
*Troparion [N]:* Zion heard of the Lord's rising, and was now filled with gladness; and the daughters thereof openly rejoiced, and preached unto the disciples, saying: Grieve ye not, the Giver of life is risen!  
*Troparion [O]:* Early in the morning with haste the women came unto the tomb, and looking in with trembling, they saw the Angels in white apparel, and being seized with fear, they heard: Fear ye not, the Giver of life is risen!  
*Theotokion [S]:* Glorifying thee in song as the radiant cloud of the spiritual Sun, O full of divine grace, we cry: Enlighten our hearts, driving away all the darkness of despondency and passions, O Bride of God!  
*Ode 9. Heirmos: The Father without beginning...*  
*Troparion [O]:* When the sun saw Thee, the Sun of Righteousness, stretched out upon the Cross, it hid its light, the stones split, and hades speedily sent forth the dead, who praise Thy power, O all-powerful Giver of life!  
*Troparion [T]:* He Who was in the insatiable belly, pre-eminently and gloriously emptied it as almighty, and immediately arising in glory...  

*On Wednesday of the Third Week after Pascha:*  
*Troparion [T cont.]:* ...was seen of the divine disciples: Him let us worship with the Father and the Spirit! *Glory:*  
*Troparion [N]:* The Lord is risen! the holy Angel who appeared in the tomb cried unto the Myrrh-bearing women: Go ye and tell this unto His disciples weeping and mourning in Galilee, that they may see Him there! *Both now: Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion [E]:* To be delivered from perils and afflictions, and passionate tribulations and darkness, and the manifoldly woven snares of the evil one, O all-immaculate one: pray unceasingly unto Christ our God, for those who ever bless thee!  
## On Wednesday Evening of the Third Week
*At "Lord, I have cried", we set 6 verses: 3 for the Feast, and 3 from the Menaion.*  
*Stichera of the Feast, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѡ҆ древа):*  
Having heard the joy from the youths sitting by the tomb of the Word, the Myrrh-bearing women showed a most diligent running, having followed in haste the rank of the former Myrrh-bearers: as heralds of good tidings having seen the Resurrection from the depths of hades, announcing unto the initiates Him Who is risen for our sake!  
Having anticipated deep dawn, with fear the Myrrh-bearing women came unto the tomb, bringing myrrh unto Christ the Giver of life, Who was numbered among the dead, and Who put hades to death; but the divine Angel stood before them, crying: Why seek ye with the dead Him Who is life-bearing and alive? Go ye, proclaiming the Resurrection!  
Thou bearest upon thy shoulders Him Who rests upon the Cherubim, O blessed friend Joseph, as another bier upon thy shoulders, Him Who was incarnate before, and now is mortified, Who made alive us that were deadened: beholding Whose Resurrection, thou dost rejoice with the honored Myrrh-bearing women, even though ye bitterly lament His mortification!  
*Glory... Both now... Tone 4:*  
Mary Magdalene and the other Mary came unto the tomb seeking the Lord, and saw the Angel as lightning sitting upon the stone, and saying unto them: Why seek ye the Living among the dead? He is risen as He said; in Galilee ye shall find Him: unto Whom let us cry aloud: O Lord risen from the dead, glory to Thee!  
*Then: O Gladsome Light:*  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2:*  
Thy Resurrection, O Christ Savior, hath enlightened the whole universe, and Thou hast summoned Thy creation: almighty Lord, glory to Thee!  
*Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, unto Thee that dwellest in heaven [LXX Ps. 122:1]:*  
Thou didst magnify in the universe, O Christ, the names of the supreme Apostles: they learned the ineffable things of heaven, they gave healings unto earthly men, and their eyes cured the very passions; the wonder-workers from among fishermen, who from the depths brought the doctrines of the grace of theology: for their sake, O Merciful One, grant us Thy great mercy!  
*Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us [LXX Ps. 122:3]:*  
*For the Martyrs:* Great is the glory which ye acquired, O Saints, through faith; for not only in suffering did ye conquer the enemy, but even after death ye drive away spirits and heal the infirm, physicians of souls and bodies: pray ye unto the Lord that our souls may find mercy!  
*Glory... Both now... Tone 2:*  
The women poured out myrrh with tears upon Thy tomb, and their mouths were filled with joy when they said: The Lord is risen!  
*Then: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart: Trisagion: Troparion (see fol. 134).[^589] And the Dismissal.*  
## On Thursday of the Third Week after Pascha
*At Matins, at "God is the Lord", look for the Troparion of the Feast. After the First Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 2, Podoben: "Exceedingly blessed" (Пребл҃гослове́нна):*  
Bearing myrrh in secret unto Thy burial, the women came unto the tomb early...  

*On Thursday of the Third Week after Pascha:*  
...they came early, fearing the readiness of the Jews, and disregarding the guard of the soldiers; but frail nature overcame courage, for understanding ordered what was well-pleasing unto the Giver and God: therefore in harmony they cried aloud: Arise, O Lord, help us, and deliver us for Thy name's glory!  
*Troparion:* Thou Who didst give the fishermen words above the orators, and didst send them as preachers throughout all the earth, unenfeebled by Thy love for mankind, O Christ God: by them establish Thy Church, and send down Thy blessing upon the faithful, O Thou Who alone art merciful and the Lover of mankind!  
*For the Martyrs:* Martyrs of the Apostles, and holy Prophets, Venerable and Righteous ones, who have fought the good fight and kept the faith, having boldness before the Savior: entreat Him on our behalf, we pray, that our souls may be saved! *Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion:* We have become partakers of the divine nature through thee, O Virgin Theotokos: for thou didst bear God incarnate for us; therefore as is due we all piously magnify thee!  
*After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щя̀):*  
Bringing myrrh, O Savior, the women sought with diligence to anoint Thee, O Lover of mankind; but from the tomb the Angel cried aloud unto them: The Giver of life and Lord is risen, trampling down hades and death! *Glory... Both now... That same hymn. "Having seen the Resurrection of Christ": Psalm 50: Canon of the Feast, and 2 from the Menaion. Photogogikon of the Feast.*  
*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 2:*  
The Angel indeed brought the "Rejoice!" unto her that was full of grace before Thy conception, O Lord; but an Angel rolled away the stone of Thy glorious tomb at Thy Resurrection: the one indeed, in place of sorrow, announced tokens of gladness; but the other, in place of death, proclaimed unto us the Master, the Giver of life: therefore we cry aloud unto Thee: O Benefactor of all, Lord, glory to Thee! *(Twice).*  
Ever reproached by unrighteous deeds, and fleeing unto Thee, the truly existing God, we bring the voice of Thy disciples, saying: Save us, O Master, we perish! Show now also unto our enemies, we pray, that Thou protectest the people and savest from perils, by the prayers of the Apostles; overlook our sins, according to Thy great goodness, O Lord!  
*For the Martyrs:* Every city and country honors your relics, O Passion-bearing Martyrs: for having suffered lawfully, ye received the heavenly crown; and therefore ye are the boast of priests, the victory of kings, and the beauty of churches!  
*Glory... Both now... Tone 2: The Myrrh-bearing women: (see fol. 145).*[^590]  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѡ҆ древа):*  
When Thou didst arise as God, then didst Thou grant the Myrrh-bearing women to rejoice, O Word, saying: I have not yet ascended unto My Father, and for the rest touch Me not; go ye only, tell My friends: Behold, He goes before you! Therefore having been seen of them after these things, by breathing upon them Thou didst give them the Holy Spirit, O Savior!  
*Verse: Lord, Thou hast been favorable unto Thy land [LXX Ps. 84:2]:*  
At the swift running of the Myrrh-bearing women announcing Christ's Resurrection from the dead, the initiates did not believe, but hastened to see the tomb; and seeing the linen winding-sheet and the napkin in the tomb, but Himself not seen at all: therefore having recognized the Resurrection, they are seen as preachers in the world, proclaiming this also unto the nations!  
*Verse: Mercy and truth are met together; righteousness...*  

*On Thursday of the Third Week after Pascha:*  
...and peace have kissed each other [LXX Ps. 84:11]:  
The unapproachable Light hath appeared unto us, Christ the Lord, beautiful from the tomb: hades is despoiled, Satan is abolished, the ends of the world rejoice, creation dances, the Church of Christ sings celebrating, keeping the memory of noble Joseph, and with him of the Myrrh-bearing women accomplishes the solemn feast!  
*Glory... Both now... Tone 2:*  
Why do ye pour out myrrh with tears, O women disciples? The stone is rolled away, the tomb is emptied: behold corruption trampled down by Life, the seals bearing witness clearly, the fiercely sleeping guards of the disobedient, the mortal race saved by the Flesh of God, hades lamenting! Run ye with joy, say unto the Apostles: Christ Who put death to death, the Firstborn from the dead, goes before you into Galilee!  
*Then: Troparion (see fol. 134).[^591] First Hour, and the Dismissal.*  
*On that same Thursday, at the Beatitudes, the Triodion [Triodes], bearing the acrostic: "Asmado to Theo", that is: "Singing a song unto God":*[^592]  
*Tone 2. Ode 4. Heirmos: I heard, O Lord, Thy report...*  
*Troparion [A]:* Thou didst ascend the Cross willingly, O Lord, girded with power, O Merciful One; descending with Thy soul into hades, Thou didst loose the strong bonds of hades, and didst raise the dead with Thyself, O Compassionate One!  
*Troparion [S]:* Terribly didst Thou shatter the bars and gates of hades by Thy might, O Master, and didst despoil his wealth: therefore do we glorify Thy manifold condescension and Thy honorable Resurrection!  
*Troparion [M]:* To anoint Thy most pure Body, the women came unto the tomb; and finding the Angel sitting upon the stone, O Christ, they were amazed with fear, with whom we hymn Thy rising!  
*Theotokion [A]:* O holy one of the Holy, beyond mind didst thou bear the Theotokos, Who grants cleansing, deliverance, and forgiveness of sins unto the faithful through thy mediation, who glorify His Resurrection!  
*Ode 8. Heirmos: The commandment of the tyrant...*  
*Troparion [D]:* Thou didst exalt human nature, O Christ, by Thy being lifted up upon the Cross, and didst cast down the enemy who fell from on high into the abyss; and after burial Thou didst arise, despoiling death!  
*Troparion [O]:* Thou didst shatter the bars of hades, O Christ, and in courage didst lead forth the bound captives, who glorify Thy power and Thy third-day rising from the tomb!  
*Troparion [T]:* When of old the choir of holy Apostles saw Thee risen from the tomb in glory, O Christ, being filled with gladness, they cried: Glory to Thy rising!  
*Theotokion [O]:* Thy weeping, O most pure and holy one, was turned into joy when thou didst behold Thine own Son and God, Christ, risen from the dead on the third day in glory, and having enlightened the world!  
*Ode 9. Heirmos: Him Who was before the sun...*  
*Troparion [O]:* Thou didst appear crucified between two thieves, O greatly Merciful One, and wast laid in the lowest pit, and didst raise those who were in darkness and the shadow of death: therefore loving Thy third-day Resurrection, O Compassionate One!  
*Troparion [Th]:* Death no longer rules over men, even as it is written, since Christ the Immortal arose, having despoiled the insatiable realms of hades: let the whole universe celebrate and rejoice! *Glory:*  
*Troparion [E]:* The Angel shining before the tomb said unto the Myrrh-bearing women: Why seek ye with weeping Him Who is free among the dead? As He said before, He is already risen; go ye, crying aloud with joy! *Both now: Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion [O]:* Thou didst appear more spacious than the heavens, O Virgin, having contained God Who was laid beneath the earth by grace, and by His death bestowed immortality upon all: Whom piously glorifying, we bless thee as is due!  

## On Thursday Evening of the Third Week
*At "Lord, I have cried", we set 6 verses: 3 for the Feast, and 3 from the Menaion.*  
*Stichera of the Feast, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (ѽ Пресла́вное чꙋ́до):*  
Following the honored footsteps of Christ Who appeared, and serving Him with zealous disposition, the Myrrh-bearing women by righteousness would not abandon Him even when dead, but went and brought myrrh with tears, being prayerfully moved: therefore do we celebrate your holy memory!  
Desiring to see the Life mortified in the tomb, the company of divine women came by night and heard the Angel: As Christ foretold, He is risen! Go ye with haste, say unto His disciples, and cast away despondency from your soul, having received ineffable joy instead of weeping!  
Today we the faithful dance at your memory, O Myrrh-bearing women, glorifying the all-good Lord Who glorified you, Whom pray ye unceasingly that we may obtain eternal and enduring glory and the brightness of the Saints, having boldness toward Him always, O divinely-blessed ones!  
*Glory... Both now... Tone 2:*  
Having arisen early and with haste reached Thy tomb, the Myrrh-bearing women sought Thee, O Christ, to anoint Thy most pure Body; and having heard the words of the Angel, in joy-creating images they proclaimed unto the Apostles that Thou art risen, O Author of our salvation, having despoiled death, and granting unto the world eternal life and great mercy!  
*Then: O Gladsome Light:*  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2:*  
Those who ever enjoyed Thy gifts cried out for crucifixion, and asked to receive a malefactor instead of the Benefactor, the murderers of the Prophets; but Thou wast silent, O Christ, enduring their audacity, willing to suffer and save us as the Lover of mankind!  
*Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes, unto Thee that dwellest in heaven [LXX Ps. 122:1]. Resurrectional:*  
Come, let us worship God the Word, begotten of the Father before the ages, incarnate of the Virgin Mary; for having endured crucifixion, He delivered Himself unto burial as He willed, and arose from the dead, saving me, the wandering man!  
*Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us [LXX Ps. 122:3]:*  
Not having desired earthly pleasures, the Passion-bearers were accounted worthy of heavenly goods, and became fellow-citizens with the Angels: O Lord, by their prayers have mercy and save us!  
*Glory... Both now... Tone 6:*  
Joseph begged the Body of Jesus, and laid it in his own new tomb; for it was fitting for Him to go forth from the tomb as from a bridal chamber, Who shattered the dominion of death and opened the gates of paradise unto men: glory to Thee!  
*Then: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart: Trisagion: Troparion: And the Dismissal.*  
## On Friday of the Third Week after Pascha
*At Matins, at "God is the Lord", look for the Troparion of the Feast. After the First Stichologia, Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*  
We worship Thy most pure image, O Good One, asking forgiveness for our transgressions, O Christ God; for of Thine own will Thou wast pleased to ascend the Cross in the flesh, that Thou mightest deliver those whom Thou didst fashion from the bondage of the enemy: therefore thankfully do we cry aloud unto Thee: Thou didst fill all with joy, O our Savior, Who camest to save the world!  
Not having forbidden the tomb-stone to be sealed, Thou didst arise and grant unto all the rock of faith: Lord, glory to Thee!  
*For the Martyrs:* When the Martyrs of the Lord contended in labors, then the boldness of the ungodly was darkened through their faith; and when they divinely abolished the delusion of idols...  

*On Friday of the Third Week after Pascha:*  
...then they received crowns of victory from on high, and they pray for our souls! *Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion:* Beholding Thee dead and stretched upon the Tree, O Christ, the Virgin and Thy Mother, weeping bitterly, said, O my Son: What is this fearful mystery! O Thou Who bestowest eternal life upon all, how willingly upon the Cross dost Thou suffer a reproachful death?  
*After the Second Stichologia, Sessional Hymn, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щя̀):*  
The women warmly bring myrrh unto the tomb, O Savior, the brightness of the Angel informing their souls; and they proclaimed Thee as God unto all, and cried unto the disciples: Truly from the tomb is risen the Life of all! *Glory... Both now... That same hymn. "Having seen the Resurrection of Christ": Psalm 50: Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 6, and from the Menaion on 4. And the Photogogikon of the Feast.*  
*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 2:*  
We worship the Tree of Thy Cross, O Christ God: Thou didst show us the tree of life, as we faithfully believe in Thee; and thereby having abolished the dominion of him that had the power of death, Thou didst make alive us who were deadened by sin: therefore we cry aloud unto Thee: O Benefactor of all, Lord, glory to Thee! *(Twice).*  
Every breath and all creation glorifies Thee, O Lord, for by the Cross Thou didst abolish death, that Thou mightest show unto the people Thy Resurrection from the dead, as the only Lover of mankind!  
*For the Martyrs:* Having suffered for Christ even unto death, O Passion-bearing Martyrs, your souls indeed are in heaven in the hand of God, and throughout all the world your relics are honored: priests and kings bow down, and all the people rejoicing customarily cry aloud: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His Venerable ones [LXX Ps. 115:6]!  
*Glory... Both now... Tone 4:*  
O Myrrh-bearing women, why came ye unto the tomb? Why seek ye the Living among the dead? The Lord is risen, take courage! cries the Angel.  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѡ҆ древа):*  
The unapproachable Light hath appeared unto us, Christ the Lord, beautiful from the tomb: hades is despoiled, Satan is abolished, the ends of the world rejoice, creation dances, the Church of Christ sings celebrating, keeping the memory of all-wise Joseph, and with him of the Myrrh-bearing women honors the solemn feast!  
*Verse: Lord, Thou hast been favorable unto Thy land [LXX Ps. 84:2]:*  
A feast hath appeared unto us of the divinely-honored Myrrh-bearing women, and of wise Joseph, as another spring giving forth life, pouring out waters of grace unto the world, shedding forth the warm streams of Christ's rising; the assemblies of the faithful celebrate and cry aloud: Glory to Him Who hath granted His Resurrection unto the ends of the earth!  
*Verse: Mercy and truth are met together; righteousness and peace have kissed each other [LXX Ps. 84:11]:*  
Bestowing unceasing and divine joy upon the divine and holy disciples, the women disciples appeared, Christ's Myrrh-bearers, having fulfilled the command of the divine Angel, at whom they were amazed, beholding him in white robes, hades despoiled by the Resurrection caused by the King Who died for our sake!  
*Glory... Both now... Tone 4: With fear the women came: (see fol. 144).*[^593]  
*Then: Troparion: And the Dismissal. And the First Hour.*  
*On that same Friday, at the Beatitudes, the Triodion [Triodes], bearing the acrostic: "Doxa Theo amin", that is: "Glory to God, amen". A work of Joseph:*[^594]  
*Tone 2. Ode 5. Heirmos: The enlightenment of those in darkness...*  

*On Friday of the Third Week after Pascha:*  
*Troparion [D]:* The assembly of lawless Jews of old, lifting Thee upon the Tree, put Thee to death, O Lover of mankind; but laid in the tomb, Thou didst shatter all the bonds of hades, and didst raise the dead from the ages!  
*Troparion [O]:* When Thou didst renew the immortality of all from corruption, then in courage didst Thou lead forth all the bound, and didst arise from the tomb, O Giver of life, having destroyed death, and having enlightened us by Thy rising!  
*Troparion [X]:* Seeing the Angel of old shining in strange apparel upon the tomb, the women were amazed, and he cried unto them: Fear ye not; Christ is risen, go ye, announce unto His friends!  
*Theotokion [A]:* Thou wast shown forth, O Virgin, as the Orient of the Sun of Righteousness: seeing Him arisen as He said and having raised the dead, and having enlightened the whole world, thou didst rejoice, singing praise, O pure one, as unto the Lover of mankind!  
*Ode 8. Heirmos: Unto the image of gold...*  
*Troparion [Th]:* Being God, O Good One, Thou didst deign to become man, and willingly endured crucifixion upon the Tree, for the salvation of those who ever cry in faith: Let all creation together sing unto the Lord, and pre-exalt Him unto the ages!  
*Troparion [E]:* In the lowest pit Joseph laid Thee, having wrapped Thee in winding-sheets and with myrrh and aloes; but Thou didst arise, O Savior, and of those who faithfully cry: Let all creation together sing unto the Lord, and pre-exalt Him unto the ages!  
*Troparion [O]:* Having arisen, O Almighty One, Thou didst appear unto the disciples, and didst say unto them: Go into the world, proclaim My dispensation, singing: Let all creation together sing unto the Lord, and pre-exalt Him unto the ages!  
*Theotokion [A]:* Cleanse our hearts, O Virgin Bride of God on high, who sawest the all-holy God the Word arisen, and in gladness crying: Let all creation together sing unto the Lord, and pre-exalt Him unto the ages!  
*Ode 9. Heirmos: Every tongue is at a loss...*  
*Troparion [M]:* Only was Thy Cross lifted up at the place of the skull, O Savior, and the dwelling of death was shaken from the depths, and those sitting in the shadow of death were immediately loosed indeed from unlooseable bonds, crying: Glory to the power of Thy might!  
*Troparion [I]:* The earth was shaken, and the stones split through fear, all the mountains and hills were moved, and the sun was darkened, seeing Thee hanging upon the Cross, O Christ: the bars of hades and the gates of iron were crushed, when by Thy rising from among the dead Thou didst raise the world together with Thyself! *Glory:*  
*Troparion [N]:* Seeing Thee dead of old upon the Cross, O Master, the noble Arimathean took Thee down from the Tree, and having wrapped Thee in linen cloths, with much fear laid Thee in a new tomb with haste: therefore as Thou didst foretell, Thou didst arise on the third day, O Word! *Both now: Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion:* Beyond the laws of nature, O all-immaculate Lady, ineffably didst thou bear the Word; and when thou sawest Him crucified by the counsel of the lawless, and laid in the tomb, and arisen from among the dead, thou wast filled with joy: Him pray thou for us who hymn thee!  
## On Friday Evening of the Third Week
*At "Lord, I have cried", we set Stichera: on 6 for the Feast, and 3 from the Menaion.*  
*Festive Idiomelon Stichera, Tone 2:*  
The Myrrh-bearing women, taking spices deep in the morning, reached the Lord's tomb, and finding what they did not expect, they pondered, reverencing the rolling away of the stone...  

*On Friday of the Third Week after Pascha:*  
...and one said unto another: Where are the seals of the tomb? Where is Pilate's guard, and the secure protection? But the shining Angel became an instructor unto the uncomprehending women, and said unto them: Why seek ye with weeping the Living, Who hath made the human race alive? Christ our God is risen from the dead as almighty, granting unto us all incorruption and life, enlightenment and great mercy!  
Why do ye pour out myrrh with tears, O women disciples? The stone is rolled away, the tomb is emptied: behold corruption trampled down by Life, the seals bearing witness clearly, the fiercely sleeping guards of the disobedient, the mortal race saved by the Flesh of God, hades lamenting! Run ye with joy, say unto the Apostles: Christ Who put death to death, the Firstborn from the dead, goes before you into Galilee!  
Having arisen early and with haste reached Thy tomb, the Myrrh-bearing women sought Thee, O Christ, to anoint Thy most pure Body; and having heard the words of the Angel, in joy-creating images they proclaimed unto the Apostles that Thou art risen, O Author of our salvation, having despoiled death, and granting unto the world eternal life and great mercy! *Glory... Tone 6:*  
The Myrrh-bearing women, having reached Thy tomb and seen the seals of the tomb, not finding Thy most pure Body, weeping came with haste, saying: Who stole our hope? Who took the dead naked one anointed with myrrh, the only consolation of His Mother? Oh, how He that makes the dead alive is mortified! How He that despoiled hades is buried! But arise, O Savior, by Thine own power, as Thou saidst, on the third day, saving our souls! *Both now: Theotokion, Tone 2:*  
The shadow of the law hath passed away: (see fol. 147).[^595]  
*Then: O Gladsome Light:*  
*At the Aposticha, Resurrectional Stichera, Tone 2:*  
Come, let us worship God the Word, begotten of the Father before the ages, incarnate of the Virgin Mary; for having endured crucifixion, He delivered Himself unto burial as He willed, and arose from the dead, saving me, the wandering man!  
*Verse: The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty [LXX Ps. 92:1]:*  
Christ our Savior, having torn asunder the handwriting against us by nailing it to the Cross, hath abolished the power of death: we worship His third-day Resurrection!  
*Verse: For He hath established the world, which shall not be moved [LXX Ps. 92:1]:*  
With the Archangels let us hymn the Resurrection of Christ; for He is the Redeemer and Savior of our souls, and in fearful glory and mighty power He comes again to judge the world which He created!  
*Verse: Holiness becomes Thine house, O Lord, unto length of days [LXX Ps. 92:5]:*  
Thy Resurrection, O Christ Savior, hath enlightened the whole universe, and summoned Thy creation: almighty Lord, glory to Thee!  
*Glory... Both now... Tone 5: Thou Who clothest Thyself with light: (see fol. 122).[^596]*  
*Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart: Trisagion: Troparion according to the Typikon (see fol. 134).[^597] And the Dismissal.*  
## On Saturday of the Third Week after Pascha
*At Matins, at "God is the Lord", Troparion of the Feast. After the First Stichologia, Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*  
Not having forbidden the tomb-stone to be sealed, Thou didst arise and grant unto all the rock of faith, incorruption and life: Lord, glory to Thee!  
Not having left the most pure bosom in the highest...

...burial and Resurrection didst Thou accept for all: Lord, glory to Thee! *Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
All beyond understanding, all most glorious are thy mysteries, O Theotokos: with purity sealed and virginity preserved, thou art known to be a true Mother, having borne the true God; Him pray thou to save our souls!  
*After the Second Stichologia, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "When the Myrrh-bearing women" (Мѵроно́сица жена́):*  
The choir of Thy disciples with the Myrrh-bearing women concordantly rejoices; for a common feast we celebrate with them, unto the glory and honor of Thy Resurrection; and for their sake we cry unto Thee, O Lord Lover of mankind: Grant unto Thy people great mercy! *Glory... Both now... The same.*  
*Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ: Psalm 50 [LXX Ps. 50]. Canon of the Feast, and from the Menaion.*  
*At the Praises, Resurrectional Stichera, on 4, Tone 2:*  
Every breath and all creation glorifies Thee, O Lord, for by the Cross Thou hast abolished death, that Thou mightest show unto the people Thy Resurrection from among the dead, as the only Lover of mankind.  
Let the Jews say: How did the soldiers lose the King Whom they guarded? Why then did the stone not preserve the Rock of life? Either let them produce Him that was buried, or let them worship Him that is risen, saying with us: Glory to the multitude of Thy compassions, O our Savior, glory to Thee!  
Rejoice, O ye peoples, and be glad! For the Angel sitting upon the stone of the tomb, he brought us good tidings, saying: Christ is risen from the dead, the Savior of the world, and hath filled all things with fragrance: rejoice, O ye peoples, and be glad!  
The Angel indeed brought the "Rejoice!" unto her who was full of grace before Thy conception, O Lord; and the Angel rolled away the stone of Thy glorious tomb at Thy Resurrection: the one indeed, in place of sorrow, announcing images of joy; the other, in place of death, proclaiming unto us the Master, the Giver of life. Therefore we cry unto Thee: O Benefactor of all, Lord, glory to Thee! *Glory... Both now... Tone 4:*  
Magdalene and the other Mary came unto the tomb seeking the Lord, and they saw the Angel like lightning sitting upon the stone, and saying unto them: Why seek ye the Living among the dead? He is risen as He said; in Galilee ye shall find Him: unto Whom let us cry: Arisen from the dead, Lord, glory to Thee!  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Automela (Самоподо́бны):*  
When from the Tree the Arimathean took Thee down dead, the Life of all, with myrrh and a winding-sheet he wrapped Thee, O Christ, and was moved with love in heart and lips to kiss Thy most pure Body; yet shrinking in fear, rejoicing he cried unto Thee: Glory to Thy condescension, O Lover of mankind! *Verse: Lord, Thou hast been favorable unto Thy land [LXX Ps. 84:1]:*  
When in a new tomb Thou wast laid for all, O Redeemer of all, all-derided hades trembled, seeing Thee; the bars were broken, the gates were shattered, the tombs were opened, and the dead arose: then Adam gratefully, rejoicing cried unto Thee: Glory to Thy condescension, O Lover of mankind!  
*Verse: Mercy and truth are met together [LXX Ps. 84:10]:*  
When the Powers beheld Thee, O Christ, slandered as a deceiver by the lawless, they were amazed at Thine ineffable longsuffering, and the stone of the tomb sealed by the very hands that pierced Thy ribs; yet rejoicing at our salvation they cried unto Thee: Glory to Thy condescension, O Lover of mankind! *Glory... Both now... Tone 6:*  
Joseph asked...  

...Joseph asked for the Body of Jesus, and laid It in his new tomb; for it was fitting for Him to go forth from the tomb as from a bridal chamber: O Thou Who hast shattered the dominion of death, and opened the gates of Paradise unto men, glory to Thee!  
*Then: It is good to give praise [LXX Ps. 91:2]: Trisagion: Dismissal Troparion: First Hour. And the Dismissal.*  
*On the Same Saturday: At the Beatitudes, a Four-Ode Canon (Tetraodion)*  
*Tone 2. Ode 6. Heirmos: From the depths of sins...*  
*Troparion:* The Myrrh-bearing women reached Thy tomb weeping, and the Angel announced joy unto them, crying aloud: Christ is risen, proclaim it unto the world!  
*Troparion:* At Thy betrayal the sun concealed its radiance; but Israel recognized not Thee, the God and Creator, Who willingly laid down Thy life for all.  
*Troparion:* Let all creation rejoice, for Christ is risen from the tomb, having despoiled all the treasure-houses of hades; and let it celebrate the common feast of gladness! *Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion:* Though thou hast borne an Infant, yet didst thou remain a Virgin, even as thou wast before childbearing, O pure Virgin Theotokos; for He Who was born renewed nature as God!  
*Ode 7. Heirmos: The furnace of old bubbling up...*  
*Troparion:* The senseless people of the Jewish assembly, in place of the good deeds which Thou didst bestow upon them, nailed Thee to the Cross, O Christ.  
*Troparion:* A fountain of mercy didst Thou place Thyself for all, that having loosed the cause of the debt of death, we might hymn Thee, O Lover of mankind.  
*Troparion:* By Thy three-day death and life-bearing burial, Thou hast granted eternal life unto those in the tombs, who faithfully hymn Thee, O Savior. *Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion:* Unto thee alone, the mediatress of joy, befitts the "Rejoice!" from the Angel, which we the faithful cry unto thee: O Virgin, all-immaculate Maiden!  
*Ode 8. Heirmos: The furnace of the children was dismayed...*  
*Troparion:* The godless decree was abolished, and the seals became an illusion unto the Jews: He that was buried arose, striking awe into all; for God willingly desires to suffer, but by His own will orders all things as He wills; therefore as Lord we hymn Him unceasingly, and pre-exalt Him unto the ages!  
*Troparion:* Go, he said unto the women, with countenance like lightning: Christ is risen; proclaim henceforth His Resurrection unto the world therein, and weep not, for it is not a time for tears; for death no longer reigns, hades no longer domineers, the sin of gluttony shall no longer conquer!  
*Troparion:* Hidden, O Master Christ, is our life in Thee, Who didst suffer in the flesh and arise from among the dead, O Word, and Who livest ever; in Whom we Christians hope to have part at Thy fearful Second Coming, faithfully and worthily hymning Thy divine passions and Thy Resurrection from among the dead. *Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion:* Our Fore-mother received through thee release from the ancient condemnation; for no longer in sorrow doth she bear children, because thou, O Birthgiver of God, hast borne Christ: therefore worthily do we bless thee, hymning Him that is truly born without seed, and pre-exalting Him unto the ages!  
*Ode 9. Heirmos: Thou hast magnified, O Christ...*  
*Troparion:* Hear the voice of thy Bridegroom, O Church, cries the divine David: for unto thee from His ribs was granted water...  

...water with divine blood, for the cleansing of transgressions; for He hath delivered thee from the deceit of idols, as the Lover of mankind.  
*Troparion:* Thou hast shone forth life by Thy creative rising; unto a multitude of the nations, O Christ, who inquired not neither sought Thee, hast Thou appeared in divine glory, but the Jewish people who believed not in Thee hast Thou reproved! *Glory:*  
*Troparion:* The curse that sprouted unto me through the deceit of the tree in Eden hast Thou loosed, O Word, going even unto the Cross according to the Father's counsel by Thine own sovereign will; therefore all creation hymns and magnifies Thine ineffable self-emptying! *Both now: Theotokion:*  
*Theotokion:* Thou hast drawn unto Thyself a holy people that were far off, O Savior, being incarnate of the Virgin, and those that were not Thy people hast Thou called Thy divine people: therefore all we now worship Thee, magnifying Thee!  
*At the Liturgy:*  
*Prokeimenon, Tone 1: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel [LXX Ps. 67:36]. Verse: In the congregations bless ye God [LXX Ps. 67:27]. Epistle from the Acts, pericope 22 [Acts 9:31-42]: In those days Peter went throughout all parts. And for the departed, Prokeimenon, Epistle, Gospel, and Communion Verse (see fol. 226).[^598] Alleluia, Tone 1: Truth hath sprung out of the earth [LXX Ps. 84:12]. Verse: The righteous cried [LXX Ps. 33:18]. Gospel from John, pericope 55 [John 15:17-16:2]: The Lord said unto His disciples: These things I command you. Communion Verse: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous [LXX Ps. 32:1]. Alleluia, thrice.*  

## Fourth Sunday after Pascha: Sunday of the Paralytic
*On Saturday Evening: At Small Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried", we set 4 verses, and sing Resurrectional Stichera, Tone 3: "By Thy Cross, O Christ Savior...", "All things are enlightened...", "We glorify the power of Father and Son...", "Unto Thy precious Cross...". Glory... Both now... Theotokion Dogmatic, Tone 3: (Tone 5: "Jesus went up to Jerusalem...": See all these Stichera below at Great Vespers).*  
*Theotokion Dogmatic, Tone 3:*  
A very great wonder! A Virgin giving birth, and the One born is God before the ages! A most glorious birthgiving, and accomplished beyond nature! O fearful mystery, which even when conceived remains ineffable, and when beheld cannot be comprehended! Blessed art thou, O all-pure Virgin, daughter of earthly Adam, and revealed as Mother of God the Most High: Him pray thou to save our souls!  
*At the Aposticha, Sticheron: "By Thy Passion...". Then of the Theotokos, Tone 3, Podoben: "Great is the power of Thy Cross" (Ве́лїа си́ла крⷵта̀ Твоегѡ̀): Verse: Hear, O daughter, and see [LXX Ps. 44:11]:*  
Having obtained through thee a great beneficence, O all-pure one, the choir of Angels glorifies thy childbearing; for He was pleased to be born in thy womb out of extreme compassion, delivering the human race. *Verse: I will remember thy name [LXX Ps. 44:18]:*  
Thee as refuge and divine protection do all we sinners in life possess, O Virgin...  

...in life possess, O Virgin: therefore we pray thy compassion, turn not away from us, O merciful one, but have mercy and save us! *Verse: The rich among the people shall entreat thy face [LXX Ps. 44:13]:*  
The "Rejoice!", O Theotokos Mother ever-Virgin, do we earthly servants worthily bring unto thee with Gabriel the divine Prince of orders, as mediatress of joy, who hast been gladness unto the world! *Glory... Both now... Tone 4: "In Solomon's Porch...": Seek at Great Vespers: At the Aposticha, Glory... And the rest as usual. Troparion: Tone 3. And the Dismissal.*  
*At Great Vespers, as usual, we sing: "Blessed is the man", the whole Kathisma [LXX Pss. 1–3]. On "Lord, I have cried", we set 10 verses, and sing Resurrectional Stichera, 3; and Anatolian, 4; and for the Paralytic, 3. If there be no All-Night Vigil, we say also the Sticheron of the Litiya.*  
*Resurrectional Stichera, Tone 3:*  
By Thy Cross, O Christ Savior, the power of death was broken, and the devil's deceit abolished; and the human race, saved by faith, ever brings a song unto Thee!  
All things were enlightened by Thy Resurrection, O Lord, and Paradise was opened again; and all creation, praising Thee, ever brings a song unto Thee!  
We glorify the power of the Father and the Son, and we hymn the Godhead of the Holy Spirit, the undivided, uncreated Godhead, the Consubstantial Trinity, reigning unto ages of ages!  
*Other Stichera, Anatolian [Tone 3]:*  
Unto Thy precious Cross do we bow down, O Christ, and Thy Resurrection do we hymn and glorify: for by Thy wound were we all healed!  
We hymn the Savior incarnate of the Virgin: for He was crucified on the Cross, and arose on the third day, granting unto us great mercy!  
Unto those in hades Christ descended and brought good tidings, saying: Be of good cheer, now have I conquered! I am the Resurrection, I will bring you up, having broken the gates of death!  
Standing unworthily in Thy most pure house, we send up an evening hymn, crying out of the depths, O Christ God: O Thou Who hast enlightened the world by Thy three-day Resurrection, deliver Thy people from the hand of Thine enemies, O Lover of mankind!  
*Then Idiomelon Stichera of the Paralytic, Tone 1:*  
Having fashioned man with Thy most pure palm, Thou didst come, O Merciful One, to heal the sick, O Christ: the paralytic at the Sheep Pool hast Thou raised up by Thy word. The disease of the woman with an issue of blood didst Thou heal. The daughter of the Canaanite woman who was vexed didst Thou pity, and the request of the Centurion Thou didst not despise; therefore do we cry: Almighty Lord, glory to Thee!  

The Paralytic, being an unburied corpse, seeing Thee cried aloud: Have mercy on me, O Lord, for my bed became my tomb! What gain is life unto me? I have no need of the Sheep Pool, for I have no man to cast me in when the water is troubled; but unto Thee, the Fountain of healings, do I come, that I also may cry with all: Almighty Lord, glory to Thee!  
*Glory... Tone 5:*  
Jesus went up to Jerusalem unto the Sheep Pool, which is called in Hebrew Bethesda, having five porches; for in them lay a multitude of the sick: for an Angel of God descended each year and troubled it, and granted health unto them that approached with faith. And the Lord, seeing a man of many years, saith unto him: Wilt thou be made whole? The sick man answered: Lord, I have no man, when the water is troubled, to put me into the pool; I have spent all my substance upon physicians, and was not vouchsafed to obtain mercy. But the Physician of souls and bodies saith unto him: Take up thy bed and walk, preach My power and great mercy unto the ends of the earth! *Both now: Theotokion, Tone 3:*  
How should we not marvel at thy God-manbearing childbearing: (see fol. 194).[^599]  
*Entrance. O Gladsome Light. Prokeimenon: The Lord is King [LXX Ps. 92:1], with its verses.*  
*At the Litiya, Stichera of the holy temple. Glory... Tone 5:*  
At the Sheep Pool a man lay in infirmity, and seeing Thee, O Lord, he cried: I have no man, when the water is troubled, to put me into it; and when I come, another steps down before me and receives healing, whilst I depart infirm! And straightway the Savior, having compassion, saith unto him: For thy sake became I man, for thy sake was I clothed in flesh, and sayest thou: "I have no man"? Take up thy bed and walk! All things are possible unto Thee, all things obey, all things submit: remember all of us and have mercy, O Holy One, as the Lover of mankind! *Both now: Theotokion, Tone 5:*  
Thou art temple and gate, palace and throne of the King, O all-pure Virgin, through whom my Redeemer Christ the Lord appeared unto those sitting in darkness, being the Sun of Righteousness, wishing to enlighten that which He fashioned after His own image with His hand; therefore, O all-hymned one, as having motherly boldness toward Him, unceasingly pray that our souls may be saved!  
*At the Aposticha, Resurrectional Sticheron, Tone 3:*  
O Christ, Who by Thy Passion didst darken the sun, and by the light of Thy Resurrection didst enlighten all things, accept our evening hymn, O Lover of mankind!  
*According to the order indicated (see fol. 230):[^600] We sing the Resurrectional Sticheron, then sing the Paschal Stichera with their verses, and Glory... "In Solomon's Porch...", "Resurrection Day...": thus the Greek Typikon indicats. Take note: In Rossia however, omitting these Stichera, after the first Resurrectional Sticheron the alphabetical ones are sung.*  
*Other Stichera, Alphabetical, Tone 3: Verse: The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty [LXX Ps. 92:1]:*  
Thy life-receiving rising, O Lord, hath enlightened the whole universe, and recalled on high Thine own creation that was corrupted: therefore, delivered from the curse of Adam, we cry: Almighty Lord, glory to Thee!  
*Verse: For He hath established the world, which shall not be moved [LXX Ps. 92:1]:*  
Being unchangeable God, in flesh suffering wast Thou changed; and creation, enduring not to see Thee hanging, trembled with fear, and groaning hymns Thy longsuffering; and having descended into hades, Thou didst arise on the third day, granting life unto the world and great mercy!  
*Verse: Holiness becomes Thine house, O Lord, unto length of days [LXX Ps. 92:5]:*  
That Thou mightest deliver our race from death, O Christ, Thou didst endure death; and having arisen from among the dead as Firstborn, Thou didst raise up together with Thyself those that recognized Thee as God, and hast enlightened the world: Lord, glory to Thee! *Glory... Tone 8:*  

In Solomon's Porch, there lay a multitude of the sick; and at Mid-feast, Christ found a paralytic lying thirty-eight years, and with gentle voice saith unto him: Wilt thou be made whole? The sick man answered: Lord, I have no man, when the water is troubled, to put me into the pool. But He said unto him: Take up thy bed and walk: behold, thou art made whole, sin no more!
