By the prayers of the Theotokos send down upon us great mercy! *Both now: Theotokion, Tone 8:*  
O Virgin unwedded, who didst conceive God in the flesh without seed, Mother of God the Most High: accept the supplications of thy servants, O all-immaculate one, granting unto all cleansing of transgressions; having now accepted our entreaties, pray that we all may be saved!  
*Then: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart [LXX Luke 2:29–32]: Trisagion: and after Our Father:*  
*Dismissal Troparion, Tone 3:*  
Let the heavenly things be glad, and let the earthly things rejoice, for the Lord hath wrought might with His arm: He hath trampled down death by death, He is become the Firstborn of the dead; from the belly of hades hath He delivered us, and granted unto the world great mercy! *Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
Thee, who didst mediate the salvation of our race, do we hymn, O Virgin Theotokos; for in the flesh assumed from thee, Thy Son and our God, having accepted the Passion through the Cross, hath delivered us from corruption, as the Lover of mankind!  
*The service of the saint of the day is sung at Small Compline.*  
*Take note: If the memory of the Holy Great-Martyr George or of the Holy Apostle John the Theologian fall upon this Fourth Sunday after Pascha, let it be sung according to the Typikon indicated above on the Sunday of the Holy Myrrh-bearing Women (see fol. 231).[^601]*  
*If however there be no Vigil, then the Trinity Canon sung at Midnight Office, which bears the acrostic: "ᾌδω Τριάς, σὺν τιμῇ μίαν θεαρχίαν" (transliterated into Cyrillic: А҆́ИДО ТРІ́АС СОҮТІ́Н МІ́АН ѲЕА́РХІ́АН), which is: "I sing, O Trinity, of Thy one Godhead": by Mitrophanes (see in the Octoechos), Tone 3.[^602]*  
*On the Fourth Sunday after Pascha: At Matins*  
*At "God is the Lord", Resurrectional Troparion, twice. Glory... Both now... Theotokion.*  
*After the First Stichologia, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*  
Christ is risen from among the dead, the firstfruits of them that slept, the Firstborn of creation, and Maker of all that are made: the corrupted nature of our race hath He renewed in Himself: no longer dost thou domineer, O death; for the Master of all hath broken thy power!  
Having tasted death in the flesh, O Lord, Thou didst cut off the bitterness of death by Thy rising, and having strengthened man against it, recalling the overcoming of the first curse: O Defender of our life, Lord, glory to Thee! *Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
At the beauty of thy virginity and the exceeding brilliance of thy purity was Gabriel amazed, crying unto thee, O Theotokos: What worthy praise can I bring unto thee? What name shall I call thee? I am at a loss and in dread! Therefore as I was commanded, I cry unto thee: Rejoice, O thou full of grace!  
*After the Second Stichologia, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "At the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства твое́гѡ):*  
Having assumed all that is human, all our lot didst Thou deign to have nailed unto the Cross, O my Creator, accepting to endure death as man, that Thou mightest free humanity from death as God; therefore as Giver of life we cry unto Thee: Glory, O Christ, to Thy compassion!  
The incomprehensible mystery of the Crucifixion and the ineffable wonder of the Resurrection do we faithful mystically theologize; for today death and hades are despoiled, and the human race is clothed in incorruption: therefore giving thanks we cry unto Thee: Glory, O Christ, to Thy Resurrection! *Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*  
The heavenly things rejoiced with love, and the earthly things trembled with dread...  

...when the most pure voice came upon thee, O Theotokos; for one was the celebration unto both, when He delivered the First-formed from death: therefore with the Angel do we cry unto thee: Rejoice, O pure Virgin Mother!  
*After the Amomos, Hypakoe, Tone 3:*  
Astonishing by his appearance, shedding dew with his words, the radiant Angel said unto the Myrrh-bearing women: Why seek ye the Living in the tomb? He is arisen, emptying the graves: understand the Changeless One to have abolished corruption: say ye unto God: How fearful are Thy works [LXX Ps. 65:3], for Thou hast saved the human race!  
*Hymns of Ascent, Tone 3. Antiphon 1:*  
Thou didst take captive the captivity of Zion from Babylon; and draw me also from passions unto life, O Word!  
They that sow in the south with divine tears shall reap the ears of grain in everlasting life-bearing joy!  
Unto the Holy Spirit belongs all beneficence: even as unto the Father and the Son He shines forth together, in Whom all things live and move!  
*Antiphon 2:*  
Unless the Lord build the house of virtues, in vain do we labor; and if He protect the soul, no enemy shall destroy our city!  
By the Spirit made sons unto Thee, O Christ, as unto the Father, the saints are the fruit of the womb ever!  
By the Holy Spirit is foreseen all holiness and wisdom; for He sanctifies every creature: unto Him let us burn incense, for He is God, even as unto the Father and the Word!  
*Antiphon 3:*  
Blessed are they that fear the Lord, that walk in the paths of His commandments; for they shall eat the life-bearing fruit of all virtues!  
Be glad around thy table, beholding thy sons, O chief Shepherd, bearing children of good deeds!  
Unto the Holy Spirit belongs all wealth of glory, from Whom grace and life come unto every creature; for He is hymned together with the Father and with the Word!  
*Then the Prokeimenon, Psalm 95 [LXX Ps. 95:10], Tone 3: Say among the nations that the Lord is King, for He hath established the world, which shall not be moved. Verse: Sing unto the Lord a new song, sing unto the Lord, all the earth [LXX Ps. 95:1]. Let every breath. Gospel from Luke, pericope 115 [Luke 24:12-35]. Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ. Then: Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ, thrice. After Psalm 50, the rest as usual.*  
*Then the Canon of Pascha with its Theotokia, on 8, written on the Sunday of the Holy Myrrh-bearing Women (see fol. 232);[^603] and of the Paralytic, on 6. And at the end, the Heirmos of the present Canon, again as in the Greek editions, and in certain Slavonic ones. Katavasia: "Resurrection Day": both choirs together.*  
*Canon, Tone 3. Ode 1. Heirmos: Marvelous art Thou, Who workest glorious wonders, Thou art God, Who didst dry up the deep, and drown the armed force, and save the people who sing unto Thee as our Redeemer God, for He is glorified!*  
*Troparion:* Performing signs, working wonders, O only King, Thou didst willingly endure crucifixion as compassionate, and by death hast Thou made us alive!  
*Troparion:* In Thy rising, O people, let us faithfully celebrate today: hades was despoiled, and with haste gave up the prisoners whom it held, who hymn the majesty of God!  
*Troparion:* By divine power, O Word of God Christ, having of old strengthened the Paralytic and commanded him to take up his bed, heal my soul, grievously sick for a long time!  
*Troparion:* Into the Sheep Pool an Angel used of old to descend, and healed one each year; but Christ now cleanses unnumbered multitudes by divine Baptism!  
*Unto the Archangel Michael:*  
*Troparion:* O Prince of the Angels, Archistrategos of the supreme ministers: cover and preserve from all temptations those who have gathered in thy precious temple, hymning God!  
*Triadikon:*  
*Troparion:* In three Persons, yet one essence, do we unceasingly hymn the unoriginate God with the bodiless hosts, glorifying the Father and the Spirit, Who possess an undivided kingdom and dominion!  
*Theotokion:*  
*Troparion:* Deliver thy city always, O all-pure one, which faithfully confesses thee, from barbarian captivity, civil war, the sword, and all other threats!  
*Katavasia: Resurrection Day. Ode 3. Heirmos: My barren and childless soul, acquire fruitful praise, and rejoicing cry aloud: My heart is established, there is none holy, and there is none righteous beside Thee, O Lord!*  

*Troparion:* Beholding Thee of old hanging upon the Tree, O Word, the sun hid its light, and all the earth shook, and the dead arose when Thou becamest dead, O Almighty One!  
*Troparion:* When Thou camest with Thy soul into the bowels of the earth, hades with haste gave up the souls it held, who cried a joyous song unto Thy power: O only Lord!  
*Troparion:* My soul, grievously languishing for many years, do Thou heal, O All-good One, even as of old Thou didst heal the Paralytic, that I may follow Thy paths which Thou hast shown unto them that love Thee!  
*Unto the Archangel Michael:*  
*Troparion:* With the supreme ranks, O Archangel of God, make entreaty for those who faithfully hymn thee, preserving and guarding us who are assailed by worldly passions! *Glory:*  
*Triadikon:*  
*Troparion:* Cry: Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit; for God is one essence, Whom all the heavenly Powers glorify with fear, crying: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou! *Both now:*  
*Theotokion:*  
*Troparion:* Thou hadst seedless conception, and childbearing beyond understanding, O Virgin pure Mother: a fearful deed, a very great wonder, revered by Angels and glorified by men, O Maiden, full of grace!  
*Katavasia: Come, let us drink a new drink. Kontakion of Pascha: Though Thou didst descend into the grave; and the Ikos. In the Greek Typikon these are not appointed here, but later.[^604]*  
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*  
Thy voice alone strengthened the Paralytic, as the cosmic Word Who appeared on earth for our sake alone out of compassion: therefore carrying his bed he walked by, even though the Scribes, seized with the envy of malice, endured not to behold what was done, paralyzing their souls! *Glory... Both now... Theotokion: "At the beauty of thy virginity" (see fol. 162).[^605]*  
*Ode 4. Heirmos: Habakkuk foresaw the overshadowing mountain, even thy most pure womb, O pure one: therefore he cried: God shall come from Theman, and the Holy One from the mountain of the overshadowing forest!*  
*Troparion:* The Jewish assembly, inflamed with envy, crucified Thee upon the Tree, O Lord; and having abolished the sentence of death, as the Mighty One Thou didst arise, raising the world together with Thyself!  
*Troparion:* O women with myrrh, why seek ye the unemptied Myrrh? He is risen, said the one clothed in white garments unto the Myrrh-bearing women, after Whom the world was filled with spiritual fragrance!  
*Troparion:* Bearing the form of a servant, out of great compassion Thou didst come walking, and didst make whole him that lay in infirmity for many years, commanding him also to carry his bed!  
*Troparion:* The Angel of the Lord indeed descended every year into the pool and troubled it, making whole one man only; but Christ by divine Baptism saves an unnumbered multitude!  
*Unto the Archangel Michael:*  
*Troparion:* O Prince of the Angels and guide of the wandering, chief Captain of the Lord, come now among us, and present the prayers of all unto the one Creator and Lady!  
*Triadikon:*  
*Troparion:* United in essence and divided in Persons is the Holy Trinity: the Father unoriginate, the Son co-eternal, and the Holy and one almighty Spirit!  
*Theotokion:*  
*Troparion:* How thou sucklest is concealed: how didst thou bear an Infant older than Adam? How dost thou carry upon thine arms the Son Who sits upon the shoulders of the Cherubim? Even as He knows, Who hath joined Himself unto all!  
*Ode 5. Heirmos: With Thy light that knows no evening, O Christ, enlighten my humble soul, and guide me in Thy fear; for Thy commandments are light!*  
*Troparion:* Lifted up upon the Tree of the Cross, and having raised up the whole world, and being among the dead, O God, Thou didst raise up the dead of the ages!  

*Troparion:* Christ is risen as He said, and hath despoiled all the kingdoms of hades, and appeared unto the Apostles, granting unto them eternal joy!  
*Troparion:* Radiant in white appearance was the Angel seen by the women: Weep no longer, your Life is risen, Who hath made alive the dead in the tombs!  
*Troparion:* As Thou didst raise up the Paralytic, O Christ, heal my soul paralyzed by transgressions, and guide me to walk in Thy straight paths!  
*Unto the Archangel Michael:*  
*Troparion:* O Prince of the Angels Michael, save the people gathered today in thy temple, who proclaim the mighty works of God! *Glory:*  
*Triadikon:*  
*Troparion:* Equally revered Tri-hypostatic Unity, faithfully divided indeed in hypostases, yet united in essence: Father, Son, and divine Spirit! *Both now:*  
*Theotokion:*  
*Troparion:* Thy seedless childbearing, O pure one, Thine incomprehensible birthgiving do we hymn, blessing thee as Mother of the Creator of all and our Lady!  
*Katavasia: Rising early at dawn. Ode 6. Heirmos: The deep of passions hath risen against me, and the tempest of contrary winds: but anticipate me Thou, O Savior, and save me, even as Thou didst save the Prophet from the beast!*  
*Troparion:* Willingly lifted up upon the Tree, Thou wast laid as dead in the tomb, and together didst make alive the dead in hades, O Christ, arisen by divine power!  
*Troparion:* Hades, meeting Thee below, O Compassionate One, was embittered, swiftly giving up the bound who hymn Thy fearful Resurrection, O Savior, in unceasing voices!  
*Troparion:* The divine disciples, beholding Christ the Life of all arisen from the tomb, with great love and upright spirit, and spiritual gladness, bowed down before Him!  
*Troparion:* He that of old lay in sickness upon his bed for many years, being healed by Thy command, O Christ, glorified and hymned Thy compassion, O Giver of life!  
*Unto the Archangel Michael:*  
*Troparion:* O Prince of the Angels Michael, standing before the Master's throne, come into our midst, guiding unto the paths of life us who possess thee as a fervent intercessor!  
*Triadikon:*  
*Troparion:* In Persons do I revere Thee as Trinity, and in essence do I proclaim Thee as Unity: unoriginate Father, Son, and upright Spirit, God over all, with the supreme and fearful hosts!  
*Theotokion:*  
*Troparion:* He Who upholds all things by His divine nod is held in thy virgin embrace, O Theotokos, delivering us all on high from the hand of the slavery of the evil one, as Compassionate!  
*Katavasia: Thou didst descend into the lowest parts of the earth.*  
*Kontakion, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дѣ́ва дне́сь):*  
My soul, O Lord, grievously paralyzed by all manner of sins and unseemly deeds, do Thou raise up by Thy divine loving-kindness, even as Thou didst raise up the Paralytic of old, that being saved, O Compassionate One, I may cry unto Thee: Glory, O Christ, to Thy dominion! *(Refrain: O Christ, grant me healing).*  
*Ikos:*  
O Jesus God, Who holdest the ends of the earth in the hollow of Thy hand, Who art co-unoriginate with the Father, and with the Holy Spirit rulest over all: in the flesh didst Thou appear, healing sicknesses, and Thou didst drive away passions; Thou didst enlighten the blind man, and the Paralytic didst Thou raise up by Thy divine word, commanding him suddenly to walk, and to take upon his shoulders the bed that bore him; therefore all we with him hymn and cry out, O Compassionate One: Glory, O Christ, to Thy dominion!  
*Synaxarion on the Fourth Sunday after Pascha: On the Paralytic.*  
*Bless, father, to read.*  
*Verses:*  
*The voice of Christ which today strengthened the Paralytic,*  
*Itself ever becomes healing unto all.*  
*On this same day, on the Fourth Sunday after Pascha, we celebrate the memory of the Paralytic, and as is meet we celebrate this miracle of our Lord and God and Savior Jesus Christ: for this also was appointed here because it was performed at the time of the Hebrew Pentecost; for He was in Jerusalem for the Feast, and at the five-porched pool...*  

...which Solomon built, which also was called the Sheep Pool; for in the washing-place they washed the entrails of the sheep slaughtered for sacrifice, or because the first one entering therein when the water was troubled once a year by an Angel appeared whole. There He finds a man thirty-eight years in infirmity, and being at a loss who might cast him into the water as he lay; from whom also we are instructed how good a thing is waiting and patience, and that because Baptism was to be given for the cleansing of every sin, God provided in the Old Covenant that good should be wrought through miracles, so that when the former should come, it might be easily received. Unto this Paralytic therefore Jesus comes, in some such manner also for my sake, and questions him; and he sets forth his lack of anyone to assist him; and Christ, knowing how to cleanse and heal him of his disease, saith: "Take up thy bed and walk." And straightway he appears whole, and taking his bed upon his shoulders, that the deed might not seem an apparition, he carried it to his house. But it being the Sabbath, he was forbidden by the Jews to walk; yet he set forth Him that healed him, as having said to walk on the Sabbath, not knowing Who He was; for Jesus, it is said, had turned aside and hidden Himself, a multitude being gathered there. Afterward Jesus found him in the temple, and saith unto him: "Behold, thou art made whole; sin no more, lest a worse thing come unto thee." Now some have said that Christ said this—speaking unjustly—because that man was later to give Jesus a blow when He stood before the high priest Caiaphas, and thereby to inherit a worse trial than paralysis in eternal fire, not to be tormented for thirty-eight years only, but everlastingly. But rather did Christ show that the disease of paralysis had befallen him because of sins. Not every disease however is from sins, but also from natural infirmity, and from overeating, and senseless carelessness, and many other causes it comes. Having understood therefore that it was Jesus Who had made that man whole, the holy Paralytic made it known unto the Jews. And they, being moved to wrath, sought to slay Christ because He broke the Sabbath; but He said many things unto them, proving that it is lawful to do good even on the Sabbath, and that He Himself is the One Who commanded to keep the Sabbath, that He is equal to the Father, and even as the Father still works, so also doth He. Be it known also that this Paralytic is different from the one in Matthew; for the latter took place in a house, with men serving him, and hearing "Thy sins are forgiven thee"; but this one took place in the Porch, having no man, even as the holy Gospel saith, and taking up his bed, even as the other. Now it is celebrated at this time because this also occurred during Pentecost, even as the events of the Samaritan Woman and the Blind Man; for Thomas and the Myrrh-bearers we celebrate in confirmation of Christ's Resurrection from among the dead; and the rest until the Ascension because these things took place at the time of the Hebrew Pentecost, which occurred among the Jews at various times, and as John thus briefly recorded them. Through Thine ineffable compassion, O Christ our God, have mercy on us: Now and ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.  
*Ode 7. Heirmos: He Who bedewed the flame in the furnace and saved the children unburnt: Blessed art Thou unto the ages, O Lord God of our fathers!*  
*Troparion:* Beholding Thee stretched upon the Tree, the sun hid its light, unable to shine upon the world when Thou, O King of all, didst willingly set for the enlightenment of all the nations!  
*Troparion:* Thou didst empty the tombs and take hades captive by Thine all-powerful might; therefore do we hymn Thy precious and divine Resurrection, O Christ!  
*Troparion:* Why seek ye the Living as dead? He is arisen, He is not in the tomb! cried of old the divine Angel, shining with radiance unto the Myrrh-bearing women.  
*Troparion:* Healing by a word the Paralytic lying in infirmity for many years, Thou didst cry: Take up thy bed and walk, singing the majesty of God!  
*Unto the Archangel Michael:*  
*Troparion:* O Michael, chief Captain of the Lord, guide and cover from all evils by thine intercession those who faithfully gather in thy divine temple unto the praise of God!  
*Triadikon:*  
*Troparion:* O Father, save from all tribulations by Thy protection those who hymn Thee as God and Master of all, and make them partakers of Thy good things! *Both now:*  
*Theotokion:*  
*Troparion:* Having willed to bear beyond the Word Him Who was begotten before all ages...

...Him Who was begotten ineffably of the unoriginate Father: therefore, O pure one, do we call thee blessed. *Katavasia: He Who bedewed the flame.*  
*Ode 8. Heirmos: Unto God Who is unceasingly glorified in the highest by Angels, let the heavens of heavens, the earth, the mountains, the hills, and the abyss, and all the human race: bless Him with songs as Creator and Deliverer, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!*  
*Troparion:* When Thou, our Savior, wast crucified, the veil was rent, and death gave up the dead whom it had swallowed, and hades was laid bare, seeing Thee in the nether regions of the earth!  
*Troparion:* Where is thy sting, O death? Where is thy victory, O hades? When the King arose, thou wast put to death and destroyed, and no longer rulest; for the Mighty One hath released those whom thou hadst bound!  
*Troparion:* Make haste, announce the Resurrection to the Apostles! cried the young man who appeared in radiant apparel unto the Myrrh-bearing women: The Master is risen, and with Him the dead from of old, most marvelously!  
*Troparion:* The Paralytic lying bedridden for many years cried out: From thence,[^606] have mercy on me, O Deliverer, held fast by helplessness! But He commands him to take up his bed with diligence, and to walk uprightly!  
*Unto the Archangel Michael:*  
*Troparion:* As chief leader of the incorporeal hosts, O commander of the angelic ranks, entreat with them for our deliverance from transgressions, the correction of our life, and the enjoyment of the eternal blessings in the world to come!  
*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord:*  
*Triadikon:*  
*Troparion:* Let us with one accord hymn the uncreated and indivisible Essence, the three-hypostatic Godhead, God the Father unoriginate, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, with the Seraphim crying out the fearful hymn!  
*Both now:*  
*Theotokion:*  
*Troparion:* Of old Isaiah beheld thee, O ever-virgin Maiden, as a scroll wherein the unoriginate Word was inscribed by the Father's finger; save from all irrationality us who hymn thee with sacred words!  
*We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord:*  
*Katavasia. Heirmos: "This chosen and holy day." We do not sing "More honorable".*  
*Ode 9. Heirmos: On Mount Sinai Moses saw thee in the bush, having received unburnt the fire of the Godhead in thy womb; and Daniel saw thee as an uncut mountain; and Isaiah proclaimed thee a budding rod from the root of David.*  
*Troparion:* When Thou, O Jesus, wast lifted up upon the Tree, Thou didst raise us up with Thyself; and having been laid in a tomb, Thou didst resurrect all the dead who were in the tombs, who hymn Thine incomprehensible dominion and Thine invincible might!  
*Troparion:* Radiant didst Thou shine forth from the tomb like a Bridegroom from a bridal chamber, O all-comely Word, and Thou didst destroy the dark kingdom of hades and lead forth the captives, who with one accord cry out: Glory to Thy glory, glory, O Jesus our God, to Thy Resurrection!  
*Troparion:* Bearing sighs and tears with ointments, the women reached the tomb in grief, and were taught of Christ's glorious Resurrection by faith, which we celebrate, rejoicing in spiritual gladness!  
*Troparion:* At Thy command, O Christ, Thou didst grant health of body, and he who had formerly been paralyzed was seen running swiftly and carrying the bed upon which he had lain for many years, singing praises to Thy manifold power!  
*Unto the Archangel Michael:*  
*Troparion:* Entreat enlightenment for us, O great Archistrategos, ever standing before the great Light, and calm our life when assaulted at all times by the serpent, that we may worthily be marked with a radiant manner of life!  
*Glory:*  
*Triadikon:*  
*Troparion:* Light and Lights, Life and Life, I glorify Thee in all piety, O Father, Word, and Holy Spirit: Three-hypostatic Unity, indivisible Dominion, unconfused Godhead, crying with the hosts on high: Holy, Holy, Holy!  

*Both now:*  
*Theotokion:*  
*Troparion:* From thy luminous womb the great Sun, Christ, came forth, O pure one, and enlightened the world with radiant beams, delivering it from the darkness of transgression; therefore do we hymn thee as the cause of all good things, O Bride of God!  
*Katavasia. Heirmos: "Shine, shine, O new Jerusalem."*  
*Then: "Holy is the Lord our God."*  
*Exapostilarion: "Having fallen asleep in the flesh" (see fol. 172)[^607].*  
*Another Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ ꙋ҆слы́ши):*  
The Shepherd Who loves mankind and all-compassionate Lord came unto the Sheep Pool to heal infirmities; and finding a man who had lain there for many years, He cried unto him: Take up thy bed, and walk in upright ways! *(Twice)*  
*Glory, Both now: The same.*  
*At the Praises, Stichera of the Resurrection, on 4, Tone 3:*  
Come, all ye nations, understand the power of the awesome mystery: for Christ our Savior, the Word Who was in the beginning, was crucified for our sake, and willingly buried, and arose from the dead, that He might save all things: let us worship Him!  
Thy guards told of all the marvelous deeds, O Lord; but the council of vanity, having filled their right hand with a bribe, thought to hide Thy Resurrection, which the world glorifies: have mercy on us!  
All things were filled with joy, having received the proof of the Resurrection: for Mary Magdalene came to the tomb and found an Angel sitting upon the stone, shining in his raiment and saying: Why seek ye the Living among the dead? He is not here, but is risen, as He said, going before you into Galilee!  
In Thy light, O Master, shall we see light, O Lover of mankind; for Thou art risen from the dead, granting salvation unto the human race, that all creation may hymn Thee, the only sinless One: have mercy on us!  
*Other Stichera by Anatolios:*  
The Myrrh-bearing women brought a morning song unto Thee with tears, O Lord; for having scents of sweet fragrance, they reached Thy tomb, striving to anoint Thy most pure Body; but the Angel sitting upon the stone announced good tidings unto them: Why seek ye the Living among the dead? For having trampled down death, He is risen as God, granting great mercy unto all!  
Shining upon Thy life-bearing tomb, the Angel said unto the Myrrh-bearing women: The Deliverer Who emptied the tombs hath overthrown hades, and is risen on the third day, as the only God and All-powerful!  
Mary Magdalene, having come on the first day of the week, sought Thee in the tomb; but not finding Thee, she wept and cried out with lamentation: Woe is me, my Savior! Thou hast been stolen away, O King of all! But a pair of life-bearing Angels cried out from within the tomb: Why weepest thou, O woman? I weep, she said, because they have taken my Lord from the tomb, and I know not where they have laid Him. But turning back, when she saw Thee, straightway she cried out: My Lord and my God, glory to Thee!  
The Jews shut up Life in the tomb, but the Thief opened delight with his tongue, crying out and saying: O Thou Who wast crucified for my sake, Who didst hang with me upon the Tree, and didst appear unto me sitting upon the Throne with the Father: for He is Christ our God, Who hath great mercy!  

*Glory, Tone 8:*  
O Lord, Thou didst not heal the Paralytic in the pool, but Thy word renewed him; nor did his disease of many years hinder him, for the swift working of Thy voice was shown forth, and he cast away his burdensome weight and carried the load of his bed as evidence of the multitude of Thy compassions: glory to Thee!  
*Both now:*  
*Exceedingly blessed art thou: Great Doxology. And the Dismissal.*  
*Gospel Sticheron, Tone 4:*  
Early in the deep dawn, the women came unto Thy tomb, O Christ, but the Body beloved by them was not found. Therefore, while they were perplexed, men in shining garments stood before them, saying: Why seek ye the Living among the dead? He is risen, as He said before; why remember ye not His words? Having believed them, they proclaimed what they had seen; but the good tidings seemed as an idle tale: so slumberous were the disciples yet. But Peter ran, and having seen, glorified in himself Thy marvelous deeds!  
*First Hour. Patristic Instruction. And final Dismissal.*  
*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Oktoechos, Tone 3, on 4; and from the Canon of the Paralytic, Ode 6, on 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 1: Let Thy mercy, O Lord, be upon us, according as we have hoped in Thee. Verse: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous. Epistle in Acts, section 23 [Acts 9:32–42]. Alleluia, Tone 5. Verse: I will sing of Thy mercies, O Lord, forever. Verse: For Thou hast said: Mercy shall be built up forever. Gospel from John, section 14 [John 5:1–15]. Communion Hymn: Receive the Body of Christ. Another: Praise the Lord from the heavens.*  
*Notice: It should be known that the Order of the Paralytic is sung on Sunday, and on this Tuesday it is given back.*  
*On the same Sunday Evening, at "Lord, I have cried":*  
*Stichera of the Feast, 3; and from the Menaion, 3.*  
*Stichera of the Feast, Tone 4, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ пресла́вномꙋ чꙋдесѝ):*  
O most marvelous wonder! The Creator of the ages became a bearer of flesh, and our nature was impoverished, He so willing in His compassion; and having dwelt with mankind, He enlightened the Hebrews with manifold miracles: therefore did He of old heal the Paralytic, saying unto him: Take up thy bed, and walking, go unto thy house!  
O Savior, my God and Lord, willing to resurrect fallen mankind, Thou didst walk upon earth as man, healing every disease because of Thy great tenderheartedness. Therefore, having come unto the Sheep Pool, Thou didst raise up the Paralytic whole of old by Thy word, who had lain there for thirty and eight years!  
The envious Hebrews, consumed with envy at Thy beneficence, O all-good Lord, sought a pretext, and raging with irritation, being themselves lawless in the Law continually, sought to slay Thee, the true Life, on account of the Sabbath: because Thou hadst shown forth a whole man in health, who was formerly paralyzed!  
*Glory, Both now, Tone 5:*  
At the Sheep Pool a man lay in disease; and seeing Thee, O Lord, he cried: I have no man, that when the water is troubled, he might cast me into it; and when I come, another steps down before me and receives healing, while I lie in disease! And straightway the Savior, having compassion, saith unto him: For thy sake became I man, for thy sake did I clothe Myself in flesh, and sayest thou: "I have no man"? Take up thy bed and walk! All things are possible unto Thee, all things give ear, all things submit: remember all of us, and have mercy, O holy One, as Lover of mankind!  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 3:*  
By Thy Cross, O Christ Savior, the dominion of death was destroyed, and the devil's deceit was abolished; and the human race, being saved through faith, ever brings hymnody unto Thee!  
*Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes [Ps. 122:1].*  
An evening hymn do we bring unto Thee, O Christ, with incense and spiritual songs: Have mercy, O Savior, on our souls!  
*Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us [Ps. 122:3].*  
Great is the power of Thy Cross, O Lord, for it was planted in this place and works in the world, and hath shown forth Apostles from fishermen, and Martyrs from the nations, that they may intercede for our souls!  

*Glory, Both now, Tone 8:*  
O Thou Who didst fashion man with Thy most pure hand, Thou didst come, O compassionate Christ, to heal the diseased: Thou didst heal the Paralytic at the Sheep Pool by Thy word; Thou didst cure the illness of the woman with an issue of blood; Thou didst have mercy on the demon-possessed daughter of the Canaanite woman; and Thou didst not despise the centurion's petition: therefore do we cry: O Almighty Lord, glory to Thee! *Then: Resurrection Troparion, and Dismissal.*  
## On Monday of the Fourth Week after Pascha
*At Matins, at "God is the Lord", Resurrection Troparion, once; Glory, Both now, Theotokion.*  
*After the First Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*  
Christ is risen from the dead, the first-fruits of those who have fallen asleep: the Firstborn of creation, and Maker of all that hath come into being; He hath renewed in Himself the corrupted nature of our race: no longer dost thou rule, O death; for the Master of all hath destroyed thy dominion!  
Repent, O my soul, while living upon the earth: for dust in the tomb sings not, nor is it delivered from transgressions. Cry out unto Christ God: O Knower of hearts, I have sinned: before Thou judgest me, have mercy on me!  
*Martyrikon:* The grace of your patience conquered the wiles of the author of evil, O blessed Passion-bearers: therefore were ye counted worthy of eternal blessedness; but pray unto the Lord, O Martyrs, true witnesses, to save the flock of the people named after Christ! *Glory, Both now: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (see fol. 162)[^608].*  
*After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ́ дѣ́вьства):*  
Those paralyzed by envy found an opportunity for relaxation, meditating upon the healing of the Paralytic on the Sabbath, saying: It is not lawful to heal on the Sabbath, nor to break the Sabbath rest of the fathers; not knowing Thee as Master of the Law and Physician of our souls. *Glory, Both now: The same.*  
*Then: "Having seen the Resurrection of Christ", once. Psalm 50. Canons of the Paralytic, on 8; and from the Menaion, on 4. Kontakion and Sessional Hymn of the Feast.*  
*At the Praises, Stichera, on 4, Tone 3:*  
Come, all ye nations, understand the power of the awesome mystery: for Christ our Savior, the Word Who was in the beginning, was crucified for our sake, and willingly buried, and arose from the dead, that He might save all things: let us worship Him! *(Twice)*  
Performing hymnody many times, I was found finishing in sin; proclaiming hymns with my tongue, yet meditating on shameful things with my soul: but correct both, O Christ God, through repentance, and have mercy on me!  
*Martyrikon:* Let us honor the memory of the holy Passion-bearers, O ye companions; for having become a spectacle unto Angels and men, they received the crown of victory from Christ, and intercede for our souls!  
*Glory, Both now, Tone 6:*  
Being an unburied dead man, the Paralytic, seeing Thee, cried out: Have mercy on me, O Lord, for my bed hath become a grave unto me! What profit is my life to me? I have no need of the Sheep Pool; for there is no one to cast me in when the waters are troubled: but unto Thee, the Fountain of healings, do I come, that I also may cry with all: O Almighty Lord, glory to Thee!  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 3, Podoben: "Great is the power of Thy Cross, O Lord" (Ве́лїа Крⷵта):*  
Seeing the power of signs and miracles, those held fast by envy and strong in malice believed not that Thou art the Son of God and the Creator of the ages, Who didst show forth the Paralytic made whole by a word!  
*Verse: Thy mercies, O Lord, will I sing forever [Ps. 88:1].*  
The keepers of the Sabbath, seeing the Paralytic strengthened on the Sabbath, were darkened in soul, and cried out swiftly as lawbreakers: It is not lawful to heal on the Sabbath, and to defile the Sabbath!  
*Verse: For Thou hast said: Mercy shall be built up forever [Ps. 88:2].*  
Not knowing Thee, the Creator and Lord of the Sabbath...  

...those who keep the Sabbath considered the strengthening of the Paralytic, which took place on the Sabbath, saying that it was not good to take up a bed on the Sabbath day!  
*Glory, Both now, Tone 6: Jesus went up to Jerusalem (see fol. 167)[^609].*  
*Then: "It is good to give thanks unto the Lord." First Hour. And the Dismissal.*  
*At the Beatitudes, the Triodion, whose acrostic is: ΑΝΕΣΤΗ Ο ΣΩΤΗΡ [ANESTI O SOTIR], that is: "The Savior is risen"[^610].*  
*Tone 3. Ode 1. Heirmos: Let us divide the sea:*  
*Troparion:* Having ascended on high, Thou, O Savior, didst take captive the captivity held in hades, as the Mighty One; and having risen on the third day from the tomb, O Lover of mankind, Thou didst raise us up with Thyself as Almighty!  
*Troparion:* Why seek ye the Living among the dead? said the radiant Angel of old to the Myrrh-bearing women at the tomb, appearing unto them: Go quickly, make haste to announce His Resurrection to the Apostles!  
*Troparion:* Seeing Thee of old slain, O Savior, and laid in a new tomb, hades remained all dead and inactive; while all the dead, made alive by Thy Resurrection, rejoiced, being delivered from their bonds!  
*Theotokion:*  
*Troparion:* Having ineffably conceived the incarnate Lord from thy most pure blood, O Mother of God, Who for our sake received death in the flesh, and having seen Him risen from the tomb, thou didst joyfully glorify and magnify Him!  
*Ode 8. Heirmos: The Babylonian furnace:*  
*Troparion:* Seeing the Lord crucified of old, the sun hid its light, and the rocks were split, the mountains trembled, and the earth shook; and the captives of old were loosed from their bonds!  
*Troparion:* When hades saw Thee descending into the lower parts of the earth, O Word, it trembled and released all the dead, who hymn Thy dominion and Thy tenderheartedness!  
*Troparion:* Let us shout with mystical voices and clap our hands faithfully, and having formed choirs, proclaiming His power, let us cry out: Bless the Lord, all ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!  
*Theotokion:*  
*Troparion:* Thou didst conceive Him Who contains all things, O all-immaculate one, and didst bear Him in the flesh Who took death captive by His three-day death: entreat Him to put to death the passions of my soul and save me!  
*Ode 9. Heirmos: Moses prefigured in the burning bush:*  
*Troparion:* O most comely Christ, how did the brightness of Thy beauty set upon the Cross? How did the earth endure seeing Thee hanging? How didst Thou endure unjust slaughter? We hymn Thy manifold and ineffable condescension, O Word!  
*Troparion:* By Thy three-day burial the enemy was taken captive, the bonds of hades were loosed for the dead, death was slain, and the kingdoms of hades were emptied: therefore do we honor and magnify Thee in hymns, O Giver of life!  
*Glory:*  
*Troparion:* O eternal Life, how didst Thou willingly taste of death for our sake, O Savior? How didst Thou descend to the dead, being their Resurrection, and empty the treasuries of hades? Therefore do we devoutly honor and magnify Thee in hymns!  
*Both now:*  
*Theotokion:*  
*Troparion:* Deliver this city and the people who flee unto thee, O pure Mother of God who didst bear Christ, from all opposing wrath, and captivity, and the invasion of nations, that we may worthily magnify thee in hymns!  
*On the same Monday Evening, at "Lord, I have cried":*  
*Stichera of the Feast, 3; and from the Menaion, 3. Stichera of the Feast: Glory: On the Paralytic (see fol. 167)[^611], Tone 1: Fashioning man with Thy most pure hand...*  

...Being an unburied dead man, the Paralytic (see fol. 170)[^612].  
*Glory, Both now, Tone 5: Jesus went up to Jerusalem (see fol. 167)[^613]. Then: "O gladsome Light." Prokeimenon of the day.*  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 3:*  
All things were enlightened by Thy Resurrection, O Lord, and Paradise was opened again; and all creation, praising Thee, ever brings hymnody unto Thee! *Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes.*  
Save me, O Lord my God, for Thou art the salvation of all: the tempest of passions troubles me, the burden of transgressions drowns me; stretch forth a hand of help to me, and lead me up unto the light of compunction, as the only tenderhearted One and Lover of mankind! *Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us.*  
*Martyrikon:* Great is the power of Thy Martyrs, O Christ; for they lie in the tomb and cast out spirits, and have abolished the power of the enemy through their faith in the Trinity, having contended for piety! *Glory:*  
*Both now, Tone 8: In Solomon's Porch (see fol. 164)[^614].*  
*Then: "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart." Trisagion prayers. Troparion (see fol. 164)[^615].*  
## On Tuesday of the Fourth Week after Pascha
*At Matins, at "God is the Lord", Resurrection Troparion, Tone 2; Glory, Both now, Theotokion.*  
*After the First Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*  
Having tasted death in the flesh, O Lord, Thou didst cut off the bitterness of death by Thy rising, and having strengthened man against it, recalling the victory over the ancient curse, O Defender of our life: Lord, glory to Thee!  
How long, O my soul, dost thou remain in sins? How long dost thou delay repentance? Call to mind the judgment to come, cry out unto Christ God: I have sinned, O sinless One, have mercy on me!  
*Martyrikon Sessional Hymn:*  
Ye shine with faith as exceedingly bright luminaries, O holy ones, lovers of piety, all-laudable Passion-bearers: for not fearing the wounds of the tormentors, ye overthrew all impiety, having the invincible victory of the true Cross! *Glory, Both now, Theotokion:*  
O Mother of God, refuge and strength, mighty help of the world: save thy servants from every necessity by thine intercessions, O only blessed one!  
*After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ́ дѣ́вс):*  
Those paralyzed by envy found an opportunity for relaxation, meditating upon the healing of the Paralytic on the Sabbath, saying: It is not lawful to heal on the Sabbath, nor to break the Sabbath rest of the fathers; not knowing Thee as Master of the Law and Physician of our souls. *Glory, Both now: The same. "Having seen the Resurrection of Christ." Psalm 50.*  
*Canons of the Feast: Of the Paralytic, on 8; and from the Menaion, on 4. Kontakion and Sessional Hymn of the Feast.*  
*At the Praises, Stichera, on 4, Tone 3:*  
Thy guards told of all the marvelous deeds, O Lord; but the council of vanity, having filled their right hand with a bribe, thought to hide Thy Resurrection, which the world glorifies: have mercy on us!  
Gather my scattered mind, O Lord, and cleanse my cooled heart, giving me repentance as unto Peter, sighs as unto the Publican, and tears as unto the Harlot, that with a great voice I may cry unto Thee: O God, save me, as the only tenderhearted One and Lover of mankind!  
Having cast away the fear of kings and tormentors, the soldiers of Christ boldly and manfully confessed Him, the Ruler, Lord God, and our King; and they intercede unceasingly for our souls!  
*Glory, Both now, Tone 8:*  

O Lord, Thou didst not heal the Paralytic in the pool (see fol. 168)[^616].  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 3, Podoben: "Great is the power of Thy Cross, O Lord" (Ве́лїа Крⷵта):*  
Thy Word appeared unto paralyzed limbs as a life-bearing bond, O my Life-creating Word, in witness whereof, beyond hope, was the taking up of the bed, which he carried at Thy command, having lain upon it for a long time!  
*Verse: Thy mercies, O Lord, will I sing forever.*  
Fulfilling the command, the Paralytic rejoiced in the all-powerful might of the Master, and walking, carried his bed, crying out in witness: He Who healed me commanded me to carry this! *Verse: For Thou hast said: Mercy shall be built up forever.*  
Lying in the burden of many years, the Paralytic cried out: Have mercy on me, O Christ, Deliverer of limbs bound by helplessness! And the Savior, having strengthened the man's limbs, commanded him to take up his bed!  
*Glory, Both now, Tone 5: At the Sheep Pool a man lay (see fol. 167)[^617].*  
*Then: "It is good to give thanks." Resurrection Troparion, and its Theotokion. And Dismissal.*  
*At the Beatitudes, the Triodion, whose acrostic is: (ΨАЛМΩΔΙΑ ΙΩΣΗΦ )[PSALMODIA IOSIF], that is: "Chanting of Joseph"[^618].*  
*Tone 3. Ode 2. Heirmos: As the shower upon the herb:*  
*Troparion:* Let us shout psalms, let us clap our hands joyfully: the Giver of life is risen from the dead!  
*Troparion:* Thou didst ascend the Cross, O Christ, girded with power, and Thou art risen from the dead, as Thou didst foretell!  
*Troparion:* An Angel clothed in white was seen by the women, crying: He is risen, Who is the Resurrection and Salvation of the faithful!  
*Troparion:* Striving to anoint Him, the women went unto the tomb; and seeing Him risen, they worshipped Him!  
*Theotokion:*  
*Troparion:* As a morning sun, O Virgin, holding Him in thy hands, thou didst dawn upon us who were in darkness!  
*Ode 8. Heirmos: Unto God Who is unceasingly glorified by Angels:*  
*Troparion:* The lawbreaking Hebrew council crucified Thee upon the Tree, as Thou didst divinely heal the harm that befell mankind through the tree; therefore with joyful voices do we ever hymn Thee!  
*Troparion:* Equal to the Father and the Spirit, O Christ the Son, Thou didst endure the Cross, and being numbered among the dead, Thou didst empty the kingdoms of hades most marvelously, and rising, didst resurrect all creation with Thyself!  
*Troparion:* See, O hades, where is thy sting? Where is thy victory, O death? By Christ the Savior, the only Giver of life, risen from the dead, hast thou been put to death: Whom with the Father and the Spirit we, the faithful, hymn!  
*Theotokion:*  
*Troparion:* That God might deify man, He became man from thee, O all-immaculate and pure one; and having seen Him coming forth most comely from the tomb as Conqueror of hades, thou wast filled with joy!  
*Ode 9. Heirmos: On Mount Sinai:*  
*Troparion:* As a lamb to the slaughter wast Thou led willingly, O Master and Lord, neither crying out nor calling, as it is written; having by Thy death, O Word, given life unto the dead who hymn Thy most pure Passion, and an awesome resurrection from hades!  
*Troparion:* Thou hast renewed us who were defiled, O Jesus, King of all, by Thy precious and awesome Resurrection, and didst grant Thy peace unto the disciples, O Lover of mankind: grant this unto us also, who hymn Thy divine rising!  
*Glory:*  
*Troparion:* The precious company of the Myrrh-bearing women came swiftly unto Thy tomb, O Master, faithfully to anoint Thee; and they saw an Angel sitting in white robes, and he said unto them:  

...saying unto them: Why seek ye to see the Living among the dead?  
*Both now:*  
*Theotokion:*  
*Troparion:* Thou didst bear the Giver of light and the Lord, being the gate of the Light, and seeing Him shining from the tomb, thou didst worship with joy: but entreat Him, O Virgin, to grant joy unto those who weep, O pure intercessor, for consolation in that world!  
## On Wednesday of the Fourth Week after Pascha: Mid-Pentecost
*(see fol. 175)[^619].*  
*For the saint of the day, we sing at Compline; only for Great Saints with an entrance.*  
*On Tuesday Evening, after "Christ is risen", the usual Kathisma.*  
*At "Lord, I have cried", we set 6 verses, and sing Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "The Lord having ascended the Cross" (Гдⷭ҇ь въше́дъ на Крⷵтъ):*  
Today the Mid-feast of days, beginning from the Savior's Resurrection, and sealed by the divine Pentecost, having the brightness of both, uniting both, and imparting the glory of the Master's Ascension to come, is pre-eminently honored!  
Zion heard and was glad when the Resurrection of Christ was announced, and its faithful sons rejoiced seeing this, and washing away the defilement of the murder of Christ by the Spirit, they prepare to celebrate the joyful Mid-feast of both! *(Twice)*  
The swift pouring forth of the divine Spirit upon all hath drawn near, as it was written of old: the pre-enactment proclaims it; for having reached the Mid-feast after Christ's Death, Burial, and Resurrection, it shows forth the Comforter's coming, given by Him unto His disciples in unfailing promise! *(3 times)*  
*Glory, Both now, Tone 6:*  
As the Feast of Thy Resurrection and the divine coming of Thy Holy Spirit reaches its midpoint, O Christ, we who are gathered together hymn the mysteries of Thy wonders: therefore send down upon us Thy great mercy!  
*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. Three Readings.*  
*The Reading from the Prophecy of Micah [Micah 4:2–3, 6:1–5, 8; 5:4][^620]:*  
Thus saith the Lord: Out of Zion shall go forth the Law, and the Word of the Lord from Jerusalem; and He shall judge between many peoples, and shall rebuke strong nations afar off. For all peoples shall walk each in his own way, but we will walk in the Name of the Lord our God forever. Thus saith the Lord Almighty: Hear, O ye mountains, the judgment of the Lord, and ye foundations of the earth; for the Lord hath a controversy with His people, and will plead with Israel: O My people, what have I done unto thee? Or wherein have I grieved thee? Or wherein have I burdened thee? Answer Me! For I brought thee up out of the land of Egypt, and redeemed thee out of the house of bondage, and sent before thy face Moses, Aaron, and Miriam. O My people, remember now what Balak king of Moab devised against thee! If it hath been told thee, O man, what is good, or what the Lord requirs of thee, but to do justice, to love mercy, and to be ready to walk with the Lord thy God? For the Lord shall be magnified in strength, and shall shepherd His flock in humility, even unto the ends of the earth!  
*The Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah [Isaiah 55:1–3, 6–13][^621]:*  
Thus saith the Lord: Ye that thirst, go to the water; and as many as have no money, go, buy, and eat and drink without money and without price, wine and fatness. Thus, thus saith the Lord Almighty unto you: O My people, draw water with joy out of the fountains of salvation!  

And in that day thou shalt say: Sing unto the Lord, call upon His Name, declare His glorious deeds among the nations; remember that His Name is exalted, O My people! Give ear unto Me, and ye shall eat good things, and your soul shall delight itself in good things. Incline your ears, and follow My ways: give ear unto Me, and your soul shall live in good things; and I will make an everlasting covenant with you; and call upon Him while He is near you. Let the ungodly forsake his ways, and the unrighteous man his counsels, and let him return unto the Lord, and he shall find mercy: for He will abundantly pardon your sins. For My counsels are not as your counsels, neither are your ways My ways, saith the Lord. But as the heaven is distant from the earth, so is My way distant from your ways, and your thoughts from My thoughts. For as rain or snow comes down from heaven, and returns not thither, until it waters the earth, and causes it to bring forth and bud, and gives seed to the sower, and bread for food: so shall My word be, whatsoever goes forth out of My mouth; it shall not return unto Me void, until it accomplishes all whatsoever I willed, and I will prosper thy ways and My commandments. For ye shall go forth with gladness, and be taught with joy. For the mountains and hills shall break forth before you with joy, and all the trees of the field shall clap their branches. And instead of the brier shall come up the cypress, and instead of dust shall come up the myrtle; and the Lord shall be for a Name, and for an everlasting sign, and shall not fail, saith the Lord, the Holy One of Israel.  
*The Reading from Proverbs [Proverbs 9:1–11][^622]:*  
Wisdom hath built a house for herself, and supported it with seven pillars. She hath slaughtered her beasts, she hath mingled her wine in her bowl, and prepared her table. She hath sent forth her servants, calling with a high proclamation to the bowl, saying: Whosoever is simple, let him turn aside unto me! And to those who want understanding she saith: Come, eat of my bread, and drink of the wine which I have mingled for you! Forsake foolishness, that ye may reign forever; and seek wisdom, and correct understanding in knowledge. He that reprovs evil men shall get to himself dishonor, and he that rebuks an ungodly man incurs a reproach. Rebuke not evil men, lest they hate thee; rebuke a wise man, and he will love thee. Give an opportunity to a wise man, and he will be wiser; instruct a righteous man, and he will receive more instruction. The fear of the Lord is the beginning of wisdom, and the counsel of the holy is understanding; for to understand the Law is the mark of a good mind. For in this manner thou shalt live a long time, and years of life shall be added unto thee.  
*Then let us all say: The Ektenia. "Vouchsafe, O Lord." And we complete the Evening Prayers.*  
*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon Stichera, Tone 1:*  
The Mid-feast of days of Pentecost hath arrived, wherein Christ, revealing in a small measure His divine power, strengthened the Paralytic by a word, raising him from his bed, and working wonders in a God-befitting manner in the created Body, granted unto such men eternal life and great mercy!  
*Verse: Remember Thy congregation which Thou hast purchased [Ps. 73:2].*  
Thou didst come into the Temple, O Wisdom of God, as the Feast reached its midpoint...  

...as the Feast reached its midpoint, teaching and rebuking the unbelieving Jews, the Pharisees and Scribes, and crying out with boldness unto them: Let him that thirsts come unto Me and drink living water, and he shall never thirst forever: he that believs in My goodness, rivers of living water shall flow from his belly unto eternal life! O Thy goodness and Thy tenderheartedness, O Christ our God, glory to Thee!  
*Verse: God is our King before the ages [Ps. 73:12].*  
When the Mid-feast of the Feast was come, Jesus went up into the Temple and taught the disobedient Jews, saying: Let him that thirsts come unto Me and drink living water, and he shall never thirst forever: he that believs in Me, rivers shall flow from his belly, and he shall have the light of life!  
*Glory, Both now, Tone 8:*  
When the Feast reached its midpoint, while Thou, O Savior, wast teaching, the Jews said: How knows this Man letters, having never learned? not understanding that Thou art the Wisdom that established the world: Glory to Thee!  
*Then: "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart." Troparion, Tone 4:*  
When the Feast hath reached its midpoint, quench the thirst of my soul with the waters of piety: for unto all, O Savior, didst Thou cry: Let him that thirsts come unto Me and drink! O Fountain of life, Christ God, glory to Thee!  
*Priest: Wisdom. And the Dismissal according to custom.*  
*At Compline, we sing the stichera and canon with the ordinary order (see fol. 175)[^623].*  
*Rubric of the Typikon from the manuscript of the Hieromonk, chapters 44 and 52, and of the printed Typikon:[^624]*  
*If the Paschal Key is 7, on April 23, the feast of the Holy Great-Martyr George falls on this Wednesday of Mid-Pentecost. But if the Key is 9, then on May 8, the Memory of the Holy Apostle John the Theologian.[^625] This instruction servs for both; likewise on their Apodosis:*  
*If the Superior wills to celebrate a Vigil:*  
*On Tuesday Evening, at Little Vespers: Stichera of the Feast, and [Both now:] To the Saint, unto 4; or repeat if 3 stichera. Glory: to the Saint. Both now: of the Feast.*  
*At the Aposticha: to the Saint: we sing the stichera of Great Vespers with his verses. Glory: to the Saint. Both now: of the Feast. After the Trisagion prayers, Troparion of the Saint. Glory, Both now: of the Feast. Little Litany, and Dismissal.*  
*At Great Vespers: We chant the Psalter: "Blessed is the man", the first Stasis. At "Lord, I have cried", Stichera of the Feast, 6; and to the Saint, 4. If there is no Vigil, then Stichera of the Feast, 3; and to the Saint, 3. Glory: to the Saint. Both now: of the Feast. Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. All the readings of the Feast, and of the Saint. At the Litiya, the Sticheron of the Temple, and Idiomelon Stichera to the Saint. We also sing here the Aposticha of the Saint from Little Vespers, 3 verses, if they were not sung at Little Vespers. Glory: to the Saint. Both now: of the Feast. At the Aposticha: of the Feast with its verses. Glory: to the Saint. Both now: of the Feast.*  
*At the Blessing of the Loaves: Troparion of the Feast, twice; and to the Saint, once. Reading of the Saint: if the Apostle, then from the Apocalypse. When there is no Vigil, after "Our Father", Troparion to the Saint. Glory, Both now: of the Feast, and Dismissal.*  
*At Matins: At "God is the Lord", Troparion of the Feast, twice; Glory: to the Saint; Both now: of the Feast.*  
*After the First Kathisma, Sessional Hymn and reading of the Feast. If the Apostle, then after each Kathisma and apostolic reading, Polyeleos and all the Sessional Hymns of the Saint, likewise after the First and Second Stichologia. Glory: Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos; Both now: his Theotokion; or the glorious Sessional Hymn of the Feast, and reading of the Saint. If the Apostle, then his Life.*  
*The First Antiphon of the Graduals, Tone 4. Prokeimenon: "In all the earth"; and the Gospel of the Saint.*  
*"Having seen the Resurrection of Christ", 3 times. Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Saint. Canon of the Feast, Tone 4, with the Heirmos, on 6; and to the Saint, on 4. Second Canon of the Feast, Tone 8, on 4; and to the Saint, on 4. The other Canon, if there is one, is sung at the Molieben. Katavasia: Heirmos of the Feast, and of that Tone, both choirs together. After Ode 3: Kontakion and Ikos of the Saint, and his Sessional Hymns, 2; Glory, Both now: of the Feast, and reading of the Saint. After Ode 6: Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast, and reading in the Prologue. At Ode 9: We do not sing "More honorable". Exapostilarion of the Feast; Glory: to the Saint; Both now: of the Feast. At the Praises, Stichera of the Feast, 3; and to the Saint, 3. Glory: to the Saint; Both now: of the Feast. Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, Troparion of the Saint; Glory, Both now: of the Feast. Litanies. Catechesis of St. Theodore the Studite; and Dismissal. The usual procession; and if thou wilt, in the Porch. At the Hours, Troparion of the Feast; Glory: to the Saint; Both now: Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father", we alternate the Kontakia.*  
*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Feast, Ode 3, on 4. After the Entrance, Troparion of the Feast, and to the Saint. Glory: Kontakion of the Saint; Both now: Kontakion of the Feast. Prokeimenon and Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Hymn: first of the Feast, then of the Saint.*  
*On Wednesday of Mid-Pentecost: At Midnight Office.*  
*After "I believe in one God": After the first Trisagion, we say the Troparion of the Feast; after the second Trisagion, Kontakion of the Feast. We do not say the prayers for the dead.*  
*At Matins: After the Six Psalms, at "God is the Lord", Troparion of the Feast, 3 times.*  
*After the First Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предвари́):*

Knowing in these thoughts the ponderings of hearts, standing in the midst of the sanctuary He cried aloud, exposing the false and speaking truth: "Why seek ye to kill Me, the Giver of life, as the Feast is halved?" crying boldly: "Judge not looking upon the face, ye transgressors of the Law!" Glory... and now... the same.

*And a Reading in the Commentary on the Gospel according to John.*

*After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn. Tone 5. Podoben: "The Co-unoriginate Word" (Събезнача́лное Сло́во):*
Standing in the Temple as the venerable Feast of Pentecost reached its midpoint, the Master of all, conversing with the Hebrews, openly rebuked their tyrannical audacity with great boldness, as King, Son, and God; and unto us all in His tender mercy He grants great mercy.

Glory... both now and ever... the same. "Having seen the Resurrection of Christ..." Psalm 50 [LXX 50]. Two Canons of the Feast, set for 14. The heirmoi of both canons twice, and the troparia twice. Afterward the Katavasia: the heirmos of the second canon, both choirs together.

*Canon by Kyr Theophanes. Whose acrostic or initial lettering is: MESINEORTON TONMEGISTON AINESO, that is: "I will praise the midpoint of the Great Feasts."*[^626]

*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Israel of old, traversing the deep of the Red Sea with dryshod feet, overcame the power of Amalek in the wilderness by the cross-shaped hands of Moses.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast revealed unto us, O Master, the great and transcendent benefactions of Thine entire divine Incarnation, pouring forth graciously the gifts and heavenly brightness of grace.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst come, O Christ, manifestly pouring forth the pearls of the Godhead in the midst of the Feast; for Thou didst become a joyous Feast unto the saved, and the Mediator of our salvation.

*Troparion:*
Thou becamest unto us Wisdom, Righteousness, Lord, and Redemption from God, leading us from earth unto heavenly height, and granting the Divine Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
Thy flesh saw no corruption in the grave, O Master; but as Thou wast conceived without seed, having not known corruption according to nature, Thou didst not naturally submit to decay.

*Second Canon, the composition of Kyr Andrew of Crete.*[^627]

*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos: "Thou hast solidified the sea..." (Мо́ре ѡ҆гꙋсти́лъ є҆сѝ):*

*Troparion:*
Clap your hands, ye nations, weep, ye Hebrews! For Christ the Giver of life has broken the bonds of hades, raised the dead, and driven away sickness: This is our God, Who grants life unto them that believe in His Name!

*Troparion:*
Thou hast shown forth a wonder, turning water into wine; Thou Who of old in Egypt didst turn the rivers into blood, O Master, and Who didst raise the dead: Thou hast accomplished this second sign.

*Troparion:*
Glory, O Savior, to Thine ineffable counsel! Glory to Thine emptying, whereby Thou hast renewed us!

*Troparion:*
Being the ceaseless stream of true life, O Lord, Thou art our resurrection! Willingly didst Thou labor, O my Savior, and willingly didst Thou thirst, submitting to the laws of nature; and coming in the flesh unto Sychar, Thou didst ask water of the Samaritan woman to drink.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst bless the loaves, Thou didst multiply the fishes, O incomprehensible God, and didst satisfy the multitudes bountifully; and Thou didst promise the ever-flowing spring of Wisdom unto them that thirst: Thou art the Savior our God, Who gives life unto them that believe in Thy Name.

*Triadikon:*
I glorify the Three unoriginate and co-enthroned: the Father, unoriginate God, and the Co-unoriginate Son, and the Co-eternal Spirit do I venerate in truth: worshipping one thrice-radiant Essence, one Principle supremely unoriginate, I honor the unoriginate Godhead and Essence.

*Theotokion:*
Alone didst thou contain thy Creator in thy womb, O Mother of God, and in the flesh didst bear Him ineffably, and didst remain a Virgin, in no way having thy virginity stained: Him, O pure one, ceaselessly beseech as thy Son for thy flock and God.

*Katavasia, Heirmos:*
Thou hast solidified the sea, drowning cruel Pharaoh with his chariots, and didst save the people with dryshod feet, O Lord, and led them unto the mountain of sanctuary as they cried: Let us sing unto Thee our God a hymn of victory, for He has been glorified unto ages!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, my refuge and firm support!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst open the fountains of life-creating waters unto the Church, crying: "If anyone thirst, let him come eagerly and drink, O Good One!"

*Troparion:*
Foreannouncing that Thou wouldst ascend from earth unto heaven, Thou didst promise to send the Holy Spirit, descending from heaven.

*Troparion:*
The Lord, quiet in His life-giving, Who was born of the Virgin, grants immortality unto all the faithful, as He is tender-hearted.

*Another Heirmos: "My heart is established in the Lord..." (Ꙋ҆тверди́ся срⷵце моѐ въ Гдѣ́):*

*Troparion:*
"Judge not according to appearance, ye Judeans," the Master taught as He arrived in the Sanctuary, as it is written, at the midpoint of the Feast of the Law.

*Troparion:*
Be not deceived, O lawless Judeans! For Christ has come, Whom the Prophets foretold, coming forth from Zion and calling back the world!

*Troparion:*
Even if ye believe not the words, O Judeans, believe the works of the Savior! Why are ye deceived, rejecting the Holy One, of Whom Moses wrote in the Law?

*Troparion:*
If it was necessary in every way for the Messiah to come, O Judeans, and Christ the Messiah is now come, why are ye deceived, rejecting the Righteous One, of Whom Moses wrote in the Law?

*Triadikon:*
We worship Thee, O unoriginate Father in essence; we sing also of Thy Co-unoriginate Son and devoutly of the All-Holy Spirit, as three by nature, yet one God.

*Theotokion:*
Being One of the Trinity, having become flesh, Thou wast seen without altering Thy Lordship, nor burning the uncorrupted womb of her that bore Thee, being wholly God and fire.

*Katavasia, Heirmos: "My heart is established in the Lord, my horn is exalted in my God; my mouth is enlarged over mine enemies; I have rejoiced in Thy salvation."*

*Sessional Hymn. Tone 8. Podoben: "Of the Wisdom and Word upon Thy breast" (Премꙋ́дрости сло́ва на пе́рси):*[^628]
Standing in the midst of the sanctuary as the Feast was divinely halved, Thou didst cry aloud: "Let him that thirsts come unto Me and drink; for he that drinks of these My divine waters shall pour forth rivers of My commandments from his belly! And he that believes in Me, Who was sent from the Divine Father, shall be glorified with Me!" Therefore we cry unto Thee: Glory to Thee, O Christ God, for Thou hast richly poured forth the waters of Thy love for mankind upon Thy servants!

Glory... both now and ever... *Theotokion. Podoben: The same:*
Pouring forth the water of Wisdom and Life unto the world, O Savior, Thou callest all to draw the waters of salvation; for man, having received Thy divine law, quenches within himself the coals of delusion. And so unto ages he shall never lack Thy satisfaction, O King Most High! Therefore we glorify Thy dominion, O Christ God, asking that remission of transgressions be sent down upon Thy servants.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Christ the Sun of Righteousness, the Church stood in her station, worthily crying: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Having shattered the gates of death by Thy power, Thou didst make known the ways of life, and didst open the doors of immortality unto them that cry with faith: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Possessing the midpoint and the end of the ages, and holding the beginning as Unoriginate, Thou didst stand in the midst of the temple: "O divinely-wise, enjoy the divine gifts!"

*Troparion:*
As God and possessing authority over all, as Mighty One Thou hast cast down the power of death; and Thou didst promise, O Christ, to send the Holy Spirit Who proceeds from the Father.

*Theotokion:*
Thou grantest grace richly unto them that hymn thee, and having borne without corruption the Pre-eternal Word, O unwedded Mother, do thou, O most pure one, entreat remission of sins for them.

*Another Heirmos: "The Prophet Habakkuk..." (Пррⷵкъ А҆вва́кꙋмъ):*

*Troparion:*
If the Messiah must come, and Christ is the Messiah, O lawless ones, why believe ye not in Him? Behold, He has come and testifies by the things which He does: He made water into wine, and strengthened the paralytic by a word!

*Troparion:*
Not understanding the Scriptures, ye are all deceived, O lawless Hebrews! For in truth Christ has come and enlightened all; He has shown forth many signs and wonders, yet in vain do ye reject the true Life!

*Troparion:*
"One work have I shown you, and already ye all marvel," Christ cried unto the Judeans: "Ye circumcise a man on the Sabbath," He said: "Why then slander ye Me, Who by a word raised up the paralytic?"

*Troparion:*
"Many works have I done, and for which work do ye stone Me?" Christ cried softly unto the Judeans: "Is it because I made a man whole and sound by a word? Judge not according to appearance, ye men!"

*Troparion:*
Working in the Apostles, and Thyself honored in the Prophets through the Spirit, O Christ, the unoriginate Offspring of the Father's essence, Thou hast called the nations unto knowledge by many signs.

*Triadikon:*
O indivisible Trinity, Unity! Unoriginate Father, and Son, and Holy Spirit, common Trinity in Unity, equal in honor, co-enthroned, life-creating, uncreated God: save them that worship Thee, and deliver us from perils and afflictions!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
Having contained in thy womb the uncontainable God, O Virgin Bride of God, pure Mother: cease not praying for us, that through thee we may obtain deliverance from the distresses that encompass us, who ever flee unto thee!

*Katavasia, Heirmos:*
The Prophet Habakkuk foresaw with spiritual eyes Thy coming, O Lord, therefore he cried: God shall come from Theman! Glory to Thy glory, O Christ, glory to Thy condescension!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou, O my Lord, art come as a light into the world, a holy light turning from gloomy ignorance them that praise Thee with faith.

*Troparion:*
Having reached the midpoint of the divine Feasts, let us hasten, O divinely-wise, to attain the perfection of divine virtue!

*Troparion:*
Truly sacred is the present celebration of Mid-Pentecost: for it is the root of the great Feasts, and shines on both sides!

*Theotokion:*
Whose ineffable and all-pure birth from the Virgin the archangelic host cannot comprehend, O Savior greatly merciful!

*Another Heirmos: "O Lord our God, give us peace..." (Гдⷭ҇ь Бг҃ъ нашъ, ми́):*

*Troparion:*
Thou hast adorned Thine Apostles with miracles, Thou hast shown Thy disciples to be marvelous, glorifying them throughout the whole world, O our Savior, and giving unto them Thy Kingdom.

*Troparion:*
The disciples enlightened all the ends of the earth with wonders and teachings, preaching in diverse manners the Word of Thy Kingdom, O Christ our Savior.

*Troparion:*
We send up praise unto Thy Kingdom, and offer singing unto Thee, Who didst appear on earth for our sake, enlightening the world and calling back Adam.

*Triadikon:*
Glory to Thee, O Holy Father, unbegotten God! Glory to Thee, O Word timeless and Only-begotten! Glory to Thee, O Divine Spirit, co-enthroned and consubstantial with the Father and the Son!

*Theotokion:*
Thy womb became a holy Table, holding the Heavenly Bread, Christ, of Whom everyone that eats dies not, as He said, O Mother of God, Sustainer of all!

*Katavasia, Heirmos:*
O Lord our God, give us peace! O Lord our God, possess us, O Lord, for beside Thee we know no other: we call upon Thy Name!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood which for mercy's sake flowed from Thy side.

*Troparion:*
The midpoint of Pentecost has arrived today, having shone forth from divine Pascha and being illumined with most divine brightness, whence it shines through the grace of the Comforter!

*Troparion:*
Standing in the Temple, O Christ, Thou didst speak unto the gatherings of the Judeans, revealing Thy glory and declaring Thy kinship with the Father!

*Theotokion:*
Be thou my protectress and an unbreakable wall, delivering me from worldly temptations, O only Mother of God, and illuminating me with divine radiance!

*Another Heirmos: "As the waters of the sea..." (Ꙗ҆́кѡ во́ды мор):*

*Troparion:*
Upholding all the ends of the earth, Thou didst ascend, O Jesus, and didst teach the multitudes in the Sanctuary words of truth as the Feast reached its midpoint, as John cries aloud.

*Troparion:*
Having opened Thy lips, O Master, Thou didst proclaim unto the world the unoriginate Father and the All-Holy Spirit, preserving the kinship of both even after Thine Incarnation.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst accomplish the Father's work, and confirmed Thy words with deeds, working healings and signs, O Savior: restoring the paralytic, cleansing lepers, and raising the dead!

*Troparion:*
The unoriginate Son became a Beginning, taking upon Himself our nature; and in the midst of the Feast He taught, saying: "Come ye unto the Fountain, and draw ceaseless life!"

*Triadikon:*
One is the Godhead of the Trinity, a Tri-hypostatic Essence, uncreated and indivisible, all-glorious: Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, as being Three, yet One!

*Theotokion:*
We sing of thee as Virgin after childbirth, we glorify thee as Virgin and Mother, the only pure Maiden and Bride of God: for from thee in truth God was incarnate, restoring us to life!

*Katavasia, Heirmos:*
As the waters of the sea, O Lover of mankind, the waves of life tempestuously toss me; therefore like Jonah I cry unto Thee, O Word: Lead up my life from corruption, O merciful Lord!

*Kontakion. Tone 4. Podoben: "Lifted up upon the Cross" (Възнесы́ися на Крⷵтъ):*
At the midpoint of the Feast of the Law, O Creator and Master of all, Thou didst say unto those present, O Christ God: "Come and draw the water of immortality!" Therefore we fall down before Thee and faithfully cry: Grant us Thy compassions, for Thou art the Fountain of our life!

*Ikos:*
Water my soul, wounded by the lawless transgressions of sins, with the drops of Thy blood, and make it fruitful with virtues: for Thou didst tell all to come unto Thee, O All-Holy Word of God, and to draw the water of incorruption, living and cleansing the sins of them that sing of Thy glorious and divine resurrection, granting from on high unto Thy disciples who know Thee as God the strength of the Spirit Who truly descended: for Thou art the Fountain of our life!

*Then the Prologue. After it:*

*Synaxarion on Wednesday of Mid-Pentecost. Bless, Father.*[^629]

*Verses:*
Standing in the midst of the Teachers, Jesus instructs; in the midst of the Feasts, He shows Himself as Mediator.

On the Wednesday of the Paralytic, we celebrate the Feast of the Midpoint of Pentecost, in honor of the great two days of feasts—Pascha, I mean, and Pentecost—as uniting and joining them together. And it occurred in this manner: After Christ had performed the supernatural miracle upon the Paralytic, the Judeans being scandalized concerning the Paralytic because it was done on the Sabbath, sought to kill Him. He therefore departed into Galilee, and staying in the mountains there, He satisfied five thousand men with five loaves and two fish, besides women and children. Afterwards, when the Feast of Booths arrived—and this was also a great feast among the Judeans—He went secretly up unto Jerusalem. And at the midpoint of this feast, He entered into the Sanctuary and taught. Now the Sanctuary is the dwelling place of the One God, where love and peace, faith and chaste wisdom reside. Therefore it behooves the Hierarch of God who serves God to be peaceful and gentle, not thievish, not deceitful, not covetous of silver: so that he may first settle God in his own soul, and thereafter illumine the people according to his image and struggle. It may likewise be said of every believer, if he become a purified sanctuary, where the Lord God may dwell and abide. And all marveled at His reading, and envying Him they said: "How knows this man letters, having never learned?" For being the New Adam, He was, like the first Adam, filled with all wisdom, and also as God. Again, they all murmured, and rushed together to murder Him; but Christ, using greatness against them, said unto them as they disputed concerning the Sabbath: "Why seek ye to kill Me?" And they said: "Hast thou a demon? Who seeks to kill thee?" And He extended this further unto earlier things: "If ye dispute concerning the Law," He said, "why are ye angry with Me, because I made a man entirely whole on the Sabbath? Moses also gave this ordinance, and when circumcision falls on the Sabbath, ye circumcise." Having spoken many things unto them concerning this, and shown Himself to be the Giver of the Law and equal unto the Father, especially on the last great day of the Feast, He was pelted with stones by them; but not one stone touched Him. And passing by thence, He found a man blind from birth, and opening his eyes, He granted him sight.

It should be known that there were three great feasts among the Judeans. The first was Pascha, which was kept in the first month, having the commemoration of the passage through the Red Sea. The second was Pentecost, commemorating their dwelling in the wilderness after the passage through the Red Sea; for they remained fifty days in the wilderness until they received the Law, and also in honor of the number seven, which was held in honor among them. The third venerable feast was the Tabernacles, commemorating the tabernacle of witness, which Moses saw in the cloud upon the Mountain, and which Bezaleel the chief builder framed and erected, which was celebrated for seven days, having the memory of the ingathering of fruits and the resting in the wilderness. When that feast was being celebrated, Jesus stood and cried with a loud voice: "If anyone thirst, let him come unto Me and drink!" In that very teaching Christ showed Himself to be the Messiah, having become our Mediator and Reconciler with His eternal Father. For this reason, celebrating the present feast, and naming it Mid-Pentecost, we sing of Christ the Messiah, and set forth the venerable link between both great feasts. For this reason also, as it seems, the Feast of the Samaritan Woman is celebrated after it: because there also many things were spoken concerning Christ the Messiah, and concerning water and thirst, as here in John the Evangelist.[^630] The Sunday of the Blind Man follows that of the Samaritan Woman. By Thine unsearchable mercy, O Christ our God, have mercy and save us, now and ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace the Children of Abraham, burning with the love of piety rather than with flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord![^631]

*Troparion:*
Having destroyed the power of death by the might of Thy power, Thou didst show forth the paths of life unto men who cry gratefully unto Thee: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Seeing Thee bearing flesh, the assemblies of the rulers understood Thee not, O Word of God; but we sing unto Thee: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O sanctified divine habitation of the Most High! For through thee joy has been given unto them that cry: Blessed art thou among women, O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady!

*Another Heirmos: "The Chaldean furnace..." (Халде́йскаѧ пе́щъ ѡ҆́гне):*

*Troparion:*
Thou didst labor in the flesh, O Rest of all; Thou didst thirst willingly, O Fountain of wonders; Thou didst ask for water, O Jesus Who didst create the water of life!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst converse with the Samaritan woman, O Lord, exposing the foolishness of the lawless Hebrews: for she believed Thee to be the Son of God, whereas they rejected Thee!

*Troparion:*
Living and flowing water, the water of immortality, an ever-living fountain didst Thou promise to give, O Savior, unto them that receive in faith Thy Spirit Who proceeds from the Father.

*Troparion:*
With five loaves Thou didst feed thousands of hungry people, and didst multiply the fragments of twelve basketfuls remaining from the satisfaction, showing Thy glory unto the sacred disciples.

*Troparion:*
