He that eats My Flesh shall live unto ages; and he that drinks My Blood abides in Me, O my Savior, and I abide in him, and I will raise him up at the last day.

*Troparion:*
Marvel, O Master, at what a dispensation! Awesome in divine miracles in all things: Thou didst transform diseases, Thou didst raise the dead, and didst enlighten the blind as God.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst cleanse lepers, Thou didst make the lame to walk, Thou didst strengthen the paralytic, Thou didst heal the issue of blood, Thou didst walk upon the deep, showing Thy glory unto Thy sacred disciples.

*Triadikon:*
We worship Thy pure Father, O Lord, and the grace of the Spirit, Whom Thou, being God, didst distribute unto Thine Apostles, sending them forth unto the preaching.

*Theotokion:*
In thy womb thou didst contain the uncontainable Word; at thy breasts thou didst nurse the Sustainer of the world; in thine arms thou didst carry our Upholder, O pure Mother of God!

*Katavasia, Heirmos:*
The Chaldean furnace, blazing with fire, was cooled by the descent of the Spirit of God; and the Children sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Daniel, stretching out his hands, stopped the gaping mouths of the lions in the pit; and the Children, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Fair, having arisen from the grave, adorned with the glory of the Godhead, Thou didst appear unto Thine Apostles, O Lord, promising to send them the operation of the Spirit, as they cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Having put hades to death as God, O most life-giving One, Thou didst pour forth eternal life unto all, which now the light-bearing days of these joys openly manifest, as they cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Appearing as a ray like the Sun of Righteousness, O Christ, Thou didst send Thine Apostles into the world, bearing Thee, the unapproachable Light, cleansing the gloom of ignorance for them that cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Behold, now from the tribe of Judah has come forth the Prince and Leader, for thou, O all-immaculate one, hast borne Christ God, the expectation of the nations promised of old, unto Whom we sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Another Heirmos: "The Angels and the heavens on the throne..." (А҆́ггли и҆ нб҃са̀, на прⷵто́лѣ):*

*Troparion:*
Come, ye people, behold Him Who is hymned upon the throne of Glory, Who is blasphemed by lawless people; and seeing Him, sing of Him Who was called Messiah in the Prophets!

*Troparion:*
Thou art in truth Christ, Who art come into this world, Whose song and remission of transgressions are given to the disciples: Thou art in truth life unto them that have believed in Thee!

*Troparion:*
The Wisdom of God, when the Feast reached its midpoint, as it is written, stood in the Temple and taught: that in truth this was Christ the Messiah, through Whom is salvation!

*Troparion:*
On the Sabbaths, O Christ, and throughout all days Thou didst show forth deeds by signs, accomplishing diverse sacred works on each day; but the deceived people burned with fury!

*Troparion:*
"The paralytic who lay for many years," they said, "He healed on the Sabbath, and transgresses the Law!" The Judeans bitterly reviled Him.

*Troparion:*
"Did not Moses give you the Law? He commands to be circumcised, and on the Sabbath ye circumcise a man, lest the Law of commandments be broken!" Christ said unto the Judeans.[^632]

*Troparion:*
The senseless ones, bowing down to oblivion concerning the reasons for the life of those who lived in the wilderness, blaspheming moved their unrighteous tongues against Him Who instructed them in things of light.

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*

*Triadikon:*
One God is the Trinity: the Father having not passed into Sonship, nor the Son changed into procession; but the personal properties of both, Light of Light, I glorify unto ages!

*Theotokion:*
Say, how didst thou bear Him Who shone forth timelessly from the Father and is hymned with the Holy Spirit? Or how did He, being One, by good pleasure deign to be born of thee, O Mother of God?

*Katavasia, Heirmos:*
Him Who is borne on the throne of glory by Angels and the heavens, and is ceaselessly glorified as God: bless, sing, and exalt Him unto all ages!

*We sing not "More honorable".*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
The cornerstone Christ, cut without hands from the unhewn mountain, from thee, O Virgin, has joined the separated natures; therefore rejoicing we magnify thee, O Mother of God!

*Troparion:*
Having learned from Christ a new and honorable way of life, let us all strive usefully to preserve it unto the end, that we may receive the coming of the Holy Spirit.

*Troparion:*
Called again unto a divine life through the power of the Mediator, Who poured Himself out even unto the form of a servant and lifted us up: Him do we worthily magnify!

*Troparion:*
Thou, O Giver of life, having clothed my mortality with the garment of immortality and the grace of incorruption, O Savior, didst raise me up with Thyself and brought me unto the Father, having ended my temporal warfare!

*Theotokion:*
Knowing thee, O Virgin, to be the root, fountain, and cause of incorruption, all we faithful honor thee with praises: for thou hast poured forth substantial immortality unto us!

*Another Heirmos: "Virginity is foreign to mothers..." (Чꙋ́жде є҆́стъ мт҃рѧмъ):*

*Troparion:*
When the Judean Feast reached its midpoint, Thou didst ascend, O my Savior, unto Thy Sanctuary, and didst teach all; and the Judeans marveled, saying: "Whence knows this man letters, having never learned?"

*Troparion:*
Pouring forth the gifts of healings, my Redeemer works wonders and signs, driving away diseases and healing the sick; but the Judeans raged at the multitude of His miracles!

*Troparion:*
Rebuking the disobedient Judeans, my Redeemer cried: "Judge not according to appearance, but judge righteous judgment: for the Law commands every man to be circumcised even on the Sabbath!"

*Troparion:*
Greater wonders didst Thou grant unto Thy disciples, O Savior, as Thou didst promise, sending them unto the nations to preach Thy glory; and they proclaimed unto the world Thy resurrection, grace, and Incarnation.

*Troparion:*
"If ye circumcise a man on the Sabbath, lest the Law be broken, why now are ye angry with Me, because by a word I made a whole man sound? Ye judge according to the flesh," Christ said unto the Judeans.

*Troparion:*
Thou Who didst heal the withered hand by a word, heal the ancient withered land of my heart, O Word, and show me fruitful, that I also, O Savior, may produce fruits of warm repentance!

*Troparion:*
Cleansing my leprous heart, and enlightening the eyes of my soul, O Word:

raise me up who lie on a bed of sickness, even as Thou didst raise up the paralytic lying on his bed!

Glory... *Triadikon:*
Foreign is it unto the lawless to glorify the unoriginate Trinity: the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the uncreated Almighty Dominion, by Which the whole world was created through the power of Its might!

Both now and ever... *Theotokion:*
Thou didst contain in thy womb, O Virgin Mother, One of the Trinity, Christ the Giver of life, Whom all creation hymns, and before Whom the heavenly Thrones tremble: Him beseech, O all-blessed one, to save our souls!

*Katavasia, Heirmos:*
Virginity is foreign to mothers, and childbearing is strange to virgins; yet in thee, O Mother of God, both were accomplished: therefore all the generations of the earth ceaselessly magnify thee!

*Exapostilarion. Podoben: "The heaven with stars" (Не́бо звѣздами):*
Possessing the cup of inexhaustible gifts, grant me to draw water unto the remission of sins, for I am gripped by thirst, O only compassionate and merciful One! *(Thrice)*

*At the Praises, "Let every breath praise the Lord..." Set 4 stichera, and sing stichera in Tone 4. Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́бля):*
Wisdom and Power, the Radiance of the Father, the Co-eternal Word and Son of God, came in the flesh into the Sanctuary, and taught the Judean peoples, stiff-necked and senseless; and they marveled at the riches of wisdom, crying: "Whence knows this man letters, having never learned of anyone?"

The Messiah Lord stopped the mouths of the scribes and exposed the Judeans, crying unto them: "Judge not according to appearance, ye transgressors of the Law, as unrighteous! For on the Sabbath I raised up the paralytic; therefore I am Lord of the Sabbath and of the Law! Why seek ye to kill Me, Who have raised the dead?"

Teaching they stoned with stones, the cruel and lawless, the ungrateful Judean Assembly: they sawed Isaiah asunder with a wooden saw, they cast Jeremiah into the miry pit; and lifting up the Lord upon the Cross, they cried: "Thou that destroyest the temple, save Thyself, and we will believe!"[^633]

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 4. By Anatolios:*
Illumined, brethren, by the Resurrection of Christ the Savior, and having reached the midpoint of the gracious Feast, let us ever keep the commandments of God, that we may be worthy both to celebrate the Ascension and to receive the coming of the Holy Spirit.

*Then the Great Doxology.*

*Troparion of the Feast. Litany. First Hour. Instruction of the Saint. And the Dismissal.*

*At the Hours: Troparion and Theotokion of Mid-Pentecost.*

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes from the Canon of the Feast: Ode 3 of the First Tone, for 4; and of the Sixth Tone, for 4. After the Entrance: Troparion of the Feast. Glory... and Kontakion of the Feast. Prokeimenon, Tone 3: "Great is our Lord, and great is His power..." Verse: "Praise ye the Lord, for He is good..." Epistle in the Acts, section 34 [Acts 14:6–18]. Alleluia, Tone 4: "Remember Thy congregation which Thou hast purchased..." Verse: "God is our King before the ages." Gospel of John, section 26 [John 7:14–30]. Communion Hymn: "He that eats My Flesh and drinks My Blood abides in Me, and I in him, said the Lord. Alleluia."*[^634]

*At the Table: Consolation for the brethren.*

## On the Same Wednesday of Mid-Pentecost, at Evening

*Kathisma: the usual. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, 3; in the Menaion, 3. Stichera of the Feast. Tone 4.*

*Podoben: "Called from on high" (Зва́ныи свы́ше):*[^635]
Having destroyed the dominion of death by Thy death, O Christ, Thou hast given life unto men in Thy glorious resurrection: Thou hast raised the human race with Thyself by Thy descent; therefore we sing grateful praise unto Thee, celebrating the Feast of Thy seven-day and light-bearing resurrection, wherein the midpoint of these all-venerable days has now shone forth unto us all, O Jesus Giver of life, Benefactor of our souls!

Before Thy precious and holy Cross, working most glorious wonders among the people, at the Feast of the Law, as it is written, Thou didst stand in the midst, O all-powerful Christ, and didst cry aloud unto all: "If anyone thirst, let him come willingly unto Me, and draw rivers of divine waters, and waters of life! For the water of Life and the Power of Wisdom do I give unto all, as I willingly became like unto men, as Lover of mankind!"

Lying as on a bed of sickness, O Christ greatly merciful, because of my transgressions, and seeing myself paralyzed, knowing that out of the great deep of Thy love for mankind Thou becamest a suffering man, now invisibly raise me up as Thou saidst, and prosper me to run in the paths of Thy divine commandments: Thou Who before the eyes of the Hebrew people didst assure them by the multitude of wonders that, being God, Thou didst suffer willingly in the flesh!

*Glory to the Saint, if there be one. If not, Glory... both now and ever... Tone 6:*
When Thou didst come into the Sanctuary, O Christ God, at the midpoint of the Feast, then Thou didst teach the people, crying: "He that believes in Me, though he die, yet shall he live!" But the Judeans burned with fury, with the Pharisees and Sadducees and Scribes, saying: "Who is this that speaks blasphemies?" Not considering that Thou art before the ages, God glorified with the Father and the Spirit: glory to Thee!

*Prokeimenon of the day.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera. Tone 3:*
Thy Cross, O Christ, darkened the sun, and by the light of Thy resurrection Thou didst enlighten all things: accept our evening song, O Lover of mankind! *Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes...*

Into all the earth went forth your voice, O holy Apostles, for ye destroyed the deceit of idols, preaching the knowledge of God; this was your good contest, O blessed ones: therefore we sing and glorify your memory! *Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us...*

Thy Martyrs, O Lord, established by faith, strengthened by hope, spiritually united by the love of Thy Cross, destroyed the tyranny of the enemy, and having obtained crowns with the bodiless hosts, they pray for our souls!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 3:*
At the midpoint of the Feast, having wrought the salvation of him that is saved in the midst of the earth: hanging alive upon the Tree between two thieves, while the blasphemer remained silent, unto the believer Thou didst cry: "Today shalt thou be with Me in Paradise!" Thou didst descend into the Grave, despoiled hades, and didst arise on the third day, saving our souls![^636]

*Troparion of the Saint, if there be one. Glory... and now... If not, the same of the Feast. Litany, and Dismissal.*

## On Thursday of the Fourth Week after Pascha

*At Matins. At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, thrice.*

*After the First Kathisma, Sessional Hymn. Tone 3. Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ́ дѣ́вства):*[^637]
Taking upon Thyself all that is ours continually, Thou wast pleased, O my Creator, to be nailed upon the Cross, enduring to receive death as man, that as God Thou mightest free mankind from death: therefore as unto the Giver of life we cry unto Thee: Glory, O Christ, to Thy Resurrection!

Thou hast made bright the memory of Thy saints, O Lord, as Good One, because Thou didst strengthen them in the likeness of Thy sufferings; they manly conquered the power of Belial: therefore they also received gifts of healings: by their prayers, O Lover of mankind, grant peace unto Thy people!

Girded with the strength of Christ, and clad in the armor of light, ye manly cast down the battle-arrays of the enemy: for valiantly in the hope of life ye endured all the threats and wounds of the tormentors: therefore ye also received crowns, O long-suffering Martyrs of Christ!

*Glory... both now and ever... Theotokion:*
The Prophets proclaimed, the Apostles taught, the Martyrs confessed, and we have believed, that thou art in truth the Mother of God: therefore we also magnify thine ineffable Childbearing!

*After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn. Tone 8. Podoben: "Of the Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Standing in the midst of the Sanctuary, as the Feast was divinely halved, Thou didst cry aloud: "Let him that thirsts come unto Me and drink; for he that drinks of this My divine water shall pour forth rivers of My commandments from his belly! And he that believes in Me, Who was sent from the Divine Father, shall be glorified with Me!" Therefore we cry unto Thee: Glory to Thee, O Christ God, for Thou hast richly poured forth the waters of Thy love for mankind upon Thy servants!

*Glory... both now and ever... the same. "Having seen the Resurrection of Christ..." Psalm 50 [LXX 50]. Canon of the Feast, for 8; and in the Menaion, for 4. Kontakion and Exapostilarion of the Feast.*

*At the Praises, Stichera, for 4. Tone 3:*
In Thy light, O Master, we shall see light, O Lover of mankind: for Thou art risen from the dead, granting salvation unto the human race: that all creation may glorify Thee, the only Immortal One: have mercy on us! *(Twice)*

Having blamelessly kept the commandments of Christ, O holy Apostles, freely ye received, freely ye give, healing the passions of our souls and bodies: therefore having boldness, pray unto Him to have mercy on our souls!

Ye shine as luminaries in the world, even after death, O holy Martyrs, having fought the good fight: therefore having boldness, pray unto Christ to have mercy on our souls!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 4:*
O Lord, before Thy precious Cross, when the Feast reached its midpoint, Thou didst enter into the Sanctuary, teaching the Judeans with boldness the Law of Moses which was ordained by Thee; and being astonished, O Christ, at the ineffable mystery of Thy wisdom, murmuring against Thee, they said within themselves in envy: "How knows this man letters, having never learned?" Not knowing Thee, the Savior of our souls!

*At the Aposticha, whose acrostic is: MESOPENTIKOSTIS, that is: "Of Mid-Pentecost." Stichera, Tone 2. Podoben: "O house of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵ:):*[^638]
Great art Thou, O King, and great is Thy dominion, having mightily made war, Thou hast filled the universe with great gifts!

*Verse: Remember Thy congregation, which Thou hast purchased...*
Thou didst stand in the Temple, pouring forth streams as the Feast reached its midpoint, and watering them that thirst with divine blessings, O tender-hearted One!

*Verse: God is our King before the ages...*
Desiring to save me, Thou wast incarnate most gloriously of the unwedded Virgin, and in the midst of Thy Temple Thou didst pour forth grace unto me!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 8. By John the Monk:*
When the Feast reached its midpoint, before Thy death and glorious Resurrection, O Lord, Thou didst enter teaching, O Good One, into the Sanctuary unto the disobedient Judeans, and Pharisees, and Scribes, crying unto them: "Let him that thirsts come unto Me and drink! He that believes in Me, rivers of water of life of the Divine Spirit shall flow from his belly!" O Thine ineffable praise of wisdom: O our God Who fillest all things, glory to Thee!

*Then "It is good to give praise..." Troparion of the Feast. And the Dismissal.*

*At the Beatitudes, the Triodion, whose acrostic or initial lettering is this: AINESIS KYRIO, that is: "Praise unto the Lord."*[^639]

*Tone 3. Ode 1. Heirmos: "To Him Who of old..." (Присни́внїи го́рѣ):*

*Troparion:*
By Thine ineffable union...

likening Thyself unto men, O only supremely Blessed One; having endured the Cross in the flesh, and being counted among the dead, Thou didst rise on the third day, the Life of all![^640]

*Troparion:*
O Jesus our God, being the Water of Life, journeying Thou didst labor in the flesh unto the Well; and giving Thyself unto the Samaritan woman, Thou didst mystically pour forth the water of Thy wisdom!

*Troparion:*
Cleansing various diseases of them that came unto Thee by Thy command, O Word, Thou didst willingly endure the Cross, and being placed in the Tomb, Thou didst raise up human nature which had suffered corruption!

*Theotokion:*
Upon thee the Wisdom of the unoriginate Father came down like rain upon the fleece, O pure one; and being incarnate without change, He showed forth the way of life unto us who know thee to be Virgin after childbearing and Mother!

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "To Him Who in the flame..." (И҆́же въ пла́мени):*
Being lifted up upon the Cross, Thou didst lift up human nature with Thyself, O Word; and being placed in the Tomb, by Thy divine rising Thou didst raise up them that slept in the grave, O tender-hearted One!

*Troparion:*
Approaching Jericho and Salim, drawing near unto many waters, Thou didst come unto the Well of Jacob, O Fountain of wisdom, giving the water of salvation unto the Samaritan woman!

*Troparion:*
O Word Who didst enlighten the blind man, and by Thy command didst strengthen the paralyzed man, and didst marvelously raise up the dead man from the tomb: raise up my deadened soul!

*Theotokion:*
O Mary, container of God, thou art my refuge: deliver me from worldly scandals and bodily passions and everlasting Gehenna, that in songs I may glorify thee!

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "Thee the unburnt bush..." (Тебѐ неѡпали́мꙋю кꙋпинꙋ̀):*
Thou didst stretch forth Thy hands upon the Wood, O most merciful Christ, consuming without trace the hands of our forefather evilly stretched forth unto the eating of the tree!

*Troparion:*
Having strengthened the paralyzed man by Thy word, O Christ, and made straight the lame, Thou wast crucified, O Jesus; and having risen, Thou didst appear unto the disciples for many days!

*Glory...*
The Samaritan woman, having come to draw water, saw Thee, and receiving the water of life from Thee, she was filled, singing praise unto Thee, O Christ God!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
O wonders beyond understanding, O most pure Virgin! For thou didst bear God incarnate, Who by His divine upbringing saved all!

*On the same Thursday evening. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera. Tone 5.*

*Podoben: "Rejoice" (Ра́дꙋися):*[^641]
Today the light-bearing and fair Mid-Pentecost of the Savior's Resurrection, all-venerable, like bright midday embracing the world with divine graces, shines forth unnumbered wonders of Christ's rising! It radiates signs, points toward the Ascension on high, and reveals the dearly longed-for coming of Christ, the brightest triumph of all-venerable Pentecost: therefore it grants peace and great mercy unto our souls!

As a river of divine glory, the Lord, in the Feast now reached its midpoint, granting holy tender-heartedness unto all, cries aloud: "Let him that thirsts come unto Me and drink fervently! For being Himself the Fountain of creation and the Sea of mercy, He pours forth forgiveness unto the world, washes away transgressions, cleanses sicknesses, and saves them that celebrate His resurrection, covering with love them that honor His ascension with glory; and He grants peace and great mercy unto our souls!"

Standing in the midst of the Sanctuary, O uncircumscribed God and Lord: for Thou wast such by nature, even though for our sake Thou didst become man and didst mystically circumscribe a body, Thou didst pour forth the words of life unto all, saying unto the faithful: "Cleanse your souls, cool the heat of passions, lest by any means ye be deprived of the drink, O men; for I bestow the divine grace of the immortal and better coming Kingdom: he that partakes of Me shall be glorified with the Creator!"

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 6:*
As the Feast of Thy Resurrection, O Christ, and of the divine Coming of Thy Holy Spirit has reached its midpoint, we, having assembled, sing of the mysteries of Thy wonders: wherein do Thou send down great mercy upon us!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera. Tone 3:*
The tree of disobedience brought forth death unto the world; but the Tree of the Cross brought forth life and incorruption: therefore we bow down before Thee, the crucified Lord, that the light of Thy countenance may be marked upon us! *Verse: The Lord is risen...*

By Thy Cross, O Christ Savior, the dominion of death was destroyed, and the deceit of the devil was brought to naught; and the human race, saved by faith, ever offers song unto Thee! *Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us...*

Great is the power of Thy Cross, O Lord: for it trembled in its place and works in the world; and it made Apostles out of fishermen, and Martyrs out of the nations, that they might pray for our souls!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 8:*
When the Feast reached its midpoint, before Thy Passion and glorious Resurrection, O Lord, Thou didst enter teaching, O Good One, into the Sanctuary unto the disobedient Judeans, and Pharisees, and Scribes, crying unto them: "Let him that thirsts come unto Me and drink! He that believes in Me, rivers of water of life of the Divine Spirit shall flow from his belly!" O Thine ineffable praise of wisdom: O our God Who fillest all things, glory to Thee!

## On Friday of the Fourth Week after Pascha

*At Matins. At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, thrice.*

*After the First Kathisma, Sessional Hymn. Tone 3. Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ́ дѣ́вства):*[^642]
Having accepted to endure the Cross and death in the midst of creation, while creation beheld Thy wrath, when Thou wast well-pleased, O Savior, to have Thy Body nailed, then the sun hid its rays; and seeing this, the thief on the cross sang praise unto Thee, crying: "Remember me, O Savior!" And in the tomb, the thief received Paradise!

We the faithful bless the incomprehensible crucifixion and ineffable rising, the unutterable mystery: for today death and hades have been brought to naught, and the human race has been clothed in incorruption: therefore giving thanks we cry unto Thee: Glory, O Christ, to Thy Resurrection!

Thy thankful endurance conquered the wiles of the evil enemy, O all-praised Passion-bearers: therefore ye were counted worthy of eternal blessedness; but pray unto the Lord to save the flock of Christ-loving people, O true Martyrs!

*Glory... both now and ever... Theotokion:*
Having acquired the Cross of thy Son as a rod of strength, O Mother of God, thereby we cast down the haughtiness of the enemy, unceasingly magnifying thee with love!

*After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn. Tone 8. Podoben: "Of the Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости сло́во):*[^643]
Pouring forth the water of wisdom and life unto the world, Thou callest all to draw the waters of salvation: for a man receiving Thy divine law quenches the coals of deceit; wherein he shall never cease from being satisfied with Thee, O Master, transcendent King! Therefore we glorify Thy dominion, O Christ God, asking that forgiveness of sins be richly sent down upon Thy servants!

*Glory... both now and ever... the same.*

*“Having seen the Resurrection of Christ...” Psalm 50 [LXX 50]. Canon of the Feast, for 8; and in the Menaion, for 4. Kontakion and Exapostilarion of the Feast.*

*At the Praises, Stichera, for 4. Tone 3:*
Cast out by the taste of pleasure, I fell with a grievous fall; but Thou didst not despise me, O Master: having accepted for my sake the Crucifixion that belonged to me, Thou savest me and leadest me unto glory, O my Redeemer, glory to Thee! *(Twice)*

Come, all ye nations, understand the power of the awesome mystery: for Christ our Savior, Who was in the beginning the Word, was crucified for our sake, willingly was buried, and rose from the dead, that He might save all things: let us bow down before Him!

*Martyricon:*
Come, all ye people, let us honor the memory of the holy Passion-bearers: for having become a spectacle unto angels and men, they received the crown of victory from Christ, and they pray for our souls!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 8:*
When the Feast of Pascha reached its midpoint, Thou didst enter into the Temple of the Sanctuary, O our Savior; and standing in the midst of the people, Thou didst teach them with boldness, and didst say: "I am the Light of the world: he that follows Me shall not walk in darkness, but shall have the light of immortal life!"

*At the Aposticha, Stichera. Tone 2. Podoben: "O house of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^644]
Being wholly with God, O Word, Thou Son, desiring to deliver me from transgressions when I was perishing, Thou didst unite Thyself unto me, O Lover of mankind! *Verse: Remember Thy congregation, which Thou hast purchased...*

The shadow has passed: for behold, the Messiah, in the midst of the Feast, has shone forth grace like another morning star! *Verse: God is our King before the ages...*

Come obediently, ye that desire to drink living waters, cried my Jesus: and drink grace, rejoicing divinely!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 6:*
Let us cleanse the treasuries of thoughts and brighten the radiance of the soul, and we shall see Christ the Life, Who came into the Sanctuary through the surpassing abundance of grace, that He might conquer the enemy and save our race, by reason of the death of the Cross and the Resurrection: unto Whom let us cry: O Incomprehensible Lord, glory to Thee!

*At the Beatitudes, the Triodion, whose acrostic is: LOI DOISO THEOS, that is: "Unto God will I give praise."*[^645]

*Tone 3. Ode 5. Heirmos: "As Isaiah saw..." (Ꙗ҆́кѡ ви́дѣ И҆са́і́а):*
Wounded, O compassionate One, and willingly nailed unto the Tree, Thou deliverest them that were condemned by former transgressions; and being placed in the Tomb, Thou raisest up the dead that slept in the grave, who sing of Thy fearsome resurrection!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst rise from the dead as Thou saidst, O Marked One, and dwelling among the Apostles Thou didst cry: "Go into all the world and proclaim unto Me the great wonders that ye see!"

*Troparion:*
Heart and ear marvel, O Savior, at Thy tender-heartedness, dispensation, and condescension: for walking in the flesh upon earth, and asking water of the Samaritan woman, Thou didst give her the water of divine understanding!

*Theotokion:*
O Holy Mother of God, who in the holy of holies didst bear the Holy Word, Who sat with the unoriginate Father: unceasingly pray unto Him to sanctify and enlighten our souls and hearts, who sing of thee with love!

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "Unto the unendurable fire..." (Непостоя́нномꙋ Ѻ҆гни́):*
Being willingly lifted up upon the Cross, Thou didst liberate all human nature; and being counted among the dead, Thou Who art Life, didst give life, O Savior, unto them that sing of Thy dispensation and of Thy glorious ascension, O tender-hearted One!

*Troparion:*
The Church celebrates with joy in Thy holy rising, O Giver of life, whereby Thou didst trample down all the power of hades, and didst blunt the sting of death, O Word, and didst pour forth incorruption unto men!

*Troparion:*
The Samaritan woman, seeing Thee weary, O tender-hearted One, and asking water to drink, receives the waters of immortality, crying aloud: "Give me the water of life, O Lord, that I may never thirst during all the time of my life!"

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast shown forth as the Temple of God, O most pure Virgin Mother of God, having lent Him ensouled flesh from thy most pure blood, O Maiden: therefore we honor thee unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "In the shadow of the law..." (Въ зако́ннѣй сѣ́ни):*
The sun marveled, beholding Thee nailed upon the Tree, O Christ; the ends of the earth were shaken and all the earth trembled, the rocks were split, and hades below released the prisoners held from the ages, who sing praise unto Thee!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast placed in the Tomb, O Christ, Thou Life and Resurrection; and having risen in glory, during many days Thou didst dwell with Thine own disciples, sending them forth as preachers of Thy fearsome dispensation unto all the world!

*Troparion:*
Showing me the paths of life, O merciful One, journeying in Thy great mercy, Thou wast weary from the way, O Jesus; and unto the woman asking water of immortality Thou sendest it, as she sings of Thy great dispensation!

*Theotokion:*
Christ is incarnate of thee beyond speech, O Virgin Mother of God, He Who is the Word of the Father; and He is willingly crucified for our sake, and by His three-day rising He enlightens the ends of the world: with Whom we duly call thee blessed!

*On the same Friday evening. Kathisma: the usual.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, 3; and in the Menaion, 3. Stichera of the Feast. Tone 4.*

*Podoben: "O heavenly orders" (Нб҃нымъ чи́нѡ):*[^646]
The End of all and Giver of life, the co-everlasting Word with the Divine Father, willingly received flesh and became man, and as God poured forth teachings of ineffable wisdom unto all!

When the Judean Feast reached its midpoint, O Christ, Thou didst stand in the Sanctuary, the Master of the Law, teaching with authority, reproving the Scribes, as it is written, and astonishing the teachers by the signs of Thy wisdom!

The Giver of wisdom and Pourer-forth of good things, pouring out divine rivers from the inexhaustible fountain, cries: "Come unto Me and draw the water of life, ye that thirst; and rivers of divine gifts shall flow from your belly!"

*Glory... Tone 4:*
The midpoint of the days of Pentecost has arrived, wherein Christ, having lightly unveiled His divine power, strengthened the paralyzed man by His word, raising him from his bed; and working wonders in a God-befitting manner in His created body, He granted eternal life and great mercy unto men!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion. Tone 3:*
How shall we not marvel at thy God-manhood: of the day.[^647]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Resurrection. Tone 3:*

By Thy Cross, O Christ Savior, the dominion of death was destroyed, and the deceit of the devil was brought to naught; and the human race, saved by faith, ever offers song unto Thee! *Verse: The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty...*

All things were enlightened by Thy resurrection, O Lord, and Paradise was opened again; and all creation, praising Thee, ever offers song unto Thee! *Verse: For He has established the world...*

I glorify the power of the Father and the Son, and I hymn the Godhead of the Holy Spirit: the undivided, uncreated Godhead, the consubstantial Trinity, reigning unto ages of ages! *Verse: Holiness becomes Thine house...*

By Thy death, O Christ, Thou didst darken the sun, and by the light of Thy resurrection Thou didst enlighten all things: accept our evening song, O Lover of mankind!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 4:*
Thou didst come into the Sanctuary, O Wisdom of God: (see fol. 193r)[^648]. *Then "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart..." Troparion of the Feast. And the Dismissal.*

## On Saturday of the Fourth Week after Pascha

*At Matins. [The service is sung as directed for the preceding Saturdays.][^649] At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, thrice.*

*After the First Kathisma, Sessional Hymns. Tone 3:*
He that rose from the dead became the firstfruits of them that slept, the Firstborn of creation and Maker of all that have come to be; He renewed the corrupted nature of our race with Himself: no longer dost thou rule, O death, for the Master of all has destroyed thy dominion!

Having tasted death in the flesh, O Lord, Thou didst cut off the bitterness of death by Thy rising, and having strengthened man against it, summoning the victory over the ancient curse: O Defender of our life, Christ God, glory to Thee!

*Glory... both now and ever... Theotokion:*
Thee, the Mediatress of the salvation of our race, we hymn, O Virgin Mother of God: for by the flesh assumed from thee, Thy Son and our God, having accepted the Passion through the Cross, delivered us from corruption, as Lover of mankind!

*After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn. Tone 8. Podoben: "Of the Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
When the Feast of the Law reached its midpoint, Thou wast teaching in the Sanctuary, O my Savior, reproving the folly of the unbelieving Hebrews, and crying unto the crowds with a divine voice, as Lover of mankind Thou didst call aloud: "Let him that thirsts come unto Me and drink!" Therefore Thou didst promise to give great waters from the incorruptible fountain unto them that believe: therefore we cry unto Thee: Send down Thy Holy Spirit, O Christ God, and save us, for Thou alone art greatly merciful!

*Glory... both now and ever... the same. "Having seen the Resurrection of Christ..." Psalm 50 [LXX 50]. Canons of the Feast with Heirmoi, for 6; of the Holy Temple, for 4; and in the Menaion, for 4. Kontakion and Exapostilarion of the Feast.*

*At the Praises, Stichera of the Resurrection, for 4. Tone 3:*
"Come, all ye nations..." "All wonders conquered..." "They were filled with joy..." "In Thy light, O Master..." These four verses are written at the Praises on the Sunday: leaf 30, [number] 23.

*Here: Glory... both now and ever... Tone 4. By Anatolius:*
Having been enlightened, O brethren, by the Resurrection of Christ the Savior, and having reached the blessed Mid-Pentecost of the Feast, let us purely keep the commandments of God, that we may be worthy to celebrate the Ascension and to receive the coming of the Holy Spirit!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera. Tone 2. Podoben: "O house of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
The flowing fountain pours forth the spiritual water of wisdom: drinking thereof, we are filled with divine dogma! *Verse: Remember Thy congregation, which Thou hast purchased...*

The cruel Hebrew race was truly consumed with grief, hearing Thee, O Word, instructing the people with saving teaching! *Verse: God is our King before the ages...*

Thou didst come unto Zion, O Creator, and didst stand in the midst of Thy people, and didst give saving grace unto the people!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 4:*
O Lord, before Thy precious Cross, when the Feast reached its midpoint, Thou didst enter into the Sanctuary, teaching the Judeans with boldness the Law of Moses which was ordained by Thee; and being astonished, O Christ, at the ineffable mystery of Thy wisdom, murmuring against Thee, they said within themselves in envy: "How knows this man letters, having never learned?" Not knowing Thee, the Savior of our souls!

*On the same Saturday at the Beatitudes, the Tetraodion.*[^650]

*Tone 3. Ode 6. Heirmos: "The bottomless deep..." (Бе́здна послѣ́дне ма):*
Pierced by a spear in Thy divine side, O Giver of life, Thou didst heal man wounded by transgression by Thy divine sufferings: therefore, O Master, we sing praise unto Thy dominion!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst endure blows, O Jesus, destroying the kingdoms of hades; and Thou didst shatter the bars with Thy hand, O Savior of all, holding hades captive, and Thou didst rise on the third day!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst stand before Pilate's judgment seat like a condemned man, O Lord Who judgest all the earth; and desiring to deliver him whom Thou hadst created from hades, Thou didst descend unto him, O only Compassionate One!

*Theotokion:*
Without travail thou didst bear the Son Who before the ages was begotten of the Father, O pure one; and thou didst suffer pain seeing Him crucified in the flesh, but when He rose as God, thou didst rejoice radiantly!

*Ode 7. Heirmos: "Of old unto the golden image..." (Пе́рвѣе тѣ́лꙋ зла́томꙋ):*
Being unapproachable unto Cherubim and Seraphim, Thou didst endure Crucifixion in the flesh through the tender-heartedness of Thy mercy, that Thou mightest save Adam who had decayed through transgression!

*Troparion:*
Having descended with Thy soul into hades, Thou didst destroy its dominion, O Master; and Thou didst raise up with Thyself them that were bound therein, who sing praise unto Thy dominion, O Giver of life!

*Troparion:*
Having cast off the sorrow of despondency, the Myrrh-bearing women hastened to reach the Tomb; and seeing Christ risen from the grave, they proclaimed it unto the world!

*Theotokion:*
Beholding Christ Whom thou didst bear, O pure one, risen radiantly on the third day with glory from the dead: as His Mother, pray Him for all the world!

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "Unto the unendurable fire..." (Непостоя́нномꙋ Ѻ҆гни́):*
He Who created every breath by His Word, is willingly hung upon the Tree in the flesh by the Judeans, and is given gall to drink; and beholding it, all creation was shaken, lamenting the folly of the ungrateful!

*Troparion:*
Hades receives Thee, the Prince and Savior of the world, seeing Thee as a man; but Thou didst bind it as Lord, and those whom it formerly held guilty Thou didst release, and marvelously didst raise them with Thyself from the tomb!

*Troparion:*
Not leaving the Father's bosom, Thou didst take flesh from the Virgin for our sake, and willingly didst submit unto death; and being placed in the Tomb, Thou didst rise on the third day, O Word of God: therefore we sing praise unto Thee, the only Giver of life!

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst bear...

Thou didst bear the Son without travail, O pure one, Who before the ages is with the Father and the Holy Spirit; seeing Him hanging upon the Cross, thou didst suffer grief, but seeing Him risen from the tomb, thou rejoicest with Him unto the ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "In the shadow of the law..." (Въ зако́ннѣй сѣ́ни):*
The luminaries, seeing Thee, O Christ, hanging in the flesh upon the Cross, hid their light, and the thief beholding it confesses God; the whole earth is shaken, and the rent veil cries aloud: Glory to Thy longsuffering, O Master!

*Troparion:*
Thy descent, O King, was truly fearsome in the nether regions: for Thou didst shatter the gates and bars of hades, and didst lead captive all the prisoners it held, and didst rise mightily, unto them that proclaimed Thee to be God!

*Glory...*
Joseph faithfully took Thee down dead from the Tree, O Christ; and with myrrh, winding-sheets, and a napkin, having anointed Thee with love and joy, he pours forth a multitude of tears, and laid Thee in a sepulcher for the dead: therefore with songs we magnify Thee!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
Having conceived in thy womb Him Who is before the ages, indivisibly with the Father and the Spirit, thou didst bear Him without travail, O all-immaculate Virgin Maiden; but seeing Him suffer on the Tree, thy maternal heart was wounded: yet again by His resurrection thou rejoicest with the Son in unending life, O divinely blessed one!

*Then the Prokeimenon of the day. Epistle in Acts, section 24 [Acts 9:32–42]: "In those days, Peter passed through..." Alleluia of the day. Gospel according to John, section 31 [John 8:31–42]: "The Lord said unto those that came unto Him..." Communion hymn of the Feast: "He that eats My Flesh and drinks My Blood abides in Me and I in him, said the Lord." Alleluia.*[^651]

## Fifth Sunday after Pascha: Sunday of the Samaritan Woman

*On Saturday evening. At Small Vespers. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Resurrection, for 4. Tone 4:*
"Unto Thy life-creating Cross..." "The prohibition of the tree of disobedience..." "Thou didst break the gates of hades..." "Come, let us hymn..." *Glory... Tone 6:* "At Jacob's well..." All these seek ahead at Great Vespers.[^652]

*Both now and ever... Theotokion, Dogmatikon. Tone 4:*
Without seed thou didst conceive, and ineffably didst bring forth Him Who cast down the mighty from their thrones and exalted the humble, and Who raised up the horn of Christ for them that accomplish His Passion and Burial and glorious Resurrection: therefore, O Mother of God, cause of so many blessings, we bless thee with unceasing hymns, as thou ever prayest that our souls may be saved!

*At the Aposticha, Sticheron of the Resurrection: "The Lord, having ascended upon the cross..." (see fol. 305r)[^653]. Glory...*

*Then Theotokion, Tone 4. Podoben: "Called from on high" (Зва́ныи свы́ше):*[^654]
The Son, timeless from the unoriginate Father, for the sake of condescension and salvation unto men, became a man, being God, that He might now give Paradise unto the First-fashioned; then also shall all nature be freed from the serpent's deceit, and He shall save the fallen image, as One easily changing all things for good: therefore we honor the Mother, the Bride-chosen vessel, as undefiled, whom all of us bless as an anchor and haven! *Verse: Give ear, O daughter, and behold, and incline thine ear...*

In thy womb thou didst hold the incarnate Creator of all, O divinely blessed one, Who re-formed man who had formerly fallen through the serpent's transgression: for thou didst bear Him ineffably as God for us, and by thy childbearing thou didst free nature from corruption that had entered in: therefore we sing and glorify thy grace, O unwedded Virgin: cease not to pray that our souls may be saved! *Verse: The rich among the people shall entreat thy face...*

That thou mayest uncover unto us the multitude of thy mercy and goodness, an uncircumscribed sea, thou dost blot out all the sins of thy servants: for thou hast the power of creation, O all-immaculate one, being the Mother of God, and thou guidest all things as thou wilt by thy power; for grace from the Holy Spirit was manifestly poured out unto thee: He assists thee in all things ever, O all-praised one!

*Glory... Tone 8: "When Thou didst appear in the flesh..." Both now and ever... Tone 8: "When the Feast reached its midpoint..." Look for these below at the Aposticha, fol. 193v.*[^655]

*Troparion: "The radiant proclamation of the resurrection..." fol. 195v. Glory... both now and ever... "When the Feast reached its midpoint..." fol. 176v. And Dismissal.*

*At Great Vespers. "Blessed is the man..." the entire Kathisma.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses. And we sing Stichera of the Resurrection, 3; and Anatolian, 1; of Mid-Pentecost, 3; and of the Samaritan Woman, idiomela, 3.*[^656]

*Stichera of the Resurrection. Tone 4:*
Unceasingly bowing down before Thy life-creating Cross, O Christ God, we glorify Thy three-day Resurrection: for thereby, O Almighty One, Thou hast renewed the corrupted nature of man, and hast renewed for us the ascent unto Heaven, as the only Good One and Lover of mankind!

Thou didst loose the condemnation of the tree of disobedience,

*Fol. 194r / 194*
O Savior, being willingly nailed unto the Tree of the Cross; and descending into hades, O Mighty One, Thou didst tear asunder the bonds of death as God: therefore we bow down before Thy resurrection from the dead, crying aloud in joy: O Almighty Lord, glory to Thee!

Thou didst shatter the gates of hades, O Lord, and by Thy death Thou didst destroy the kingdom of death; and Thou didst liberate the human race from corruption, granting life, incorruption, and great mercy unto the world!

*Anatolian:*
Come, ye people, let us sing of the Savior's three-day rising, whereby we were freed from the unbreakable bonds of hades; and we all received incorruption and life, crying: O Thou Who wast crucified, buried, and hast risen, save us by Thy resurrection, O only Lover of mankind!

*Then Stichera of Mid-Pentecost, 3. Tone 4. Podoben: "The Lord ascended" (Гдⷭ҇ь взоше):*
"The midpoint of the days has arrived..." "Having heard the divine things..." "The approach of the divine..." (see fol. 173v)[^657].

*Then Stichera of the Samaritan Woman, 3. Tone 4:*
Unto the Well Thou didst come, O Fountain of wonders, in the sixth hour, to catch the fruit of Eve: for at that hour Eve went forth from Paradise through the deceit of the serpent; for the Samaritan woman came to draw water, seeing whom the Savior said: "Give Me water to drink, and I shall fill thee with flowing waters!" And running into the city, the prudent woman announced unto the people, saying: "Come, see Christ the Lord, the Savior of our souls!"

*Tone 2:*
When the Lord came unto the well, the Samaritan woman besought the tender-hearted One: "Give me the water of faith, and I shall receive the waters of the font, joy, and deliverance: O Giver of life, Lord, glory to Thee!"

*[Woodcut: Christ and the Samaritan Woman at Jacob's Well near Sychar, with the Apostles approaching on the right. Inscriptions: "Sunday of the Samaritan Woman" (Нл҃ѧ . Ѡ самарѧныни), "Jesus Christ" (І҆с҃ Хс҃), "The Samaritan Woman" (Самарѧныни), "Jacob's Well" (Студенецъ Іѧковль), "Apostles" (Апⷵли).]*[^658]

*Sticheron. Tone 3:*
The co-unoriginate and co-everlasting Son and Word of the Father came unto the Well, the Fountain of healings; and a woman of Samaria came to draw water, seeing whom the Savior said: "Give Me water to drink, and go, call thy husband!" But she, speaking as unto a man and not unto God, striving to conceal it, said: "I have no husband." And the Teacher said unto her: "Thou hast spoken truly: 'I have no husband': for thou hast had five husbands, and he whom thou now hast is not thy husband." And she, astonished at His voice, running into the city, cried unto the crowds, saying: "Come and see Christ, Who grants great mercy unto the world!"

*Glory... Tone 6:*[^659]
At Jacob's well Jesus found the Samaritan woman, and He Who covers heaven with clouds asks water of her. O wonder! He Who is invisible unto the Cherubim converses with a harlot woman, asking water, He Who suspended the earth upon the waters; seeking water, He Who pours forth fountains

*Fol. 195r / 195*
and lakes of waters, desiring truly to draw her who was hunted by the adversary enemy, and to give living water to drink unto her who was grievously burned in disorderliness, as the only Tender-hearted One and Lover of mankind!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion. Tone 4: "He Who for thy sake..." (see fol. 193r)[^660].*

*Then the Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. At the Litiya, Stichera of the Holy Monastery according to custom, and of the Mother of God from the Octoechos, Tone 6.*

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 3:*
Let Heaven and Earth rejoice radiantly today, for Christ appeared in the flesh, having become man, that He might deliver Adam from the universal curse; and He is marvelous in wonders, having come to Samaria and stood before the woman, asking water, He Who clothes the clouds with waters: therefore let all of us the faithful bow down before Him Who willingly became poor by His tender-hearted counsel!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Resurrection. Tone 4:*
O Lord, having ascended upon the Cross, Thou hast destroyed our ancestral curse; and having descended into hades, Thou hast liberated the eternal prisoners, granting incorruption unto the human race: therefore singing we glorify Thy life-creating and saving Resurrection!

*Other Stichera, one each. Verse: The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty...*
Hung upon the Tree, O only Mighty One, Thou didst shake all creation; and placed in the Tomb, Thou didst raise up them that dwelt in the grave, granting incorruption and life unto the human race: therefore singing we glorify Thy three-day rising! *Verse: For He has established the world, which shall not be moved...*

O Christ, the lawless people, having delivered Thee unto Pilate, condemned Thee in the flesh, showing themselves senseless toward their Benefactor; but willingly having endured burial, Thou didst rise on the third day of Thine own power as God, granting unending life and great mercy unto us!

*Both now and ever...*

With tears the women, having reached the Tomb, sought Thee; and not finding Thee, weeping with lamentation they cried aloud, saying: "Woe unto us, O Savior, King of all! How hast Thou been stolen away? What place holds Thy life-bearing body?" But the Angel answered them, saying: "Weep not, but go, proclaim that the Lord is risen, granting joy, for He is tender-hearted!"

*Glory... Tone 8:*
When Thou didst appear in the flesh, O Christ God, in Thine ineffable dispensation, the Samaritan woman, hearing the word of Thee the Lover of mankind, left her water-pot at the Well and ran, saying unto them that were in the city: "Come, see the Knower of hearts! Is not this indeed the expected Christ, Who has great mercy?"

*Both now and ever... of the Feast. Tone 8:*
When the Feast reached its midpoint, as Thou wast teaching, O Savior, the Judeans said: "How knows this man letters, having never learned?" Not understanding that Thou art the Wisdom that ordered the world: glory to Thee!

*At the Blessing of the Loaves, censing. Troparion: "Rejoice, O Virgin Mother of God..." thrice. If there is no vigil: Troparion of the Resurrection; Glory... both now and ever... of the Feast.*

*Concerning the Saint of the day, rubric on fol. 176v.*[^661]

*If it happens on this Fifth Sunday after Pascha that the Memory of the Holy Great-Martyr George falls, call Pascha 4 [April 23]; or of the Holy Apostle John the Theologian, call 300 [May 8]: then the service of Mid-Pentecost is left aside, and the Service of the Resurrection is sung, and of the Samaritan Woman, and of the Saint: and the rubric is written on the Sunday of the Myrrh-bearers.*[^662]

*On the Fifth Sunday after Pascha. If there is no vigil, at the Midnight Office, the Canon unto the Holy and Life-Creating Trinity, whose acrostic is: TETARTOS YMNOSTO THEO MITROPHANIS, that is: "Let the fourth song of Metrophanes be sung to God." Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos: "The Red Sea..." from the Octoechos.*[^663]

*At Matins. At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Resurrection, twice. Tone 4:*
Having learned the radiant proclamation of the Resurrection from the Angel, the women disciples of the Lord

*Fol. 196r / 196*
cast off the ancestral condemnation, and boasting unto the Apostles they said: "Death is overthrown, Christ God is risen, granting great mercy unto the world!"

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 8:*
When the Feast reached its midpoint, thirsting soul: (see fol. 176v)[^664].

*After the First Kathisma, Sessional Hymns. Tone 4:*[^665]
Looking upon the entrance of the Tomb, and unable to endure the angelic flame, the Myrrh-bearing women marvelled with trembling, saying: "Has He been stolen away, Who opened Paradise unto the thief? Or is He risen, Who proclaimed His rising before the Passion? Truly Christ is risen, granting life and resurrection unto them that are in hades!"

Thou didst swiftly anticipate, rising from the tomb as immortal, O Savior; by Thy power Thou didst raise up Thy world with Thyself, O Christ our God; Thou didst shatter the dominion of death in Thy might, Thou didst show forth resurrection unto all the world: therefore we glorify Thee, O only Lover of mankind!

*Glory... both now and ever... Theotokion:*
The mystery hidden from the ages and unknown unto the angels was made manifest upon earth through thee, O Mother of God: God being incarnate in unconfused union, and willingly accepting the Cross for our sake, whereby having raised the First-fashioned, He saved our souls from death!

*After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymns. Tone 4:*
Gabriel, having descended from the heights of heaven and approached the stone where the Rock of life was, clothed in white, cried aloud unto the weeping women: "Cease from the cry of lamentation, ye that now have that which is full of gracious joy! For He Whom ye seek weeping, take courage: having received joy, bow down before the Risen One: take courage, therefore, and let Eve also take courage!"

*Troparion:*
By Thine own free will Thou didst endure crucifixion, O Savior...

O Savior. In a new Tomb mortal men placed Thee Who didst establish the ends of the earth by Thy word: therefore the stranger was bound, death was fiercely plundered, and all they that were in hades cried aloud unto Thy life-bearing rising: "Christ is risen, He that remains the Giver of life unto the ages!"

*Glory... both now and ever... Theotokion:*
Joseph marveled, beholding that which was beyond nature; and he reflected in his mind upon the rain upon the fleece at Thine unseeded conception, O Mother of God: the bush unconsumed by fire, Aaron's rod that budded; and Thy betrothed and protector testified, crying aloud unto the priests: "A Virgin bears a child, and after childbearing remains again a Virgin!"

*Then reading in the Commentary on the Gospel according to John, Homilies 32, 33, and 34. After the Blameless [Psalm 118], the Angelic Assembly [Evlogetaria]. Hypakoe, Tone 4:*
Anticipating the morning, the Myrrh-bearing women proclaimed unto the Apostles Thy glorious resurrection, O Christ: that Thou hast risen as God, granting great mercy unto the world!

*Gradual Antiphons, Tone 4. Antiphon 1:*
From my youth many passions war against me; but do Thou Thyself protect me, and save me, O my Savior!

Ye that hate Zion, be put to shame by the Lord: for as grass ye shall be withered by the fire!

*Glory... both now and ever...*
By the Holy Spirit every soul is given life, and is exalted in purity, illumined by the Trinitarian Unity in sacred mystery.

*Antiphon 2:*
I have cried fervently unto Thee, O Lord, from the depth of my soul: let Thy divine ears be attentive to my supplication!

Everyone that has acquired hope in the Lord is higher than all they that afflict him.

*Glory... both now and ever...*
By the Holy Spirit stream the fountains of grace, watering all creation unto the renewing of life.

*Antiphon 3:*
Let my heart be lifted up unto Thee, O Word, and let nothing of the world's beauties allure me unto weakness!

As anyone has love for his own mother, so we are bound to love the Lord with yet more fervent devotion.

*Glory... both now and ever...*
By the Holy Spirit comes the wealth of the knowledge of God, of the vision of God, and of wisdom: for in Him the Word reveals all the Father's commandments.

*Fol. 197r / 197*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: Arise, O Lord, help us, and deliver us for Thy name's sake! [LXX 43:26]. Verse: O God, we have heard with our ears... [LXX 43:1].*

*Then "Let every breath praise the Lord." Verse: Praise God in His saints... [LXX 150:1]. The Resurrection Gospel: see the cyclical cycle, Pericope 103 [Luke §113].*[^666] *Then "Having seen the Resurrection of Christ..." Psalm 50 [LXX 50]. And the rest according to custom.*

*Then the Canons: of Pascha with the Heirmos and Theotokia, for 4 (see fol. 109v)[^667]; and of Mid-Pentecost, for 4 (see fol. 176v)[^668]; and of the Samaritan Woman, for 6. For the Katavasia, we sing the Heirmos of the present Canon; or as in the Greek, of Pascha: "The Day of Resurrection," both choirs together.*[^669]

*Canon of the Samaritan Woman, Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos. The composition of Joseph:*[^670]
*Heirmos:* Having struck Egypt and drowned the tyrant Pharaoh in the sea, Thou didst save the people from bondage, as in the manner of Moses they sang a song of victory: "For He is glorified!"

*Troparion:*
He that was buried has risen, and has raised up the human race in Himself: let all creation rejoice, and let the spiritual clouds drop down righteousness upon us today!

*Troparion:*
Having willingly accepted death in the flesh, Thou didst rise on the third day from the dead, O Author of life, having alone emptied the treasuries of hades and led forth the enchained souls!

*Troparion:*
Seeing the Angel shining in his appearance, the Myrrh-bearing women fled from the Tomb in fear; and having learned of Christ's resurrection, they hastened to announce it unto the Disciples!

*Troparion:*
O Lord, Who coverest the highest chambers with waters, O Son Who art the Living Water: unto the Samaritan woman who asked Thy pure water, Thou didst grant it when she recognized Thy tender-heartedness!

*Glory... Triadicon:*
O Father, Son, and Spirit, we that honor and glorify Thee with faith cry aloud: O Savior, Creator of all, grant us also cleansing of our sins, as Thou art transcendent in goodness!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O fiery Throne! Rejoice, O all-golden Lampstand! Rejoice, O Cloud of light! Rejoice, O spiritual Palace of the Word, O Table that hast decently brought forth Christ, the Bread of life!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
My heart is established in the Lord, Who grants prayer unto him that prays: for the bow of the mighty has become weak, and the feeble have girded themselves with strength!

*Troparion:*
Willingly Thou wast lifted up upon the Tree, O Word; and beholding Thee, the stones were rent asunder, all creation bowed down, and the dead rose from their tombs as from sleep!

*Troparion:*
Beholding Thee, O Word, having come in soul unto hades, all the souls of the righteous were loosed from their eternal bonds, singing hymns of praise unto Thy power which is beyond understanding!

*Troparion:*
"Why marvel ye? Why seek ye in the tomb with myrrh the Benefactor, O women? He is risen, and has raised the world with Himself!" cried the shining Angel unto the Myrrh-bearing women.

*Troparion:*
Being Life and the Fountain of immortality, Thou didst sit by the well of Thy boundless compassions, knowing how to satisfy the Samaritan woman who asked of Thee and sang unto Thee!

*Glory... Triadicon:*
One God over all in Trinity we praise, Father, Son, and Divine Spirit, Whom the heavenly ranks glorify, crying aloud thus: "Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O Lord!"

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
Having ineffably conceived in thy womb the God of all, O Virgin Mother, beyond mind and word thou didst bear Him, having remained a Virgin as before childbearing, O Bride of God!

*Kontakion of Mid-Pentecost, and Ikos: (see fol. 180r).*[^671]

*Sessional Hymn. Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Възнесы́йся):*
Let Heaven rejoice and let the earthly things exult, for Christ appeared from the Virgin as man, and delivered all mankind from corruption by His death; and with wonders Thou didst shine forth unto the Samaritan woman who asked water, granting the Fountain of healing, as Thou art immortal! *Glory... both now and ever... the same.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I heard, O Lord, Thy glory, and I was afraid; I considered Thy works, and I was amazed: for the earth was filled with Thy praise!

*Troparion:*
Let the heavens be glad, let all creation keep festival, for having appeared from the beginning unto all the all-wise Apostles,

*Fol. 198r / 198*
He shines forth radiantly!

*Troparion:*
All the power of death was swallowed up when Christ died; and the dead went forth from the tombs as from a bridal chamber at His rising!

*Troparion:*
"Why lament ye, O women? Why seek ye the Immortal One with myrrh? He is risen, as He said!" the Angel said unto the Myrrh-bearers.

*Troparion:*
Unto the Samaritan woman who asked, O Lord, Thou didst grant water: the knowledge of Thy dominion; therefore she thirsts not unto the ages, singing praise unto Thee!

*Glory... Triadicon:*
O transcendent Trinity, Father, Word, and Divine Spirit, co-essential and co-unoriginate: save us the faithful who sing unto Thee in faith!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
The Lawgiver beheld thee of old as the unconsumed bush, and Daniel contemplated thee as the Holy Mountain, O only pious Virgin Mother!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Make the light of Thy commandments shine upon me, O Lord, for my spirit awakes early unto Thee and cries aloud unto Thee: for Thou art our God, and unto Thee have I fled for refuge, O King of peace!

*Troparion:*
Reaching Thy holy Tomb early, the Myrrh-bearing women saw a young man shining radiantly and were terrified, having been taught of Thy rising, O Christ!

*Troparion:*
Death has been put to death, hades has been plundered, the bound have been loosed by Christ's rising: let us rejoice and clap our hands, celebrating radiantly!

*Troparion:*
Leap for joy, ye Apostles! Dance, ye Angels! Rejoice, all ye earthborn: the Lord is risen, corruption has been overthrown, sorrow has ceased, and Adam keeps festival!

*Troparion:*
Being the Fountain of life, O Lord, Thou didst bestow the water of forgiveness and understanding unto the Samaritan woman who asked of old: therefore we sing of Thine ineffable compassions!

*Glory... Triadicon:*

The Trinity co-essential, the Father, the Word, and the Holy Spirit, One God undivided in essence, we honor as the One Creator and Lord unto ages of ages!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
Thee do we name, O pure one: the impassable Gate, the untilled Field, the Ark that carries the Manna, the Golden Urn,[^672] the Lampstand, and the Censer of the immaterial Coal!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Let not the tempest of water drown me, neither let the deep swallow me up; for I have descended into the depths of the sea of my evils: therefore I cry unto Thee as Jonah: "Let my life be brought up from corruption unto Thee, O God!"

*Troparion:*
Unto the Cross the lawless ones nailed Thee, and with a spear they pierced Thee in the side; noble Joseph buried Thee in a new Tomb, whence having risen with glory, O Savior, Thou didst raise with Thyself all creation that sings of Thy dominion!

*Troparion:*
Powerfully and easily Thou didst shatter the bars and gates of hades, and Thou didst rise as God; and meeting the women Thou didst say: "Rejoice!" and Thou didst send them to say unto the Disciples that Life has risen and has appeared, causing eternal things to bud forth!

*Troparion:*
"Why weep ye? Why carry ye myrrh as for a dead man, O women? Christ is risen!" cried of old the young man shining with a most radiant appearance, having left the graveclothes empty: "Go, announce His rising unto His friends!"

*Troparion:*
Being the ungrudging stream of life, O Lord, and the abyss of mercy, O Compassionate One, Thou didst journey and sit beside the sworn well; and unto the Samaritan woman Thou didst cry aloud: "Give Me water to drink, that thou mayest receive the waters of forgiveness!"

*Glory... Triadicon:*
I sing of the unoriginate Father, the co-enthroned Son, and the co-essential Spirit in faith: one Substance, one Nature, and one glory, and one Kingdom of the God of all, the Creator and Sustainer of all, together with the Bodiless Powers!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
Thee, who alone didst give birth and yet didst preserve thy womb in incorruption, we praise, O pure one: the Throne of the Lord, the Gate, the Mountain, the spiritual Lampstand, the all-radiant Bridal Chamber of God, the Tabernacle of God's glory, the Ark, the Golden Urn, and the Table!

*Fol. 199r / 199*
*Kontakion, Tone 8, Podoben: "As first-fruits of nature" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки):*
Having come with faith unto the Well, the Samaritan woman, beholding Thee the Water of wisdom, O Christ, was satisfied abundantly, and inherited the Kingdom on high unto the ages, as ever-glorious!

*Ikos:*
Let us give ear unto the awesome mysteries, as John teaches us that which took place in Samaria: how the Lord conversed with the woman, having asked for water, He Who gathered the waters into their gatherings; He Who is co-enthroned with the Father and the Spirit, for He came seeking His own image unto the ages, as ever-glorious!

*Synaxarion on the Sunday of the Samaritan Woman. Bless, father, to read:*[^673]
*Verses:* Coming, O woman, to draw corruptible water,
Thou drawest the Living Water that is able to wash the soul.

On this same day, the Fifth Sunday after Pascha, we celebrate the Feast of the Samaritan Woman: because on this day Christ openly confesses Himself to be the Messiah, which is Christ (or the Anointed): for Messiah is in Hebrew, and in Greek it is called Christ. For this reason, it seems to me, this was ordered within the Pentecostarion period. And as on the preceding Sunday He worked wonders at the Pool, so on this Sunday at Jacob's Well Christ converses with the woman: which well Jacob himself dug and gave unto his son Joseph; for this was a choice place, where also near the adjacent Mount Samaria the Samaritans settled many cities. And Christ comes unto Sychar, where of old Jacob dwelt with Dinah his daughter and with his sons: Shechem the son of Hamor having seized her and violated her, therefore her brothers, being provoked unto fierce anger, entered suddenly into the city, killed all the men, and that same Shechem and his father Hamor: for Jacob had pitched his tent there and dug that well. But the Samaritans did not dwell on that mountain at first, but the Israelites; yet when they offended God, their kingdom was invaded first under Pekah, and secondly when the Assyrians came...

...and having come, they subjected them unto tribute; and a little later, when Hoshea was king, he allied himself with the Ethiopians: which the Assyrian King having learned, he deported them unto Babylon, and ordered various heathen nations to settle in Samaria. But God sent lions upon those foreigners; and the King of Assyria, having learned this, sent a priest from Judea unto them (for some were still captive there), that they might receive God's laws and be quickly turned away from idols. Yet they received only the Five Books of Moses, dwelling as it were near Mount Samaria; and they were hated by the Hebrews, who did not allow those from captivity to return unto them, because they practiced a semblance of Judaism and yet would not fall in with them, considering them abominations. Therefore they often called Christ a Samaritan, as One Who seemed to disregard certain provisions of the Law and to have celebrated as they did. The Lord therefore comes unto Sychar, having wearied Himself from the journey, and sat down at the sixth hour of the day. And a certain woman from the city came to draw water, the Disciples having gone away to buy food. Jesus therefore asks water, and she proposes not to associate with Him: for she recognized Him both from His voice and from His clothing, which was of two colors, but He brings a spiritual aspect unto her heart, showing that which is unsparing and purifying; for the Spirit is continually likened unto water and fire. But the woman, persisting, objected that He had no such water, since He carried no vessel and the well was deep; then she also recalled the word concerning forefather Jacob, that he himself dug the well, and he and his cattle drank from it, setting forth the richness of the well from this, and that it was exceedingly useful and cool. Christ does not say that He is greater than Jacob, lest He frighten the woman, but He speaks concerning the water, setting forth its supreme excellence: "Whoever drinks of that water shall never thirst at all." The woman asks for the water, and He tells her to call her husband, as she needed the understanding of deeper words. But she denies having a husband; and He Who knows all things says: "Thou hast spoken well: for thou hast had five husbands, as the Law commands, and he whom thou now hast is the sixth, as it happened unto thee unlawfully; he is not thy husband." Some indeed have supposed the five husbands to be the Five Books of Moses, which the Samaritans accepted, and the sixth to be the words of Christ, which were not yet hers, for grace had not yet been poured out. Others say they are the five laws given by God: in Paradise, after the Expulsion, under Noah, under Abraham, and under Moses; and the sixth is the Gospel, which she did not yet possess. There are also those who say they are the five senses.[^674]

*Fol. 200r / 200*
The woman answers, naming Him a Prophet; then she also questions Him concerning the Mountain, where it is proper to worship: in Samaria, or in Jerusalem? For the Samaritans did not think that God was completely everywhere, but that God dwelt only where they worshipped, on Mount Gerizim, because a blessing from God was given there, or because Abraham first erected an altar unto God there; whereas the Judeans said that it was proper to worship the One God in Jerusalem, therefore they gathered there from everywhere on the feasts. But Jesus answers that salvation is not of the world; yet He said that God is immaterial, and they that are accounted worthy to worship Him shall no longer worship in sacrifices, but in Spirit and Truth, or that they shall know God not as alone, but in the Holy Spirit and the Son, for He is the Truth. The woman says again: "I hear from the Scriptures that the Messiah comes, Who is Christ." And Jesus said: "I am He," having perceived the woman's good character. For the Samaritans also knew concerning the Messiah from the Books of Moses, especially from this: "The Lord God shall raise up a Prophet unto you," and from many other passages. And when the conversation was finished, the Disciples arrive, and are exceedingly amazed at His condescension, how He was speaking with a woman; yet they besought Him to eat, both because He was wearied and because of the burning heat of the time: but He speaks unto them concerning eternal food: the salvation of mankind, and that it is proper for them to reap the prophetic labors. But when the woman reached the city and related the things concerning herself, all were stirred up without deceit, and came unto Christ, having believed that the woman would not have accused herself unless she had understood something great. And they besought Him to abide with them a little while; and they prevailed upon Him to remain two days, and while staying He worked many miracles, which through their multitude were not written in the Gospel. And this Samaritan woman was afterwards named Photina by Christ; she also was adorned with the crown of martyrdom under Nero Caesar, together with her seven sons, after many afflictions, flayings, cutting off of her breasts, breaking of her hands, thrusting of thin reeds under her nails, pouring of molten lead into her mouth, and countless other tormenting trials, being finally tied between two palm trees and torn asunder.[^675] And let it be known: that her Holy Head, that is, her skull, the Holy Emperor Justinian honorably translated unto Constantinople, and placed it as a blessing in the Great Temple of the Church called Sophia; and the Stone upon which Christ sat when He conversed with the Samaritan woman also remains unto this day before the Parvis from the East, as one enters the Temple, on the left side in the pillar...

...healing weekly, especially them that are afflicted with fevers who touch it, and them that are sick with the ague; and it cleanses away fires and sorceries that occur. By the prayers of Thy martyr Photina, O Christ God, have mercy on us, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* Deliver us not up utterly for Thy name's sake, neither break Thou Thy covenant, and take not Thy mercy away from us, O Lord God of our fathers, Who art praised above all unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
Willingly Thou wast numbered among the lawless, O Compassionate Christ, at the time of Thy divine Passion; and seeing this, all the earth was shaken, and the stones split at the Creator's nod, and the dead from the ages were set free without hindrance!

*Troparion:*
With Thy soul Thou didst descend into the nethermost regions of hades, and by Thy manly power Thou didst loose all that were bound, whom bitter tormenting death had seized; and they cried aloud: "Glory to Thee, O God, for Thy fearful dispensation!"

*Troparion:*
"Why seek ye among the dead Him that lives unto the ages? He is risen as He said; and as ye see, the graveclothes are empty and the tomb is vacated!" cried the young man who appeared unto the Myrrh-bearing women: "Go quickly, tell the Apostles!"

*Troparion:*
"Thou art the Water of life!" cried the Samaritan woman unto Christ: "Satisfy me then, O Word, who ever thirst for Thy divine grace, that I may no longer labor, O Lord, held by the mist of ignorance, but may proclaim Thy mighty works!"

*Glory... Triadicon:*
We sing of the Father, the Son, and the Divine Spirit: the undivided Trinity, existing in one nature, distinguished in Persons, One Essence, co-eternal and unoriginate, the Creator and God of all, Whom all the heavenly ranks hymn!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
After thy childbearing in fearful manner, thou wast preserved a pure Virgin, O holy and pious one: therefore all the angelic choirs

*Fol. 201r / 201*
and all the generations of men praise thee with unceasing voices, as the pure dwelling-place of Him Whom nothing can contain!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* By Thy might, O Master, Thou hast ordered all things, and Thou hast established the earth again as Thou knowest, fixing its foundation upon the waters: therefore all of us cry aloud, singing: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, unceasingly!"

*Troparion:*
Willingly Thou didst endure death, O only Immortal One; and having plundered hades, Thou didst shatter the brazen gates, O Heavenly King, and didst snatch away the prisoners who were there from the ages, who unceasingly sing of the dominion of Thy goodness!

*Troparion:*
Willingly Thou wast lifted up upon the Tree, O Long-suffering One, and the rocks were split, the sun was extinguished, the veil of the Temple was rent in twain, the earth was shaken and trembled, hades was despoiled, and all the prisoners were loosed!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst appear, O Lord, unto them that sat in darkness, being the unwaning Light and Life of all: therefore when the assembly of the Righteous beheld Thee, O Word, they danced and cried aloud: "Thou hast come to loose all from bonds, who sing of Thy glory!"

*Troparion:*
Thou didst sit near the Well at the sixth hour, O Savior, and Thou didst grant living water and the waters of understanding unto the Samaritan woman for Thy great compassion's sake: therefore we all sing, crying aloud: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, unceasingly!"

*Let us bless the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord:*
We sing of the unoriginate Father, the co-unoriginate Son, and the Holy Spirit: One God in Trinity, unconfused, undivided, the Creator of all, One Sovereignty, having dominion over all; and we cry aloud: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, unceasingly!"

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
Isaiah was cleansed by a coal, proclaiming the spiritual Coal, Who is incarnate from thee, O pure one, beyond understanding, burning away all the material sins of men and deifying our nature through mercy, O all-immaculate one!

*Then: "We praise, we bless, and we worship the Lord." Heirmos: "By Thy might, O Master..."*
*Or as in the Greek, this is said: We do not sing "More honorable than the Cherubim."*[^676]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
He has shown strength with His arm, for He has cast down the mighty from their thrones, and exalted the humble, the God of Israel, wherein the Dayspring from on high has visited us, and has guided us into the way of peace!

*Troparion:*
Lo, Christ the Life of all was seen willingly hung upon the Tree; and beholding it, the earth was shaken, many bodies of the sleeping saints arose visibly, and the strongholds of hades were shaken!

*Troparion:*
Radiant from the Tomb as a Bridegroom from a bridal chamber Thou didst come forth, O Christ, having destroyed the tyranny of death and shattered the bars of hades by divine power, and by the spiritual light of Thy resurrection Thou didst enlighten the world!

*Troparion:*
Let all of us now assemble spiritual choirs and cry aloud: "The Lord is risen! Let the earth rejoice, let Heaven be glad, let the clouds drop down the rain of righteousness upon us who keep festival radiantly and sing unto Christ!"

*Troparion:*
The Lord, the Life of the living and the Fountain of blessings, richly pouring forth water by His teaching, cries unto the woman: "Give Me water to drink, that I may grant thee water for the deliverance of thy sins!"

*Glory... Triadicon:*
One Light undivided, the Trinity in three suns, the unoriginate Father, Son, and Spirit, the Godhead of one life and the Creator of all: of Him let us the faithful sing with the Bodiless Powers, sacredly praising Him with Thrice-Holy hymns!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
Having become the dwelling-place of the Light, O pure one, illumine the eyes

*Fol. 202r / 202*
of my soul that have been darkened by the many snares of the enemy, and grant me to behold clearly with a pure heart the Light that has shone forth from thee beyond understanding!

*Then: "He has shown strength with His arm..." Or according to the Greek recension: "Holy is the Lord our God" in Tone 4.*

*Exapostilarion of Pascha: "Having fallen asleep in the flesh..." (see fol. 109v).*[^677]

*And another of the Samaritan Woman, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀):*
*Glory...* Unto Samaria Thou didst come, O my almighty Savior, and conversed with the woman, asking water to drink, Thou Who didst bring forth water for the Hebrews from the flinty rock: whom Thou didst bring unto faith, and she now enjoys life in the heavens unto the ages!

*Both now and ever... of Mid-Pentecost:*
"Having a chalice of inexhaustible gifts..." (see Wednesday, fol. 183v).[^678]

*At the Praises, Stichera of the Resurrection, 5; and of the Samaritan Woman, 3. Stichera of the Resurrection, Tone 4:*
O Almighty Lord, Who didst endure crucifixion and death, and hast risen from the dead: we glorify Thy Resurrection!

By Thy Cross, O Christ, Thou hast loosed us from the ancient curse; and by Thy death Thou hast overthrown the devil who tormented our nature; and by Thy rising Thou hast filled all things with joy: therefore we cry aloud unto Thee: O Thou Who hast risen from the dead, Lord, glory to Thee!
