By Thy Cross, O Christ Savior, guide us unto Thy truth, and deliver us from the snares of the enemy, O Thou Who hast risen from the dead; raise us up who have fallen into sin, stretching forth Thy hand, O Lord, Lover of mankind, through the prayers of Thy saints!

Without being separated from the Father's bosom, O only-begotten Word of God, Thou didst come upon earth for Thy love of mankind, having become man without change; and Thou didst endure crucifixion and death in the flesh, being impassible in Thy Godhead:

and having risen from the dead, Thou hast granted immortality unto the human race, as the only Almighty One!

*Anatolian:*
Thou didst accept death in the flesh, mediating immortality for us, O Savior; and Thou didst dwell in the Tomb, that Thou mightest free us from hades, having raised us with Thyself: having suffered indeed as man, but risen as God: therefore we cry aloud: Glory to Thee, O Giver of life, O Lord, only Lover of mankind!

*Other Stichera of the Samaritan Woman, Tone 3:*
The rocks were split, O Savior, when Thy Cross was erected upon Golgotha; the gatekeepers of hades were terrified when Thou wast laid in the Tomb as dead: for Thou didst destroy the power of death; unto all the dead Thou didst grant immortality by Thy Resurrection, O Savior: O Giver of life, Lord, glory to Thee!

*Verse: For the sake of the palace of the kingdom and the new church...*
Let Heaven and Earth rejoice radiantly today, for Christ appeared incarnate as man, that He might deliver Adam from the universal curse; and He is marvelous in wonders, having come to Samaria and stood before the woman, asking water, He Who clothes the clouds with waters: therefore let all of us the faithful bow down before Him Who willingly became poor for our sake by His tender-hearted counsel! *Tone 6. Verse: Blessed art Thou, O Lord...*

Thus says the Lord unto the Samaritan woman: "If thou knewest the gift of God, and Who it is that says unto thee: 'Give Me water to drink,' thou wouldst have asked of Him, and He would have given thee to drink, that thou mightest never thirst unto the ages," says the Lord. *Glory... Tone 6:*

Jesus our Savior, the Life-originating Fountain, came unto the well of the Patriarch Jacob, asking water to drink of the Samaritan woman; and when she objected that the Judeans have no dealings with them, the wise Creator draws her by sweet words rather unto asking for the ever-flowing

*Fol. 203r / 203*
water; and having received this, she proclaimed unto all, saying: "Come, see the Knower of secrets, God Who has come in the flesh to save man!"

*Both now and ever... "Most blessed art thou..." Then the Great Doxology, and the rest according to custom.*

*And the Dismissal. Glory... both now and ever... Gospel Sticheron, Tone 6:*
Behold, it is early and dark; and why standest thou at the Tomb, O Mary, having much darkness in thy mind, wherein thou askest where Jesus has been laid? But behold the Disciples running together: how from the graveclothes and the napkin they discovered the Resurrection, and remembered the Scriptures concerning these things! With them we also, having believed, sing of Thee, Christ the Giver of life!

*Then the Hours, and the Catechesis of St. Theodore the Studite, and the Final Dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy. The Beatitudes from the Canon of the Samaritan Woman, Ode 3; and of Mid-Pentecost, Ode 6. Prokeimenon, Tone 3: Sing praises unto our God, sing praises! [LXX 46:6]. Verse: All ye nations, clap your hands... [LXX 46:1].*

*The Apostle in the Acts, Pericope 28 [Acts 11:19–26, 29–30]. Alleluia, Tone 4: Bend Thy bow and prosper... [LXX 44:5]. Verse: Thou hast loved righteousness... [LXX 44:8]. The Gospel from John, Pericope 12 [John 4:5–42].*[^679] *Instead of "It is truly right," we sing the Heirmos: "He that is wise..." Communion Verse: "The Body of Christ..." Another: "Praise the Lord..."*

*Be it known: that the Service of the Samaritan Woman is sung for four days: on Sunday, Thursday, Friday, and Saturday. On Monday, Tuesday, and Wednesday, the Service of Mid-Pentecost is sung; on Wednesday is its Apodosis. We sing it also on the feast itself, without entrance and readings.*[^680]

## ON THE SAME SUNDAY EVENING

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of Mid-Pentecost, 3; and in the Menaion, for the Saint, 3.*

*Stichera, Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, O container of ascetics" (Ра́дꙋйся по́стникѡвъ):*
Today the radiant and beautiful, all-honored Mid-Pentecost of the Savior's Resurrection, like a radiant midday, overshadowing the world with divine graces of Christ's rising, shining with unnumbered wonders, flashes forth signs, reveals the Ascension on high, and manifests the much-desired Coming of the Spirit, the most radiant festival of all-honored Pentecost: therefore it grants peace and great mercy unto our souls!

Like a river of divine glory, the Lord, the Feast that has reached its midpoint now, granting the stream of tender-heartedness unto all, cries aloud: "Let him that thirsts come unto Me and drink warmly! For being Himself the Fountain of creation and the Sea of mercy, He pours forth forgiveness unto the world, washes away transgressions, cleanses sicknesses, saves them that celebrate His resurrection, covers with love them that honor His ascension with glory, and grants peace and great mercy unto our souls!"

Having stood in the midst of the sanctuary, the Uncircumscribed God and Lord—for He was God by nature, even though for our sake He became man, having mysteriously appeared circumscribed in body—poured forth words of life unto all, saying unto the faithful: "Cleanse your souls, cool the burning heat of passions, lest by any means ye be deprived of the drink, O men; for I grant divine grace, of the immortal and better future Kingdom: let him share with Me the Creator, and he shall be glorified!" *Glory... both now and ever... Tone 6:*

"When the Feast reached its midpoint before the Passion..." (see fol. 187r).[^681]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Resurrection, Tone 4:*
Unceasingly bowing down before Thy life-creating Cross, O Christ God, we glorify Thy three-day Resurrection: for thereby, O Almighty One, Thou hast renewed the corrupted nature of man, and hast renewed for us the ascent unto Heaven, as the only Good One and Lover of mankind! *Verse: God has arisen, and His enemies shall be scattered... [LXX 67:2].*

I desired with tears to wash away the handwriting of my sins, O Lord, and for the rest of my life to please Thee through repentance; but the enemy deceives me and wars against my soul: O Lord, before I perish utterly, save me! *Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us... [LXX 122:3].*

*Martyricon:*
O Christ God, Who art glorified in the memorials of Thy saints: being entreated by them, send down upon us great mercy!

*Fol. 204r / 204*
"When the Feast reached its midpoint, of Thy Resurrection, O Christ, and of the divine Coming of Thy Holy Spirit: having assembled, we sing of the mystery of Thy wonders; wherein send down upon us great mercy!"

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 6:*
*“Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart...” Troparion of the Feast. Litany, and Dismissal.*

## ON MONDAY OF THE FIFTH WEEK AFTER PASCHA. AT MATINS

*At "God is the Lord," Troparion of Mid-Pentecost, thrice.*

*After the First Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
Looking upon the entrance of the Tomb, and unable to endure the angelic flame, the Myrrh-bearing women marvelled with trembling, saying: "Has He been stolen away, Who opened Paradise unto the thief? Or is He risen, Who proclaimed His rising before the Passion? Truly Christ is risen, granting life and resurrection unto them that are in hades!"

Strengthen my humble soul, O Lord, which has spent its whole life in sins; and as the harlot, accept me, O Savior, and save me!

*Martyricon:*
Today the angelic hosts have arrived in memory of the Saint, to enlighten the minds of the faithful and to illuminate the universe with grace: being entreated by them, O God, grant us great mercy!

*Glory... both now and ever... Theotokion:*
Thou art an unconquerable wall unto us Christians, O Virgin Mother of God: for fleeing unto thee we remain unharmed, and sinning again we have thee as our intercessor: therefore giving thanks we cry aloud unto thee: Rejoice, thou that art full of grace, the Lord is with thee!

*After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Събезнача́лное сло́во):*
As Thou didst stand in the Temple when the Feast reached the midpoint of all-honored Pentecost, conversing with the Hebrews, Thou didst clearly rebuke them with boldness, many tormentors boldly, as Thou art King and God: unto all of whom Thou grantest great mercy for Thy tender-heartedness' sake! *Glory... both now and ever... the same.*

*“Having seen the Resurrection of Christ...” Psalm 50 [LXX 50]. Canon of the Feast of Mid-Pentecost; and in the Menaion. Kontakion and Exapostilarion of the Feast.*

*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 4:*
O Almighty Lord, Who didst endure crucifixion and death, and hast risen from the dead: we glorify Thy Resurrection! *(Twice)*

I am a sheep of Thy rational flock, and unto Thee have I fled for refuge, O Good Shepherd: seek me who have gone astray, O God, and have mercy on me!

*Martyricon:*
Who does not marvel, beholding the good contest wherein ye struggled, O holy Martyrs? How, being in the flesh, ye conquered the bodiless enemy, confessing Christ and being armed with the Cross! Therefore ye have worthily been shown forth as expellers of demons and conquerors of barbarians: unceasingly pray that our souls may be saved!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 8: "When the Feast reached its midpoint..." (see fol. 188r).*[^682]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 6, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Е҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^683]
Having bowed the heavens, Thou didst descend, O my Savior, and didst accept flesh from the Virgin without alteration, pouring forth forgiveness unto me! *Verse: But our God is our King before the ages... [LXX 73:12].*

Thou art God, O Word; desiring to take me who was perishing away from sins, Thou didst unite Thyself unto me, O Lover of mankind! *Verse: Remember Thy congregation, which Thou hast purchased of old... [LXX 73:2].*

Thou hast come, O King, the Great Wisdom, wherewith the Father created the world all-wisely together with the Holy Spirit! *Glory... both now and ever... Tone 8:*

Let us cleanse the treasuries of our thoughts, and let us enlighten the possessions of our souls,

*Fol. 205r / 205*
and let us behold Christ the Life, Who has come into the sanctuary, rich in wisdom, that He may conquer the enemy through the Passion of the Cross and the Resurrection, and may save our race; unto Whom let us cry aloud: O Incomprehensible Lord, glory to Thee!

*At the Beatitudes, the Triodion Canon, whose acrostic is: DOXA TO THEO IMON, that is: "Glory to our God." Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos: "The captains of three..."*[^684]
Having bound the enemy's destructive dominion with bonds which Thou didst endure upon the Tree, O Lover of mankind, by Thy Cross Thou didst assemble them that were scattered, O Lord, and didst break down the middle wall of enmity: therefore we sing praises unto Thee!

As He said before the Passion, the Lord has risen with dominion, having plundered the kingdom of hades as God, and raised up all that slept from the ages, who magnify Him!

Having the appearance of visions of a sacrificial minister, the choir of the Myrrh-bearing women was instructed by him: "Lo, Christ is risen! Go, announce it unto the Disciples who weep and lament!"

*Theotokion:*
Without stars, O pure one, as a radiant Bridegroom, He Who was incarnate from thy womb beyond understanding of goodness arose from the dead: therefore seeing Him with the Apostles, thou didst glorify His three-day rising!

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "Unto the Redeemer of all..."*
Willingly stretching forth Thy palms upon the Tree, O Long-suffering One, Thou didst embrace all, and didst deliver them from the hand of the stranger: therefore with fear we glorify Thee!

When Thou wast laid willingly in the Tomb, they that slept of old in the graves arose with Thine arising from the dead, crying aloud in fear: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

Working marvelous and glorious wonders by Thy word, O Christ, the Hebrew assembly, seeing Thee, condemns Thee unto death, Who art the Life of all, O Tender-hearted Lord!

During forty days dwelling with the honored and pure Apostles, O Christ, Thou didst confirm the divine Dispensation, whereby Thou hast saved the world that sings unto Thee!

*Theotokion:*
How awesome is thy childbearing, O Immaculate one, whereby we all were delivered from the fearful fall, crying aloud: Rejoice, O Protectress of all men! Rejoice, O restoration of the fallen world!

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "Eve indeed through the disease..."*
Willingly Thou wast stretched upon the Cross; beholding Thee, O Transcendent Good One, the sun hid its rays in fear, the foundations of the earth were shaken, the rocks split, O Master, and the veil of the Temple was rent in twain!

Joseph of old having anointed Thee and wrapped Thee with the Shroud, laid Thee in a new Tomb, Who art the Life of all; and Thou didst rise on the third day, having despoiled the kingdom of hades by divine power, O Almighty One! *Glory...*

Thou wast seen by the Apostles, having risen from the tomb on the third day, and didst cry aloud: "Go, My friends and Disciples, proclaim among all the nations the wonders that ye have seen, and My power which is beyond word!"

*Theotokion:*
Seeing Him that breathes life into the dead thus risen from the tomb with glory, the All-immaculate one was filled with joy, crying aloud: "My Son and my God, I magnify Thy dispensation which is beyond word!"

## ON THE SAME MONDAY EVENING

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, 3; and in the Menaion, 3. Stichera of the Feast, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Нб҃ныхъ чино́въ):*[^685]

*Fol. 206r / 206*
The Creator of all and Giver of life, the Word co-everlasting with the Divine Father, willingly having accepted flesh from the Virgin and become man, poured forth teachings of ineffable wisdom unto all as God!

When the Judean feast reached its midpoint, O Christ, Thou didst stand in the sanctuary, by the Father's authority as Master of the Law, rebuking the Scribes, as it is written, and astonishing them by the wisdom of Thy words and the manifestations of Thy wonders!

The Giver of wisdom and Dispenser of blessings, pouring forth divine rivers from an inexhaustible fountain, cries aloud: "Come unto Me, ye that thirst, draw the water of life; and rivers of divine gifts shall flow from your belly," He says!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 4:*
When Thou didst come into the Sanctuary, O Christ God, as the Feast reached its midpoint, then Thou didst teach the people, crying aloud: "He that believes in Me, even if he die, shall live!" But the Judeans raged together with the Pharisees, Sadducees, and Scribes, saying: "Who is this that speaks blasphemies?" Not considering that Thou art our God, Who art before the ages, glorified with the Father and the Spirit: glory to Thee!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4:*
Thou didst loose the condemnation of Eve's disobedience, O Savior, being willingly nailed unto the Tree of the Cross; and descending into hades, O Mighty One, Thou didst tear asunder the bonds of death as God: therefore we bow down before Thy resurrection from the dead, crying aloud in joy: O Almighty Lord, glory to Thee! *Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes... [LXX 122:1].*

Who that is tempest-tossed and flees unto this haven is not saved? Or who that is sick and falls down before this dispensary does not receive healing...

...healing? O Creator of all things and Physician of the ailing, O Lord, before I perish utterly unto the end, save me!

*Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us... [LXX 122:3].*

*Martyricon:* Having accepted the endurance of the holy Martyrs, accept also the singing from us, O Lover of mankind, and through their prayers grant us great mercy!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 3:*
When the Feast reached its midpoint, we glorify Him that wrought salvation in the midst of the earth: for in the midst of two thieves Life was suspended upon the Tree; and unto him that blasphemed He was silent, but unto him that believed He cried aloud: "Today shalt thou be with Me in Paradise!" Thou didst descend into the Tomb, Thou didst plunder hades, and Thou didst rise on the third day, saving our souls!

## ON TUESDAY OF THE FIFTH WEEK AFTER PASCHA, AT MATINS

*At "God is the Lord," Troparion of Mid-Pentecost, thrice.*

*After the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
By Thine own voluntary will Thou didst endure the Crucifixion, O Savior, and mortal men laid Thee in a new tomb, Who by Thy word didst establish the ends of the earth: therefore the stranger was bound, death was fiercely despoiled, and all those in hades cried aloud unto Thy life-bearing resurrection: "Christ is risen, the Giver of life, abiding unto the ages!"

*Sessional Hymn of Compunction:* Consider, O my soul, how we shall stand before the Judge! For the fearful thrones shall be set, and the deeds of each one shall be exposed: then the Judge shall be inexorable; there fire shall stand fearfully, encompassing as a fierce sea and covering the ends of the earth: therefore, O soul, despising all these things, be vigilant before the end!

*Martyricon:* Thy Church, having adorned herself with the blood of Thy Martyrs in all the world as with purple and fine linen, through them cries aloud unto Thee, O Christ God: send down Thy compassions upon Thy people, grant peace unto Thy commonwealth, and unto our souls great mercy!

*Glory... both now and ever... Theotokion:*
An awesome mystery and a most glorious wonder: how the Virgin bore Thee, the Creator of all things, and after childbirth remained whole and a Virgin again! May God, born of her, establish the nations, tame the peoples, and bring peace unto the world, as the Lover of mankind!

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8: "The water of wisdom and life..." (see fol. 288)[^686]. And the customary readings there.*

*At the Praises, Stichera, 4, Tone 4:*
By Thy Cross, O Christ, Thou hast delivered us from the ancient curse; and by Thy Death Thou hast abolished the devil that tormented our nature; and by Thy rising Thou hast filled all things with joy: therefore we cry aloud unto Thee: O Lord risen from the dead, glory to Thee! *(Twice)*

Wash me with my tears, O Savior, for I am defiled with many sins: therefore I fall down before Thee, O Merciful One: I have sinned, have mercy on me, O God!

*Martyricon:* Ye became partakers with the Angels, O holy Martyrs, manfully proclaiming Christ before the judgment seat: for ye left all the fair things in the world as if they were not, and held fast unto God as a steadfast anchor: therefore, having driven away deception, ye pour forth gifts of healing unto the faithful, unceasingly praying that our souls be saved!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 4:*
When Thou didst come into the Sanctuary, O Christ God... (see fol. 206)[^687].

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 6, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Е҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
Being the end and the beginning, when the Feast reached its midpoint, Thou didst stand in the sanctuary, O my Christ, pouring forth forgiveness unto me!

*Verse: Remember Thy congregation... [LXX 73:2].*

Hearing the divine dominion of Thy resurrection, O Word, Zion rejoices with her children, singing unto Thee, the Tender-hearted One!

*Verse: But God is our King before the ages... [LXX 73:12].*

A Light from the Father, the Word, we sing; but in the latter times Thou didst appear as man, granting me the forgiveness of sins!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 8:*
When the feast of Pascha reached its midpoint... (see fol. 188)[^688].

*At the Beatitudes, Triodion, with this acrostic: NOETICALLY BY JOSEPH, that is, Song of Joseph.*[^689]

*Tone 4. Ode 2. Heirmos: "See, see..."*

*Troparion:* Moses lifted up the serpent, prefiguring of old Thy Crucifixion in the Sinai desert; and by being crucified upon the Tree, Thou didst deliver men from the serpentine enmity and assault, and didst crush the destructive head!

Bearing myrrh with tears, the Myrrh-bearing women ran with haste very early unto the divine Tomb, as the God-proclaimer said; and seeing a young man gleaming in the appearance of his vesture, they were terrified with fear!

The Hebrew people condemned the Word to die—He that heals diseases by a word, visits the wretched paralytics, and raises the dead, O Word! But Thou didst rise as God, mighty in strength, having destroyed the kingdom of hades!

*Theotokion:* He that is transcendent God appeared incarnate, O all-immaculate one, ineffably from thy blood; and having become man, He Who is Life was numbered among the dead, and arose with dominion: seeing Him, thou wast filled with joy, magnifying Him!

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "Into the blazing fire..."*

*Troparion:* Having torn asunder the handwriting of Adam by the Cross, O Lord, Thou didst pour forth salvation unto us, having willingly become a curse, O Christ: therefore we bless Thee continually unto the ages!

The company of the Disciples beheld Thee risen from the dead, O Lord, and was filled with mystical consolation, unto whom Thou didst cry aloud: "Seeing God, the Giver of life, go and proclaim Me, the King of all!"

We sing Thy precious Crucifixion, O Jesus Tender-hearted; we glorify Thy Burial and Thy fearful Resurrection, whereby we have been loosed from the insoluble bonds of hades, O Christ God of all!

*Theotokion:* Seeing Him Whom thou didst bear beyond word, O pure and Blessed one, risen from the dead, thou didst cry aloud: "How fair is Thy beauty, O my Son, for the sake of which Adam has now ascended unto his primal beauty!"

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "For He that is mighty has done..."*

When Thou wast lifted up upon the Tree, being nailed unto the Cross, the luminaries hid their light, the earth quaked, and those in the bonds of hades were delivered, O Savior!

Having shattered the bars and bonds of death, O Christ, Thou didst rise with glory in dominion, having snatched away the captives whom hades formerly held, as they sang of Thee as God!

*Glory...* The earth keeps triumph, and heaven keeps festival at the Arising of the Creator of all, whereby, O Savior, Thou hast delivered from bonds and corruption those who sing of Him!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
By the beams of thy light, O Mother of God, dispel the mist of my ignorance, that with voices of faith I may unceasingly sing of thy marvelous wonders!

*On this Tuesday evening, the entire service of the Feast of Mid-Pentecost: see as...*

*...as written for the Feast (see fol. 273)[^690], without the Paroemias and the Entrance. Likewise on Wednesday at Matins: the entire order of the Feast without change. And at the Beatitudes, Ode 4 of both Canons.*

*And the Feast of Mid-Pentecost is concluded (Apodosis). The service of the saint of the day is sung at Compline.*

## ON THE SAME WEDNESDAY OF THE FIFTH WEEK AFTER PASCHA, AT VESPERS

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Samaritan Woman, 3; and in the Menaion for the saint, 3. Stichera of the Samaritan Woman, Tone 4, Podoben: "Being called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*
Having found the Samaritan woman at the well, O Christ, Thou didst ask water to drink of her, thirsting for her amendment and drink, which was prefigured and fulfilled in deeds: for drawing drink, she is drawn away from drinking, and is filled with life-giving drink, announcing unto her fellow citizens: "Lo, Christ the Messiah, spoken of of old in the Law, has appeared as man, revealing the unutterable secrets of thoughts!"

"Lo, Christ the Messiah who is on earth has appeared in the city," says the Samaritan woman, "He Who was written to come, Who told me my deeds, the hidden things in the depth of my heart; all things that I did He told me!" Therefore the citizens ran together, and seeing her words true in all respects, and marveling at the matter, they were confirmed by the sight!

The daughter of the ancestral mother again beheld Thee, God Who of old didst walk at the sixth hour as Master in Paradise, sitting thus at the sixth hour where the spring of water and well was dripping, and asking to drink of her, that Thou mightest give her to drink the water of incorruptible life, which indeed came to pass: for having drunk of Thy life-bearing water, she proclaimed in the city the wealth of the water!

*And in the Menaion, 3. Glory... both now and ever... Tone 4:*
Thou didst come unto the well, O Wellspring of wonders, at the sixth hour, to catch the fruit of Eve: for at that hour Eve went forth from Paradise through the serpent's deception; for the Samaritan woman drew near to draw water, seeing whom the Savior said: "Give Me water to drink, and I will fill thee with flowing water!" And running into the city, the chaste woman immediately announced unto the multitudes: "Come, see Christ the Lord, the Savior of our souls!"

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4:*
Thou didst shatter the gates of hades, O Lord, and by Thy Death Thou didst destroy the kingdom of death, and didst deliver the human race from corruption, granting life and incorruption unto the world, and great mercy! *Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes... [LXX 122:1].*

Again today, by the Holy Spirit, Thou hast enlightened, O Christ; and through them wash away our sinful defilement, O God, and have mercy on us! *Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us... [LXX 122:3].*

*Martyricon:* Having boldness toward the Savior, O holy ones, unceasingly through your prayers on behalf of us sinners, ask forgiveness of sins, and for our souls great mercy!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 6:*
When the Lord came unto the well, the Samaritan woman besought the Merciful One: "Grant me the water of faith, and I shall receive the joy and deliverance of the font of water!" O Giver of life, O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Troparion of the Resurrection, and Dismissal.*

*Note: When the Key of the Paschalion reaches 4, until here the commemoration of St. George extends.*[^691]

## ON THURSDAY OF THE FIFTH WEEK AFTER PASCHA, AT MATINS

*At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Resurrection, Tone 2; and Theotokion.*

*After the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йся на Крⷭ҇тъ во́лею):*
Having been wounded willingly for our sake, and numbered among the dead, O Giver of life, and having risen on the third day, O Christ our God, Thou didst shatter the dominion of death by Thy power, making alive by Thy rising all those in hades; singing praises, all of us honor Thy rising, O Immortal Lord!

*Apostolic:* Thou hast shown forth Thy Disciples as luminaries unto the ends of the earth, O Christ, shining through Thy preaching the enlightenment of our souls; and having darkened the idol's deception by them, by disciples of piety Thou hast enlightened the world: through their prayers save our souls!

*Martyricon:* Armed with the Cross, Thy passion-bearers overcame the wiles of the evil-originating enemy, O Christ our God, and shone forth as luminaries, guiding men; they pour forth healings unto those who ask in faith: through their prayers save our souls!

*Glory... both now and ever... Theotokion:*
O all-immaculate Virgin, who didst bear the most good God, unceasingly pray Him with the Apostles to grant us before the end forgiveness of sins and amendment of life, who sing of thee with faith and love as is due, O only all-praised one!

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "O Hope of the world" (Ѡ҆пова́ні́е мі́рꙋ):*
As unto the faithful and marvelous Samaritan woman, O Lord, Thou didst open the wellspring of Thy divinity, pouring out upon her divine understanding; therefore she fell not into fainting through acceptable drinking, from which now also give us all to drink the forgiveness of sins, O Most Good One! *Glory... both now and ever... the same.*

*The Resurrection of Christ... Psalm 50. Canon of the Samaritan Woman, and in the Menaion. Kontakion, Ikos, and Exapostilarion of the Samaritan Woman.*

*At the Praises, Stichera, 4, Tone 4:*
Without separating from Thy Father's bosom, O Only-begotten Word of God, Thou didst come upon earth through love of mankind, having become unchangeably man, and didst endure the Crucifixion and Death in the flesh, being impassible in Thy Godhead; and having risen from the dead, Thou hast granted immortality unto the human race, as the only Almighty One! *(Twice)*

Thy Holy Spirit showed forth the illiterate Disciples as teachers, O Christ God, and through the multi-voiced utterance of tongues abolished deception, as the Almighty One!

*Martyricon:* How shall we not marvel at your contest, O holy Martyrs? For being clothed in mortal flesh, ye conquered the bodiless enemies; the torturers' threats did not frighten you, nor did the infliction of torments terrify you: truly ye are worthily glorified by Christ; unceasingly pray that our souls be saved!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 6:*
The co-unoriginate and co-everlasting Son... (see fol. 294)[^692].

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́бля):*
Being the Wellspring of grace and the Abyss of life, O only Merciful One, how didst Thou cry aloud, conversing with the Samaritan woman: "Give Me water to drink, that thou mayest receive forgiveness!" extolling Thy great tender-heartedness, whereby having saved all such a race, through Thy great goodness! *Verse: Draw Thy bow, and proceed prosperously, and reign for the sake of truth... [LXX 44:5].*

"Come and see a Man Who told me today...

...sitting at the well, all things that I have done! He is truly a Prophet, and great, God Who knows both hidden and revealed things!" Unto Him let us cry aloud: Grant unto those who thirst the water of life, which Thou hast, that they may not thirst again unto the ages, O Lover of mankind!

*Verse: Thou hast loved righteousness, and hated iniquity... [LXX 44:8].*

O ever-existing Wellspring, immortal Life, inexhaustible and incorruptible Stream, having journeyed close unto the well as unto a soul, when Thou didst send the Disciples for food, Thou didst converse with the woman drawing water, desiring to catch her and bring her unto the Father, as the only One unto the ages!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 3:*
Let heaven and earth rejoice today... at the Liturgy (see fol. 202)[^693].

*At the Beatitudes, Triodion, Tone 4.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos: "For the sake of love, O Tender-hearted One..."*

*Troparion:* When Thou wast lifted up willingly upon the Tree, both the earth, and the mountains and hills shook with trembling, seeing Thee suffering, O Tender-hearted and Long-suffering One!

Hades, fearing Thy taking down, O Savior, quickly surrendered the dead whom it held, who sang of Thine ineffable power!

The senseless assembly of the Hebrews, seeing Him that opened the blind eyes, put to death Him Who is the common Life and Resurrection!

*Theotokion:* Seeing Thy Son risen from the Tomb, O Virgin, with His Apostles thou didst rejoice, O pure one, with whom we glorify thee!

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "All ye works of God..."*

Seeing Thee upon the Cross, the wise thief cried aloud: "Remember me, O Master!" marveling at Thy power and at Thy fearful Dispensation which is beyond understanding!

Thou didst appear unto the women, O Christ, saying "Rejoice!" clearly as the Lover of mankind, thus assuaging grief and making men joyful by Thy rising!

Of old Thou didst grant light unto the man blind from birth, O Jesus Giver of light: so also open the pupils of my heart, to understand the light of Thy commandments!

*Theotokion:* Deliver me from the fire of Gehenna and from eternal condemnation, O pure one, having quieted the bestial surge of my thoughts, and guide me unto the quiet haven of life!

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "A stone cut without hands..."*

Thou didst stretch forth Thy palms upon the Tree, healing the soul-corrupting harm of those who first stretched forth their hand for the sake of the tree, O Master: therefore we glorify Thy incomprehensible dispensation!

The gates and bars of death were shattered by Thy descent, O Savior; by Thy power Thou didst despoil the kingdoms of hades, and having risen on the third day, Thou hast enlightened the universe!

*Glory...* Having appeared unto Thy holy Apostles after the Resurrection, O Savior, Thou didst say: "Go throughout the world, proclaim with faith the fearful and ineffable mysteries that ye have seen in Me!"

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
Thou didst behold Christ risen, O Mother of God, with the Myrrh-bearing women, and having turned weeping into joy, thou didst glorify Him: therefore with a loud voice we magnify thee!

## ON THE SAME THURSDAY EVENING

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Samaritan Woman, 3, Tone 4, Podoben: "Being called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*
"Behold Him of Whom Moses wrote beforehand in the Law that He should come, Jesus the Messiah, Who has appeared upon the earth for the sake of tender-heartedness and conversed with me as man at the well! Truly this is Christ Who comes unto me...

...Who comes unto me, who was in Sychar," the Samaritan woman persuades them, saying:

From the sweet mouth of the woman pouring forth rivers of sweet water, those in the city drinking, hastened to run quickly unto the well pouring forth an abundance of water, and to see the ever-flowing Wellspring that revives languishing souls!

Beholding the profitable Wellspring and the One resembling the human constitution, those in the city cried aloud unto the woman: "No longer do we believe because of thy word, knowing in truth that this is the Deliverance and eternal Salvation of the world!"

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 6:*
At the well of Jacob... see on folio 203[^694].

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4:*
Always having Thy Cross for help, O Christ, we easily trample upon the snares of the enemy! *Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes... [LXX 122:1].*

When Thou didst ascend the Cross, O Lord, Thou didst break our ancestral curse; and descending into hades, Thou didst liberate those bound from the ages, granting incorruption unto the human race: therefore singing we glorify Thy life-creating and saving resurrection! *Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us... [LXX 122:3].*

*Martyricon:* Living sacrifices, rational whole-burnt offerings, the Lord's Martyrs, perfect divine victims, sheep who know God and are known of God, whose fold is inaccessible to wolves: pray that we also may be saved with you beside the water of rest!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 6:*
Thus says the Lord unto the Samaritan woman: "If thou hadst known the gift of God, and Who it is that says unto thee: 'Give Me water to drink,' thou wouldst have asked of Him, and He would have given thee to drink, that thou shouldst never thirst unto the ages," says the Lord!

## ON FRIDAY OF THE FIFTH WEEK AFTER PASCHA, AT MATINS

*At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Resurrection, Tone 6; and Theotokion.*

*After the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
The Judeans nailed Thee unto the Cross, O Savior, whereby Thou hast delivered us from our enemies, O Christ our God, having stretched out Thy hands upon it by Thine own will; and by a spear Thou didst receive piercing in Thy side: glory to the multitude of Thy compassions, O Lover of mankind!

*Martyricon:* Thy Martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings received incorruptible crowns from Thee, our God: having Thy strength, they cast down the tormentors, and crushed the feeble insolence of demons: through their prayers save our souls!

*Glory... both now and ever... Theotokion:*
When Thine all-pure Mother, O Word of God, saw Thee lifted up upon the Cross, lamenting maternally she said: "What is this new and strange wonder, O my Son? How dost Thou, the Life of all, taste death, desiring to make alive those that have died, as the Merciful One?"

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
Coming according to custom to draw water from the earthly and corruptible fountain, the Samaritan woman drew living water, having thus found the Wellspring sitting upon the well which Jacob dug, pouring forth coolness upon me who am parched with flame! *Glory... both now and ever... the same.*

*We sing the Canon of the Samaritan Woman, to 8; and in the Menaion, to 4.*

*At the Praises, Stichera, 4, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ е҆сѝ зна́меніе):*
Thou hast given a sign unto those who fear Thee, O Lord: Thy precious Cross, whereby Thou hast conquered the principalities of darkness and powers...

...and powers, and hast lifted us up unto the primal beatitude: therefore we glorify Thy dispensation loving toward mankind, O Almighty Jesus, Savior of our souls!

By Thy Cross, O Christ Savior, guide us unto Thy truth, and deliver us from the snares of the enemy; O Thou Who didst rise from the dead, raise us up who have fallen into sin, stretching forth Thy hand, O Lord Lover of mankind, through the prayers of Thy Saints!

*Martyricon:* Precious is the death of Thy Saints, O Lord: for by sword and fire, and crushed deceit, they poured forth their blood, having hope in Thee to receive the reward of their labors; they endured and received from Thee, O Savior, great mercy!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 6:*
Jesus our Savior, the Wellspring of life, came unto the well of the Patriarch Jacob, asking water to drink of the Samaritan woman; but when she answered that the Jews have no dealings with them, the wise Creator led her with sweet words rather to ask for ever-living water, which having received, she proclaimed unto all, saying: "Come, see the Knower of secrets and God Who came in the flesh to save man!"

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́бля):*
Coming to draw with a bucket at the well, leaving the water-bucket, the Samaritan woman ran alone unto the city, crying aloud: "I have found the inexhaustible living-flowing Spring, whence she drew water to drink and cooled her heart which was languishing with passions!" *Verse: Draw Thy bow, and proceed prosperously, and reign for the sake of truth... [LXX 44:5].*

"In truth hast Thou spoken," said the Savior unto the Samaritan woman, "that thou hast no lawful husband today: for thou hadst five previously, and now he whom thou hast acquired is not thy husband, but a stranger to the Law!" Therefore, making haste, she cried unto those in the city, crying aloud: "I have seen a Man Who told me all things that I did in secret!" *Verse: Thou hast loved righteousness, and hated iniquity... [LXX 44:8].*

The Samaritan woman drew a stream mystically living-flowing from the incorruptible Wellspring, finding Him flowing at the well, whence she was accustomed to draw corruptible water; but He showed forth entering into her heart as another new Wellspring, bedewing the burning heat of passions!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 8:*
When Thou didst appear in the flesh, O Christ God... (see fol. 295)[^695].

*At the Beatitudes, Triodion, with this Acrostic: GLORY TO GOD, AMEN (ДО́ХА ТѠ ѲЕѠ А҆МИ́Н).*[^696]

*Tone 4. Ode 5. Heirmos: "All things marvelled..."*

*Troparion:* Bound, O Master, and nailed unto the Tree, Thou hast loosed me from the bonds of sin, and hast nailed me unto Thy love: therefore thankfully I sing of Thy majesty and ineffable power!

Seeing Thee in hades, O Savior, Adam who was condemned of old was loosed from condemnation and from long-aged bonds, crying aloud: "Glory to Thy great goodness, O Word, O Jesus Most Merciful!"

Clearly the Angel appears as a woman, sitting inside the tomb, and cried aloud: "Why have ye come early to seek? Christ is risen, having put death to death!"

*Theotokion:* O all-immaculate one, who didst bear the pre-eternal Holy One resting in the saints: seeing Him risen from the tomb as a bridegroom from his bridal chamber, thou didst magnify His descent with songs!

*Ode 8. Heirmos: "The pious youths in the furnace..."*

*Troparion:* For our sake, O Lover of mankind, Thou didst stretch forth Thy palms upon the Tree, being nailed, and pierced in Thy most pure side, healing the nature darkened in Adam: therefore praise His great tender-heartedness unto all ages!

When hades below saw Thee plundering its kingdoms, which of old it swallowed up: being deadened with fear, it surrendered all the dead, who cried aloud unto the Word: "Praise the Lord, ye works, and exalt Him supreme unto the ages!"

Unto the weeping Myrrh-bearing women, the Angel sitting upon the stone cried aloud: "Run, announce unto the Disciples the rising of the Giver of life: for He is truly risen, having overthrown the tyranny of hades and crushed the malice of the devil!"

*Glory... Triadicon:* One glory by Essence, in three Persons proclaim the one Godhead: the pre-unoriginate Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, crying aloud always: "Praise the Lord, ye works, and exalt Him supreme unto the ages!"

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
As God appeared from thee, O Virgin, without breaking the signs of virginity, so He arose, leaving the seals of the stone unshaken: seeing Him, thou wast filled with joy, glorifying His divine power unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos: "Without seed..."*

Ascending on high unto the height of the Cross, willingly, O Long-suffering One, Thou didst draw man from the pit of destruction unto the light; and having been laid in the tomb, our Life, O Christ, Thou didst raise up those sleeping in the tombs by Thy precious and glorious rising!

The choir of women brought myrrh unto Thee, the Author of life, and having seen Thee risen, they ran to announce the rising unto the Disciples assembled together and weeping, who, seeing Thee, were filled with enlightenment and joy!

*Glory...* Being in hades with Thy soul as God, O Sun Christ, Thou didst cause Thy light to shine upon those sleeping there, bound from the ages, and didst raise the saints by Thy rising, who sing of Thy goodness!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
Thou didst bear as an infant God Who upholds all, having become man through tender-heartedness, O thou who art full of grace: earnestly pray Him to enlighten our hearts, who truly bless thee as the pure Mother of God!

## ON THE SAME FRIDAY EVENING

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Samaritan Woman, 3, Tone 4, Idiomelon (Са́могла́сенъ):*
Thou didst come unto the well, O Wellspring of wonders, at the sixth hour, to catch the fruit of Eve: for at that hour Eve went forth from Paradise through the serpent's deception; for the Samaritan woman drew near to draw water, seeing whom the Savior said: "Give Me water to drink, and I will fill thee with flowing water!" And running into the city, the chaste woman immediately announced unto the multitudes: "Come, see Christ the Lord, the Savior of our souls!"

When the Lord came unto the well, the Samaritan woman besought the Merciful One: "Grant me the water of faith, and I shall receive the joy and deliverance of the font of water!" O Giver of life, O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Tone 3:*
The co-unoriginate and co-everlasting Son and Word of the Father came unto the well, the Wellspring of healings; and a woman from Samaria came to draw water, seeing whom the Savior said: "Give Me water to drink, and go, call thy husband!...

..."call thy husband!" But she, speaking as unto a man and not unto God, striving to conceal herself, said: "I have no husband." And the Teacher said unto her: "Truly hast thou spoken, 'I have no husband': for thou hadst five, and now he whom thou hast is not thy husband." But she, marveling at the word and running into the city, cried aloud unto the multitude, saying: "Come, see Christ, Who grants great mercy unto the world!"

*Glory... Tone 6:*
At Jacob's well Jesus found the Samaritan woman, and asks water of her, Who covers the heaven with clouds. O wonder! He Who is unseen by the Cherubim converses with a strange woman, asking water, Who suspended the earth upon the waters; seeking water, Who pours forth springs and lakes of water; desiring to draw her who was of old snared by the adversary enemy, and to give her to drink of the living water, who was sorely burned in disorders: as the Tender-hearted One and Lover of mankind!

*Both now and ever... Dogmatic Theotokion, Tone 4: "He Who for thy sake..." (see fol. 103)[^697].*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Resurrection, Tone 4:*
Unceasingly bowing down before Thy life-creating Cross, O Christ God, we glorify Thy three-day resurrection: for thereby, O Almighty One, Thou hast renewed the corrupted human nature, and hast renewed for us the entrance into heaven, as the only Good One and Lover of mankind! *Verse: The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty... [LXX 92:1].*

Thou didst loose the condemnation of Eve's disobedience, O Savior, being willingly nailed unto the Tree of the Cross; and descending into hades, O Mighty One, Thou didst tear asunder the bonds of death as God: therefore we bow down before Thy resurrection from the dead, crying aloud in joy: O Almighty Lord, glory to Thee! *Verse: For He has established the world, which shall not be shaken [LXX 92:1].*

Thou didst shatter the gates of hades, O Lord, and by Thy death Thou didst destroy the kingdom of death, and didst deliver the human race from corruption, granting life and incorruption unto the world, and great mercy! *Verse: Holiness becomes Thy house, O Lord, unto length of days [LXX 92:5].*

As of old upon the Cross, Thou didst break our ancestral curse; and descending into hades, Thou didst liberate the eternal prisoners, granting incorruption unto the human race: therefore singing we glorify Thy life-creating and saving resurrection!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 8:*
When Thou didst appear in the flesh, O Christ God... (see fol. 295)[^698].

*Then, "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart..." and Dismissal.*

## ON SATURDAY OF THE FIFTH WEEK AFTER PASCHA, AT MATINS

*At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Resurrection, Tone 2; and Theotokion.*

*After the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymn of the Resurrection, Tone 4:*
Looking upon the entrance of the Tomb, and unable to endure the angelic flame, the Myrrh-bearing women wondered with trembling, saying: "Has He been stolen away, Who opened Paradise unto the thief? Has He risen, Who before the Passion proclaimed His rising? Truly Christ is risen, granting life and resurrection unto those in hades!"

*Glory... both now and ever... Theotokion:*
The mystery hidden from the ages and unknown unto the Angels, through thee, O Mother of God, was revealed unto those on earth: God incarnate in unconfused union, and willingly accepting the Cross for our sake, whereby having resurrected the First-formed, He saved our souls from death!

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йся на Крⷭ҇тъ во́лею):*
Let heaven rejoice, let earthly things leap for joy, for Christ appeared from the Virgin as man, and delivered all mankind from corruption by His death, shining with wonders: unto the Samaritan woman asking water, He gives the Wellspring of healings, as the only Immortal One! *Glory... both now and ever... the same.*

*Canon of the Samaritan Woman, to 6; and of the saint whose temple it is, to 4; and in the Menaion, to 4.*

*At the Praises, Stichera of the Resurrection, 4, Tone 4:*
Having endured the Crucifixion and Death, and risen from the dead, O Almighty Lord, we glorify Thy Resurrection!

By Thy Cross, O Christ, Thou hast delivered us from the ancient curse; and by Thy Death Thou hast abolished the devil that tormented our nature; and by Thy rising Thou hast filled all things with joy: therefore we cry aloud unto Thee: O Lord risen from the dead, glory to Thee!

By Thy Cross, O Christ Savior, guide us unto Thy truth, and deliver us from the snares of the enemy; O Thou Who didst rise from the dead, raise us up who have fallen into sin, stretching forth Thy hand, O Lord Lover of mankind, through the prayers of Thy Saints!

Without separating from Thy Father's bosom, O Only-begotten Word of God, Thou didst come upon earth through love of mankind, having become unchangeable man, and didst endure the Crucifixion and Death in the flesh, being impassible in Thy Godhead; and having risen from the dead, Thou hast granted immortality unto the human race, as the only Almighty One!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 6:*
Jesus our Savior, the Wellspring of life, came unto the well of the Patriarch Jacob, asking water to drink of the Samaritan woman; but when she answered that the Jews have no dealings with them, the wise Creator attracts her with sweet words, rather to ask for ever-living water, which having received, she proclaimed unto all, saying: "Come, see the Knower of secrets and God Who came in the flesh to save man!"

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́бля):*
The woman beheld the ever-flowing Wellspring sitting upon the well; unable to endure the heat of many intolerable passions, she asked to receive the water of life poured forth from Him, which she also received from the word alone, and no longer hastens unto an earthly and corruptible fountain! *Verse: Draw Thy bow, and proceed prosperously, and reign for the sake of truth... [LXX 44:5].*

When the woman pursued Jewish customs, alleging the cause of untouchability in partaking of water, O Christ, the wise Creator, having drawn near, with sweet utterances led her to ask for this divine living-flowing water, which having drunk, she went unto the city, announcing what had taken place! *Verse: Thou hast loved righteousness, and hated iniquity... [LXX 44:8].*

"Thou hast neither bucket, and the well is deep: whence then wilt thou give me incorruptible water?" the Samaritan woman cried aloud unto Thee, O Christ, beholding Thee as man and not as God, and marveling at Thy words; Whom, having given to drink with sweet utterances, Thou didst cause to confess Thee as God and Master of creation!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 3:*
Let heaven and earth rejoice today, for Christ... (see fol. 202)[^699].

Christ appeared incarnate as man, that He might deliver Adam from the ancestral curse, and He is wondered at for His miracles in Samaria; and having stood before the woman, He converses, asking water, Who covers the clouds with waters. Therefore let us all, the faithful, bow down before Him Who willingly became poor for our sake by His tender-hearted counsel!

*At the Beatitudes, the Four-Ode Canon. Tone 4:*
*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
"I will sacrifice unto Thee with a voice of thanksgiving, O Lord," the Church cries aloud unto Thee, having been cleansed from the demonic gore by the Blood that flowed through tender mercy from Thy side!

*Troparion:*
Stretching forth Thy pure palms upon the Cross, Thou didst crush the mental Amalek by Thy divine power, and hast delivered the world from servitude, O Christ our God!

*Troparion:*
Having shattered hades by Thy fearful descent, O Christ, and having snatched away by divine power the prisoners whom it held, Thou didst arise as God from the tomb on the third day!

*Troparion:*
When the women came unto the tomb to anoint Thee with priceless myrrh, the Angel cried aloud unto them: "Weep not! For the Lord Whom ye seek is risen!"

*Theotokion:*
No longer weep, O Virgin Mother of God, for thy Son is risen as God from the tomb on the third day; but rejoice and pray unto Him that we may be saved!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
"Unto those in the furnace of the Persian children, love of piety rather than flame was burning:" they cried aloud: "Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!"

*Troparion:*
Having been suspended upon the Cross on Golgotha for our sake in the midst of robbers, Thou hast abolished the violence of the power of darkness: O Almighty Lord, God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Pierced in the ribs with a spear, O Giver of life, Thou didst pour forth unto us from the ever-flowing fountain of Thy divine Blood, O Christ, unto those who cry: "All-hymned Lord, God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!"

*Troparion:*
The Angel shining in vesture cried aloud unto the weeping women: "Why seek ye with fragrant spices as one dead Christ Who has power over life? He is risen as He said before the Passion!"

*Glory... both now and ever... Theotokion:*
He Who holds all creation and the ends of the earth took flesh from thee, O pure one, and was pleased to die for our sake; beholding Whom risen with glory from the tomb, rejoice!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
"Having stretched forth his hands, Daniel shut the gaping mouths of the lions in the pit; and the youth lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the fiery power, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*Troparion:*
By Thy wound, O Master, Thou hast healed all mankind, O Savior; and having endured death, Thou hast delivered it from corruption by Thy death, O Good One, and hast made us immortal, who cry aloud: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, sing and super-exalt Him unto the ages!"

*Troparion:*
Rejoice, all ye peoples; let all earthly and heavenly things rejoice now: for the Lord, the Giver of life, is risen from the tomb, having raised up our nature together with Himself, which cries: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, sing and super-exalt Him unto the ages!"

*Troparion:*
Having bound the tormentor, O Christ, by Thy descent, and having raised up the dead laid in the tomb as God, who sweetly sing of Thee, the Benefactor of all and Almighty: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, sing and super-exalt Him unto the ages!"

*Theotokion:*
Desiring, O Pure One, to loose the condemnation of Adam who fell in Eden, the Almighty Lord was incarnate of thee, O Most-pure One; and having been crucified and buried, He arose: save us who sing of thee in love, O Mary, Bride of God!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
"Eve indeed, through the disease of disobedience, brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Mother of God, through the Bud of thy childbearing, didst make blessing blossom forth unto the world: therefore with the choirs of Angels we magnify thee!"

*Troparion:*
By Thy lifting up upon the Cross, O Long-suffering One, the veil was rent, and the sun hid its rays; and the rocks being split showed forth the unjust slaughter which Thou didst willingly endure, O Savior, for the human race!

*Troparion:*
Pierced with a spear on the Tree, O Jesus Lover of mankind, Thou didst heal Adam who was wounded by transgression, O Long-suffering One; and being laid among the dead, as the Immortal One Thou didst lead forth all who sing of Thee!

*Troparion:*
Holding all things in Thy palm, and ordering the course of the sun by Thy nod, how wast Thou numbered among the dead, being Thyself immortal, desiring to deliver those who were wounded by the serpent's bite? We sing of Thee as the Lover of mankind!

*Theotokion:*
Beholding Thy Son dead and naked hanging upon the Tree, thou didst suffer pain; but immediately seeing Him risen on the third day, rejoice, O all-immaculate one, as Mother, and pray for those who honor thee in faith!

*Then, the Prokeimenon and Alleluia of the day. The Epistle in the Acts, Pericope 37: "In those days, Paul and Barnabas..." [Acts 15:35-41][^700]. The Gospel from John, Pericope 38: "The Lord said unto the Jews who came unto Him: My sheep hear My voice..." [John 10:27-38][^701]. Communion Hymn of the day.*

## SIXTH SUNDAY AFTER PASCHA: SUNDAY OF THE BLIND MAN

*On Saturday evening, at Small Vespers. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Resurrection, 4, Tone 5:*
*By Thy precious Cross, O Christ... Thou hast granted resurrection unto the human race... A great wonder: the Maker of things unseen... An evening adoration do we bring unto Thee...*
*Glory... Tone 5:*
*O Lord, passing by of old upon the way... (All these see at Great Vespers)[^702].*
*Both now and ever... Theotokion: the present Dogmaticon, Tone 5:*
Let us honor the divinely-fitting and pure Virgin, who is more honorable than the Cherubim; for the Maker of all, desiring to become incarnate, took up His dwelling within her ineffably. O strange matters and most glorious mysteries! Who would not marvel, hearing this: that God becomes man, and there was no alteration in Him; and having opened the virgin doors, no diminishing was left in her, even as the Prophet says: "A man shall never pass through it, save only the Lord God of Israel, Who has great mercy!" [Ezekiel 44:2] *At the Aposticha, Sticheron of the Resurrection. Then the present Stichera of the most pure Mother of God, Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, [of the ascetics]" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ по́стникѡ́мъ):*
*Verse: I will remember Thy name in every generation and generation [LXX 44:18].*
Rejoice, seal of the Prophets, preaching of the divinely-speaking Apostles! For thou didst bear for us, beyond mind and word, Him Who is truly God incarnate; through Whom having received our ancient noble birth, and attained the delights of Paradise, we magnify thee with songs as the cause of such radiant brightness: and being enriched with thee, O All-pure One, as an acceptable intercessor, to receive the attained attainment of life from thy Son, Who richly grants great mercy!

*Verse: Hear, O daughter, and see, and incline thine ear [LXX 44:11].*
Rejoice, rational Heaven, in which God was pleased to become incarnate! Vessel of divine Manna, Candlestick of the Sun, overshadowing Mountain of God, Bridal-chamber receptive of God, Table that gives life, Golden Lampstand, Light-bearing Paradise, unconsumed Bush, Ark of holiness, Heavenly Ladder, living Cloud, Rod from the root of Jesse that blossomed, O Virgin: pray unto Christ that great mercy may be given unto our souls!

*Verse: The rich among the people shall entreat thy countenance [LXX 44:13].*
Rejoice, all-immaculate one, who didst ineffably conceive the Son of God, and bear Him in the flesh, Who for our sake was truly taken from thy blood, having a rational and sovereign soul, and inescapably clothed Himself in Adam through mercy and ineffable goodness; whence in two natures He is manifested unto us, showing forth the operation of both in Himself: pray unto Him, O Christ, that great mercy may be given unto those who hymn thee!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 8:*
O Christ God, spiritual Sun of righteousness, Who by Thy most pure touch didst illuminate from both sides him who from the womb was deprived of light, and having enlightened our spiritual eyes, show us to be sons of the day, that in faith we may cry aloud unto Thee: Great and ineffable is Thy tender-hearted compassion toward us, O Lover of mankind: glory to Thee!

*At Great Vespers. We recite the Kathisma, "Blessed is the man," the entire Kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses, and sing Stichera of the Resurrection, Tone 5:*
*Verse 10: Bring my soul out of prison, that I may give thanks unto Thy name [LXX 141:8].*
By Thy precious Cross, O Christ, Thou hast put the devil to shame; and by Thy resurrection Thou hast blunted the sting of sin, and hast saved us from the gates of death: we glorify Thee, O Only-begotten One!

*Verse 9: The righteous shall wait for me, until Thou shalt recompense me [LXX 141:9].*
Thou didst grant resurrection unto the human race, led as a sheep unto the slaughter; the princes of hades were affrighted at Him, and the gates of sorrow were lifted up: for Christ, the King of glory, entered in, saying unto the prisoners: "Go forth! And ye that are in darkness, show yourselves!"

*Verse 8: Out of the depths have I cried unto Thee, O Lord; Lord, hear my voice [LXX 129:1-2].*
A great wonder! The Maker of things unseen, having suffered in the flesh through love of mankind, is risen immortal: come, families of the nations, let us bow down before Him! For by His tender mercy being delivered from deception, we have learned to hymn one God in three Hypostases!

*Other Stichera by Anatolios. Verse 7: Let Thine ears be attentive to the voice of my supplication [LXX 129:2].*
An evening adoration do we bring unto Thee, the never-setting Light, Who at the end of the ages, as in a mirror, didst shine forth in the flesh upon the world, and didst descend even unto hades, and shatter the darkness there...

...and having shown Thy resurrection unto the nations, O Giver of light, O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Verse 6: If Thou, Lord, shouldest mark iniquities, O Lord, who shall stand? For with Thee there is forgiveness [LXX 129:3-4].*
We glorify Christ, the Author of our salvation; for when He was risen from the dead, the world was saved from deception: the choir of Angels rejoices, demonic deception flees, fallen Adam arises, and the devil is abolished!

*Verse 5: For Thy name's sake have I waited for Thee, O Lord; my soul has waited for Thy word, my soul has hoped in the Lord [LXX 129:5-6].*
Those who were instructed by the guard, O lawless ones: "Hide Christ's resurrection, and receive pieces of silver, and say: While we were asleep, the dead man was stolen from the tomb!" Who ever saw, who ever heard of a dead body stolen, especially anointed and naked, having left his burial garments in the tomb? Be not deceived, O Jews: learn from the prophetic utterances, and understand that He is truly the Redeemer of the world and Almighty!

*Verse 4: From the morning watch until night, from the morning watch let Israel hope in the Lord [LXX 129:6].*
O Lord, Who didst despoil hades and trample down death, our Savior, and didst enlighten the world by the precious Cross, have mercy on us!

*Stichera of the Blind Man, Idiomelon, Tone 2:*
*Verse 3: For with the Lord there is mercy, and with Him is plenteous redemption, and He shall redeem Israel from all his iniquities [LXX 129:7-8].*
The man born blind reflected in his mind: "Was I born without eyes because of the sins of my parents? Or was I born for the reproof of the unbelief of the nations? I have no wisdom to ask when it is night, when it is day; my feet cannot endure the stumbling against stones, for I have not seen the sun shining, nor Him Who created me in His image. But I pray Thee, O Christ God, look down upon me and have mercy on me!"

*Verse 2: Praise the Lord, all ye nations; praise Him, all ye peoples [LXX 116:1].*
*And again the same Sticheron. Or see at the Litiya.*

*Verse 1: For His mercy is confirmed upon us, and the truth of the Lord endures unto the ages [LXX 116:2].*
Passing by from the temple, Jesus found a man blind from his birth, and taking pity, He put clay upon his eyes, and said unto him: "Go, wash in Siloam!" And having washed, he received his sight, sending up glory unto God. And his neighbors said unto him: "Who opened thy pupils, which none of those who see was able to heal?" And crying out, he said: "A man called Jesus, He said unto me: Wash in Siloam! and I received my sight. Truly He is the One of Whom Moses spoke in the Law, Christ the Messiah: He is the Savior of our souls!"

*Glory... Tone 5:*
O Lord, passing by of old upon the way, Thou didst find a man blind from his birth; and the disciples, being astonished, questioned Thee, saying: "Rabbi, who sinned, this man or his parents, that he was born blind?" But Thou, my Savior, didst cry out unto them: "Neither has this man sinned, nor his parents, but that the works of God should be made manifest in him. I must work the works of Him Who sent Me...

...which no man can work." And having said this, spitting on the ground and making clay, Thou didst anoint his eyes, saying unto him: "Go, wash in the pool of Siloam!" And having washed, he was made whole and cried aloud unto Thee: "I believe, O Lord!" and bowed down before Thee. Therefore we also cry aloud: Have mercy on us!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion: "In the Red Sea..." (see fol. 193)[^703].*

*At the Litiya, Stichera of the temple saint. Glory... Tone 4 [If there be no Vigil, we sing this at "Lord, I have cried"]:[^704]*
Considering his life as night, the blind man cried aloud unto Thee: "O Lord, open my pupils, O Son of David, our Savior, that with all I also may hymn Thy power!"

*Both now and ever... Theotokion, alphabetical, Tone 4:*
Grant consolation unto Thy servants, O all-immaculate one, quelling the fierce assaults against us, exchanging all our afflictions: for thee alone do we hold as a firm and sure foundation, and we have acquired thy protection; let us not be put to shame, O Mother of God, who call upon thee! Hasten unto the supplication of those who faithfully cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Mother of God, helper of all, joy, protection, and salvation of our souls!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Resurrection, Tone 5:*
Thee, the incarnate Savior Christ, Who wast not separated from heaven, we magnify with voices of song; for Thou didst accept the Cross and death for our race, as the Lord Lover of mankind; and having shattered the gates of hades, Thou didst arise on the third day, saving our souls!

*If thou wilt, sing the Stichera of Pascha with their verses; but if not, then these:*
*Stichera in alphabetical order. Verse: The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty [LXX 92:1].*
From Thy pierced ribs, O Giver of life, Thou didst pour forth drops of forgiveness, of life and salvation unto all; and in the flesh Thou didst accept death, granting us immortality; and having taken up Thy dwelling in the tomb, Thou hast freed us, resurrecting us together with Thyself gloriously as God: therefore we cry aloud: O Lord Lover of mankind, glory to Thee!

*Verse: For He has established the world, which shall not be shaken [LXX 92:1].*
Strange is Thy crucifixion and Thy descent into hades, O Lover of mankind; for having despoiled it, and with Thyself resurrected the ancient prisoners gloriously as God, Thou didst open Paradise and make him worthy to enter it: therefore unto us also who glorify Thy three-day resurrection, grant cleansing of sins, making us worthy to dwell in Paradise, as the only Compassionate One!

*Verse: Holiness becomes Thy house, O Lord, unto length of days [LXX 92:5].*
Thou Who for our sake didst accept passion in the flesh, and didst rise from the dead on the third day, heal our bodily passions, and raise us up from grievous transgressions, O Lover of mankind, and save us!

*Glory... Tone 8:*
O Christ God, spiritual Sun of righteousness, Who by Thy most pure touch didst illuminate from both sides him who from the womb was deprived of light, and having enlightened our spiritual eyes, show us to be sons of the day, that in faith we may cry aloud unto Thee: Great and ineffable is Thy tender-hearted compassion toward us: O Lover of mankind, glory to Thee!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion, Tone 8:*
O unwedded Virgin, who didst ineffably conceive God in the flesh, Mother of God Most High: accept the supplications of thy servants, O all-immaculate one, granting unto all purification of transgressions; receiving now our entreaties, pray that we all may be saved!

*Then, "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart..." Trisagion. At the Blessing of the Loaves, Troparion: "O Virgin Mother of God, rejoice..." thrice. And reading in the Acts of the Holy Apostles. And for the saint of the day, the service is sung at Small Compline.*

*If the Paschalion Key 30 ([Л]) occurs, then see the commemoration of the Holy Apostle John the Theologian on this present Sunday. And it is sung together as directed on the Sunday of the Holy Myrrh-bearers. Look on folios 234 and 235.[^705]*

*If there be no Vigil, then the Canon at the Midnight Office of the Life-creating Trinity, whose Acrostic, that is, the margin-heading, is: "The Fifth Canon unto the Thrice-radiant Light." Ode 1, Tone 5. Heirmos: "The horse and the rider:" (see in the Octoechos)[^706].*

*On the Sixth Sunday after Pascha, at Matins. At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Resurrection, Tone 5:*
Unto the Word co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit, born of the Virgin for our salvation, let us the faithful sing praises and bow down; for He was pleased to ascend the Cross in the flesh, and to endure death, and to raise the dead by His glorious Resurrection! *Glory... both now and ever... Theotokion:*

Rejoice, impassable gate of the Lord! Rejoice, wall and protection of those who flee unto thee! Rejoice, haven untroubled by storms, and unwedded one who didst bear in the flesh thy Creator and God: cease not to pray for those who hymn and bow down before thy childbearing!

*After the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymns of the Resurrection, Tone 5:*
Let us praise the Cross of the Lord, let us honor His holy Burial with hymns, and let us super-glorify His Resurrection: for as God He has raised up the dead from the tombs, having despoiled the dominion of death and the might of the devil, and has shone forth light unto those in hades!

O Lord, Thou wast called dead, Who didst put death to death; Thou wast laid in the tomb, Who didst empty the tombs; above, soldiers guarded the tomb; below, Thou didst raise up the dead from all ages, O Almighty and Incomprehensible Lord, glory to Thee! *Glory... both now and ever... Theotokion:*

Rejoice, holy Mountain and divinely-trodden! Rejoice, living Bush and unconsumed! Rejoice, only Bridge to God for the world, leading the dead unto eternal life! Rejoice, incorruptible Maiden, who without knowing man didst give birth unto the salvation of our souls!

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5:*
O Lord, the lawless ones nailed Thee condemned in their midst, and pierced Thy rib with a spear, O Merciful One; for Thou didst accept burial, whereby having broken the gates of hades, Thou didst rise on the third day: the women ran having seen Thee to announce the Resurrection unto the Apostles, O super-exalted Savior, Whom the Angels hymn: O blessed Lord, glory to Thee!

By Thy marvelous Mysteries, O my Savior, salvation came unto the world: for having risen from the tomb in a manner fitting God, Thou didst raise up with Thyself the corrupted as God: O Life of all, O Lord, glory to Thee! *Glory... both now and ever... Theotokion:*

O unwedded Mother, pure Mother of God, who didst turn Eve's grief into joy: we the faithful hymn thee and bow down before thee, for thou hast brought us out from the ancient curse; and now pray unceasingly, O all-praised, all-immaculate one, that we may be saved!

*And reading of the Commentary on the Gospel from John, Homily 56, 57, or 58.[^707]*

*After the Evlogitaria ("Blessed art Thou, O Lord..."), the Choir of Angels, Hypakoe:*
Astonished in mind by the angelic vision, and enlightened in soul by the divine resurrection, the Myrrh-bearing women announced good tidings unto the Apostles: "Proclaim among the nations the Resurrection, the Lord working together with wonders, granting great mercy unto us!"

*Gradual. Tone 5. Antiphon 1:*
When I am in affliction, I sing unto Thee, O my Savior, like David: Deliver my soul from a deceitful tongue [LXX 119:1-2].

Blessed is the life of the dwellers in the wilderness, who are winged by divine desire.

By the Holy Spirit all things are held together, both visible and invisible: for being sovereign, He is truly One of the Trinity.

*Antiphon 2:*
Let us lift ourselves up spiritually unto the mountains; go thither, whence help comes [LXX 120:1].

Let Thy right hand, stretched out unto me also, O Christ, preserve me from all deceit.

Unto the Holy Spirit, speaking of God, let us say: Thou art God, Life, Desire, Light, Understanding; Thou art Goodness, Thou reignest unto the ages!

*Antiphon 3:*
Over those who said unto me, "Let us go into the courts of the Lord," being filled with much joy, I send up prayers [LXX 121:1].

In the house of David fearful things are accomplished: for there is a fire consuming every defiled mind.

Unto the Holy Spirit belongs sovereign dignity: from Whom every living creature is animated, even as from the Father together with the Word.

*Prokeimenon:*
*Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up, for Thou reignest forever [LXX 9:33]. Verse: I will confess Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart [LXX 9:2].*

*Then, "Let every breath praise the Lord..." The Gospel from John, Pericope 64, the 8th Resurrection Gospel: "At that time, Mary stood by the tomb..." [John 20:11-18][^708]. Then, "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ..." thrice. Psalm 50.*

*The Canon of Pascha, with its Theotokia, to 8 (written on the Sunday of the Myrrh-bearers, see fol. 232)[^709]. And of the Blind Man, to 6.*

*Canon of the Blind Man. Tone 5. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
"Upon the earth on which the sun never shone nor looked down, the abyss which the bare expanse of heaven never saw: Israel passed through dry-shod, O Lord, and Thou didst lead him into Thy holy mountain, singing and chanting a song of victory!"

*Troparion:*
Having willingly accepted the Crucifixion in the flesh, O Savior, Thou didst pour forth blessing and life unto the world, O Master, the only Most-blessed Creator of all: therefore we bless Thee, we sing and glorify Thee, chanting a song of victory!

*Troparion:*
In the lowest tomb noble Joseph laid Thee dead, O Christ, and rolled a stone unto the doors of the tomb, O Long-suffering One; but Thou didst arise, O Glorious One, and hast raised up the world with Thyself, who sing and chant a song of victory!

*Troparion:*
"Why do ye bring myrrh with tears?" the Angel of the apparition said unto the honorable women; "Christ is risen! Run, tell the God-beholding disciples who weep and lament, that they may leap and dance brightly!"

*Troparion:*
Working most glorious wonders, O Savior, Thou didst heal the man blind from his birth, having anointed his eyes with clay, and saying: "Go, wash in Siloam, that thou mayest know Me as God walking upon earth, bearing flesh through tender-hearted mercy!"

*Glory...*
The faithful, honoring the one Tri-hypostatic Essence, glorify the Father and the Son, and the upright Spirit, the Creator and Lord and Redeemer of all, the one uncreated God, crying aloud with the Bodiless Hosts: "Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O King!"

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
In thy womb that knew not wedlock, O pure one, the Lord took up His dwelling through tender-hearted mercy, desiring to save man who was corrupted by the enemy's wiles: Him do thou entreat to save this flock from every captivity and invasion of nations!

*Katavasia:*
"Unto God the Savior, Who guided the people through the sea with dry feet, and drowned the whole host of Pharaoh: unto Him alone let us sing, for He has been glorified!"

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
"Establish my wavering heart, O Lord, tossed by the waves of this life, guiding it unto a quiet haven as God!"

*Troparion:*
Thou hast established the hearts of those that waver, having shaken all the earth, O Long-suffering One, by Thy precious Crucifixion which Thou didst endure in the flesh!

*Troparion:*
In a new tomb noble Joseph laid Thee, O Merciful One; and having risen from the dead on the third day, Thou hast renewed us!

*Troparion:*
"Why seek ye the Lord as dead? He is risen as He said!" the Angel, shining with the aspect of divine lightning, said unto the women.

*Troparion:*
Having healed the blind man who once came unto Thee, blind from his birth...

...Thou didst heal him, O Merciful One, who glorifies Thy dispensation and wonders!

*Glory...*
We bow down before God the Father, pre-unoriginate, and the Son, and the Divine Spirit, the Creative Nature, the Tri-hypostatic One God of all!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
From the virginal womb thou didst bear God incarnate; Him do thou entreat, O all-pure Mother of God, to have mercy on us!

*Katavasia:*
"By the power of Thy Cross, O Christ, establish my mind to sing and glorify Thy saving Ascension!"

*Kontakion of Pascha: "Though Thou didst descend into the tomb..." (see fol. 173)[^710].*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of wisdom the Guide" (Премꙋ́дрости наста́вниче):*
Passing by, the Master and Creator of all found a blind man sitting upon the way, weeping and saying: "I have not seen the sun shining in my life, nor the beams of the moon illuminating: therefore I cry aloud unto Thee: O Thou Who wast born of the Virgin, Who dost enlighten all things, enlighten me as the Tender-hearted One, that falling down I may cry unto Thee: O Master Christ God, grant remission of sins unto us, through the multitude of Thy mercy, O Lover of mankind!" *Glory... both now and ever... the same. And reading concerning the Blind Man.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
"I have heard Thy report, O Lord, and I was afraid; I understood Thy dispensation, and I glorified Thee, O Lover of mankind!"

*Troparion:*
Having stretched Thyself upon the Tree, O my Savior, Who art Life, Thou didst revive me who was slain by the tree, through Thy great mercy: therefore, O Word, I glorify Thee!

*Troparion:*
Assembling gloriously with the disciples, O Lord, Thou didst say: "Go, preach My Resurrection everywhere!"

*Troparion:*
Assuring those that love Thee of Thy rising from the tomb, O Christ, Thou didst abide with them for many days, creating joy for them!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst give eyes unto the blind man born without sight, saying: "Go, wash and see, for My glory is the glory of God!"

*Glory...*
O uncreated, unoriginate Trinity, indivisible in essence, yet divided in Hypostases: save all who faithfully glorify Thee in fear!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
We glorify thy childbearing, O pure one, which is beyond nature, faithfully blessing thee, O all-immaculate one, as the Mother of the God of all!

*Katavasia:*
"I have heard the report of the power of Thy Cross, that Paradise was opened thereby, and I cried aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!"

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
"Anticipating, have mercy on my wretched soul that wars in the night with the darkness of passions, and shine forth, O spiritual Sun, leaving bright days unto me, that the night may be illuminated like light!"

*Troparion:*
Thou didst ascend the Tree, O Merciful One, and didst lift up all thereby, and didst slay the warring serpent, and revive the creation of Thy hands, as the only God of all!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast laid in the tomb willingly, O Immortal King, and didst despoil all the kingdoms of hades, raising up the dead by Thy resurrection!

*Troparion:*
When the lawless people slew Thee on earth, O Word, Who hadst wrought great wonders: yet Thou Thyself, O Lord, the only Almighty Son, as Thou didst foretell, didst also raise the dead, O Christ!

*Troparion:*
Having opened the eyes of him who had not seen the light with sensory pupils, Thou didst enlighten the eyes of his soul, and didst cause him to glorify Thee, having recognized Thee as the Creator, Who didst appear as man through mercy!

*Glory...*
In unity of Trinity, and in Trinity a Unity, we the faithful glorify the Father and the Son and the upright Spirit, the One God, the Maker of all in truth!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
How didst thou give birth, O Virgin full of grace, pure Mother who knewest not man at all? How dost thou nourish Him Who nourishes all creation, even as He alone knows, the Creator and God of all?

*Katavasia:*
"Rising early, we cry aloud unto Thee: O Lord, save us, for Thou art our God, besides Thee we know no other!"

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
"Even as Thou didst deliver the Prophet from the beast, O Lord, so also bring me up, I pray, from the depth of unclean passions, that I may look again toward Thy holy temple!"

*Troparion:*
