Crucified with robbers, O Master, Thou didst deliver from the evil robbers—the soul-corrupting passions—O Lord Lover of mankind, all who harmonize in singing of Thy crucifixion and resurrection!

*Troparion:*
They laid Thee breathless, dead, O Christ, in the tomb, Who dost breathe life into all the dead; but having risen, O Lord, Thou didst empty all the tombs by Thy divine power, O Word!

*Troparion:*
After the Resurrection, O Christ, Thou didst say unto Thy friends: "Sit ye in Jerusalem until ye be clothed with power from on high, invincible and certain help!"

*Troparion:*
Having made clay, Thou didst anoint the eyes of the man blind from his birth, and didst grant him to receive sight, who sings unto Thee, O Word, Thy ineffable power, for the sake of which Thou hast saved the world!

*Glory...*
O Tri-hypostatic Unity, Father unbegotten, Son begotten, and Spirit proceeding: Thrice-holy Lord, One Essence and Power, save all Thy people!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
Who can tell thy greatness, O pure one? For supernaturally thou didst bear God in the flesh, Who through thee delivers the world from every sin, O all-immaculate Virgin!

*Katavasia:*
"The abyss compassed me, the whale became my tomb; but I cried aloud unto Thee, the Lover of mankind, and Thy right hand saved me, O Lord!"

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou didst appear today" (Ꙗ҆ви́ся дне́сь):*
Blinded in the eyes of my soul, I come unto Thee, O Christ, as the man blind from his birth, crying unto Thee in repentance: Thou art the most radiant Light of those in darkness!

*Ikos:*
Grant me a stream of ineffable wisdom, O Christ, and understanding from on high, O Light of those in darkness, and Guide of those that are deceived, that I may be able to recount what the divine Book of the Gospel of peace has taught: that is, the wonderworking of the blind man; for being blind from birth, he receives both sensory and spiritual eyes, crying out in faith: "Thou art the most radiant Light of those in darkness!" And the Prologue.

*Synaxarion on the Sunday of the Blind Man:*
*Verses:*
{ Bestower of light, Who art Light from Light:
{ From birth a blind man Thou dost make to see today, O Word! }

On this same day, the Sixth Sunday after Pascha, we celebrate the miracle of our Lord God and Savior Jesus Christ wrought upon the man blind from his birth; for this also is an illumination, even as was that concerning the Samaritan woman and the Paralytic. It came to pass on this wise: As Christ was conversing with the Jews, showing Himself equal to the Father and saying, "Before Abraham was, I am" [John 8:58], they cast stones at Him; and going forth from thence, He finds a blind man stumbling. Now he was so from his birth, having only the form and hollows of eyes. The Savior having found him in this state, is asked by His disciples, who had heard concerning the Paralytic: "Behold, thou art made whole; sin no more!" [John 5:14], and that the sin of parents falls upon the children: "Rabbi, who sinned, this man or his parents, that he was born blind?" [John 9:2]. In another way they also held a certain Epicurean opinion: that souls pre-exist, and having sinned bodilessly, are brought into bodies.[^711] Removing all these notions, Christ said: "Not for these reasons, but that the works of God might be made manifest" [John 9:3]—that is, Mine. For the word was not concerning the Father, and "that" is of result, not of cause. And having said this, Christ spat upon the ground, and made clay, and anointed the place of his eyes, and commands him to go wash in the pool of Siloam, that He might show that He is the One Who in the beginning took dust from the earth and fashioned man; and since He creates this which is the most lordly part in the body among the ignorant, showing that He is the One Who gives motion also to the soul's power. And He did not anoint with water, but with spittle, that it might be known that all grace was from the spittle of His mouth, and that He wished to send him to Siloam. And He commands him to wash, lest anyone attribute the healing to the earth and clay of that place. He sends him to Siloam that there might be many witnesses of the healing; for many met him as he went, having his eyes anointed with clay. Some say that having washed, he did not wash away the mud that was from the spittle, but that very mud, by the application of the moist matter, was transformed into the structure of the eyes.

Now Siloam is interpreted "Sent" [John 9:7]: for that pool was outside the city of Jerusalem. In the days of Hezekiah, when enemies besieged the city and occupied Siloam, the water was suddenly diverted from thence; previously, they had dug a cistern and a conduit within to receive the waters. And he who was sent thither by the command of the Prophet Isaiah, the water again went forth and was drawn; but if any of the enemies wished to draw, the springs of waters were withheld; and so it was from that time. Therefore, that Christ might show that He also is from God, on this account He sends the blind man thither, and the blind man immediately obeys. Some indeed thought that "Siloam" is interpreted "Sent" because of this blind man who was sent by Christ. The blind man, having washed, receives sight by a certain ineffable power, without even the sufferer himself knowing the mystery. And his neighbors and those otherwise acquainted with him, seeing him seeing well, were unbelieving; but he confessed that he was formerly blind, and being questioned concerning the cause of his receiving sight, he preached Christ the Healer of passions. The Pharisees, hearing of this most glorious sign, again blaspheme the Savior, saying that He keeps not the Sabbath. For it was on the Sabbath that what was done unto the blind man was wrought: therefore a division arises among them, some saying that Jesus is from God because of the wonders performed, and others that He is not from God because He keeps not the Sabbath. Those who held a good opinion of Him question the blind man: "What sayest thou of Him?" And he proclaims Him a Prophet, which was considered more honorable by some. But they again disbelieve that Christ granted healing unto one who was blind; therefore they summon his parents, not believing the witnesses, wishing rather to conceal it; but they make it yet more manifest, the parents confessing all, although they were afraid of being put out of the synagogue, referring the deeds unto his age. And again they say unto the blind man: "Give glory to God, for the healing is from Him, and not from Christ; for He is a sinner, being a breaker of the Sabbath!" But he, wishing to show by deeds that He is God, said: "I know not this; one thing I know, that whereas I was blind, now I see!" [John 9:25]. Again they said unto him: "How did He open thine eyes?" But he, being weary, speaks not in detail, but summarily: that if He were not from God, He could not have done such a sign; and first he is reviled by them as being an examiner and having become His disciple, and because he said that no one has ever opened the eyes of one born blind: for others indeed enlightened the blind, but one born blind not a single one! Having mocked him, they cast him far out of the synagogue. After these things Jesus finds him, and says unto him: "Dost thou believe in the Son of God?" And he, having learned Who He is, bowed down before Him and becomes His disciple, confessing His beneficence [John 9:35-38]. This can also be interpreted anagogically, that is, mystically: The blind man is the people from the Gentiles, whom Christ, passing by, found in their dwelling on earth instead of heaven; or because He came for the Hebrew people, but passing by, He came also unto the Gentiles. And having spat upon the ground and made clay, He anointed: instead of teaching, and before that, because He came down as a drop upon earth and was incarnate of the Holy Virgin. Then also He delivered Divine Baptism, which is Siloam.[^712] Thereafter the Christian people from the Gentiles dare all things for Christ, being persecuted, and bear witness, and afterwards were preached and glorified by Him. By Thine unsearchable mercy, O Giver of light, Christ our God, have mercy on us, now and ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
"The prayer of the youths quenched the fire, the furnace dew-dropping became a divine workshop of a wonder: neither burning nor consuming those who hymn the God of our fathers!"

*Troparion:*
When Thou wast lifted up upon the Tree, O Savior, the sun was extinguished, the earth was shaken, creation quaked, and from the tombs the dead arose!

*Troparion:*
When Thou didst arise from the dead, O King, the souls of those that slept there arose together, glorifying Thy power, for the sake of which the bonds of death were loosed, O Christ!

*Troparion:*
Early in the morning the women's choir came to anoint Thee; and having learned that Thou, O Lord, wast risen, they rejoiced with the holy disciples: for whose sake grant us purification from evil!

*Troparion:*
With clay Thou didst anoint the eyes of the blind man, and didst command him to go to Siloam; and having washed, he received his sight, hymning Thee, O Christ, King of all!

*Glory...*
The Father unoriginate, and the Son co-unoriginate, and the Holy Spirit do we hymn: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God, King of all!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
After childbirth thou wast revealed a virgin, O pure one; for thou didst bear God...

...Who newly intersected nature, O all-pure one, by His own power; entreat Him to save us all!

*Katavasia:*
"He Who saved the children that chanted praise in the fiery furnace, blessed is the God of our fathers!"

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
"Ye angelic choirs and human nature, the incarnate King and Creator of all: ye priests, sing praise; ye people, bless and highly exalt Him unto all the ages!"

*Troparia:*
The angelic ranks, seeing Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Christ, King of all, and all creation changed by fear, were astonished, hymning Thy love for mankind!

Hades below, seeing Thee, groaned, and swiftly surrendered the dead held there from the ages, O Christ, who sing of Thy love for mankind!

Having wrought fearful wonders, O Christ, Thou wast willingly lifted up upon the Cross and joined with the dead, having put hades to death and of old delivered all from bonds!

When the blind man approached Thee, O Christ, Thou didst command him to go unto the pool of Siloam, and to wash and receive sight, and to proclaim Thee as God manifested in the flesh for the salvation of the world!

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord:*
O indivisible Trinity, unchangeable Unity, God of men and Creator of all: save Thy chanters and those who faithfully bow down before Thy dominion from every assault!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
O all-pure Virgin Mother of God, ever entreat thy Son that He put me not to shame in the day of judgment, but number me among the chosen sheep!

*We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord, singing and exalting Him above all unto all the ages.*

*Katavasia:*
"Him Who was begotten of the Father before the ages as Son and God, and in the latter times was incarnate of the Virgin Mother: ye priests, sing praise; ye people, highly exalt Him unto all the ages!"[^713]

*(In the margin: As on the Octoechos, we do not sing.)*[^714]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
"For He that is mighty hath magnified thee [LXX Luke 1:49], O Virgin, having revealed thee pure after childbirth, as having borne without seed her own Creator: therefore we magnify thee as the Mother of God!"

*Troparia:*
Nailed unto the Tree of the Cross, O Christ God, having appeared, Thou didst conquer the opposing powers of the enemy and didst abolish the ancestral curse, O Savior: therefore according to duty we magnify Thee!

When hades below saw Thee, O Word, in the abyss, it grew deaf and released all the dead thence, who recognized the dominion of Thy power: together with them, according to duty, we magnify Thee!

Beholding Him Who wrought signs and fearful wonders, the Jewish assembly, out of envy, [denied Him] Who despoiled hades by His Resurrection and raises up all as the Mighty One!

Thou didst arise from the dead, as Thou saidst, O Giver of life, and didst appear unto the holy disciples after the Resurrection, having wrought signs and enlightened the blind: together with them we magnify Thee unto the ages!

*Glory...*
I honor the Light of the Father; I glorify the Light of the Son; and the Light that is the upright Spirit: one indivisible Light, understood in three Persons, God and King of all creation!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
Revealed broader than the heavens, O pure Virgin, thou didst contain in the flesh the uncircumscribed God, and didst bear Him for the redemption of all who hymn thee with undoubting faith!

*Katavasia:*
"Thee who beyond mind and word art the Mother of God, who in time didst ineffably bear the Timeless One: we the faithful magnify thee with hymns with one accord!"

*Exapostilarion: "Having fallen asleep in the flesh..." (see fol. 176)[^715].*

*Another, of the Blind Man, Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены оуслышите):*

Blinded in the eyes of my mind, O Lord, do Thou enlighten me from dark sin, mercifully implanting humility, and wash me with tears of repentance!

*Glory... both now and ever...:*
Passing by, our Savior found a man blind from his eyes; spitting upon the ground and making clay, He anointed him, sending him to Siloam to go and wash; and having washed, he came seeing Thy light, O my Christ!

*At the Praises, Stichera on 8. Tone 5:*

*Sticheron 1:*
O Lord, while the tomb was sealed by the lawless, Thou didst come forth from the tomb even as Thou wast born of the Mother of God; Thy bodiless Angels understood not how Thou wast incarnate, nor did the soldiers guarding Thee perceive when Thou didst rise: for both things were sealed unto those who would examine them, but wonders were revealed unto those who worship the mystery in faith. Unto us who sing praise thereto, grant joy and great mercy!

*Sticheron 2:*
O Lord, having broken the eternal bars and torn asunder the bonds, Thou didst rise from the tomb, leaving behind Thy burial clothes as a witness of the true three-day Resurrection; and Thou didst go before them into Galilee, Thou Who wast guarded in the cave: great is Thy mercy, O incomprehensible Savior, have mercy and save us!

*Sticheron 3:*
O Lord, the women ran unto the tomb to see Thee, Christ, Who didst suffer for our sake; and approaching, they found an Angel sitting upon the stone, terrified with fear; and he cried aloud unto them, saying: "The Lord is risen! Say unto the disciples that He is risen from the dead, saving our souls!"

*Sticheron 4:*
O Lord, even as Thou didst go forth from the sealed tomb, so also didst Thou enter in unto Thy disciples while the doors were shut, showing them the bodily wounds which Thou didst accept, O Savior, in Thy long-suffering: for as from the seed of David Thou didst endure wounds, so as the Son of God Thou hast set the world free: great is Thy mercy, O incomprehensible Savior, have mercy and save us!

*Other Stichera, Anatolian:*

*Sticheron 5:*
O Lord, King of the ages and Creator of all, Who didst accept crucifixion and burial in the flesh for our sake, that Thou mightest deliver us all from hades: for Thou art our God, beside Thee we know no other!

*Sticheron 6:*
O Lord, who can declare Thy radiant wonders? Or who shall announce Thy fearful mysteries? For Thou didst become man for our sake as Thou Thyself didst desire; Thou didst reveal the dominion of Thy power: for by Thy Crucifixion Thou didst open Paradise unto the thief, and by Thy burial Thou didst shatter the bars of hades, and by Thy Resurrection Thou hast enriched all things: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Sticheron 7:*
The Myrrh-bearing women, having reached the tomb very early, sought to anoint Thee with myrrh, the immortal Word and God; and instructed by the Angel's words, they returned with joy to announce openly unto the Apostles that Thou art risen, O Life of all, granting cleansing and great mercy unto the world!

*Then the Sticheron of the Blind Man. Tone 8:*

*Sticheron 8:*
For the tender mercy of Thy loving-kindness, O Christ God, having become incarnate, Thou didst make him who was deprived of light from the womb worthy of divine illumination through the tender mercy of Thine ineffable compassion: with Thy creative fingers Thou didst anoint with clay from spittle. O Son, Giver of light, illuminate also our spiritual senses, as the only begrudgingless Giver!

*Glory... Tone 8:*
Who can utter Thy mighty acts, O Christ? Or who can number the multitude of Thy wonders? For even as Thou wast seen as two-fold upon earth through goodness, two-fold also didst Thou grant healings...

...unto the suffering: for not only didst Thou open the bodily eyes of the man blind from the womb, but the spiritual ones also; therefore he glorifies Thee Who confessest Thee as God and grantest great mercy unto all!

*And "Most blessed art thou..." Then the Great Doxology. Resurrection Troparion only. And the rest as usual. And dismissal. And the Departure.*

*Glory... both now and ever... Gospel Sticheron, Tone 8:*
Mary's warm tears are not shed in vain; for behold, she was granted the teaching Angels, and the vision of Jesus Himself: yet she still minds earthly things, as a weak woman; therefore she is also sent away, not to touch Christ; but nevertheless she is sent as a herald unto Thy disciples, bearing the good tidings unto them, announcing the ascension unto the Father's portion. With her, vouchsafe us also Thine apparition, O Master and Lord![^716]

*Then the First Hour in the Narthex. And the Catechesis of St. Theodore the Studite. And the Final Dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes from the Canon, Ode 3 and 6, or of the present Tone on 8. Prokeimenon, Tone 8: Pray ye and render thanks unto the Lord our God [LXX 75:12]. Verse: In Judah God is known; His Name is great in Israel [LXX 75:2]. Epistle in the Acts, Pericope 38 [Acts 16:16-34][^717]. Alleluia, Tone 8: Look upon me and have mercy on me [LXX 118:132]. Verse: Order my steps according to Thy word [LXX 118:133]. Gospel from John, Pericope 34 [John 9:1-38][^718]. Communion Verse: "Receive the Body of Christ..." Another: "Praise the Lord..."*

## ON THE SAME SIXTH SUNDAY, AT VESPERS

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Blind Man, 3; and in the Menaion, 3.*

*Stichera, Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, O container of ascetics" (Радуйся постнических):*[^719]

*Sticheron 1:*
O Light unto all human nature, and Eye of the body of this world, O Word and Son of God, and recognized as the Shepherd and Creator and Fashioner of all: now by the bestowing of sight and by spittle ineffably enlightening the blind man with Thy fashioning fingers, and with clay and vision, who from birth had not obtained the sign to see the sun, now sees Thee, the sweet Sun, beholding the Image in deed, Who didst fashion him for the sake of abundant tender mercy!

*Sticheron 2:*
Enriched with the lack of spirit and the clothing of human flesh, the part sprinkled [with clay], he who first came forth blind from his mother's womb, thought that he was not in creation through the deprivation of eyes, unto which were multiplied the stumblings of his body by his feet, which were stony stumblings; but through Thee having obtained this wealth also, he sees the Light of the world, and proclaims Thee, the Builder of lights, God and Master of creation, and Fashioner of all the world!

*Sticheron 3:*
With mouth and soul and mind, he who formerly was blind confessed the Fashioner of clay and Giver of the pupils of the eyes by spittle, and the common Enlightenment, the universal Overseer and Builder of creation, Who for the sake of the tender mercy of creation became man, and was crucified as though without power, Whom the scribes with envy and hatred toward Him—since they themselves were powerless to see, groping all the more—judge him cast out from the synagogue, who once gave pupils unto the blind man, both spiritual and bodily!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 2:*
The man born blind said within his thoughts: "Was I born without eyes because of the sins of my parents? Or was I born for a reproof because of the unbelief of the nations?" Not knowing to ask: "When is night? When is day?" My feet cannot endure the stumbling against stones, for I saw not the sun shining, nor Him Who fashioned me in His image: but I entreat Thee, O Christ God, look down upon me and have mercy on me!

*At the Aposticha, Resurrection Stichera, Tone 5:*

*Sticheron 1:*
By Thy precious Cross, O Christ, Thou hast put the devil to shame; and by Thy Resurrection Thou hast blunted the sting of sin and saved us from the gates of death: we glorify Thee, the Only-begotten!

*Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes [LXX 122:1]...*

*Sticheron 2:*
O Lord, sinning unceasingly and being accounted worthy of Thy love for mankind without understanding, do Thou overcome mine obscurity, O only Good One, and have mercy on me!

*Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us [LXX 122:3]...*

*Martyrion:*
Having disregarded all earthly things, O blessed Passion-bearers, and manfully dared unto tortures, ye did not miss your hopes, but became heirs of the Heavenly Kingdom, O all-praised Martyrs: having boldness before God the Lover of mankind, ask peace for the world and great mercy for our souls!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 2: "Passing by, Jesus..." (see fol. 320)[^720]. Resurrection Troparion, Tone 5, and its Theotokion.*

## ON MONDAY OF THE SIXTH WEEK AFTER PASCHA, AT MATINS

*At "God is the Lord," Resurrection Troparion, Tone 5, with its Theotokion.*

*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymns, Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*

*Sessional Hymn 1:*
Let us praise the Cross of the Lord, let us honor the Holy Burial with hymns, and let us highly glorify His Resurrection: for as God He raised the dead from the tombs, having despoiled the dominion of death and the might of the devil, and unto those in hades He caused Light to shine!

*Sessional Hymn 2:*
Let us all overcome and meet Christ with a multitude of oil and bright lamps, that we may be accounted worthy of entering into the Bridal Chamber; for unto those found outside the doors, the Judge will cry out: "Have mercy on me!"...

*Sessional Hymn 3:*
O Lord, Thy Passion-bearers, having emulated the cup of Thy Passion, left behind worldly beauty and became partakers with the Angels: by their supplications grant peace unto our souls, O Christ, and great mercy!

*Glory... both now and ever... Theotokion:*
With the Heavenly Angels, with earthly men, in the voice of rejoicing let us cry out unto the Mother of God: Rejoice, door broader than the heavens; rejoice, only salvation of earthly beings; rejoice, pure one; rejoice, full of grace, who didst bear the incarnate God!

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*
Before Thy Cross, O Compassionate One, and Thy Resurrection, as Thou wast journeying, the man blind from birth cried out warmly: "O Son of David, have mercy on me! Grant light unto mine eyes, that I may see Thee naked!" Having anointed his pupils with clay from spittle, O Word, Thou didst grant him much light! *Glory... both now and ever... the same.*

*Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ... Psalm 50. And we sing the Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 8, and of the Menaion on 4. Kontakion and Exapostilarion of the Feast.*

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4. Tone 5:*

*Sticheron 1:*
O Lord, while the tomb was sealed by the lawless, Thou didst come forth from the tomb even as Thou wast born of the Mother of God; Thy bodiless Angels understood not how Thou wast incarnate, nor did the soldiers guarding Thee perceive when Thou didst rise: for both things were sealed unto those who would examine them, but wonders were revealed unto those who worship the mystery in faith. Unto us who sing praise thereto, grant joy and great mercy!

*Sticheron 2:*
Woe is me! Unto whom am I likened? Unto the barren fig tree; and I fear the curse and cutting down: but O heavenly Husbandman, Christ God, make my withered soul fruitful, and receive me as the Prodigal Son, and have mercy on me!

*Sticheron 3:*
O Lord, I fear Thy terror, yet I do evil unceasingly: who before the judgment seat fears not the judge? Or who desiring healing angers the physician, even as I do? O Long-suffering Lord, have compassion on my weakness, and have mercy on me!

*Sticheron 4 (Martyrion):*
The blessed army of the Heavenly King: for though the Passion-bearers were earthly-born, yet they hastened to attain unto the angelic dignity, having despised their bodies; and for their sufferings they were accounted worthy of the honor of the Bodiless Hosts: by their supplications, O Lord, send down upon us Thy great mercy!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 4:*
Thinking his whole life to be night, the blind man cried aloud unto Thee, O Lord: "Open my pupils, O Son of David, our Savior, that with all men I also may hymn Thy power!"

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 5, Podoben: "O venerable father" (Преподо́бне о́тче):*

*Sticheron 1:*
Seeing the blind man who had received sight, they quickly beheld him: darkening both understanding and soul and mind unto those who saw his untimely recovery of sight; and looking closely, they asked in rage: "How seest thou now as one of those who see the light, being blind from birth, sitting by the ways and begging?" Therefore he pointed out Him Who gave light and created the luminaries in the world, of Whom the co-unoriginate Son of God is preached, manifested in the latter times from the Virgin as man, through the tender mercy from the Holy Spirit!

*Verse: Look upon me and have mercy on me [LXX 118:132].*

*Sticheron 2:*
As though burdened with a heavy load and earthly weight, the blind man sorrowed while living in the world, stumbling with his feet along the streets; then swiftly becoming greatly rich with sight, therefore he flees unto the Giver of light, from Whom he receives light to see, and with his eyes to behold the Creator, Who according to His likeness and according to His image created human nature from the earth of old, and now by clay and spittle hath enlightened his pupils, granting him in His love for mankind to see the sun!

*Verse: Order my steps according to Thy word [LXX 118:133].*

*Sticheron 3:*
The blind man beholding the light saw the Word of the Father conformed unto human likeness, from Whom he sees the light-bearing and day-originating sun, even as the rest, timely rejoicing in the most beautifully-adorned vision, wherewith he was adorned, walking uprightly and traversing the paths without labor, recognizing the Son of God Who was proclaimed, Who became man for the sake of the utmost dispensation; and remaining God, He became man, taking what He was not, yet preserving their unconfused union!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 5:*
O Lord, passing by of old along the way, Thou didst find a man blind from birth; and the disciples being astonished asked Thee, saying: "Master, who did sin, this man or his parents, that he was born blind?" But Thou, my Savior, didst cry out unto them: "Neither hath this man sinned, nor his parents, but that the works of God should be made manifest in him. I must work the works of Him that sent Me, which no man can work." And having said this, spitting upon the ground and making clay, He anointed the eyes of the blind man, saying unto him: "Go, wash in the pool of Siloam!" And having washed, he was made whole, and cried aloud unto Thee: "I believe, Lord!" and he worshipped Thee. Therefore we also cry aloud: Have mercy on us!

*At the Beatitudes, the Tri-ode Canon, whose acrostic, or marginal verse, is: ΔΟΞΑ ΘΕΩ [Glory to God is due].[^721]*

*Tone 5. Ode 4. Heirmos:*
"Let us sing unto the Lord Who wrought salvation..."

*Troparia:*
By Thy precious Cross, O Lord, we were delivered from the ancient curse; therefore we glorify Thy goodness!

Willingly crucified, Thou didst empty the great treasures of death, O Christ our Savior: Him do we glorify!

Seeing the strange radiant appearance of the Angel, the Myrrh-bearing women perceived the divine Resurrection of the Creator, being filled with joy!

*Theotokion of Ode 4:*
Thou wast the dawn, O all-pure Virgin, of the Sun of righteousness, through Whom we were delivered from the darkness of ignorance!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
"The Creator of all creation..."

*Troparia:*
Seeing God crucified in the flesh, the sun was darkened, and the earth was shaken, and rocks were split asunder!

On the third day Thou didst rise from the dead, O Christ, and didst raise up all that sat in darkness and the shadow of death!

After Thy rising Thou didst appear unto Thine Apostles, and didst fill them with ineffable joy, O Compassionate One!

*Glory...*
Unto the all-holy Father, and the co-eternal Word, and the uncreated divine Spirit, let us sing to the Trinity unceasingly!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
Deliver us from torture and eternal condemnation, O pure Mother of God, who hymn thee!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
"Thee, blessed among women..."

*Troparia:*
Thou didst stretch out Thy hands upon the Cross, O Compassionate One, and thereby didst draw unto Thyself me who had been drawn out by many wiles of the evil one!

Thou didst raise all with Thyself, O Good One, entering the gates of hades, O Christ, and didst lift up those who hymn Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Glory...*
Thou didst pour forth life unto the dead, O Christ, and having risen on the third day, Thou didst ascend in glory while the honorable disciples beheld Thee!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
Make supplication for us, O all-pure Virgin, unto thy Son and the God of all, who hymn thee in faith!

## ON THE SAME MONDAY EVENING

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 5, Podoben: "O venerable father" (Преподо́бне о́тче):*

*Sticheron 1:*
With mouth and soul and mind, he who formerly was blind confessed the Fashioner of clay and Giver of the pupils of the eyes by spittle, and the common Enlightenment, the universal Overseer and Builder of creation, Who for the sake of the tender mercy of creation became man, and proclaimed Him as the Almighty God, Whom the scribes through envy and disputation with Him—since they were unable to see, groping all the more—judge cast out from the synagogue, who once gave pupils unto the blind man, both spiritual and bodily!

*Sticheron 2:*
Unto the height of light-bearing deeds ascended the blind man who was enlightened by the light of the knowledge of God, although formerly suffering blindness in the windows of his senses, enduring bodily and mental obscurity: being enlightened by new outpourings of light from the Word, he recognizes the Giver of light and the Fashioner of light, Who cried from the tomb on the third day, and terrified the disciples by His rising at midnight: from Whom shone forth the light of the Resurrection unto men held in darkness, through tender mercy and great mercy!

*Sticheron 3:*
Beholding the light-bearing radiance that shone forth, proclaimed on the Sabbath unto the light-bearing blind man, those learned in the law of Moses were troubled, being blinded in their manner, seeing the shadow and not understanding the foreshadowing, scrutinizing the law, wherein they saw not the Giver of light, Who willed to cleanse the blind man by washing and the spittle of new creation: united with him, let us behold that God, exposing the blindness of the Pharisees by the sight of him who received the ray!

*Then in the Menaion. Glory... both now and ever... Tone 8:*
O noetic Sun of righteousness, Christ God, Who by Thy most pure touch didst enlighten both eyes of him who was deprived of light from the womb, and having illuminated our spiritual eyes, show us to be sons of the day, that in faith we may cry aloud unto Thee: Great and ineffable is Thy beneficence toward us, O Lover of mankind: glory to Thee!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 5:*

*Sticheron 1:*
Granting resurrection unto the human race, He was led as a sheep to the slaughter [LXX Isaiah 53:7]; the princes of hades were terrified thereat, and the gates of grief were lifted up: for Christ the King of glory entered in, saying unto the captives: "Go forth! and ye that are in darkness, be uncovered!"

*Verse: Unto Thee have I lifted up mine eyes [LXX 122:1]...*

*Sticheron 2:*
O Lord, I fear Thy terror, yet I do evil unceasingly: who before the judgment seat fears not the judge? Or who desiring healing angers the physician, even as I do? O Long-suffering Lord, have compassion on my weakness, and have mercy on me!

*Verse: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us [LXX 122:3]...*

*Martyrion:*
Clothed with the shield of faith and strengthened with the sign of the Cross, Thy holy ones manfully gave themselves over unto tortures, and abolished the deceit and boldness of the devil, O Lord: by their supplications, as the Almighty God, send down peace upon the world and great mercy upon our souls!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 8:*
For the tender mercy of Thy loving-kindness, O Christ God, having become incarnate, Thou didst make him who was deprived of light from the womb worthy of divine illumination through the tender mercy of Thine ineffable compassion: having anointed his pupils with clay from spittle with Thy creative fingers: do Thou Thyself, Giver of light, now also illuminate our spiritual senses, as the only begrudgingless Giver!

## ON TUESDAY OF THE SIXTH WEEK AFTER PASCHA, AT MATINS

*At "God is the Lord," Resurrection Troparion, Tone 5, with its Theotokion.*

*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymns, Tone 5:*

*Sessional Hymn 1:*
O Lord, Thou wast named dead, Who didst put death to death; Thou wast laid in the tomb, Who didst empty the tombs; above, the soldiers guarded the tomb; below, Thou didst raise up the dead from the ages: O Almighty and Incomprehensible Lord, glory to Thee!

*Sessional Hymn 2:*
When the Judge sits, and the Angels stand by, the trumpet sounds, and the flame burns: what wilt thou do, O my soul, when led unto judgment? For then thy deeds shall appear, and thy secret sins shall be reproved: therefore before the end cry aloud unto the Judge: O God, be merciful unto me and save me!

*Martyrion:*
Today the memory of the Passion-bearers shines forth, for it hath illumination from the heavens: the choir of Angels keeps festival, and the human race celebrates together: therefore by their supplications may our souls find mercy!

*Glory... both now and ever... Theotokion:*
O all-pure Mother of God, wall of Christians, deliver thy people as is thy custom, who diligently cry aloud unto thee to stand against enemies and haughty thoughts, that we may cry unto thee: Rejoice, thou who art full of grace!

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*
Having received his sight, the blind man is seen through spittle, the human forsaking of Him Who created of old, who from birth saw not the great sun: therefore he sends up thanksgiving unto God from his soul, beholding His image created according to likeness, the image of the Creator! *Glory... both now and ever... the same.*

*We sing the Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 8, and of the Menaion on 4. Kontakion and Exapostilarion of the Feast.*

*(Rubric note: If Key of Paschalion 8 [И] occurs, then here also the Forefeast of the Ascension is read. Behold.)*[^722]

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4. Tone 5:*

*Sticheron 1:*
O Lord, having broken the eternal bars and torn asunder the bonds of the tomb, Thou didst rise, leaving behind Thy burial clothes as a witness of Thy true three-day Burial; and Thou didst go before them into Galilee, Thou Who wast guarded in the cave: great is Thy mercy, O incomprehensible Savior, and have mercy on us!

*Sticheron 2:*
Despise the multitude of my sins, O Lord, Who wast born of the Virgin, and cleanse all mine iniquities, granting me a thought of conversion, as the only Lover of mankind: I pray Thee, and have mercy on me!

*Martyrion:*
Thy Passion-bearers, O Lord, emulating the angelic ranks, endured tortures as if bodiless, having a like-minded hope, the enjoyment of promised goods: by their supplications, O Christ our God, grant peace unto Thy world and great mercy unto our souls!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 8:*
Who can utter Thy mighty acts, O Christ? Or who can number the multitude of Thy wonders? For even as Thou wast seen as two-fold upon earth through goodness, two-fold also didst Thou grant healings unto the sick: for not only didst Thou open the bodily eyes of the man blind from the womb, but the spiritual ones also. Therefore he confesses Thee as God, crying aloud, Who grantest great mercy unto all!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 5, Podoben: "O venerable father" (Преподо́бне о́тче):*

*Sticheron 1:*
Beholding the light-bearing radiance that shone forth, proclaimed on the Sabbath unto the light-bearing blind man, those troubled by the laws of Moses, being blinded in their manner, see the shadow, not understanding the foreshadowing, breaking the laws, wherein they saw not the Giver of light, Who willed to cleanse the blind man by washing and the spittle of new creation: united with him, let us behold that God, exposing the blindness of the Pharisees by the sight of him who received the ray!

*Verse: Look upon me and have mercy on me [LXX 118:132].*

*Sticheron 2:*
Morning dawned unto him who sat in the darkness of the night of blindness, the much-suffering blind man, by washing in the streams of the pool of Siloam, and by the divine command of light unto his eyes, therefore he sees anew in a light-bearing manner: exposing the dark gloom of the night-working legal scribes, and the darkened blindness of these men, by the light-bearing illumination, wherein he reads in the scriptures of the blindness of old foreshadowed, by the bright illuminations given by the Word!

*Verse: Order my steps according to Thy word [LXX 118:133].*

*Sticheron 3:*
Unto the height of light-bearing deeds ascended the blind man who was enlightened by the light of the knowledge of God, although formerly suffering blindness in the windows of his senses, enduring bodily and mental obscurity: being enlightened by new outpourings of light from the Word, he recognizes the Giver of light and the Fashioner of light, Who cried from the tomb on the third day, and terrified the disciples by His rising at midnight: from Whom shone forth the light of the Resurrection unto men held in darkness, through tender mercy and great mercy!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 8: "O noetic Sun of righteousness, Christ God..." (see fol. 332)[^723].*

*At the Beatitudes, the Tri-ode Canon, the work of Joseph, whose acrostic or marginal verse is: ὙΜΝΩΔΙΑ ΙΩΣΗΦ [Hymnody of Joseph]: that is, The Song of Joseph.[^724]*

*Tone 5. Ode 6. Heirmos:*
"See ye, see ye..."

*Troparia:*
We hymn Thy three-day Burial, O Christ, and Thy most radiant Resurrection do we ever praise in faith!

Him Who was crucified for our sake, and laid in the tomb, and enlightened the world by His rising, Christ do we glorify!

O unsetting Sun, Christ, Who wast buried and didst rise from the dead, enlighten the thoughts of us all to glorify Thee unceasingly!

*Theotokion of Ode 6:*
I pray unto the Word incarnate from thee: have compassion, O Virgin Mother of God, that He may have compassion on us, the humble!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
"The Creator of all creation..."

*Troparia:*
Even as Moses lifted up the serpent of old, so wast Thou lifted up upon the Tree, O Compassionate One, and didst drive away the serpentine venom and malice of the fierce enemy, and didst save us!

Glory to Thy glory, O most glorified Lord! For Thou wast laid in the tomb willingly as dead, and didst rise as God, and hast raised up the world with Thyself by Thy rising, as the Almighty!

"Behold, the Giver of life is risen from the dead!" the shining Angel cried unto the women of old: "Weep no more therefore, but announce the Resurrection unto the Apostles!"

*Theotokion:*
O Holy Lord, Who wast born of the pure Virgin, and crucified and buried willingly, and didst rise from the dead: by her supplications send down Thy mercy upon all!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
"Let us magnify Christ..."

*Troparia:*
As a lamb Thou wast led to the slaughter, O Master Christ God, and by Thy wounds which Thou didst endure willingly, Thou didst quench all the evil multitude of the demons!

The bars of hades were shattered by Thy coming, O Christ, and they that were corrupted from the ages arose, hymning Thee as the Victor and Ruler of all!

*Glory...*
Thy holy rising from the tomb is life, O Christ, and Thine Ascension is ever joy: which in faith we now celebrate!

*Both now and ever... Theotokion:*
Having borne for us the Light from Light, O all-pure Virgin, thou didst destroy the darkness of godlessness: therefore all we earthly-born magnify thee in faith!

## ON THE SAME TUESDAY EVENING

*After the customary Kathisma, at "Lord, I have cried," we sing 6 Stichera of the Blind Man, Idiomela:*

*Tone 2:*

*Sticheron 1:*
The man born blind said within his thoughts: "Was I born without eyes because of the sins of my parents? Or was I born for a reproof because of the unbelief of the nations?" Not knowing to ask: "When is night? When is day?" My feet cannot endure the stumbling against stones, for I saw not the sun shining, nor Him Who fashioned me in His image: but I entreat Thee, O Christ God, look down upon me and have mercy on me!

*Sticheron 2:*
Passing by from the temple, Jesus found a man blind from birth; and taking compassion, He placed clay upon his eyes, and said unto him: "Go, wash in Siloam!" And having washed, he received his sight, sending up glory unto God. And his neighbors said unto him: "Who opened thine eyes, which none of those that see was able to heal?" But he crying aloud said: "A man called Jesus, He said unto me: Wash in Siloam, and I received sight. He is truly the One of Whom Moses spoke in the Law: Christ the Messiah; He is the Savior of our souls!"

*Tone 4:*

*Sticheron 3:*
Thinking his whole life to be night, the blind man cried aloud unto Thee: "O Lord, open my pupils, O Son of David, our Savior, that with all men I also may hymn Thy power!"

*Tone 5:*

*Sticheron 4:*
O Lord, passing by of old along the way, Thou didst find a man blind from birth; and the disciples being astonished asked Thee, saying: "Master, who did sin, this man or his parents, that he was born blind?" But Thou, my Savior, didst cry out unto them: "Neither hath this man sinned, nor his parents, but that the works...

...of God should be made manifest in him. I must work the works of Him that sent Me, which no man can work." And having said this, spitting upon the ground and making clay, He anointed his eyes, saying unto him: "Go, wash in the pool of Siloam!" And having washed, he was made whole, and cried aloud unto Thee: "I believe, Lord!" and he worshipped Thee. Therefore we also cry aloud: Have mercy on us!

*Tone 8:*

*Sticheron 5:*
O noetic Sun of righteousness, Christ God, Who by Thy most pure touch didst enlighten both eyes of him who was deprived of light from the womb, and having illuminated our spiritual eyes, show us to be sons of the day, that in faith we may cry aloud unto Thee: Great and ineffable is Thy beneficence toward us, O Lover of mankind: glory to Thee!

*Sticheron 6:*
For the tender mercy of Thy loving-kindness, O Christ God, having become incarnate, Thou didst make him who was deprived of light from the womb worthy of divine illumination through the tender mercy of Thine ineffable compassion: having anointed his pupils with clay from spittle with Thy creative fingers: do Thou Thyself, Giver of light, now also illuminate our spiritual senses, as the only begrudgingless Giver!

*Glory... both now and ever... Tone 8: "Who can utter Thy mighty acts, O Christ?..." (see fol. 333)[^725]. Then the Prokeimenon of the day.*

*At the Aposticha, Resurrection Sticheron, Tone 5:*
Thee, the incarnate Savior Christ, Who yet wast not separated from the heavens, in the voices of song let us magnify: for Thou didst accept the Cross and death for our race, as the Lord Lover of mankind; and having despoiled the gates of hades, Thou didst rise on the third day, saving our souls!

*Then after this, the Stichera of Pascha with their verses:*
*Verse: Let God arise, and let His enemies be scattered [LXX 67:2]...*
*And the rest. Glory... both now and ever... "This is the day of Resurrection... Christ is risen..." in a great voice, thrice, even as was depicted above on the very day of Pascha on fol. 170[^726].*

*"Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart..." Trisagion. Resurrection Troparion, Tone 5: "Unto the Word co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit..." And its Theotokion: "Rejoice, door of the Lord..." Litany and Dismissal. For the services of the saints whose service occurs on Wednesday, it is sung here at Compline.*

*Rubric for the Concurrence of Key 8 ([И]) of the Paschalion:*

*Be it known: that if Key 8 ([И]) of the Paschalion should occur, then on May 8 the Feast of the Holy Apostle John the Theologian falls on the Wednesday before the Ascension.[^727]*

*On Tuesday Evening, at Small Vespers: Stichera of the Apostle on 4. Glory... of the Apostle. Both now and ever... the same. At the Aposticha: Stichera of the Apostle with their own verses. Glory... of the Apostle. Both now and ever... the same. Troparion of the Apostle. Glory... both now and ever... Theotokion according to the Tone of the Troparion. Small Litany and Dismissal.*

*At Great Vespers, after the customary Psalm, we sing: "Blessed is the man..." the 1st Stasis. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Blind Man on 6, and of the Apostle on 4. Glory... of the Apostle. Both now and ever... of the Blind Man, Tone 8: "Who can utter..." Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. Readings of the Apostle. At the Litiya: Stichera of the Temple, and of the Apostle, 4 Idiomela, Tone 4. Glory... of the Apostle. Both now and ever... Theotokion from the alphabetical dogmatics.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Apostle with their verses. After these, the verses: "Let God arise..." and the rest; the Stichera of Pascha with their verses. Glory... of the Apostle. Both now and ever... of Pascha: "This is the day of Resurrection..." Then: "Christ is risen..." thrice. "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart..." Trisagion; and after the Our Father: At the Troparia: Troparion of the Apostle, twice; and "O Virgin Mother of God," once. Reading of the Apostle, written by Prochorus his disciple.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion: "Christ is risen..." twice; Tone 5: "Unto the Word co-unoriginate..." Glory... of the Apostle. Both now and ever... Theotokion 4 of that tone. After the 1st Kathisma: Sessional Hymn of the Resurrection, and reading of the Feast. After the 2nd Kathisma: Sessional Hymn of the Blind Man, and reading. Then the Polyeleos, and the Sessional Hymns of the Apostle, all once. Glory... both now and ever... Theotokion 4 of that tone, and reading of the Apostle. Graduals: 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon: "Let every breath praise the Lord..." and Gospel of the Apostle. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ..." thrice. Psalm 50. Sticheron of the Apostle. The Canon of Pascha with the Theotokia and Heirmoi on 6, and of the Apostle on 4, and of the Blind Man on 4. We sing at each Ode: Troparia of the Blind Man, 2; Glory... Trinity hymn; Both now and ever... Theotokion. Katavasia, Heirmos: "Unto God the Savior..." both choirs together. The Forefeast [of the Ascension] in the Canon is here omitted, and to sing it at Matins the rubric directs ahead to fol. 333[^728]. After Ode 3: Kontakion and Ikos of the Apostle. Then the Hypakoe of Pascha: "Anticipating the morning..." Glory... Sticheron of the Apostle. Both now and ever... of the Blind Man; and reading of the Apostle. After Ode 6: Kontakion and Ikos of Pascha. At Ode 9: The Magnifications are not sung. Exapostilarion: "Having fallen asleep in the flesh..." Glory... of the Apostle. Both now and ever... of the Blind Man. At the Praises, Stichera of the Apostle on 4. Then the verse: "Let God arise..." and the Stichera of Pascha with their verses. Glory...*

*Glory... of the Apostle. Both now and ever... Tone 5: "This is the day of Resurrection..." "Christ is risen..." thrice; Great Doxology; Resurrection Troparion. Glory... of the Apostle. Both now and ever... Theotokion: the first of the Second Tone. Litany, Catechesis, and Dismissal. Procession into the Narthex, and the First Hour there. At the Hours: Resurrection Troparion. Glory... of the Apostle. Both now and ever... Theotokion of the Hours. After the Our Father: Resurrection Kontakion of Pascha, and of the Apostle, alternating.*

*At the Liturgy: Beatitudes: Ode 3 of Pascha on 4, and of the Apostle, Ode 6 on 4. After the Entrance, Troparion of the Resurrection, and of the Apostle. Glory... Kontakion of Pascha. Both now and ever... Kontakion of Pascha. Prokeimenon and Alleluia of Pascha, and of the Apostle. Epistle and Gospel of the day and of the Apostle. Communion Hymn: "Receive the Body of Christ..." or "Praise the Lord..."; another: "All the earth..."*

## ON WEDNESDAY OF THE SIXTH WEEK AFTER PASCHA

*Apodosis of the Feast of Pascha.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Resurrection Troparion twice: "Unto the Word co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit..." Glory... both now and ever... its Theotokion. After the 1st Kathisma: Resurrection Sessional Hymn. Glory... both now and ever... Theotokion: written in the Sunday of the Blind Man (see fol. 323)[^729]. And the reading from John. After the 2nd Kathisma: Sessional Hymn of the Blind Man. Then: "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ..." Psalm 50. Three Canons: of Pascha with the Heirmos on 6, written in the Sunday of the Myrrh-bearers (see fol. 202)[^730]; and of the Blind Man on 4, (see fol. 322)[^731]; and of the Forefeast on 4, (see fol. 333)[^732].*

*(Rubric note in margin: If the Gospel of John falls on this Wednesday, then read this Canon.)*

*Canon of the Forefeast of the Ascension, bearing an acrostic, that is, marginal verse, according to the alphabet.[^733]*

*Tone 5. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
"Unto Him Who drowned the horse and his rider in the Red Sea, Who crushes wars with His lofty arm, let us sing unto Christ, for He hath been glorified!"

*Troparia:*
On high unto the Father Christ ascends, and brings the flesh which He received for our sake: unto Him let us sing in praises today, singing a song of victory!

The divine books of the scriptures, and the most wise preachings of the preachers, clearly have received their fulfillment: for after the Resurrection, Christ in His goodness ascends with glory into the heavens!

The earth mystically keeps festival, and the heavens are filled with gladness at the Ascension of Christ, Who by grace united what was formerly sundered, and destroyed the ancient wall of enmity!

*Katavasia: "Unto God the Savior..."*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
"Thou Who didst establish upon nothing the earth by Thy command, and didst suspend its weight unsteadied: upon the immovable rock of Thy commandments, O Christ, establish Thy Church, O only Good One and Lover of mankind!"

*Troparia:*
Having bound death by Thy death, O Word, Thou didst rise from the dead on the third day; and unto the heavens, O Savior, Thou didst ascend with glory, while the bodiless hosts sang praise unto Thine ineffable dispensation!

*Glory...*
Thou didst come unto the earthly, O Christ, without body; and clothed in flesh, Thou didst endure the Cross; and having risen from the dead, Thou didst ascend with glory unto Thy Father, the Source of all, peacefully ordering all things!

*Both now and ever...*
Bearing the wandering sheep upon the earth, O unforgetting Word, Thou didst restore it, and having ascended unto heaven, Thou didst sit in glory at the right hand of Thy Father: glory to Thy great tender mercy!

*Kathisma. Sessional Hymn of the Second Tone, of the Blind Man: "With spiritual eyes..." (see fol. 324)[^734]. Then, his Sessional Hymn: (see fol. 323). Glory... both now and ever... of the Forefeast, the same as the morning.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
"Perceiving Thy divine dispensation, the Prophet Habakkuk cried aloud of old with trembling: Thou didst come forth for the salvation of Thy people, to save Thine anointed ones!"

*Troparia:*
Having of old had compassion upon our nature that was wronged by sin, O God and King, through Thine assumption thereof, we sing praise unto Thy rising with fear, and Thy divine Ascension!

The ranks of the bodiless hosts are amazed, beholding Thee, O Christ, bearing an earthly body, and having ascended upon a cloud, and mounting unto the heavens!

*Glory...*
Seeing Thy garments dyed in blood at Thine ascension, O Christ, King of all, the angelic Hosts were terrified, and worshipped with fear and joy!

*Both now and ever...*
Let us clap our hands: the Lord, having risen from the dead, ascended unto the heavens, the Angels being subject unto Him, as unto our Creator and God!

*Katavasia: "I have heard the report of the power of the Cross..."*

*On Wednesday of the 6th Week after Pascha:*

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
"O Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, rising early unto Thee I cry: Enlighten my soul darkened by passions, O Christ, as the only Compassionate One!"

*Troparia:*
Christ, having become man, taking away the condemnation of the divided, rose and ascended unto the heavens, honoring them with the session with the Father, whom He raised up!

*Glory...*
Making Thy disciples joyful after the Resurrection, O Christ, Thou didst ascend on high unto Thy Father, from Whom Thou wast never separated, even though Thou didst converse with men!

*Both now and ever...*
Having fulfilled the sweet-voiced foreshowings of the Law and the giving of the Prophets, O Christ, Thou didst ascend, a cloud taking Thee up, O Savior, unto the heavens: rising early we cry unto Thee!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
"The deep of passions and the tempest of contrary winds rise up against me; but do Thou, O Savior, anticipation-wise draw me out and save me from corruption, even as Thou didst save the Prophet from the beast!"

*Troparia:*
A strange sight didst Thou appear unto the Angels, bearing the form of man, by Thy fearful ascension, O Lover of mankind; therefore with fear we sing praise unto Thee!

The Lord of all honorable ones ascends with glory brightly unto His unoriginate Father: all creation now keeps festival, dancing for joy!

*Both now and ever...*
Let every tongue sing today with shouting: Christ ascended unto heaven in the divine sound of the trumpet, and hath not forsaken those whom He loved!

*Katavasia: "The abyss encompassed me..."*

*Kontakion of Pascha: "Though Thou didst descend into the tomb..." and Ikos: (see fol. 173)[^735]. And the Prologue there.*

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
"O Thou Who art exalted above the fathers and our God, blessed art Thou!"

*Troparia:*
Having rent asunder the middle wall of enmity, O Lord, by Thy Cross and Passion, borne upon a cloud, Thou didst ascend with glory unto Thine unoriginate Father!

Trembling, the Hosts cried aloud: "Lift up the gates! Christ hath come bearing an earthly body, and having slain Beliar by His divine Passion!"

*Glory...*
"Why are Thy garments red, O Lover of mankind?" the divine Hosts asked: "I have trodden the winepress alone,"[^736] Christ cried aloud as He ascended!

*Both now and ever...*
In hymns we harmoniously glorify Thy Crucifixion and Resurrection, O Christ, brightly keeping festival, honoring Thine Ascension unto ages!

*Katavasia: "In the furnace of fire..."*

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
"Thee, the Fashioner of all creation, whom the Angels hymn, ye youths bless, ye priests sing, ye people exalt above all unto all ages!"

*Troparia:*
The light-bearing cloud took Thee up from the Mount of Olives, Who art the Light from Light, while all Thy divine disciples beheld, glorifying Thee unto all ages!

Let us clap our hands in the manner of psalms: Christ our God is risen, and hath ascended thither whence He came down unto us in His love for mankind, having reconciled what of old was sundered!

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord:*
Hymned as the Good One, Christ ascended upon the cherubic chariots, and flew as upon the wings of noetic Ranks, Whom we exalt above all unto all ages!

*Both now and ever...*
Risen from the tomb, Thou didst appear unto the disciples, and having led them up unto the Mount of Olives, whence a radiant cloud took Thee up, it led Thee unto the heights with glory!

*Then: We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord... Katavasia: "From the Father before the ages..." We do not sing the Magnificat ("More honorable...").*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
"Incorruptibly didst thou conceive the Word in thy womb, O Mother of God, and didst bear the Joy of the world: thee do we magnify!"

*Troparia:*
All creation offers a precious festival unto Thy supreme glory, O Word of God, wherewith Thou didst bring unto Thy Father our nature which Thou didst assume, unchangeably beyond word!

As God, O Savior, Thou didst shatter the gates of hades and the bars, and having risen from the dead, Thou didst ascend unto the heavens, while the angelic Ranks cried in astonishment: "Lift up the gates!"

*On Wednesday of the 6th Week after Pascha:*
The divine disciples stood by, and the Angels appeared manifestly, crying aloud unto them: "He Whom ye see ascending into the Heavens, so shall He come with glory to judge all!"

*Glory...*
The peace which Thou didst grant unto the disciples, O Christ, when ascending on high of old, that also send down richly upon us, preserving all in love, that with one accord we may magnify Thee as Savior!

*Both now and ever...*
Thou wast truly the light-bearing chariot of the noetic Sun, O undefiled and Pure One, whereby they that sat in darkness beheld the light of understanding, worthily glorifying thee!

*Exapostilarion:*
*"Having fallen asleep in the flesh..." twice: (see fol. 176)[^737]. Another, of the Blind Man. Glory... both now and ever... "With my spiritual eyes..." (see fol. 327)[^738].*

*At the Praises: "Let every breath..." we set 4 verses, and we sing matching Stichera of the Blind Man, Tone 2: "The man born blind..."; "Passing by from the temple, Jesus..."; then Tone 4: "Thinking his whole life to be night..."; Tone 5: "O Lord, passing by of old...": written on Tuesday Evening (see fol. 339)[^739].*

*Then both choirs uniting, we sing the Stichera of Pascha with their own verses. Sticheron: "Let God arise..." Glory... both now and ever... "This is the day of Resurrection..." "Christ is risen..." thrice. Great Doxology. Troparion, Tone 5: "Unto the Word co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit..." Glory... both now and ever... its Theotokion. Litany and Dismissal. The rubric for Pascha Evening, (see fol. 186)[^740]. And at the First Hour, in the Narthex, and the final Dismissal.*

*And the Hours we sing of Pascha.*

*At the Liturgy: Antiphons of Pascha. Prokeimenon of Pascha: "This is the day which the Lord hath made..." and Alleluia of Pascha. Epistle and Gospel of the day; and the rest of the service all of the Resurrection. Communion Hymn: "Receive the Body of Christ..." And instead of "Blessed is He that comes in the Name of the Lord," we sing: "Christ is risen..." thrice. And instead of "Let our mouths be filled," "Christ is risen..." thrice. And instead of "Blessed be the Name of the Lord," and "I will bless the Lord," we sing: "Christ is risen..." many times. Then the Priest makes the Dismissal of Pascha in the Narthex. And all the rest we sing even as was indicated on Pascha itself (see fol. 171)[^741]. And so the Feast of Pascha is completely taken leave of on this day.*

*At the Trapeza there is consolation for the brethren: we eat oil and fish, and drink wine, giving thanks unto God.*

---

### Notes

[^527]: The woodcut icon of Thomas Sunday (Antipascha): Folio 200 (p204–p205) features a prominent woodcut depicting Christ appearing behind shut doors and inviting Thomas to thrust his hand into His side, inaugurating Part III of the Flowery Triodion.
[^528]: Authorship inscription to John the Monk (*І҆ѡа́нна Мона́ха* / St. John of Damascus) for the Doxastikon of Thomas Sunday Vespers: "While the doors were shut, Thou camest unto the disciples, O Christ...", preserved as a distinct recensional heading in the 1642 Slozka imprint.
[^529]: The Midnight Office Triadic Canon by Metrophanes of Smyrna (9th c.): Chanted when an All-Night Vigil cannot be celebrated; the heading preserves the Greek alphabetic acrostic in Cyrillic transliteration: `ΜΙΑΝ ΣΕ ΜΕЛΠΩ ΤΗΝ ТРІСНЛІОН ФҮСІН` ("Singing, I hymn Thee, the one Tri-radiant Nature").
[^530]: Typikon Chapter 105 (*Глава̀, р҃е*) in the 1642 Lviv Triodion: Comprehensive rubrical statute governing the concurrence of the Feast of the Holy Great-Martyr George (*Yurii*, April 23) when it coincides with the Sunday of Saint Thomas (Antipascha).
[^531]: The Canon of Thomas Sunday, composed by St. John of Damascus (*І҆ѡа́нна Мона́ха*), Tone 1: With the poetic heirmos "Let us all sing a song of victory..." and the Paschal katavasia "The Day of Resurrection" (*Voskreseniia den*).
[^532]: Appointed Patristic homily after Ode 3 of Matins: St. Gregory the Theologian, Oratio 44 (*In novam Dominicam* / *Ѡ҆ бновле́нїа почита́ити* / "On the New Sunday: to honor renewal is an ancient law"), celebrating the renewal of the week and creation through the Resurrection.
[^533]: The metrical verses and prose Synaxarion of Thomas Sunday: Attributed in the printed heading to Nikephoros Kallistos Xanthopoulos (c. 1256–1335), detailing the theology of the "Eighth Day" and the historical circumstances of the Apostle Thomas's doubt and confession.
[^534]: Folk-etymological and anatomical gloss on the Greek name *Didymus* (*Близне́цъ* / Twin): The Synaxarion records the Byzantine tradition that Thomas had two joined fingers on his right hand from birth (the middle and index fingers), alongside the primary translation of the Aramaic name *T'oma* as "Twin".
[^535]: The Heirmos of Ode 7: "When the musical harmony summoned the people to worship the image...", drawing upon the biblical narrative of the Three Holy Youths before Nebuchadnezzar's golden image in Babylon (Daniel 3:5–7) and the conversion of the fiery furnace into dew.
[^536]: Katavasiai of Ode 7 and Ode 8 of Thomas Sunday: The Katavasia of Ode 7 is the Paschal Heirmos "The Children delivered from the furnace" (*Otroki ot peshchi*); for Ode 8, "This chosen and holy day" (*Sei ubo narechennyi*). The rubric notes that the Magnificat (*Chestneishuiu*) is omitted, moving directly to Ode 9.
[^537]: The Exapostilarion (*Svitylen*) of Thomas Sunday Matins: Chanted in Tone 4 to the melody "Having Adorned the Heavens with Stars" (*Nebo zvezdami*): "Examining with thy hand the wounds of My members, be not faithless unto Me, O Thomas...", followed by the Doxastikon of the Praises ("After eight days from Thy rising, O Jesus, King...").
[^538]: Studite Catechesis and Litiya rubrics in the narthex (*Pritvor*): The 1642 Lviv Triodion preserves the ancient monastic practice of holding a procession to the narthex before the Third Hour, reading the Catechetical Instructions (*Oglasheniia*) of St. Theodore the Studite after the First Hour, and celebrating the Divine Liturgy earlier due to the fatigue of the All-Night Vigil.
[^539]: The Great Prokeimenon of Thomas Sunday Evening (Antipascha Vespers): Chanted in Tone 7 (Psalm 76:14–16 LXX): "Who is so great a god as our God? Thou art the God Who workest wonders!", with its three appointed verses, commemorating God's wonders in delivering His people.
[^540]: Daily Paschal opening statute: The rubric prescribes the singing of "Christ is risen from the dead" (thrice) in a quiet voice at the beginning of all daily liturgical services (the Hours, Vespers, Compline, Midnight Office, and Matins) from Antipascha until the Wednesday of the eve of the Ascension of Christ.
[^541]: Sessional Hymns (*Sedalny*) of Monday Matins of the Second Week after Pascha: Appointed in Tone 1, commemorating the Roman guards stunned as dead by the dazzling angel at the sepulcher, followed by the penitential and martyric stichera.
[^542]: Recensional editorial note in the margin of folio 209: The Ruthenian print notes that the penitential troparion "Conceived in transgressions am I, a prodigal..." (*V bezzakoniiakh zachat esm*) is absent from contemporary Greek printed imprints (*v ellinskom nest*), yet is deliberately retained here for customary liturgical usage (*obache upotrebleniia radi ostavikhom*).
[^543]: Monastic Mesoria (*Mezhochasie*): The appointment of the intermediate Third and Sixth Hours to be recited privately in the cells by the brethren during Paschaltide, continuing the ancient Studite-Jerusalem monastic rule.
[^544]: Ruthenian editorial preface on the Beatitudes Triodia (*na Blazhennakh Tripiesnets*): A notable liturgical-scholarly comment by the Lviv editors explaining that while contemporary Greek Pentecostaria do not contain the Triodia of St. Joseph the Hymnographer at the Beatitudes, the Ruthenian tradition faithfully retains them from historical Slavonic manuscripts, correcting their syntax and orthography while safeguarding pure Orthodox doctrine.
[^545]: Acrostic of the Monday Paschaltide Beatitudes Triodion: Composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer, bearing the Greek alphabetic acrostic `АСЕІДЕССІѠСИФ` (*Asei de se Ioseph* / "Joseph also hymns thee"), preserved in Cyrillic transliteration in the printed heading.
[^546]: Lectionary reading for Monday of the Second Week after Pascha: Gospel according to John, Pericope 6 (John 2:1–11), recounting the first miracle of Christ at the Marriage of Cana in Galilee, read at the Divine Liturgy.
[^547]: Liturgical structure of Tuesday of the Second Week after Pascha (Thomas Week): The ongoing post-festive celebration of Thomas Sunday interwoven with the general martyric hymns and the Resurrectional Stichera of Tone 4.
[^548]: Acrostic of the Tuesday Paschaltide Beatitudes Triodion: Composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer, bearing the Greek alphabetic acrostic `ѰАЛѠ ѲЕѠ ІѠСИФ` (*Psalo Theo Ioseph* / "I will sing unto God, Joseph"), transliterated in Cyrillic in the printed heading.
[^549]: Liturgical precedence of the Cross on Paschaltide Wednesdays and Fridays: A core rubrical statute stating: "Be it known that on Wednesdays and Fridays, the Stichera and Sessional Hymns of the Cross precede those of the Resurrection, because our Lord was first crucified and thereafter arose from the dead."
[^550]: Old Testament typological prefigurations in Wednesday Matins: The binding and sacrifice of Isaac upon the mount (Genesis 22) and the descent of Jonah into the deep (Jonah 1–2) as prophetic types of the Passion, burial, and third-day Resurrection of Christ.
[^551]: Podoben "O House of Ephratha" (*Dome Evfrathov*): Chanted in Tone 2 on Wednesday Aposticha of Thomas Week. The stichera poetically depict St. Thomas touching the unconsumed divine fire, urging the faithful to purify their hands and spiritual senses to celebrate Christ's Passion and Resurrection.
[^552]: Acrostic of the Wednesday Paschaltide Beatitudes Triodion: Composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer, bearing the Greek alphabetic acrostic `ΑΙΝΩ ΘΕΩ ΙΩΣΗΦ` (*Aino Theo Ioseph* / "Joseph hymns God"), transliterated directly into Cyrillic as `ДІНОѲЕѠНІѠСИФ` (with Slavonic explanation `Хвали́ Бг҃а І҆ѡ́сифъ`), with its troparia assigned to the Beatitudes verses across Odes 1, 8, and 9.
[^553]: Katavasia Heirmos of Ode 8 on Wednesday Beatitudes: Chanted as "A marvelous wonder: the burning bush remained unconsumed" (*Chudo prevelie: v kupine neopalimoi*), celebrating the divine fire that does not burn mortal flesh, typologically connecting the bush seen by Moses with Thomas touching Christ's side.
[^554]: Poetic accumulation of Paschal titles: In the Stichera of the Feast at Wednesday Vespers (Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree"), the hymnographer piles up ecstatic epithets: "The present Feast is Pascha, mystical Pascha, divine Pascha, saving Pascha, Pascha leading us unto immortal life, Pascha taking away every sorrow from our midst, Pascha granting the grace of gladness unto the disciples", maintaining the festive Paschal atmosphere throughout the Afterfeast of Antipascha.
[^555]: Patristic harp metaphor for the Martyrs: In the Martyric Sticheron at Wednesday Vespers Aposticha, the choir of holy martyrs is praised as "the all-harmonious harp (*vsetroinaia tsevnytsia*), moved by the Holy Spirit", which destroyed pagan idol shrines and proclaimed the Consubstantial Trinity across the nations.
[^556]: The Apostolic catalogue of Thursday Praises: Appointed on Thursday Matins (Thursday being the weekly liturgical commemoration of the Holy Apostles), this sticheron explicitly enumerates the Twelve Apostles: Peter, Paul, Luke, Matthew, Mark, John, Andrew, Thomas, Bartholomew, Simon the Cananite, James, and Philip, uniting them in the glory of the risen Lord.
[^557]: The theological and empirical polarity of John and Thomas: The Praises Doxastikon of Thursday Matins (Tone 4) draws a profound patristic contrast between St. John the Theologian, who reclined upon Christ's breast at the Mystical Supper and drew forth the deep contemplation of the Word's divinity, and St. Thomas, who touched His wounded side and provided the empirical, tactile confirmation of His bodily Resurrection.
[^558]: Acrostic of the Thursday Paschaltide Beatitudes Triodion: Composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer, bearing the Greek alphabetic acrostic `ΥΜΝΩ ΔΙ' ΙΩΣΗΦ` (*Ymno di' Ioseph* / "I sing through Joseph"), rendered in Cyrillic transliteration as `ѴМНѠ ДІА ІѠСИФ` (with the Slavonic gloss `Пѣ́нїе І҆ѡ́сифово`), distributed across Odes 1, 8, and 9.
[^559]: Liturgical precedence of the Cross on Paschaltide Fridays: Even within the joyful post-festive season of Pascha and Antipascha, Friday services retain their primary focus on the Holy Cross and the voluntary crucifixion of Christ, as reflected in the First Kathisma Sessional Hymn ("We venerate the wood of Thy Cross...") and the Stavrotheotokion.
[^560]: Acrostic of the Friday Paschaltide Beatitudes Triodion: Composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer, bearing the Greek alphabetic acrostic `ΔΟΞΑ ΤΩ ΘΕΩ ΑΜΗΝ` (*Doxa To Theo Amen* / "Glory to God, Amen"), transliterated in Cyrillic as `ДО́ХА ТѠ ѲЕѠ АМИН` (with the Slavonic gloss `Сла́ва Бг҃ꙋ а҆ми́нь`), distributed across Odes 5, 8, and 9.
[^561]: Codicological folio cross-reference: The rubric directs the reader to the Dogmatic Theotokion of Tone 4 (*Vsemirnuiu slavu*) printed on folio 147 (`л: рмз`), corresponding to physical leaf `p151` in this edition.
[^562]: The Paschaltide memorial canon statute: The rubric explicitly mandates that the Oktoechos Canon for the Departed (*Kanon o usopshykh*) of the current Tone, which is normally sung on Saturday Matins in non-Paschal seasons, must be sung on Friday evening at Compline (*na Pavechernitsakh*) throughout the fifty days of Pentecostarion, keeping Saturday Matins purely Resurrectional.
[^563]: Acrostic of the Saturday Paschaltide Beatitudes Tetraodion: Composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer, bearing the Greek alphabetic acrostic `ΠΡΩΤΟΝ ΜΕΛΠΩ ΜΕΛΟΣ` (*Prōton melpō melos* / "I sing the first song"), transliterated in Cyrillic as `ПРѠТОН МЕЛПО МЕЛѠС` (with the Slavonic gloss `Пе́рвое пою̀ Пѣ́нїе`). As a Saturday canon, it consists of four odes (Tetraodion: Odes 6, 7, 8, and 9), of which Odes 6, 7, and 8 appear on leaf p230, and Ode 9 on leaf p231.
[^564]: Saturday Apodosis (otdaniye) of Thomas Week and the resumption of the regular Saturday order: The rubric specifies that at the conclusion of the Liturgy on the Saturday of Renewal Week (Thomas Week), the post-festive celebration of St. Thomas is dismissed, and from this point forward until the Ascension of the Lord, every Saturday service is celebrated according to its complete standard order.
[^565]: The Dogmatic Theotokion of Tone 2 at Small Vespers: The rubric notes that according to the monastic practice of the Syncletics (the ancient Constantinopolitan or Studite monastic synclitic custom), the unusual Theotokion "O exceedingly great mystery!" (*O preveliya tainika!*) is sung at Small Vespers in place of the standard Great Vespers Dogmatikon (*Preide sen zakonnaya*).
[^566]: Podoben "When from the Tree" (*Egda ot dreva*): Appointed at Small Vespers for the Aposticha Theotokia, opening with the beloved Ruthenian Marian hymn "To all who sorrow, joy" (*Vsem skorbyshchym radost*), imploring the Mother of God as protectress of the afflicted, nurturer of the poor, consolation of strangers, staff of the blind, and visitant of the infirm.
[^567]: Codicological folio cross-reference: The rubric directs the reader to the Dogmatic Theotokion of Tone 2 ("The shadow of the law hath passed away", *Preide sen zakonnaya*) printed on folio 147 (`л: рмз`), corresponding to physical leaf `p151` in this edition.
[^568]: Codicological folio cross-reference: The rubric directs the reader to the Aposticha Doxastikon of Holy and Great Saturday ("Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment", *Tebe odeyushchagosya svetom*) printed on folio 122 (`ркв`), corresponding to physical leaf `p126` in this edition.
[^569]: Seasonal dispensation for the blessed bread (*emvle* / *artos*) at all-night vigils: The Typikon specifies that from April 23 (Feast of St. George) until September 24 (Feast of St. Thecla), because the summer nights are short, the blessed bread from the Artoklasia is not divided and eaten in the church following Great Vespers, but is taken to the refectory and distributed to the brethren at the beginning of the post-liturgical common meal.
[^570]: Liturgical confluence for the Feast of the Holy Great-Martyr George: A comprehensive Markian-type Typikon chapter detailing how the services are combined when the feast of St. George (April 23) coincides with the Sunday of the Myrrh-bearing Women, the Sunday of the Paralytic, or the Sunday of the Samaritan Woman, including specific rubrics for Vespers, Matins, and Liturgy.
[^571]: Acrostic of the Sunday Paschaltide Midnight Office Trinity Canon: Composed by Metrophanes of Smyrna, bearing the Greek alphabetic acrostic `ΤΟ ΤΡΙΣΣΟΝ ΥΜΝΩ ΘΕΑΡΧΙΑΣ ΣΕΛΑΣ` (*To trisson ymno thearchias selas* / "I sing the threefold light of the Godhead"), transliterated directly into Cyrillic as `ТО ТРІССӦН ҮМНѠ ѲЕӒРХІАЄ СЕЛА́С` (with the Slavonic explanation `Тргубый пою Богоначалїя свѣтъ`).
[^572]: Codicological folio cross-reference: The rubric directs the reader to the Resurrectional Evlogitaria ("The assembly of the Angels was amazed", *Angelsky sobor*) and Hypakoe of Tone 2 printed on folio 133 (`л: рлг`), corresponding to physical leaf `p137` in this edition.
[^573]: Acrostic of the Paschal Theotokia: In addition to the Paschal Canon of St. John of Damascus, a set of Theotokia composed by St. Theophanes the Branded is sung, bearing his name in the Greek acrostic `ѲЕѠФАНИС` (`ΘΕΟΦΑΝΗΣ` / *Theophanes*) across the first troparia of Odes 1 through 9.
[^574]: Second Canon of the Sunday of the Myrrh-bearing Women: Composed by St. Andrew of Crete in Tone 2, with the troparia arranged to carry his acrostic pattern (`На нем же поглавным Сн: Гласъ в`), featuring an expressive poetic dialogue between the Myrrh-bearing Women, Joseph, Nicodemus, and the Angel.
[^575]: Synaxarion of the Sunday of the Holy Myrrh-bearing Women, Righteous Joseph of Arimathea, and Nicodemus: Composed by Nikephoros Kallistos Xanthopoulos (c. 1256–1335), detailing the biblical and patristic traditions concerning Joseph and Nicodemus (including Nicodemus' expulsion from the Sanhedrin, Joseph's imprisonment and miraculous liberation, and their early written accounts of the Passion and Resurrection).
[^576]: Genealogy of Salome and the Myrrh-bearing Women according to the Byzantine-Ruthenian Synaxarion: Preserving the ancient Eastern tradition recorded by St. Epiphanius of Salamis and Nikephoros Kallistos that Joseph the Betrothed had four sons (James the Brother of the Lord/the Less, Joses, Simeon, and Jude) and three daughters (Esther, Tamar, and Salome the wife of Zebedee) by his first marriage, thereby making John the Evangelist and James the sons of Zebedee relatives of the Lord according to the flesh.
[^577]: Codicological folio cross-reference: The rubric refers to folio 133 (`сл҃г`), corresponding to physical leaf `p237` in this edition, where the Heirmoi, Troparia, and Theotokia of Ode 7 of the Paschal Canon of St. John of Damascus are printed.
[^578]: Third Matins Resurrection Gospel Sticheron (Eothinon 3), Tone 6 (*S myry prishedshim yazhe s Marieyu zhenam*): Attributed to Emperor Leo VI the Wise (886–912), based on the third Sunday Matins Resurrection Gospel reading from Mark 16:9–20 appointed for this Sunday.
[^579]: Codicological folio cross-reference: The rubric directs the reader to the Small Compline Trinity hymn printed on folio 138 (`сл҃и`), corresponding to physical leaf `p242` in this edition.
[^580]: Codicological folio cross-reference: The rubric refers to the weekday Matins beginning on folio 138 (`сл҃и`), corresponding to physical leaf `p242` in this edition.
[^581]: Greek Alphabetic Acrostic of the Monday Paschaltide Triodion at the Beatitudes: Composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer (c. 816–886), bearing the Greek acrostic `ᾌδει τάδε Ἰωσήφ` (*Adei tade Ioseph* / "Joseph sings these things"), transliterated directly into Cyrillic in the 1642 text as `А҆ДЕІ ТА ДЕ ІѠСИФ` (with the Slavonic gloss `Пое́тся І҆ѡ́сифъ`), with the letters distributed across the troparia of Odes 1 (`А-Д-Е-І`), 8 (`Т-А-Д-Е`), and 9 (`І-Ѡ-С-И-Ф`).
[^582]: Codicological folio cross-reference: The rubric refers to the Doxastikon of the Myrrh-bearing Women printed on folio 142 (`рм҃в`), corresponding to physical leaf `p246` in this edition.
[^583]: Codicological folio cross-reference: The rubric directs the reader to folio 121 (`ска҃`), corresponding to physical leaf `p125` in this edition, where the Aposticha Doxastikon of Holy and Great Saturday (*Pochto myro slezami, o uchenitsy, rastravlyayete?*) is printed.
[^584]: Codicological folio cross-reference: The rubric directs the reader to the Festive Troparia of the Myrrh-bearers printed on folio 144 (`рм҃д`), corresponding to physical leaf `p248` in this edition.
[^585]: Codicological folio cross-reference: The rubric refers to the Tuesday Matins Praises Doxastikon printed on folio 142 (`рм҃в`), corresponding to physical leaf `p246` in this edition.
[^586]: Greek Alphabetic Acrostic of the Tuesday Paschaltide Triodion at the Beatitudes: Composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer, bearing the acrostic `Χριστέ, σῶσόν με` (*Christe, soson me* / "O Christ, save me"), transliterated into Cyrillic as `ХРЇСТЕ СѠСОН МЕ` (with the Slavonic gloss `Хе҃ сп҃си мя`), spanning Odes 2, 8, and 9.
[^587]: Codicological folio cross-reference: The rubric directs the reader to the Aposticha Doxastikon of Holy and Great Saturday ("Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment", *Tebe odeyushchagosya svetom*) printed on folio 122 (`рк҃в`), corresponding to physical leaf `p126` in this edition.
